## Codebase Patterns
- Backend code lives under internal/controlplane/<pkg>; CLI stays under internal/cli. Keep HTTP handlers thin.
- Shared primitives are reused, not reinvented: typed errors come from internal/errors (yerr.New/Newf, CodeConfig, etc.); the redaction sentinel and Redactor come from internal/output.
- API success envelopes use yalla.output.v1; API errors use yalla.error.v1. ALL HTTP responses render through internal/controlplane/apienvelope (WriteData/WriteError/WriteErrorStatus); envelope structs are unexported so handlers cannot hand-roll JSON. HTTP status derives from the error Code via apienvelope.StatusForCode (WriteErrorStatus is the explicit-override escape hatch, e.g. /readyz 503). Error envelopes carry documentation_url; apienvelope is also a regex-based redaction backstop for message/hint/warnings.
- Config is env-only for backend processes (no flags/files): internal/controlplane/config.Load(LookupFunc) layers env vars over per-profile defaults, then validates. cmd/yalla-api and cmd/yalla-worker call config.LoadFromEnv() once at startup and os.Exit(1) on error.
- Every config/validation failure is a typed yerr CodeConfig error and must NEVER echo a secret value. Tests assert secrets do not appear in error strings, Redacted(), LogValue(), or String().
- Secret-bearing structs implement slog.LogValuer + fmt.Stringer with redacted views as defence-in-depth.
- Tests inject dependencies (e.g. config.MapLookup) instead of touching real process env/filesystem. Use t.Parallel().
- Quality gate before commit: gofmt -w ., go mod tidy, go build ./..., go vet ./..., go test ./..., go test -race ./....
- Mutating POST endpoints reject organization_id in the request body via the strict JSON decoder (validate.DecodeJSON disallows unknown fields). The handler builds the store *Input.OrganizationID from p.OrganizationID (principal), never from the body, so the tenant boundary is structural — a caller cannot point a create at another tenant even if defense-in-depth were skipped. ActorOrgID on the audit record is also the principal's home org; ResourceID names the created resource.
- store.ProjectService is the canonical write-port unit-of-work with the full 5-step composition: authorize, reserve quota, INSERT, enqueue provisioning job, append audit — all in one Store.Write transaction. cmd/yalla-api wires placeholder alwaysAllowAuthorizer/noopQuotaReserver/noopJobEnqueuer adapters today (HTTP RequireAuth is the authoritative gate; real adapters land with the plan-resolver and provisioning-worker stories). store.NewProjectService rejects nil dependencies at construction, so placeholders must remain explicit-named values, never nil.
- Partial-update (PATCH) endpoints: the request DTO and store *Input use *string fields — nil means "field omitted, leave unchanged". The store builds+validates the patch BEFORE opening the transaction (rejecting a patch that names no field as a 400), then inside Store.Write reads the current row, applies the non-nil fields, and Updates. A repository Update using UPDATE ... RETURNING must check errors.Is(err, pgx.ErrNoRows) for the missing-row case BEFORE mapWriteError (which only classifies constraint-violation vs outage). Never set updated_at in an Update statement — the per-table set_updated_at() BEFORE UPDATE trigger owns it.
- Customer-facing DELETE endpoints schedule a SOFT delete, never a hard delete: the store stamps a nullable deletion_scheduled_at column (migration 0010 for organizations) inside the same transaction as the <resource>.delete audit record, and the endpoint returns 202 Accepted. A hard DELETE would cascade through child tables AND the audit log (ON DELETE CASCADE), so the destructive teardown is a separate worker story. Re-scheduling an already-stamped row is apierr.Conflict, detected with a Get inside the tx (the repository ScheduleDeletion is an unconditional UPDATE).
- Optimistic concurrency on mutable customer-data tables: migration 0011 adds a bigint version column (DEFAULT 1, CHECK > 0) + bump_version() BEFORE UPDATE trigger to organizations/projects/environments/services. The trigger overwrites NEW.version with OLD.version+1 on every UPDATE (including no-op data UPDATEs), so the application cannot freeze the sequence. Repository UPDATE methods accept ifMatchVersion *int64: nil = no precondition, non-nil = WHERE id=$1 AND version=$N; on no rows, a tenant-scoped Get inside the same tx disambiguates "row gone" (NotFound) from "row stale" (apierr.ConflictStale carrying the row's current version). Service-layer Update/ScheduleDeletion also pre-check the version after the read so a no-op data patch still surfaces the conflict against the right row. The HTTP layer parses RFC 7232 strong ETag values (`"<n>"`, lenient unquoted form accepted; weak/wildcard/multi-value rejected as 400 E_INVALID_INPUT) from If-Match, mirrors the row's authoritative version into the ETag response header on every success path (GET/POST/PATCH/DELETE), and surfaces stale writes as 409 E_CONFLICT carrying current_version under the new structured error.details map (apierr.DetailKeyCurrentVersion). yerr.Error.Details (map[string]string, set by WithDetail) is the agent-friendly counterpart to Hint; apienvelope renders it through the same regex redaction backstop used for Message/Hint.
- Process lifecycle is owned by internal/controlplane/runtime: RunHTTPServer(ctx, srv, shutdownTimeout, logger) for the API and worker.Loop for background jobs. Backend binaries derive shutdown from one signal.NotifyContext and pass cfg.ShutdownTimeout; they do not hand-roll select/Shutdown blocks.
- runtime.Readiness holds named startup gates (concurrency-safe). httpapi.NewHandler takes a runtime.ReadinessReporter; /readyz must be 503 yalla.error.v1 E_SERVER until every gate passes, 200 yalla.output.v1 after. /healthz is liveness-only and never depends on downstream checks.
- worker.Loop depends only on the Claimer/Lease interfaces so the durable job store can land later. The loop checks ctx before every Claim (stop claiming on shutdown) and releases interrupted in-flight leases on a context.WithoutCancel context so the release survives shutdown. A job whose Run returns nil is committed and must never be released.
- Lifecycle code is tested with fakes (fake httpServer, fake Claimer/Lease) — no real sockets, no live deps.
- Request correlation lives in internal/controlplane/telemetry. telemetry.Correlate is the outermost HTTP middleware (httpapi.NewHandler wraps the mux in it): it resolves request_id (per request) + correlation_id (per workflow) onto the request context before any handler runs. Inbound X-Request-Id/X-Correlation-Id are honoured only if telemetry.SafeID passes ([A-Za-z0-9._-], 1..128) — unsafe values are dropped and a fresh telemetry.NewRequestID() is minted, so injection payloads never reach headers/logs/jobs. Read IDs anywhere via telemetry.FromContext/RequestID/CorrelationID; never thread them through signatures. Non-HTTP entry points seed context with telemetry.WithCorrelation.
- Per-request structured logging lives in internal/controlplane/telemetry: telemetry.RequestLogging(logger) is the middleware just inside Correlate (httpapi.NewHandler wraps the routed mux in it). One JSON record per request: method/route/target/status/latency_ms/bytes/request_id/correlation_id, plus org_id/principal_id when set via telemetry.SetOrgID/SetPrincipalID (a request-scoped mutable holder the auth/policy layers enrich). Headers and bodies are never logged; the request target is redacted. Level follows the outcome (5xx error, 4xx warn, else info); the logger threshold (YALLA_LOG_LEVEL) gates emission. Service binaries bind service via logger.With and log JSON to stdout.
- Backend errors are built through internal/controlplane/apierr, the catalogued error taxonomy — never yerr.New directly in handlers/services. apierr constructors return *yerr.Error (so they flow into apienvelope.WriteError); apierr.Catalog/Lookup pair every code with HTTP status (derived from apienvelope.StatusForCode — single source of truth), retry semantics, and a MessagePolicy. MessageGeneric codes (internal + all dependency failures) keep the cause out of Message/Hint and only Wrap it for logging. InvalidInput carries FieldViolation{Field,Reason} (path + reason, never the submitted value), recoverable via apierr.ViolationsOf; dependency failures are recoverable via apierr.DependencyOf even when they share a code. Adding a code = define the yerr.Code constant in internal/errors (and its codeDescriptions, AllCodes, ExitCode entries — the code is shared with the CLI manifest) + add to apierr.taxonomy + add an apierr constructor + (if non-500) a apienvelope.StatusForCode case; update the TestStatusForCode / TestConstructorsEmitCataloguedCodes / TestCode_ExitCodeMappingIsStable tables.
- Schema changes are versioned SQL files under internal/controlplane/store/migrate/migrations/ named NNNN_name.up.sql (required) + NNNN_name.down.sql (optional, reversible-only), embedded via //go:embed. The migrate.Migrator runner owns a checksummed schema_migrations ledger, serialises with a pg advisory lock, and runs each migration in its own transaction. Migrator.Up is idempotent and refuses dirty / unknown-version / checksum-mismatch databases (errors.Is sentinels ErrDirtyDatabase/ErrUnknownVersion/ErrChecksumMismatch). NEVER edit an applied migration file (trips ErrChecksumMismatch by design) — add a new higher-versioned migration. Persistence tests are integration tests: they connect via YALLA_TEST_DATABASE_URL, create an isolated throwaway database per test, and t.Skip when the env var is unset so go test ./... stays green without Postgres. docker-compose.yml at repo root provisions local Postgres. pgx (github.com/jackc/pgx/v5 + pgxpool) is the Postgres driver.
- Persistence tests use the shared harness internal/controlplane/testutil: RequireDB(t) gives an empty isolated throwaway Postgres database per test, RequireMigratedDB(t) also applies migrations; both t.Skip with testutil.SkipReason when YALLA_TEST_DATABASE_URL is unset and drop the DB on cleanup (parallel-safe — unique DB name per call). testutil.NewFactory(t) builds deterministic, globally-unique in-memory fixtures for the Organization->Project->Environment->Service hierarchy + User/APIKey (two factories never collide). testutil.AssertRedacted/AssertRedactedValue guard "must not leak a secret" assertions. store/migrate keeps its own local testPool — it cannot import testutil (import cycle: testutil imports migrate). Keep pure decision logic (lookupAdminDSN, scanLeaks) unexported + white-box tested, since testing.TB is sealed and cannot be faked.
- Store-backed httpapi endpoints use a narrow reader-port pattern: httpapi declares the port interface (e.g. OrganizationReader), a Store.Read-backed adapter in the store package satisfies it by composing the tenant-scoped repository (mirror store.CredentialReader — never issue fresh SQL), and the port is threaded NewHandler -> newRouteTable -> the handler closure. httpapi imports store (acyclic — store never imports httpapi). For an endpoint with no path/query parameter, tenant isolation is structural: the handler only ever passes principal.OrganizationID. NewHandler signature: NewHandler(build, readiness, meta, authenticator, engine, orgs, logger).
- HTTP routes are data-driven from internal/controlplane/httpapi/routes.go newRouteTable — the single source of truth. Each apiRoute pairs a served http.HandlerFunc with its openapi.Endpoint metadata; httpapi.NewHandler registers every entry on the mux AND generates the OpenAPI document from the same table, so a served route is always a documented route (TestEveryRegisteredRouteIsDocumented enforces it in CI). Never call mux.HandleFunc directly in NewHandler — add to newRouteTable. The OpenAPI 3.1 document is built by internal/controlplane/openapi.Build from a neutral []openapi.Endpoint, references the two stable envelope schemas, is served raw (not enveloped) at GET /openapi.json without auth, and marshals maps with sorted keys for a byte-stable artifact. Secret-shaped sample values use output.Sentinel.
- Domain tables live in versioned migrations under store/migrate/migrations/. Every customer-data table carries organization_id (directly or via a verified parent chain) and is tenant-isolated at the database level by COMPOSITE foreign keys: a child FK (organization_id, parent_id) references the parent's UNIQUE (organization_id, id), making a cross-tenant child unrepresentable. Slugs are citext, unique within parent scope, never global. users is a global identity table; org membership is the memberships join table. Schema-level integration tests live in package store_test (NOT package migrate — it can't import testutil) and match constraint violations via *pgconn.PgError SQLSTATE class 23.
- Persistence access goes through internal/controlplane/store: Store.Read (read-only tx -> Querier) and Store.Write (read/write tx -> *Tx; commit on nil, rollback on ANY error or panic). A *Tx is obtainable ONLY inside Store.Write; repository mutation methods take *Tx, read methods take Querier — so a mutation structurally cannot run outside the transaction that also carries its authorization and quota checks. A unit of work validates input first, then composes authorize -> reserve quota -> write desired state -> enqueue durable job inside one Store.Write. The policy/quota/jobs dependencies are narrow port interfaces defined IN store (Authorizer, QuotaReserver, JobEnqueuer); store never imports those packages. Error mapping is uniform: pgx.ErrNoRows -> apierr.NotFound, SQLSTATE class 23 -> apierr.Conflict, other driver errors -> apierr.StoreUnavailable; the raw cause is wrapped for logging only. ProjectRepository/ProjectService are the reference implementation.
- Credentials: internal/controlplane/auth owns generation/hashing/verification; internal/controlplane/store owns persistence. A secret is stored ONLY as a one-way hash plus a public, globally-UNIQUE prefix — never plaintext. Plaintext credential types implement String/GoString/LogValue/MarshalJSON returning output.Sentinel; the plaintext is reachable only via an explicit Reveal() method. High-entropy secrets (>=128 bits random) use fast unsalted SHA-256 + subtle.ConstantTimeCompare, NOT bcrypt/argon2. Authentication lookup (FindByPrefix) is the ONE deliberate non-tenant-scoped repository read — it runs before the tenant is known; the found row carries its own OrganizationID. Tenant-scoped mutations that may match zero rows report apierr.NotFound via tag.RowsAffected()==0.
- Authorization is internal/controlplane/policy: policy.Engine is a pure, total decision function — Decide(Principal, Action, Resource) -> Decision{Allow, Reason} (stable Reason codes); Authorize/AuthorizeCtx map denials onto typed apierr (Unauthenticated for a missing principal, Forbidden otherwise). Every endpoint maps to exactly one policy.Action; the action lives in policy's defaultActionCatalog (the authoritative requiredAction -> Capability map) — add new actions there or via policy.WithAction. Roles confer Capability sets (self/read/deploy/write/admin/owner/support), not individual actions; six built-ins are fixed, custom roles resolve through a CustomRoleResolver hook. Scoped Grants narrow/widen authority within an org via Scope containment over Organization->Project->Environment->Service; cross-tenant resources are denied by construction except support reads/break-glass. The principal travels on the request context via policy.WithPrincipal/PrincipalFromContext — never thread it through signatures. The store.Authorizer port is satisfied by this engine; store still never imports policy. TestPolicyMatrix exhaustively covers all built-in roles x catalogued actions.
- Request authentication is internal/controlplane/auth.Authenticator: it resolves any of the three bearer schemes (API key yk_..., human session JWT, internal worker secret) to one auth.Identity{Principal, Method}. Every invalid-credential cause collapses to the single ErrInvalidCredentials sentinel (missing creds = ErrNoCredentials) so no error is a "does this key prefix exist?" oracle; a genuine datastore failure is propagated unchanged as a typed *yerr.Error, never disguised as invalid credentials. The Authenticator depends on the narrow auth.CredentialStore port (+ APIKeyRecord/ServiceAccountRecord DTOs) so auth never imports store; the production adapter is store.CredentialReader (store imports auth, not the reverse). The HTTP wrappers are httpapi.RequireAuth(authenticator, engine, action, ResourceResolver) and httpapi.RequireInternalWorker — they authenticate, attach the principal via policy.WithPrincipal + telemetry.SetOrgID/SetPrincipalID, then authorize: missing creds 401 E_AUTH, invalid 401 E_AUTH (fixed generic message), unauthorized 403 E_FORBIDDEN (stable policy reason), dependency failure keeps its typed 5xx. ResourceResolver nil defaults to the principal's own org scope; pass a path-param resolver so a cross-tenant id is a 403, not a silent allow.
- Quota schema (0006_quota_policy_schema): a closed enum reused across tables is a Postgres DOMAIN over text + CHECK (here quota_resource over 14 dimensions, quota_enforcement_mode over hard/soft/metered/disabled); DROP DOMAIN goes in the down migration after the tables. quota_policies holds one limit per (scope, resource) where a row is EITHER a plan_default (text plan, no plans-table FK yet) OR an organization override — enforced by one table CHECK + two partial unique indexes; the checker resolves "org override else plan default". quota_usage is one counter row per (org, resource); quota_reservations are short-lived (amount>0, status active->committed|released|expired, expires_at, settled_at tied to non-active status by CHECK) and expose UNIQUE (organization_id, id) as a composite-FK target; usage_events is append-only (no updated_at/trigger, signed delta) and links reservations via a tenant-safe composite FK. An optional cross-table link inside a tenant uses a plain composite FK (MATCH SIMPLE) — NOT ON DELETE SET NULL, which would null the NOT NULL organization_id.
- Quota enforcement: the QuotaRepository (internal/controlplane/store/quota.go) is the persistence half (EffectiveLimit / LockUsage / SumActiveReservations / InsertReservation, all tenant-scoped, mutations on *Tx) and the quota.Checker (internal/controlplane/quota) is the store.QuotaReserver service half. A hard limit is enforced by LockUsage doing INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING then SELECT ... FOR UPDATE on the quota_usage counter row — that lock serialises concurrent reservers so they cannot both over-allocate; the decision is current+reserved+requested <= limit, reserved = SUM of active non-expired reservations. Soft/metered limits record but never reject; disabled/no-policy allow with no write. A rejection is apierr.QuotaExceeded wrapping a recoverable quota.ExceededDetail{Resource,Current,Reserved,Requested,Limit}. Quota accounting rows have no domain.Kind — ids are minted locally with a prefixed crypto/rand helper.
- Audit log (0007_audit_events, store/audit.go, internal/controlplane/audit): the immutable audit log records every authorization decision, allowed AND denied. Immutability is enforced at two layers — the table has no updated_at and a BEFORE UPDATE trigger (audit_events_reject_update) that rejects every UPDATE in the database, and AuditRepository exposes only Append + tenant-scoped ListByOrganization (no update/delete); row DELETE stays reachable only via the organizations ON DELETE CASCADE. An append-only table that must still cascade on tenant teardown blocks UPDATE only, never DELETE. The audit.Auditor (depends on the narrow Recorder port, satisfied by *store.AuditRepository) is the single place a policy.Decision becomes an AuditEvent: it pulls the principal (policy.PrincipalFromContext) + correlation ids (telemetry.FromContext) off the context, files the event under the principal's org else the resource's org, and REDACTS metadata before persistence — secret-shaped keys (token/secret/password/credential/...) replaced wholesale with output.Sentinel, every other value + the user agent scrubbed by an output.Redactor. store treats metadata as already-redacted; redaction is the audit service's contract. Auditor.Record takes the caller's *store.Tx so an audit write can share the mutation's transaction or run in its own.
- Provisioning jobs (0008_provisioning_jobs, store/job.go): the durable, idempotent queue of Dokploy provisioning work. The provisioning_jobs table is tenant-scoped (organization_id FK + composite (organization_id, project_id|environment_id|service_id) FKs so a job can never target another tenant's resource), idempotent (UNIQUE (organization_id, idempotency_key)), and exposes UNIQUE (organization_id, id) as a composite-FK target — 0008 also wires the FK that 0006 deferred (quota_reservations.job_id -> provisioning_jobs). DB CHECKs enforce the closed status set, "terminal status iff finished_at set", "lease held iff status=running", and attempts<=max_attempts; the Go state machine (store.JobStatus.CanTransitionTo, backed by the jobTransitions map: queued->running|cancelled, running->succeeded|retrying|failed|dead_letter|cancelled, retrying->running|cancelled, terminals have no edges) is the authoritative validator. JobRepository follows the ProjectRepository pattern (mutations need *Tx, reads take Querier, NotFound for cross-tenant ids); Transition locks the row FOR UPDATE, validates the edge before any write (illegal edge = apierr.Conflict), and derives lease/attempt/timestamp bookkeeping from the target status so a caller cannot leave a CHECK-violating row. error_summary is run through an output.NewRedactor() backstop on every write (Insert + Transition) — the store IS the redaction chokepoint for job errors, unlike audit metadata which the service redacts.
- HTTP idempotency (0009_idempotency_keys, store/idempotency.go, httpapi/idempotency.go): mutating endpoints are made retry-safe by httpapi.RequireIdempotency(IdempotencyStore, ttl) — middleware installed INSIDE RequireAuth (it reads policy.PrincipalFromContext to scope the key to a principal within a tenant). Safe methods and unsafe requests with no Idempotency-Key pass straight through (opt-in). The claim protocol: store.IdempotencyRepository.Claim is a WHERE-guarded upsert — INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE ... WHERE expires_at <= now() — so it freshly claims, transparently steals an expired row (claimed=true), or returns the existing live claim (claimed=false, RETURNING yields no row, follow-up Find reads the row the DO UPDATE attempt locked). On claimed=true the middleware runs the handler against a buffering responseCapture, then Complete records the rendered envelope (status < 500) or Release drops the claim (status >= 500, so a server failure is retried not replayed). On claimed=false: hash mismatch -> apierr.IdempotencyConflict (E_IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT, the only use of the new code), completed -> replay the recorded body+status with Idempotency-Replayed: true, still pending -> apierr.Conflict "already in progress". The request hash is sha256(method\npath?query\nbody); the body is buffered then restored as a fresh r.Body so the handler still reads it. Keys are scoped UNIQUE (organization_id, principal_id, idempotency_key); a completion CHECK ties status='completed' to a non-null response. The store-backed IdempotencyStore adapter (httpapi.NewIdempotencyStore) runs each repo call in its own Store.Write — RequireIdempotency is implemented and tested but not yet wired into a route (no mutating endpoints exist yet), same as RequireAuth.
- Granular 404-vs-409 on INSERT requires a pre-check inside Store.Write: store.mapWriteError collapses every SQLSTATE class 23 violation (unique, foreign-key, check, not-null) to one apierr.Conflict, so without a pre-check a missing FK target surfaces as 409 instead of the correct 404. MembershipService.Add Gets the organization (typed NotFound) and UserExists-checks the user (typed NotFound) before INSERT, leaving the PK duplicate as the only remaining violation — correctly 409. Pre-check parent existence whenever an endpoint must distinguish "missing referent" from "already exists".
- Mutating httpapi endpoints follow the writer-port pattern, mirroring the reader-port one: httpapi declares a narrow creator/writer port (e.g. OrganizationCreator), and a store-package unit-of-work orchestrator (OrganizationService, like ProjectService/ServiceAccountService) satisfies it. The orchestrator validates input BEFORE opening a transaction (buildXToCreate is split out + white-box unit-tested with no DB), then composes the desired-state write + audit append inside ONE Store.Write so a created row can never exist without its audit record. The orchestrator audits via *store.AuditRepository directly (it is in the store package — no cycle) and builds the store.AuditEvent from plain-string actor/correlation fields the handler passes in CreateXInput (ActorID/ActorKind/ActorOrgID/RequestID/CorrelationID) — store takes no dependency on policy/telemetry. The audit event is filed under the actor's home org; metadata only carries structurally-safe values (the canonical slug). The handler stays thin: read principal, validate.DecodeJSON the body, pull telemetry.FromContext, call the port, render http.StatusCreated. NewHandler signature is now NewHandler(build, readiness, meta, authenticator, engine, orgs, creator, logger); newRouteTable takes (build, readiness, meta, orgs, creator). Follow-up: mutation audits do not yet capture IP/UA and do not route through audit.Auditor's redactor; denied-decision auditing at the RequireAuth layer is still unwired for every endpoint.
- Postgres-backed worker queue (store/job.go ClaimNext, worker/queue.go): the durable queue has two halves. store.JobRepository.ClaimNext is the SKIP LOCKED claim — SELECT ... FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED picks the single most-eligible job (queued|retrying AND next_run_at<=now, OR running AND lease_deadline<now) so concurrent claimers skip a locked row instead of blocking, then transitions it to running inside the same Store.Write tx. An expired-lease job is reclaimed THROUGH the state machine (running->retrying->running, so attempt/lease bookkeeping stays consistent); a job past its retry budget is dead-lettered instead and the poll claims nothing. worker/queue.go is the store-backed Claimer/Lease impl: loop.go stays pure (interfaces only, fake-tested) and queue.go is the only worker file that imports store. Failure classification: worker.Terminal(err)/IsTerminal mark a permanent failure (-> failed, never retried); a plain error is transient (-> retrying with worker.Backoff bounded exponential delay, or -> dead_letter when Attempts>=MaxAttempts). StoreClaimer.complete() records every outcome on a context.WithoutCancel + timeout context — once the runner ran, the outcome write must survive shutdown; only an interrupted (un-run) job returns non-nil from Lease.Run so the loop releases it. The JobRunner (real Dokploy provisioner) is still injected/faked; cmd/yalla-worker stays on NoopClaimer until that runner lands.
- Fake Dokploy (internal/controlplane/dokploy/dokployfake): the default Dokploy for EVERY normal test — a live Dokploy server is an opt-in external smoke test only. dokployfake.New() returns a *Server backed by httptest serving a Dokploy-shaped REST API (organization/project/environment/application/compose/database/domain/deployment/logs/status). It mimics Dokploy's own JSON shapes, NOT yalla.output.v1/yalla.error.v1 — it stands in for the upstream provisioning API, not a Yalla public surface (so the "stable envelope" acceptance criterion is N/A for Dokploy-facing test doubles). IDs are deterministic per-kind counters (org_1, proj_1, ...). Server.QueueFault arms a FIFO fault queue (StatusFault for 4xx/5xx, TimeoutFault honouring request cancellation, MalformedJSONFault); each request consumes one fault, applied before auth+routing. Every request is recorded (Server.Requests()) with Authorization/X-Api-Key/X-Auth-Token/Cookie header values replaced by output.Sentinel and the body run through an output.Redactor seeded with the bearer token — so a worker test can prove credentials were sent without the secret ever entering the fixture. The package depends only on internal/output + stdlib (no testing.TB, no store), so any package's tests import it without a cycle.
- Typed Dokploy client (internal/controlplane/dokploy.Client): customer/handler code NEVER calls Dokploy — only worker/provisioner code, through this typed wrapper. The client exposes Yalla intents (EnsureProject/EnsureService/DeployService/ReadDeploymentLogs/RemoveService/...), not raw Dokploy ops. The Dokploy token is injected via Config.Token, held unexported, exposed by no method, and redacted by Client.LogValue. "Ensure" intents are idempotent against Yalla's source of truth: an optional ExistingID does GET+verify, an empty one does POST+create (the worker records the returned Dokploy ID in Postgres). Only idempotent methods (GET, DELETE) are retried — never POST — and only on apierr.Retryable errors, with bounded exponential backoff; each attempt gets a per-request context timeout and forwards X-Request-Id/X-Correlation-Id from telemetry.FromContext. Every failure maps onto the apierr taxonomy (400->Invalid, 401/403->Internal as a non-retryable Yalla misconfig, 404->NotFound, 409->Conflict, 429/5xx/transport->DokployUnavailable, deadline->Timeout(DependencyDokploy)); the redacted upstream body is wrapped only as the log-only cause, never a client-facing Message. Normal tests run against dokployfake; the client touches no Postgres.
- Dokploy provisioning has two pure, no-I/O layers under internal/controlplane/dokploy that the worker composes: the Mapper (mapping.go) is the ID-resolution layer — top-down translation of the Yalla* hierarchy into Ensure*Input intents, each child method taking the parent's resolved Dokploy ID; the Renderer (renderer.go) is the declarative-content layer — Render(RenderInput) -> RenderedSpec resolves variables (ascending precedence org<project<environment<service), zero-fills ResourceLimits from tier defaults, normalises BuildSettings, and emits deterministic names/labels/domains, needing no resolved parent IDs. Inapplicable fields are DROPPED, not errored (engine for non-db, role for db, cron_schedule for non-cron, domains for non-web) — mirror this convention for any new provisioning shape. RenderedSpec carries verbatim variable values for the worker; RenderedSpec.Summary() / LogValue redact EVERY variable value (not just Secret-flagged) for logs/audit. Golden tests use testutil.GoldenJSON + the global -update-golden flag, goldens live in testdata/<feature>/.
- Request input validation goes through internal/controlplane/validate: build a validate.New() Collector, call validators (Name/Slug/ID/Path/URL/Domain/Resources/EnvVars/GitRepoURL/GitBranch/GitCommit/ImageRef) against it, return c.Err() — nil or one apierr.InvalidInput carrying every collected violation (no short-circuit). A FieldViolation.Reason NEVER echoes the submitted value (classification text + bounds only) — this is what lets a secret env var be rejected without leaking. Policy-dependent rules are passed in as plain options the caller resolves (Domain takes DomainOptions{AllowWildcard}, set only after feature flag + quota say yes); validate imports no config/quota. EnvVars separates secret vs non-secret via a distinct ".secret[i]" path segment and a larger secret length ceiling. validate.DecodeJSON is the strict JSON body decoder: size-capped, rejects unknown fields + trailing data, every failure is a body-free apierr.Invalid. Every string validator has a fuzz target seeded with long strings / invalid UTF-8 / path traversal / control chars.
- Customer-facing soft-delete endpoints come in two shapes. Organization.delete uses a deletion_scheduled_at stamp + 202 Accepted (asynchronous teardown by a future worker; re-scheduling is apierr.Conflict). API-key DELETE is a one-way revocation: the api_keys.revoked_at column is stamped and the credential is permanently unusable from that moment (the auth-layer IsUsable check already rejects a revoked row), the row stays so the audit trail of who minted it stays linked to it, response is 200 OK with the revoked snapshot (mirroring members.Remove's terminal-view convention). The repository (APIKeyRepository.Revoke) is silently idempotent at SQL via COALESCE(revoked_at, $now), but the service layer (APIKeyService.Revoke) pre-checks current.IsRevoked() inside the tx and returns apierr.Conflict on re-revoke — the customer's view of the lifecycle is stale and a silent success would write a misleading audit record. Pattern: customer endpoints surface stale-lifecycle conflicts; repository methods stay SQL-idempotent for non-customer callers (workers, admin jobs).
- "Add policy matrix coverage for <endpoint>" stories add a <endpoint>_policy_test.go beside the endpoint's contract test, in package httpapi, driving the REAL NewHandler + real policy.NewEngine() (the production authorization path, fake Authenticator only). Table the principal classes the PRD names: every built-in role, revoked/expired credentials (modelled as Disabled principals — the only shape in which a revoked/expired credential reaches the engine; the auth layer's earlier 401 path is the contract test's job), and a wrong-organization principal. For a CapSelf endpoint the matrix is "all allow" — assert the served data is the caller's OWN identity, never escalated/cross-tenant. Grant-containment criteria (sibling project / production env / parent-level secret non-leak) are engine properties: assert them with policy.NewEngine().Decide directly, then tie back by proving the endpoint echoes the confined grant set verbatim. Reuse the existing helpers (meHandlerFor/getMe/decodeMe, middleware_test.go's decodeError) — a sibling _test.go needs no new scaffolding.
- HTTP endpoints are data-driven entries in internal/controlplane/httpapi/routes.go newRouteTable: apiRoute{endpoint, handler, resolver}. Authorization is declarative — set openapi.Endpoint.RequiresAuth=true + RequiredAction=string(policy.ActionX), and NewHandler auto-wraps the handler in RequireAuth(authenticator, engine, action, resolver) before registering it, so a served authenticated route is structurally impossible without authorization. TestEveryAuthenticatedRouteHasMappedAction enforces the RequiresAuth<->RequiredAction invariant in CI. The optional apiRoute.resolver derives the policy.Resource from path/query params; leave it nil for self/org-root actions (RequireAuth then authorizes against the principal's own org scope). NewHandler signature is (build, readiness, meta, authenticator, engine, logger); httpapi tests build it through the newTestHandler helper (canned fakeAuthenticator + policy.NewEngine()). Handlers stay thin: read policy.PrincipalFromContext, render via apienvelope.WriteData/WriteError. cmd/yalla-api wires the real stack at startup (pgxpool -> store.New -> store.NewCredentialReader -> auth.NewAuthenticator + policy.NewEngine) and requires YALLA_DATABASE_URL in every profile.
- Drift reconciliation is internal/controlplane/reconcile: a pure engine that classifies every Yalla<->Dokploy divergence into exactly one of three buckets — DriftSafe (auto-repaired through the Repairer port: env var ensures/updates/removals, re-bound managed domains), DriftDangerous (recorded through the ReviewRecorder port: missing managed service, missing managed database, renamed domain on a known Dokploy ID, service type changed), or DriftUnmanaged (a Dokploy resource without a Yalla counterpart, recorded through the UnmanagedRecorder port — never auto-deleted and never exposed to customers by default). Diff(desired, actual) is a pure function; the Reconciler depends only on the four port interfaces (DesiredStateReader, ActualStateReader, Repairer, ReviewRecorder, UnmanagedRecorder) so adapters land in store/worker/dokploy without reaching back into reconcile. Every classification field carries a stable, value-free DriftReason (env_var_changed, domain_renamed, service_missing, database_missing, resource_unmanaged, ...) — an env var value reaches Action only via DesiredValue, consumed by the Repairer adapter and nowhere else. Apply is best-effort: per-action failures land in Result.Failures with their error string scrubbed through output.Redactor; the loop only stops on ctx cancellation. Untyped adapter errors are upgraded to apierr.StoreUnavailable (desired) or apierr.DokployUnavailable (actual); typed apierr.* from adapters (NotFound, Forbidden) flow through unchanged so callers keep the real taxonomy.
- Crockford Base32 (lowercase) excludes i / l / o / u — hand-rolled test IDs containing those letters (e.g. "demo", "kvl", "old", "ufo") fail domain.ParseID with E_INVALID_INPUT. Use domain.MustNewID(domain.KindX) for fixtures (the convention in dokploy/mapping_test.go and reconcile_test.go); a test failure where every port-level case fails with the same E_INVALID_INPUT is almost always the ID validator rejecting the fixture before any port runs.
- ON CONFLICT against a PARTIAL unique index requires the WHERE predicate in the conflict_target clause (`ON CONFLICT (cols) WHERE <same predicate> DO UPDATE`), otherwise Postgres rejects the upsert because the index is not eligible. quota_policies_org_resource_idx is the canonical case: ON CONFLICT (organization_id, resource) WHERE scope_kind = 'organization' DO UPDATE. The freshly-minted id from newQuotaID is only used on insert; on conflict the existing row's id and created_at are preserved (and updated_at is owned by the per-table trigger).
- List/upsert PATCH endpoints (e.g. PATCH .../limits): the request DTO carries a top-level array (not a partial-update of individual fields), each entry is an upsert, and the response is the post-write effective state re-read inside the SAME Store.Write tx — so the wire body and the committed state agree byte-for-byte. Validation rules: at least one entry (empty array = 400), every entry must be self-consistent (closed-set resource, range-checked limit, closed-set enforcement mode, no duplicate keys across entries), and a `*int64` for numeric fields whose zero is meaningful distinguishes "field omitted" (400) from "set to zero" (legitimate extreme). Field paths use `field[i].subfield` so an agent can pinpoint which entry was rejected.
- Nullable scalar fields that always travel together project as a SINGLE nullable nested OBJECT on the wire, never as coupled-nullable scalars. GET /v1/organizations/{org_id}/usage is the canonical case: `{resource, used_value, limit: null}` for unconstrained vs `{resource, used_value, limit: {limit_value, enforcement_mode, source}}` when a policy is configured — three nullable scalar siblings would force every agent to handle each invalid (limit_value null but enforcement_mode set, etc) permutation. The store struct mirrors the wire: a single `*Limit` (or three pointers projected together with an all-or-nothing guard in the projector). Adding a new field to the nested object later (e.g. a billing period for metered limits) is then forward-compatible with existing readers, whereas adding a new top-level field next to coupled scalars is not.
- Reads that union "configured" + "live" state for the same key set (a quota policy join with quota_usage, a desired-state join with reconciled actual state, ...) use ONE SQL statement with FULL OUTER JOIN + COALESCE(left.key, right.key) for the projection key, never two queries with a Go-side merge. The repository method takes (organizationID, plan) and stays tenant-scoped at every leg of the join — the CTE shape `WITH policies AS (... WHERE org=$1 OR plan=$2), usage AS (... WHERE org=$1) FULL OUTER JOIN ... ORDER BY resource` is the reference; two queries would force the handler to re-implement deterministic ordering AND would leak observability (the two queries can see different snapshots).
- HTTP reads of an "already-redacted at persistence" surface (audit log, job error_summary, redacted reservation reasons, ...) project the persisted values onto the wire VERBATIM — the handler must not silently re-scrub or un-scrub sentinel values. The contract is "redaction is the writer's job, the reader trusts the chokepoint": audit.Auditor redacts metadata + IP + UA before AuditRepository.Append persists the row (the store treats them as clean), and the HTTP handler renders them as-is. Pin this structurally with a "feed the fake reader a row whose values are output.Sentinel, assert the wire renders Sentinel" test — a future regression that "cleans up" sentinels in the projection fails this test, not only review.
- Endpoints that expose N coupled scalar fields that are EITHER all-present OR all-absent project as ONE nullable nested OBJECT on the wire, not N coupled-nullable scalars. Canonical cases: usage.limit ({limit_value, enforcement_mode, source} or null), audit_event.actor ({id, kind} or null for an unauthenticated denied event), audit_event.resource ({id, kind} or null for an organization-root action). An agent reads one boolean (`actor != null`) instead of validating N invalid combinations. The store struct mirrors the wire (a single `*Foo` pointer or N pointers projected together with an all-or-nothing guard). Adding a new field to the nested object later is forward-compatible.
- audit.read is the load-bearing example of a "read action that is NOT CapRead" in the policy catalog (catalog.go: ActionAuditRead -> CapAdmin). The audit log cannot be listed by a viewer or a developer — only by an owner, admin, or a support principal performing a cross-tenant read. A boilerplate "every in-tenant role allowed" assumption is wrong for audit.read; the policy matrix and a contract-test "viewer denied in own tenant" assertion together pin the property (the contract test makes a future accidental drop to CapRead a contract failure, not only a matrix failure).
- Organization-scoped variables (0012_organization_variables, store/organization_variable.go, httpapi/variables.go): the lowest-precedence layer of the Organization -> Project -> Environment -> Service variable hierarchy. The table mirrors the established conventions — composite UNIQUE (organization_id, id) as the FK target for future scope-narrowing override tables; UNIQUE (organization_id, key) for per-tenant key uniqueness (an env var named DATABASE_URL in two orgs is two rows); ON DELETE CASCADE from organizations; bigint version + bump_version() trigger for optimistic concurrency. The repository follows the AuditRepository read pattern: ListByOrganization is tenant-scoped at the SQL predicate (cross-tenant id matches no rows, empty list, never another tenant's data), ordered deterministically by (key ASC, id ASC). The wire redaction contract is wire-side at the projection chokepoint, NOT store-side: store.OrganizationVariable.Value carries the literal verbatim (a future write path needs lossless round-trip), but httpapi/variables.go's organizationVariableOf replaces is_secret=true values with output.Sentinel on every projection — agents can never read a secret value back through this endpoint. Defence-in-depth: OrganizationVariable.LogValue redacts every value (regardless of is_secret) at the slog boundary, so a stray log record that captures the struct cannot leak the literal. Non-secret values pass through verbatim so customers can audit their own organization-wide defaults.
- The env.write policy matrix (BE-0111, internal/controlplane/httpapi/variables_put_policy_test.go) is the structural twin of the CapWrite/no-cross-tenant-support family (project/environment/service create/update/delete) and the load-bearing distinction from the env.read matrix (BE-0108): same {org_id} resolver, same organizationIDResolver, but Developer JOINS the allow row (RoleDeveloper holds CapWrite), Viewer/CI/Support all deny via ReasonDeniedNoCapability, and the engine's cross-tenant clause (`required == CapRead || required == CapSupport`) excludes CapWrite — so a Support principal cannot cross tenants and cannot mutate its OWN home tenant either. The grant-containment row mirrors the env.read suite verbatim except that the org-level allow row tests a Developer grant (CapWrite) instead of a Viewer grant (CapRead); the Viewer grant at the same org scope is asserted to DENY, locking the CapWrite requirement against the grant path so a future catalog drift in either direction fails here. Wire-level redaction (organizationVariableOf -> output.Sentinel) is pinned on the allow-by-role AND allow-by-grant branches, and deny-path leak checks use a distinct seededPostWriteOrgVariables fixture (POST_WRITE_REGION/POST_WRITE_SECRET with the recognisable "post-write-plaintext-correcthorsebatterystaple" needle) so the matrix's request body (MATRIX_KEY/matrix_value) and the env.read suite's seededOrgVariables (REGION/DATABASE_URL) cannot false-positive a leak check. Cross-tenant assertions on the wire pin BOTH the submitted-payload non-echo (MATRIX_KEY, matrix_value) and the post-write-data non-echo — the policy boundary is the only place an env mutation is gated, so a denied caller seeing either would itself be a contract violation.
- The CapWrite-on-nil-resolver policy matrix (BE-0123, internal/controlplane/httpapi/projects_create_policy_test.go) is the structural sibling of the env.write matrix above (BE-0111) but for endpoints that use a NIL ResourceResolver instead of organizationIDResolver — POST /v1/projects is the reference. The wire surface offers NO path/body field that could name another tenant (the strict JSON decoder rejects organization_id in the body, the contract suite TestCreateProjectIgnoresOrganizationIDInBody pins that), so tenant isolation on the wire is STRUCTURAL: the creator is always invoked with principal.OrganizationID and there is no caller-controlled value the policy boundary could fail open on. The cross-tenant probe therefore moves OFF the wire and ONTO a sub-test loop (cross_tenant_owner/admin/developer/viewer/ci/support, each t.Parallel()) that authorizes the action against a foreign-org Resource directly through policy.NewEngine().Decide; EVERY role — including Support — must deny via ReasonDeniedCrossTenant, because CapWrite is outside the engine's `roleCaps.has(CapSupport) && (required == CapRead || required == CapSupport)` exception. The role matrix proper still allows Owner/Admin/Developer (ReasonAllowedByRole) and denies Viewer/CI/Support (ReasonDeniedNoCapability) at the principal's own home org root. Grant-containment for a nil-resolver write authorizes against the org root, so project/env/service-scoped admin grants all deny with ReasonDeniedOutOfScope (covers() one-way); an org-level VIEWER grant denies with ReasonDeniedNoCapability (the load-bearing distinction from a CapRead matrix where the same grant ALLOWS), an org-level DEVELOPER grant allows via ReasonAllowedByGrant. Deny-path leak needles cover BOTH the caller-supplied request-body fields AND the canned-creator-output fields (two recognisable values per field, e.g. prj_matrix_alpha vs the request projectID) — a denied response leaking either is a separate regression direction, kept in one guard. Future CapWrite-on-nil-resolver endpoints (none yet planned; this is currently the only one) can copy this shape verbatim.
- Hard-delete vs soft-delete decision rule: a customer-facing resource takes a SOFT delete (deletion_scheduled_at on organizations + 202 Accepted, revoked_at on api_keys + 200 OK with revocation snapshot) ONLY when the row carries a continuing tie that must outlive the resource — an audit-log FK that must keep the row reachable, a child cascade that must be ordered carefully, or a "lifecycle stale" property the customer's audit trail depends on. A resource with no continuing tie (the audit log records the deletion independently, no child tables, no FK ties) takes a HARD delete with a 200 OK response carrying the row's snapshot exactly as it stood at the moment of removal (DELETE /v1/organizations/{org_id}/variables/{key}, BE-0115). The store repository method uses `DELETE FROM ... WHERE org=$1 AND key=$2 RETURNING ...` so one round trip removes the row AND yields the snapshot the orchestrator returns to the wire; pgx.ErrNoRows -> apierr.NotFound (a cross-tenant key is indistinguishable from a missing row). The service files an audit event in the SAME tx as the delete, with Metadata={variable_id: row.ID} — customer-supplied key + the deleted value are never recorded, so the audit row is auditable without ever observing the variable name or its value. An agent can confirm what was deleted (id, key, version, timestamps) without a separate read; 204 No Content would have been less useful here.
- Audit-event listing in tests: store.AuditRepository.ListByOrganization is ORDER BY occurred_at DESC, id DESC — events[0] is the NEWEST event. A test that reads events[len-1] gets the OLDEST event, which in a "seed then mutate" fixture is the seed event (with seed metadata), NOT the mutation under test. A test that probes the post-mutation state through ANOTHER mutation (e.g. a second Replace to verify the deleted row is gone) files yet another audit event and pushes the mutation-under-test to events[1] — so audit assertions must run BEFORE any further state-changing call, and post-state probes must read directly through the repository (NewOrganizationVariableRepository().ListByOrganization), NOT through another service-level mutation.
- Deep-resource resolver pattern (BE-0124, GET /v1/projects/{project_id}, internal/controlplane/httpapi/projects.go projectIDResolver): when an endpoint targets a resource named in the path but the path has NO org_id segment (sub-organization endpoints like /v1/projects/{project_id}, future /v1/.../environments/{environment_id}, /v1/.../services/{service_id}), the ResourceResolver pulls the principal's home OrganizationID from the request context with policy.PrincipalFromContext(r.Context()) — RequireAuth attaches the principal BEFORE invoking the resolver, so the context already carries it even though the ResourceResolver signature is func(r *http.Request) policy.Resource. The resolver returns Scope{OrganizationID: principal.HomeOrg, ProjectID: r.PathValue("project_id"), Kind: domain.KindProject}; do NOT look up the resource's real org from the database inside the resolver (keep the resolver pure path+context, no I/O). The policy engine then authorizes against the principal's own home org root combined with the path id, so a project/environment/service-scoped grant for THAT resource (containment match) authorizes via ReasonAllowedByGrant while a grant for a SIBLING resource fails the engine's covers() check; cross-tenant project_ids reach the tenant-scoped repository query with the principal's home org and surface as a deterministic 404 (E_NOT_FOUND) from the persistence layer, NOT a 403 from policy — which is acceptable per the acceptance criteria ("cross-tenant IDs must behave as not found or forbidden"). The Support principal's cross-tenant read capability does NOT apply through this endpoint because the resource scope is pinned to the principal's home org, not the path's tenant; support cross-tenant reads remain available through endpoints whose path DOES carry an {org_id} (organizationIDResolver). PATCH/DELETE on the same resource adopt the same resolver — handlers should call PathValue and use principal.OrganizationID for the store call, never trust a caller-supplied org id. The canonical regression check is TestGetProjectCrossTenantIDBehavesAsNotFound (the response body must not echo a foreign organization id either).
- Reader-port extension pattern (BE-0124): when a new endpoint on the same resource family lands (e.g. GET /v1/projects/{project_id} alongside GET /v1/projects), extend the existing reader port in place rather than declaring a parallel sibling interface — ProjectReader now has BOTH ListProjects and GetProject; OrganizationReader has GetOrganization and is used by both list and by-id endpoints. The fakeFooReader in the *_test.go is similarly extended in place (added project/getErr/gotGetOrgID/gotGetProjectID fields on fakeProjectReader for the by-id tests); the zero value of the extended fake still works for every prior call site (the other test files / suites that never reach the handler) so no cross-test churn is needed. The store-backed adapter pattern stays the same: *Reader composes *Repository (never re-issues SQL), each new method opens its own short-lived Store.Read tx and delegates to an existing Querier-accepting repository method, inheriting that method's tenant-scoping guarantees for free. Auth-error sentinel naming gotcha: the auth package exports the PLURAL form (auth.ErrInvalidCredentials, auth.ErrNoCredentials) — singular forms do not exist; tests that exercise the invalid-credential path must use the plural names to match the writeAuthError errors.Is dispatch.
- Deep-resource PATCH pattern (BE-0127, PATCH /v1/projects/{project_id}, internal/controlplane/httpapi/projects.go updateProjectHandler): sub-organization PATCH endpoints reuse the deep-resource resolver (projectIDResolver) to authorize against the (principal home organization, {project_id}) resource the path names — same resolver, same RequiredAction (policy.ActionProjectUpdate), and the store call is built from principal.OrganizationID and r.PathValue("project_id"), NEVER from the request body. The request DTO exposes ONLY the partially-updatable fields (Slug *string, DisplayName *string); the strict JSON decoder rejects an unknown organization_id field as a stable 400 E_INVALID_INPUT, so a body-injected org_id can never reach the store layer. Optimistic concurrency is bolted on by reading the optional If-Match header through the existing parseIfMatchVersion (the same parser organizations PATCH/DELETE already uses, accepting `"<n>"` strong ETag and the lenient unquoted form), forwarding the *int64 to the store as IfMatchVersion, and mirroring the returned row's authoritative version back into the ETag response header on success via writeOrganizationETag — the helper is generic in body despite the historical name, so the same call shape works for projects, future environments, services, etc. The handler stays thin: read principal, parse If-Match, validate.DecodeJSON, call port, render. The orchestrator (ProjectService.Update) follows the OrganizationService.Update shape exactly: validate input BEFORE opening the tx (buildProjectUpdate + validateProjectDisplayName are split out, unit-testable without a DB, and reject an empty patch as 400 InvalidInput), then in ONE Store.Write tx Get current, version pre-check (current.Version != *IfMatchVersion -> apierr.ConflictStale(current.Version)), apply non-nil fields, repo.Update(tx, desired, IfMatchVersion), audit.Append — a denial at any step rolls every other step back, so a row mutation and its audit record commit together or not at all. The store layer files the audit event under the actor's home organization with Metadata={"updated_fields": "slug,display_name"} — stable wire field names only, never the submitted values, so the audit trail records the SHAPE of the mutation without leaking input. classifyProjectConcurrencyMiss mirrors classifyOrganizationConcurrencyMiss: on the version-checked UPDATE matching zero rows, a tenant-scoped Get inside the same tx disambiguates "row gone" (NotFound) from "row stale" (ConflictStale carrying the row's current version) — the Get itself is tenant scoped, so a cross-tenant id still surfaces as NotFound and cannot reveal another tenant's data. Pre-existing test failures gotcha: TestAPIKeyServiceCreate* and TestOrganizationVariableServicePatch* fail on HEAD due to unrelated pre-existing issues with the testutil.Factory's id/slug formats vs the production-grade ParseSlug validator; verified by stashing the BE-0127 change and reproducing the failures on the base commit. These are NOT caused by BE-0127 and are out of scope.

## 2026-05-15 - BE-0103
- What was implemented: GET /v1/organizations/{org_id}/audit-events, the
  organization-scoped audit-trail read endpoint — the first endpoint to
  expose the immutable audit log on the public API.
  - internal/controlplane/store/auditreader.go (new): AuditEventReader is
    the store-backed read adapter for the audit-events surface. It mirrors
    UsageReader and LimitsReader — it composes
    AuditRepository.ListByOrganization through a short-lived read-only
    transaction (Store.Read), so the repository's tenant-scoping guarantees
    are inherited for free and the adapter cannot smuggle a mutation past
    Store.Write. The constructor rejects a nil store at construction
    rather than on the first request (fail-fast posture).
  - internal/controlplane/store/auditreader_test.go (new): four
    integration tests against an isolated migrated Postgres database —
    happy-path read with newest-first ordering, tenant isolation
    (two-org seed proves a cross-tenant id never returns another tenant's
    rows), empty list for an unknown/cross-tenant org id (no 404), and
    NewAuditEventReader(nil) rejection.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/audit_events.go (new): AuditEventReader
    port + listAuditEventsHandler. Request shape: only the {org_id} path
    parameter and an optional ?limit= query in [1, 200] (default 50,
    handler-side parseAuditEventLimit clamps + validates before any
    database work runs, so a malformed or out-of-range limit is the
    stable 400 E_INVALID_INPUT). Response is yalla.output.v1 carrying
    {events: [...]}: actor and resource are nullable nested OBJECTS
    (mirroring the limit/null convention the usage endpoint pins) — an
    unauthenticated denied event has Actor=null; an organization-root
    action has Resource=null. The wire trusts the persistence-layer
    redaction contract: metadata, IP address, and user agent reach this
    handler already redacted by audit.Auditor, so the projection renders
    them verbatim — a TestAuditEventProjectionDoesNotReintroduceRedaction
    proves the handler must NOT silently un-redact a sentinel value. The
    handler is thin: read principal, parse limit, call port, render.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/audit_events_test.go (new): 11 contract
    tests in package httpapi exercising the real NewHandler + real policy
    engine (fake Authenticator + fake AuditEventReader only) — happy-path
    forwarding (org_id + default limit reach the reader; nullable actor/
    resource project correctly), empty-list for an org with no history,
    ?limit= forwarding, malformed-limit 400 (no value-leak in the
    rejection), out-of-range-limit 400 (table-driven over 0/-1/201/9999),
    store outage 503 (no raw-cause leak), unauthenticated 401 (reader
    never runs), cross-tenant 403 (reader never runs, no events leaked),
    viewer-without-CapAdmin 403 (the load-bearing distinction from
    CapRead actions: a viewer can list members but cannot list the audit
    log), missing-reader 500, OpenAPI route-table contract, and the
    redaction-trust proof above.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/routes.go: registered the new apiRoute
    with RequiresAuth=true, RequiredAction=policy.ActionAuditRead,
    resolver=organizationIDResolver (so a cross-tenant {org_id} is a 403
    BEFORE the handler runs). audit.read is CapAdmin in the policy
    catalog (catalog.go:125: ActionAuditRead -> CapAdmin); the
    cross-tenant support-read exception is preserved by the engine, the
    same as the limits.read and usage.read endpoints. Added the
    tagAuditEvents="audit-events" OpenAPI tag.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/server.go: NewHandler signature gained
    AuditEventReader (one new positional arg threaded into
    newRouteTable).
  - cmd/yalla-api/main.go: builds the AuditEventReader at startup
    (composed over the existing dataStore) and threads it into
    NewHandler.
  - Mechanical churn: 19 NewHandler/newRouteTable call sites across
    *_test.go files received a positional fakeAuditEventReader{} (or one
    more nil for newRouteTable). A python script applied a multi-pattern
    substitution: single-line `fakeUsageReader{}, fakeAPIKeyReader{}` ->
    `fakeUsageReader{}, fakeAuditEventReader{}, fakeAPIKeyReader{}`,
    multi-line `fakeUsageReader{},\n\t+fakeAPIKeyReader{}` -> the same
    insertion, plus an edge case in usage_test.go where the
    `listUsageHandlerFor` helper passes its named `reader` parameter at
    the usage position (and a nil at the usage position for the
    missing-reader test). All 19 call sites compiled cleanly after the
    substitution; only the listUsageHandlerFor case (line 67) needed a
    targeted follow-up edit.
- Files changed: internal/controlplane/store/{auditreader.go (new),
  auditreader_test.go (new)}, internal/controlplane/httpapi/{audit_events.go
  (new),audit_events_test.go (new),routes.go,server.go,usage_test.go +
  18 sibling test files updated by substitution},

- Verification run: gofmt -w .; go mod tidy; go build ./...; go vet ./...;
  go test ./... (full suite); go test -race ./... (full suite);
  YALLA_TEST_DATABASE_URL=... go test -race -count=1 -run

- Results: all my changes pass (-race included). goimports / golangci-lint /
  staticcheck / govulncheck still not installed locally (documented in
  earlier iterations).
- Remaining risks / follow-ups:
  - BE-0104 (contract tests for GET /v1/organizations/{org_id}/audit-events)
    and BE-0105 (policy matrix coverage, the unblocked p=1) are now ready
    to land. audit_events_test.go already covers BE-0104's surface
    closely — the explicit-redaction proof, all four CRUD-style failure
    modes, and the viewer-denied property — but the two remain separate
    PRD stories. BE-0105 should table every built-in role x audit.read,
    revoked/expired Disabled principals, and a wrong-organization
    principal (the same pattern as limits_policy_test.go and
    usage_policy_test.go).
  - Filtering (by action, by decision, by actor, by date range) and
    cursor-based pagination are NOT in scope here — the endpoint serves
    the newest N rows in [1, 200]. A separate "audit events filtering"
    story would add query parameters; the wire envelope is
    forward-compatible (events[] can grow optional sibling fields).
  - The wire projection trusts the persistence-layer redaction contract.
    If a future "raw audit event admin view" surface is needed for
    Yalla operators, it must NOT go through this customer-facing
    endpoint — it would be a separate /admin/... route with its own
    policy action and its own redaction policy.
- Learnings for future iterations:
  - For a NewHandler signature change with ~19 call sites in *_test.go,
    a single bulk substitution covers the bulk of the churn IF the
    target substring is regular. The two regular forms in this codebase
    are single-line `fakeUsageReader{}, fakeAPIKeyReader{}` and
    multi-line `fakeUsageReader{},\n\t+fakeAPIKeyReader{}`. The
    exceptions are (a) helpers that pass a named parameter at the new
    port's position (e.g. `, reader,`) and (b) "missing-reader" tests
    that pass nil at the new port's position. Both need targeted follow-up
    edits — a perl/python pass over the common pattern + a manual `grep
    -rn 'fakeOldReader,\n\t+fakeAPIKeyReader{}'` for the residue is the
    reliable workflow.
  - audit_events.go is the third endpoint to use the
    "nullable-nested-object" wire convention (after usage.go and the
    actor/resource pair here). The pattern: when N coupled scalar fields
    are EITHER all-present OR all-absent, project them as a single
    nullable nested object on the wire (`actor: {id, kind}` vs
    `actor: null`), not N coupled-nullable scalars. The agent reads one
    boolean (actor != null) instead of validating N invalid combinations.
    Adding a new field to the nested object later is forward-compatible
    with existing readers.
  - The store contract for audit metadata is "already redacted at
    persistence". A consumer (HTTP handler, future audit-event admin
    surface) must NOT silently un-redact a sentinel value — the wire
    mirrors persistence verbatim. The
    TestAuditEventProjectionDoesNotReintroduceRedaction test pins this
    structurally: feed a fake reader rows whose IP/UA/metadata-value
    are output.Sentinel and assert the wire renders Sentinel, not the
    empty string. A future regression that "cleans up" sentinel values
    in the projection fails this test.
  - audit.read is the load-bearing example of "a read action that is
    NOT CapRead". A viewer (CapRead, CapDeploy in the catalog) cannot
    list the audit log; only CapAdmin (owner, admin) and CapSupport
    (cross-tenant) can. This means the boilerplate "list endpoint" tests
    must NOT assume "every role allowed in the tenant" — the policy
    matrix lands per-action. A viewer-denied contract test (not just a
    matrix test) is good defence-in-depth because it makes a future
    accidental drop to CapRead a contract-test failure, not only a
    matrix-test failure.
---

## 2026-05-15 - BE-0097
- What was implemented: PATCH /v1/organizations/{org_id}/limits, the
  organization-scoped quota-overrides write endpoint.
  - internal/controlplane/store/limitsservice.go (new): LimitsService
    composes the unit of work — buildLimitsUpdate validation (closed-set
    resource, non-negative limit, closed-set enforcement mode, no
    duplicates, default enforcement_mode='hard'), an OrganizationRepository
    pre-check that turns a missing tenant into the typed NotFound (so a
    missing org is a 404, not the FK-constraint 409 that mapWriteError
    would otherwise collapse to), QuotaRepository.UpsertOrganizationPolicy
    upserts inside one *Tx, an immutable AuditRepository.Append with
    metadata.updated_resources = sorted closed-set names, and a post-write
    re-read of effective limits inside the same tx so the wire body matches
    the committed state byte-for-byte. PlanLookup convention mirrors
    LimitsReader — nil falls back to DefaultPlan.
  - internal/controlplane/store/quota.go: new QuotaRepository.
    UpsertOrganizationPolicy uses `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (organization_id,
    resource) WHERE scope_kind='organization' DO UPDATE` to target the
    partial unique index quota_policies_org_resource_idx (a partial index
    requires the matching WHERE clause in the conflict_target). The
    freshly-minted id is only used on insert; on conflict the existing
    row's id and created_at are preserved, and updated_at is owned by the
    per-table trigger.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/limits.go: LimitsUpdater port + the PATCH
    handler. Request DTO carries `limits: [{resource, limit_value,
    enforcement_mode?}, ...]`; limit_value is a *int64 pointer so
    `{"limit_value":0}` (zero is a meaningful extreme) is distinguished
    from a missing field (handler-side 400 with stable
    "limits[i].limit_value must be supplied" violation). Empty patch is
    400. Handler is thin: decode, validate "at least one entry" + "every
    entry supplies limit_value", call updater, render the post-write
    effective limits in updateLimitsPayload (same wire shape as
    listLimitsPayload).
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/routes.go: registered the new apiRoute
    with RequiresAuth=true, RequiredAction=policy.ActionLimitsWrite,
    resolver=organizationIDResolver (so a cross-tenant {org_id} is a 403
    BEFORE the handler runs). limits.write is CapAdmin (catalog.go:
    ActionLimitsWrite -> CapAdmin) and has NO cross-tenant support
    exception — the load-bearing distinction from limits.read (CapRead).
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/server.go: NewHandler signature gained
    LimitsUpdater (one new positional arg threaded into newRouteTable).
  - cmd/yalla-api/main.go: builds the LimitsService at startup (composed
    over the existing OrganizationRepository/AuditRepository plus a fresh
    QuotaRepository) and threads it into NewHandler.
  - Tests: 11 new httpapi contract tests in limits_patch_test.go (happy
    path with limit forwarding + actor wiring proof, zero-limit-round-trip,
    missing limit_value 400, empty-array 400, malformed body 400,
    unauthenticated 401, cross-tenant 403, viewer-in-tenant 403 — the
    CapAdmin proof, not-found org 404, store outage 503 with no raw-cause
    leak, missing-updater 500, OpenAPI route-table contract). 4 internal
    unit tests for buildLimitsUpdate + NewLimitsService nil-dependency
    guards (no DB). 7 store integration tests (happy-path upsert + audit
    record + sorted updated_resources metadata, update-overrides-existing
    keeps a single row in the partial unique index, override-beats-plan-
    default in the post-write readback, missing-org NotFound, tenant
    isolation, invalid input never opens a transaction, missing actor org
    is Internal). All 18 test functions pass with -race.

  limitsservice_internal_test.go (new),limitsservice_test.go (new)},

  go test -race -count=1 -run 'TestLimits|TestQuota|TestUpdateLimits|TestListLimits|TestBuildLimits|TestOrganizationService' ./internal/controlplane/store/... ./internal/controlplane/httpapi/...

  staticcheck / govulncheck still not installed locally (documented in
  earlier iterations).
  - Pre-existing TestAPIKeyService* integration tests fail on main as well
    (confirmed by stashing my changes and re-running with
    YALLA_TEST_DATABASE_URL set) — these are unrelated to BE-0097 and are
    outside this story's scope.
- Remaining risks / follow-ups:
  - BE-0098 (contract tests for PATCH /v1/organizations/{org_id}/limits)
    and BE-0099 (policy matrix coverage, the unblocked p1) are now ready.
    limits_patch_test.go covers most of BE-0098's surface, but the two
    remain separate PRD stories.
  - Removing an organization override (reverting to plan default) is NOT
    in scope here: this endpoint upserts only. A separate "remove
    override" story (or supporting a sentinel value) would land later if
    the customer surface needs it.
  - The audit event currently records updated_resources as
    "projects,services" (closed-set names sorted). It does NOT record the
    submitted limit_value/enforcement_mode pairs — those are sensitive
    business inputs that a future audit-redaction story may decide to
    record under a different policy. The shape is forward-compatible: the
    metadata map can grow without breaking the wire contract.
- Learnings for future iterations:
  - ON CONFLICT against a PARTIAL unique index MUST repeat the WHERE
    predicate in the conflict_target clause: Postgres treats the partial
    index as not eligible for ON CONFLICT otherwise. The pattern is `ON
    CONFLICT (cols) WHERE <same predicate> DO UPDATE`.
  - Adding a new dependency to a constructor with ~17 call sites (the
    NewHandler / newRouteTable signature) is genuinely O(N) churn in tests
    — but every test file follows the same wiring pattern, so a single
    consistent substring substitution covers the bulk of it. The two
    files where the substring differed (api_keys_test.go used `reader,
    fakeAPIKeyReader`, limits_test.go used `nil, fakeAPIKeyReader{}` in
    the missing-reader test) needed targeted edits, but the rest fell out
    of one mechanical search/replace.
  - For numeric request fields whose zero is meaningful (a limit of 0
    forbids the resource), use `*int64`. A non-pointer int64 cannot
    distinguish "field omitted" from "set to zero", and the body decoder
    would silently treat the latter as the former — leaking a real client
    intent through a default-zero floor.
  - The handler's "at least one entry / every entry supplies limit_value"
    rejection mirrors the store layer's identical violation. This is
    intentional symmetry — both layers must validate at the boundary —
    not duplication. The field paths match byte-for-byte
    ("limits[i].limit_value"), so a future caller cannot tell whether the
    rejection came from the handler or the store, which keeps the wire
    contract stable across refactors.

  cmd/yalla-api/main.go, ralph/prd.json, ralph/progress.txt.
- Verification run: gofmt -w .; goimports -w .; go mod tidy; go build ./...;
  go vet ./...; go test ./...; go test -race ./....
- Results: all passed. goimports present; golangci-lint, staticcheck,
  govulncheck still not installed locally (documented in earlier iterations).
- Remaining risks / follow-ups:
  - "Record an audit event for every mutating request and every denied
    authorization decision" is N/A for GET /v1/me: it is a non-mutating read,
    and RequireAuth does not yet emit audit events for denied decisions (no
    audit wiring exists in the HTTP path). Auditing denied authorization
    decisions in RequireAuth is a cross-cutting follow-up that should land as
    its own story so every endpoint gets it uniformly, not piecemeal.
  - "Unit tests cover ... validation failure" and "Integration tests run
    against isolated Postgres migrations": GET /v1/me has no request body, no
    path params, and no query params, so there is no input to validate; and
    the handler touches no database (the principal is fully resolved by the
    auth layer onto the request context), so there is no Postgres integration
    surface. The unauthenticated / invalid-credential / disabled-principal
    tests cover the rejection space instead.
  - BE-0041 (contract tests for GET /v1/me) and BE-0042 (policy matrix
    coverage for GET /v1/me) are now unblocked — me_test.go already covers
    much of BE-0041's surface, but those remain separate PRD stories.
  - cmd/yalla-api now fails fast without YALLA_DATABASE_URL in every profile
    (config only required it for strict profiles). This is deliberate: the
    control plane cannot authenticate a request without its credential store,
    and there is no separate "bootstrap the DB pool" story in the PRD. Local
    dev uses the repo docker-compose Postgres.
- Learnings for future iterations:
  - The endpoint triplet pattern in the PRD (implement @ p2, contract tests
    @ p2, policy matrix @ p1) means the p1 story is always blocked on its p2
    implementation. The implementation story is the real unblocker; pick it
    even though a later sibling carries the higher priority number.
  - NewHandler had ~15 call sites across httpapi tests. Adding a thin
    newTestHandler(build, readiness, meta, logger) helper that injects canned
    auth deps kept the churn to one helper + a mechanical rename, and is the
    right move whenever a widely-called constructor gains a dependency.
  - openapi.Endpoint already carries RequiresAuth + RequiredAction, so the
    route table needed no auth-dependency parameters — NewHandler reads those
    fields and wraps the handler itself. Only the optional ResourceResolver
    needed a new apiRoute field.
  - pgxpool.New / pgx.ParseConfig errors can echo the connection string, so
    a pool-construction failure is logged with a fixed secret-free message,
    NOT err.Error(). pool.Ping errors are connection-level and safe to log.
  - policy.Engine.Decide denies a Disabled principal (ReasonDeniedPrincipalDisabled)
    BEFORE the CapSelf shortcut, so even a self action like auth.me is 403 for
    a revoked principal — that is the clean "authorization failure" test case
    for any self endpoint.
