## 2026-05-15 - BE-0036
- What was implemented: internal/controlplane/validate, the centralized
  request-validation toolkit (validate.go, network.go, resources.go, refs.go,
  json.go).
  - Collector accumulates apierr.FieldViolation entries (stable dotted field
    path + value-free reason) and produces exactly one apierr.InvalidInput via
    Err(); OK()/Violations()/Add()/Addf() round it out.
  - Validators: Name (display name: trimmed, UTF-8, no control chars, rune
    bound), Slug (delegates domain.ValidateSlug), ID (domain.ParseID + kind
    check, value-free reason so a cross-tenant probe is not an oracle), Path
    (repo-relative; rejects absolute, backslash, NUL, ".." traversal),
    URL (http/https only, rejects userinfo so a token cannot leak into stored
    state), Domain (DomainOptions.AllowWildcard gates a single leading "*."
    label — caller resolves feature flag + quota; per-label DNS validation),
    Resources (cpu/memory/replicas: zero=unset, reject negative/over-ceiling,
    per-dimension sub-paths), EnvVars (POSIX names, UTF-8/NUL/length on values,
    duplicate detection; secret vs non-secret split via the ".secret[i]" path
    segment + MaxSecretValueLen ceiling), GitRepoURL (https/ssh/git + scp-like
    git@host:path, rejects embedded credentials), GitBranch (check-ref-format
    subset), GitCommit (7-40 hex), ImageRef (Docker reference subset, rejects
    ".." components + uppercase repo + malformed tag/digest).
  - DecodeJSON: strict, size-capped (custom limitedReader -> errBodyTooLarge),
    DisallowUnknownFields, rejects trailing data; every failure maps to a
    body-free apierr.Invalid / apierr.InvalidInput so a secret in a malformed
    body never leaks through the decode error.
  - Tests: validate_test.go (Collector, every validator success+failure,
    secret/non-secret separation proof, "never echoes the value" proofs for
    Name/Slug/ID/URL/EnvVars/DecodeJSON) + fuzz_test.go (8 fuzz targets:
    FuzzName/Path/Domain/EnvVarName/EnvVarValue/DecodeJSON/ImageRef/GitBranch,
    each seeded with long strings, invalid UTF-8, path traversal, control
    chars; each asserts no panic and re-derives the accept invariants).
  - internal/controlplane/validate/AGENTS.md documents the Collector pattern,
    the no-echo rule, options-not-lookups, field-path conventions, and the
    fuzz-every-validator convention.
- Files changed: internal/controlplane/validate/{validate.go,network.go,
  resources.go,refs.go,json.go,validate_test.go,fuzz_test.go,AGENTS.md} (new),
  ralph/prd.json, ralph/progress.txt.
- Verification run: gofmt -w .; go mod tidy; go build ./...; go vet ./...;
  go test ./...; go test -race ./...; plus a 4s -fuzztime smoke of all 8 fuzz
  targets.
- Results: all passed. goimports, golangci-lint, staticcheck, govulncheck not
  installed locally (documented in earlier iterations).
- Remaining risks / follow-ups:
  - Acceptance criteria "stable yalla.output.v1/error.v1 envelopes",
    "isolated Postgres migrations", and "authorization failure / not-found
    unit tests" are N/A: validate is a pure, in-memory package with no HTTP
    surface, no Postgres, and no auth — covered success + validation-failure
    + fuzz exhaustively instead (the established pattern for pure packages).
  - validate defines its own EnvVar/ResourceRequest input shapes rather than
    importing dokploy's Variable/ResourceLimits — validate is the request
    layer, dokploy is the provisioning layer, and validate must not depend on
    dokploy. When handlers land they will validate the request DTO, then map
    the validated values into the dokploy/store shapes.
  - The validators are implemented and tested but not yet wired into any
    handler (no customer-facing endpoints exist yet). The first endpoint
    story will call validate.New() + the relevant validators in its handler.
- Learnings for future iterations:
  - apierr.InvalidInput already trims + de-dups blank violations and sorts
    deterministically, so a Collector can append freely and rely on it.
  - The Crockford ID alphabet INCLUDES z but EXCLUDES i/l/o/u — to build an
    intentionally-malformed ID suffix for a test, use 'l' (or i/o/u), not 'z'.
  - io.LimitReader reports io.EOF at the cap, indistinguishable from a
    truncated body. For a "body too large" error you need a custom reader
    that returns a distinct sentinel (limitedReader -> errBodyTooLarge).
  - encoding/json reports an unknown field as a plain errors.New whose message
    is `json: unknown field "<name>"` — there is no typed error to errors.As
    against; match on the message prefix.
  - "Separates secret and non-secret fields" was satisfied structurally: the
    EnvVar.Secret flag selects both the length ceiling and the field-path
    segment (".secret[i]" vs "[i]"), so the path alone tells a caller which
    kind failed without inspecting the value.

## 2026-05-14 - BE-0003
- What was implemented: Service lifecycle and graceful shutdown for the API and worker.
  - internal/controlplane/runtime/lifecycle.go (new): Readiness type — concurrency-safe named
    startup gates (NewReadiness/Set/MarkReady/Ready/Snapshot/PendingGates) with the
    ReadinessReporter interface; RunHTTPServer(ctx, srv, shutdownTimeout, logger) — runs
    ListenAndServe in a goroutine, returns early on a pre-shutdown serve error, and on ctx
    cancellation calls Shutdown with a fresh context.WithTimeout so a wedged request cannot
    block exit. http.ErrServerClosed is treated as success.
  - internal/controlplane/runtime/lifecycle_test.go (new): readiness transitions/regression,
    no-gates-immediately-ready, snapshot-is-a-copy, graceful shutdown on cancel, shutdown-timeout
    bounding, and serve-error propagation (fake httpServer, no real socket).
  - internal/controlplane/worker/loop.go (new): Loop with Claimer/Lease interfaces. Run checks
    ctx before every Claim (stops claiming on shutdown), backs off on empty queue / claim error,
    and on an interrupted in-flight job releases the lease on a context.WithoutCancel context
    bounded by ReleaseTimeout. A job whose Run returns nil is committed, never released.
    NoopClaimer is the placeholder Claimer until the durable Postgres job store lands.
    (replaced worker/doc.go — package doc now lives in loop.go.)
  - internal/controlplane/worker/loop_test.go (new): processes-then-stops-claiming-on-cancel,
    in-flight lease released on shutdown, completed job NOT released, retry-after-claim-error,
    NoopClaimer clean shutdown (fake Claimer/Lease).
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/server.go: NewHandler now takes a runtime.ReadinessReporter;
    /readyz returns 503 + yalla.error.v1 E_SERVER until gates pass, 200 + yalla.output.v1 after.
    /healthz documented as liveness-only.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/server_test.go: NewHandler call sites updated; added
    TestReadyzReflectsReadinessTransitions (503 unready -> 200 ready).
  - internal/controlplane/config/{config.go,load.go}: added YALLA_SHUTDOWN_TIMEOUT
    (EnvShutdownTimeout) + Config.ShutdownTimeout (time.Duration), per-profile defaults
    (local 15s, test 2s, staging/production 25s), Go-duration parsing, and 1s..5m range
    validation as a typed yerr.CodeConfig error. Wired into RedactedConfig/LogValue/String.
  - internal/controlplane/config/config_test.go: added TestShutdownTimeoutResolution and three
    validation-failure rows (invalid duration, too small, too large).
  - cmd/yalla-api/main.go: builds runtime.Readiness("startup"), runs a background
    runStartupChecks placeholder that flips the gate, and delegates the server lifecycle to
    runtime.RunHTTPServer with cfg.ShutdownTimeout.
  - cmd/yalla-worker/main.go: runs worker.Loop{Claimer: NoopClaimer{}, ReleaseTimeout:
    cfg.ShutdownTimeout} driven by signal.NotifyContext.

  internal/controlplane/worker/{loop.go,loop_test.go} (new), internal/controlplane/worker/doc.go

- Learnings for future iterations:
  - RunHTTPServer takes an unexported httpServer interface ({ListenAndServe; Shutdown}); *http.Server
    satisfies it and tests pass a fake — exported func + unexported interface param works fine.
  - Lease.Run's contract: nil return == committed (never release); non-nil + shutdown == release.
    This avoids the double-run hazard of releasing a job that actually finished.
  - context.WithoutCancel (Go 1.21+) is the clean way to give shutdown-time cleanup work a context
    that is not already cancelled. go.mod is on go 1.25.
  - Adding a non-secret config field only needs Config + Load + Validate + Redacted/LogValue/String
    + tests; the redaction tests only assert secrets are absent, so a new plain field is safe.

## 2026-05-14 - BE-0004
- What was implemented: Canonical API envelope package for every control-plane HTTP response.
  - internal/controlplane/apienvelope/apienvelope.go (new): single canonical renderer.
    successEnvelope (schema_version, ok=true, data, request_id, optional warnings) and
    errorEnvelope (schema_version, ok=false, error{code,message,hint?,documentation_url?},
    request_id) are UNEXPORTED — handlers cannot hand-roll JSON, the only exits are
    WriteData / WriteError / WriteErrorStatus + the unexported writeJSON. StatusForCode
    pins the documented Code->HTTP-status mapping (400/401/403/404/409/429/501/502/504/499/500).
    WriteError derives status from the error Code; WriteErrorStatus is the explicit override
    (e.g. /readyz 503 with E_SERVER). DocURLForCode/DocsBaseURL build the stable doc URL.
    envelopeRedactor = output.NewRedactor() scrubs Authorization headers / token query params
    from message, hint, and warnings as a defence-in-depth backstop. Nil error -> E_INTERNAL/500.
  - internal/controlplane/apienvelope/apienvelope_test.go (new): success envelope shape +
    omitempty warnings, warnings included, WriteError table (validation/auth/forbidden/
    not-found/conflict) asserting derived status + full envelope, StatusForCode full mapping

    (successEnvelope/errorEnvelope/errorPayload/writeData/writeError/writeJSON); handlers and
    notFoundRecorder now call apienvelope.*. Added requestID(r) bridge returning "" until the
    request-ID middleware story (BE-0006) wires real IDs — the envelope field is present today.

  internal/controlplane/httpapi/server.go, internal/controlplane/AGENTS.md, ralph/prd.json,

## 2026-05-14 - BE-0005
- What was implemented: Stable backend error taxonomy for the control plane.
  - internal/errors/errors.go: added two shared codes — CodeQuotaExceeded ("E_QUOTA_EXCEEDED")
    and CodeUnavailable ("E_UNAVAILABLE") — with descriptions, ExitCode mappings (7 / 8), and
    AllCodes entries. Quota is distinct from rate-limit (persists until limit raised);
    Unavailable is a Yalla-owned-dependency failure, distinct from CodeServer (Dokploy upstream).
  - internal/controlplane/apienvelope/apienvelope.go: StatusForCode now maps E_QUOTA_EXCEEDED
    -> 429 and E_UNAVAILABLE -> 503; doc comment updated. Remains the single source of truth
    for code->status.
  - internal/controlplane/apierr/apierr.go (new package): the catalogued backend error taxonomy.
    MessagePolicy (MessageSpecific/MessageGeneric); Dependency enum (dokploy/postgres/queue/
    network); CatalogEntry + taxonomy map + Lookup/Catalog (HTTP status derived from
    apienvelope, never stored); Retryable(err). Constructors all return *yerr.Error:
    Unauthenticated, Forbidden, NotFound, Conflict, InvalidInput (sorted FieldViolations),
    Invalid, QuotaExceeded, DokployUnavailable, StoreUnavailable, QueueUnavailable,
    NetworkFailure, Timeout, Internal. FieldViolation{Field,Reason} carries a dotted path +
    reason, never the submitted value. validationError/dependencyError are wrapped as the
    Cause so ViolationsOf / DependencyOf recover structured detail; MessageGeneric codes keep
    the cause out of Message/Hint and only Wrap it for server-side logging.
  - internal/controlplane/apierr/apierr_test.go (new): catalogue determinism (status ==
    apienvelope, all >= 400, policy + description present), sorted+category coverage, Lookup
    miss, every constructor emits a catalogued code, auth/forbidden defaults, NotFound echoes
    only the caller id, InvalidInput sorts/trims/de-dups violations and exposes field paths in
    the hint, degraded InvalidInput, ViolationsOf/DependencyOf false for unrelated errors,
    QuotaExceeded hint, dependency errors distinguished (store vs queue share E_UNAVAILABLE but
    DependencyOf separates them), MessageGeneric codes never echo the cause but keep it in the
    chain, apienvelope redaction backstop scrubs secrets from a MessageSpecific message,
    Retryable matrix, FieldViolation.String.
  - internal/errors/errors_test.go + apienvelope/apienvelope_test.go: extended the ExitCode and
    StatusForCode tables with the two new codes.
  - internal/controlplane/AGENTS.md: documented that backend errors must be built through apierr.
- Files changed: internal/controlplane/apierr/{apierr.go,apierr_test.go} (new),
  internal/errors/{errors.go,errors_test.go}, internal/controlplane/apienvelope/{apienvelope.go,
  apienvelope_test.go}, internal/controlplane/AGENTS.md, ralph/prd.json, ralph/progress.txt.
- Verification run: gofmt -w .; go mod tidy; go build ./...; go vet ./...; go test ./...;
  go test -race ./....
- Results: all passed. goimports, golangci-lint, staticcheck, govulncheck not installed locally.
- Remaining risks / follow-ups:
  - The Postgres integration-test acceptance criterion is N/A for a pure taxonomy package; it
    lands with the persistence stories.
  - Dependency distinction (store vs queue) is machine-recoverable via DependencyOf but is not
    yet surfaced as a structured field on the wire envelope; if a future story needs that, add
    an optional details block to apienvelope rather than forking the envelope.
  - The errors.go doc comment still describes the package as "the CLI's" contract; it is now
    shared CLI + control-plane. Left as-is to keep the change focused; revisit if it misleads.
- Learnings for future iterations:
  - No manifest parity test exists yet (grep for AllCodes/codeDescriptions found zero refs), so
    adding codes to internal/errors is currently low-risk — but still update codeDescriptions,
    AllCodes, and ExitCode together for correctness.
  - apienvelope_test.go already iterates yerr.AllCodes() asserting StatusForCode >= 400, so any
    new code added to AllCodes MUST get a StatusForCode case or that test fails.
  - errors.As traverses from *yerr.Error through its Cause, so wrapping a private detail type
    (validationError/dependencyError) as the Cause is the clean way to attach machine-readable
    structure without changing yerr.Error's shape.

## 2026-05-14 - BE-0006
- What was implemented: Request ID and correlation middleware for the control-plane API.
  - internal/controlplane/telemetry/correlation.go (new, replaces telemetry/doc.go):
    HeaderRequestID/HeaderCorrelationID stable header-name constants; Correlation struct
    (RequestID per request, CorrelationID per workflow); NewRequestID() (crypto/rand 16 bytes
    -> 32-char hex, with a nanosecond-timestamp fallback that can never panic); SafeID()
    (1..128 chars from [A-Za-z0-9._-] — rejects CR/LF/space/control/non-ascii, so an inbound
    value is safe to place verbatim into headers, logs, audit metadata, job rows);
    WithCorrelation/FromContext/RequestID/CorrelationID context plumbing; LogAttrs(ctx) slog
    helper; Correlate(next) — the outermost HTTP middleware: honours safe inbound
    X-Request-Id/X-Correlation-Id, generates a fresh request_id otherwise, defaults a missing
    correlation_id to the request_id, stores Correlation on the context, echoes both IDs as
    response headers.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/server.go: NewHandler now wraps the whole mux in
    telemetry.Correlate; requestID(r) reads telemetry.RequestID(r.Context()) instead of
    returning "". Every success, /readyz 503, and synthesised 404 envelope now carries a real
    request_id.
  - internal/controlplane/telemetry/correlation_test.go (new): SafeID table (incl. CR/LF
    header-injection, oversized, non-ascii, null byte), NewRequestID uniqueness+safety over
    1000 draws, context round-trip + zero-value + nil-ctx guard, LogAttrs, and Correlate
    middleware behaviour (generate-when-missing, honour-safe-inbound, default-correlation,
    reject-unsafe-inbound).
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/server_test.go: added request_id assertions for the success
    envelope, the /readyz error envelope, and the not-found envelope (all generated + matching
    the response header), plus echo-safe-inbound and reject-unsafe-inbound cases.
  - internal/controlplane/AGENTS.md: documented the telemetry.Correlate middleware contract.
- Files changed: internal/controlplane/telemetry/correlation.go (new),
  internal/controlplane/telemetry/correlation_test.go (new),
  internal/controlplane/telemetry/doc.go (deleted), internal/controlplane/httpapi/server.go,
  internal/controlplane/httpapi/server_test.go, internal/controlplane/AGENTS.md,
  ralph/prd.json, ralph/progress.txt.
- Verification run: gofmt -w .; go mod tidy; go build ./...; go vet ./...; go test ./...;
  go test -race ./....
- Results: all passed. goimports, golangci-lint, staticcheck, govulncheck not installed locally.
- Remaining risks / follow-ups:
  - Propagation to "audit records, job metadata, Dokploy provisioner calls" (criterion 2) and
    the "DB write / queue job / fake Dokploy" propagation tests (criterion 4) are partly N/A:
    those subsystems are still doc-only stubs. The context plumbing (WithCorrelation/FromContext)
    is the propagation mechanism they will read from; when audit/jobs/dokploy land they MUST
    pull request_id + correlation_id from telemetry.FromContext(ctx) and persist/forward them.
  - "validation failure / authorization failure" unit-test criteria have no endpoints yet;
    covered success + /readyz error + not-found envelopes instead.
- Learnings for future iterations:
  - telemetry.Correlate is the outermost middleware; future middleware (logging BE-0007, auth,
    idempotency) should assume correlation is already on the context and slot inside it.
  - httpapi.NewHandler returns the Correlate-wrapped handler, so cmd/yalla-api needs no change.
  - SafeID is deliberately strict ([A-Za-z0-9._-]) — it doubles as the header/log-injection
    guard. Don't loosen it to accept arbitrary client IDs; that charset still covers UUID/ULID.
  - When adding non-HTTP entry points (worker job runners), seed context via
    telemetry.WithCorrelation so the same request_id/correlation_id flows through jobs.

## 2026-05-14 - BE-0007
- What was implemented: Structured per-request logging middleware for the control-plane API.
  - internal/controlplane/telemetry/logging.go (new): RequestLogging(logger) middleware emitting
    exactly one JSON record per request after the handler runs — method, route (r.Pattern, the
    ServeMux-matched route, falling back to URL path), target (redacted RequestURI), status,
    latency_ms, bytes, plus request_id/correlation_id from LogAttrs(ctx). statusRecorder wraps the
    ResponseWriter to capture status + byte count (defaults to 200 on an implicit write). Level
    follows the outcome: 5xx error, 4xx warn, else info. Headers and bodies are never logged; the
    target is run through output.NewRedactor() (logRedactor) so a smuggled ?token= is scrubbed.
    requestFields is a request-scoped mutable holder seeded onto the context; SetOrgID/
    SetPrincipalID let the future auth/policy layers enrich org_id/principal_id, which are emitted
    only when non-empty. A nil logger disables logging (io.Discard) without panicking.
  - internal/controlplane/telemetry/logging_test.go (new): one-record-per-outcome table
    (success/validation-failure/auth-failure/forbidden/not-found/server-error → level + fields +
    request_id matches response header), redaction (Authorization header, Cookie, query token,
    body field — none appear in the log; target shows [REDACTED]), org/principal included when set
    and omitted when unknown, configured-level gating (INFO suppressed under ERROR threshold, 5xx
    still emitted), nil-logger safety, implicit-write status defaults to 200.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/server.go: NewHandler now takes logger *slog.Logger and wraps
    the routed mux in telemetry.RequestLogging(logger) just inside telemetry.Correlate.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/server_test.go: all NewHandler call sites updated with the new
    nil logger arg; added TestHandlerEmitsStructuredRequestLog (real logger to a buffer: route =
    "GET /healthz" ServeMux pattern, status 200, request_id matches envelope, query secret redacted).
  - cmd/yalla-api/main.go + cmd/yalla-worker/main.go: bind service via logger.With(
    slog.String("service", "yalla-api"|"yalla-worker")); yalla-api passes the logger to NewHandler.
- Files changed: internal/controlplane/telemetry/{logging.go,logging_test.go} (new),
  internal/controlplane/httpapi/{server.go,server_test.go}, cmd/yalla-api/main.go,
  cmd/yalla-worker/main.go, internal/controlplane/AGENTS.md, ralph/prd.json, ralph/progress.txt.
- Verification run: gofmt -w .; go mod tidy; go build ./...; go vet ./...; go test ./...;
  go test -race ./....
- Results: all passed. goimports, golangci-lint, staticcheck, govulncheck not installed locally.
- Remaining risks / follow-ups:
  - org_id/principal_id propagation depends on the auth/policy stories calling SetOrgID/
    SetPrincipalID; the mechanism is wired and tested but no endpoint resolves a principal yet.
  - The Postgres integration-test acceptance criterion is N/A for a pure middleware story.
  - "Tests verify stdout/stderr behavior for service processes" is covered structurally: the
    middleware writes to whatever io.Writer the logger wraps, and the binaries wire os.Stdout.
    A full process-level stdout capture test belongs with an e2e harness, not this unit story.
- Learnings for future iterations:
  - http.Request.Pattern is populated in place by http.ServeMux during ServeHTTP, and since the
    logging middleware reassigns r = r.WithContext(...) (a shallow copy) before calling next, the
    middleware's own r pointer sees the matched pattern after next returns. Use it for the "route"
    field — low-cardinality, unlike the raw path.
  - statusRecorder must default status to 200: a handler that calls Write without WriteHeader
    never triggers WriteHeader, matching net/http's implicit-200 behavior.
  - slog JSONHandler renders the auto timestamp as "time" and level as an upper-case string
    ("INFO"/"WARN"/"ERROR"); tests decode those keys directly.
  - logger.Log(ctx, level, msg, args...) accepts slog.Attr values inside the ...any args, so a
    []any of slog.String(...) (as LogAttrs returns) splats in cleanly.

## 2026-05-14 - BE-0008
- What was implemented: OpenAPI 3.1 generation + validation for the control-plane API.
  - internal/controlplane/openapi/openapi.go (new): the OpenAPI document builder.
    Build(Info, []Endpoint) -> Document. Endpoint is the neutral per-route metadata
    type (method/path/operationId/summary/description/tags/RequiresAuth/SuccessStatus/
    SuccessDescription/SuccessSchema). Every operation documents a success response and
    a "default" error response, both referencing component schemas. Components always
    define SchemaSuccessEnvelope (yalla.output.v1), SchemaErrorEnvelope (yalla.error.v1),
    SchemaErrorBody, SchemaOpenAPIDocument, and the ApiKeyAuth http/bearer security
    scheme. Public endpoints emit security:[] explicitly; RequiresAuth emits
    [{ApiKeyAuth:[]}]. All map types (Paths/PathItem/Responses/ContentMap/SchemaMap/
    SecuritySchemeMap) marshal with sorted keys via marshalSortedMap so the artifact is
    byte-stable. Examples render secret-shaped values with output.Sentinel; the
    security-scheme description carries a redacted sample bearer token. Document exposes
    HasOperation(method,path) + OperationCount() for the route-coverage test.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/routes.go (new): newRouteTable(build, readiness) is
    the single source of truth — []apiRoute pairing a served http.HandlerFunc with its
    openapi.Endpoint. Moved the /healthz, /readyz, /version handlers here from server.go.
    openAPIEndpoint() is the /openapi.json metadata (kept separate because its handler
    is built from the doc itself); openAPIDocument(build, table) folds it back in so the
    published document lists the discovery endpoint. endpointsOf projects metadata out.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/server.go: NewHandler now builds the route table,
    generates the OpenAPI doc once at startup (json.MarshalIndent; panics on a marshal
    error since the input is static), appends the /openapi.json route (serves the raw
    document — deliberately NOT enveloped, since OpenAPI tooling expects the standard
    format — with no auth), and registers every table entry on the mux in a loop.
  - internal/controlplane/openapi/openapi_test.go (new): 3.1 identity, envelope+error
    schemas defined, ApiKeyAuth scheme, every endpoint documents both responses + custom
    success status honoured, public security:[] vs authed ApiKeyAuth, HasOperation
    case-insensitive, deterministic marshal (5x) + valid JSON, examples use output.Sentinel
    and contain no unredacted secret-shaped token.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/server_test.go: TestHandlerServesOpenAPIDocument
    (200, application/json, openapi=3.1.0, info.version echoes build, paths include all
    routes, body is the raw doc not an envelope, no auth header sent),
    TestEveryRegisteredRouteIsDocumented (CI guard — every registered route in the doc,
    no extras), TestRegisteredRoutesAreServable (every documented route reachable,
    unknown path still 404), TestOpenAPIDocumentRedactsExampleSecrets.
- Files changed: internal/controlplane/openapi/{openapi.go,openapi_test.go} (new),
  internal/controlplane/httpapi/{routes.go (new),server.go,server_test.go},
  internal/controlplane/AGENTS.md, ralph/prd.json, ralph/progress.txt.
- Verification run: gofmt -w .; go mod tidy; go build ./...; go vet ./...; go test ./...;
  go test -race ./...; go test -race ./internal/controlplane/....
- Results: all passed. goimports, golangci-lint, staticcheck, govulncheck not installed locally.
- Remaining risks / follow-ups:
  - Endpoint has no request-body modelling yet (no endpoint has a request body). When the
    first mutating endpoint lands, extend openapi.Endpoint with request schema metadata
    and add a RequestBody to operationFor — keep it data-driven from the route table.
  - The route-coverage guard proves newRouteTable -> document parity. It cannot catch a
    raw mux.HandleFunc added outside the table; AGENTS.md forbids that and TestRegisteredRoutesAreServable
    plus the convention are the backstop. If a future story needs a hard guard, expose a
    mux-introspection hook.
  - Acceptance criterion 5 ("all public responses use stable JSON envelopes") does not
    apply to /openapi.json itself: the OpenAPI document is a standardised format and is
    served raw so OpenAPI tooling can consume it. Every API *operation* response still
    uses the envelopes. Validation/authorization-failure unit-test criteria are N/A —
    /openapi.json takes no input and needs no auth; covered success + not-found instead.
  - Postgres integration-test criterion is N/A for a pure API-contract story.
- Learnings for future iterations:
  - In a Go composite literal of map[string]*Schema, element values can elide &Schema{}
    (write just {...}); but a *Schema struct *field* (e.g. Items) still needs explicit
    &Schema{...}. Map/slice elements allow elision, struct fields do not.
  - json.RawMessage with `json:",omitempty"` is the clean way to embed deterministic
    example bodies — map[string]any examples would marshal with random key order and
    break a determinism test. MarshalIndent does not re-indent RawMessage, so keep
    embedded examples compact.
  - A custom MarshalJSON on a named map type makes json.Marshal deterministic without
    touching call sites; nest them (Paths -> PathItem -> Operation) and each level sorts
    independently. omitempty still works on a named map type — it is evaluated on the Go
    value (len==0) before MarshalJSON is called.
  - The /openapi.json route is self-referential (its handler needs the doc the routes
    describe). Pattern: keep its metadata in a separate openAPIEndpoint(), have the doc
    builder append it, and register the serving route last in NewHandler.

## 2026-05-14 - BE-0010
- What was implemented: Postgres migration system for the control plane.
  - internal/controlplane/store/migrate/migrate.go (new): the migration runner.
    Migration/AppliedMigration/Status types; LoadMigrations(fs.FS) parses+validates
    NNNN_name.(up|down).sql files (malformed names, missing up, empty up, conflicting
    names, duplicates -> errors), sha256-checksums each up script, returns them sorted.
    Migrator (New = embedded FS, NewWithFS = arbitrary FS for tests): Up applies pending
    migrations in order, idempotent; Down rolls back newest-first to a target version;
    Status reports applied/pending/current/dirty. Up/Down serialise via a pg session
    advisory lock, bootstrap the schema_migrations ledger (version bigint PK, name,
    checksum, applied_at, dirty), and run each migration in its own transaction (row
    inserted dirty -> SQL -> row finalised clean; a SQL failure rolls the whole tx back
    leaving no row). verify() refuses dirty / unknown-version / checksum-mismatch via
    errors.Is sentinels ErrDirtyDatabase/ErrUnknownVersion/ErrChecksumMismatch;
    ErrIrreversible guards Down across a migration with no down script.
  - internal/controlplane/store/migrate/migrations/0001_baseline.up.sql + .down.sql (new):
    baseline migration — installs pgcrypto + citext extensions and the set_updated_at()
    BEFORE UPDATE trigger function (the timestamp convention every later table uses).
    Domain tables are deliberately NOT here; they land in BE-0013 (tenant hierarchy),
    BE-0021 (quota), BE-0023 (audit), etc.
  - internal/controlplane/store/migrate/migrate_test.go (new): pure unit tests via
    fstest.MapFS (embedded parse, 6 validation-failure rows, fixture-FS success, nil
    pool); integration tests against an isolated per-test throwaway database
    (testPool helper: CREATE DATABASE / connect / DROP DATABASE ... WITH (FORCE) on
    cleanup, t.Skip when YALLA_TEST_DATABASE_URL unset): up-from-empty (ledger row +
    checksum + PK constraint + extensions + function), idempotent up, refuses
    dirty/unknown/checksum-mismatch, down is reversible + re-migratable, failed
    migration rolls back leaving no row, Status pending/applied.
  - internal/controlplane/store/AGENTS.md (new): migration conventions, domain-table
    timestamp/tenant-scoping rules, integration-test approach.
  - docker-compose.yml (new): local Postgres 16 (user/db "yalla", CREATE DATABASE priv).
  - go.mod/go.sum: added github.com/jackc/pgx/v5 v5.9.2 (+ pgxpool).
- Files changed: internal/controlplane/store/migrate/{migrate.go,migrate_test.go,
  migrations/0001_baseline.up.sql,migrations/0001_baseline.down.sql} (new),
  internal/controlplane/store/AGENTS.md (new), docker-compose.yml (new), go.mod, go.sum,
  ralph/prd.json, ralph/progress.txt.
- Verification run: gofmt -w .; go mod tidy; go build ./...; go vet ./...;
  go test ./...; go test -race ./...; go test -race ./internal/controlplane/store/...
  with YALLA_TEST_DATABASE_URL pointing at a local Postgres 16 container (all 8
  integration tests RUN, not skipped, and PASS).
- Results: all passed. goimports, golangci-lint, staticcheck, govulncheck not installed
  locally (documented). Integration tests skip cleanly when YALLA_TEST_DATABASE_URL is
  unset, so CI without Postgres still goes green; CI WITH Postgres exercises them.
- Remaining risks / follow-ups:
  - The runner is not yet wired into cmd/yalla-api or cmd/yalla-worker startup.
    BE-0009's runStartupChecks + runtime.Meta.SetMigrationVersion are the seams: a
    later story (or BE-0013 when there is real schema to apply) should connect a
    pgxpool, run Migrator.Up, resolve Status().Current onto runtime.Meta, and gate
    the "migrations" readiness check on it.
  - Acceptance criterion 3 ("constraints for tenant isolation, uniqueness, FKs") is
    only partially demonstrable here — there are no domain tables yet. BE-0010 ships
    the migration *system* + the baseline conventions (set_updated_at, extensions);
    the schema_migrations ledger's PK is the one constraint validated by tests.
    Domain-table constraints land with BE-0013/BE-0021/BE-0023 migrations.
  - Criteria 5 (JSON envelopes) and the "authorization failure / not-found" half of
    criterion 6 are N/A: migrate is a persistence-layer package with no HTTP surface.
    Covered success + validation-failure thoroughly instead.
  - BE-0011 (persistence test harness) should generalise the testPool helper into
    internal/controlplane/testutil so every persistence package shares one isolated-
    database fixture instead of re-implementing it.
- Learnings for future iterations:
  - pgx v5 routes Exec/tx.Exec by argument count: a query with ZERO args uses the
    simple query protocol, which allows multiple ;-separated statements in one call —
    so a single .up.sql file with several DDL statements (and dollar-quoted $$ bodies)
    runs fine via one conn.Exec(ctx, mig.UpSQL). A query WITH args uses the extended
    protocol (one statement only).
  - The advisory lock must be held on a single pinned connection: acquire one with
    pool.Acquire(ctx) and run lock + all migration work + unlock on that *pgxpool.Conn,
    not on the pool (the pool hands out different conns).
  - Release the advisory lock on context.WithoutCancel(ctx) so an already-cancelled
    caller context still releases it.
  - Per-test isolation: pgx.Identifier{name}.Sanitize() safely quotes a generated DB
    name; DROP DATABASE ... WITH (FORCE) (PG13+) evicts stragglers. Register cleanups
    so they run LIFO: admin pool close FIRST (runs last), then DROP, then test pool
    close (runs first) — pool closed before drop, admin still open to drop.
  - slog.New(slog.DiscardHandler) (Go 1.24+) is the clean nil-logger fallback.
  - Validation logic that takes an fs.FS (LoadMigrations) is unit-testable with
    fstest.MapFS — no database needed for the "validation failure" acceptance criterion.

## 2026-05-14 - BE-0011
- What was implemented: the reusable persistence test harness under
  internal/controlplane/testutil (previously a doc.go-only stub).
  - internal/controlplane/testutil/database.go (new, replaces doc.go): the
    package doc + the isolated-database harness. EnvDatabaseURL constant

    DB type embeds *pgxpool.Pool + carries the generated Name. RequireDB(t)
    provisions an empty, crypto-random-named, isolated throwaway Postgres
    database (skips with SkipReason when the env var is unset, drops the DB on
    cleanup with WITH (FORCE); cleanup order: pool.Close -> DROP -> admin.Close).
    RequireMigratedDB(t) layers migrate.New(...).Up on top. lookupAdminDSN
    (pure, getenv-injected) is the unit-testable skip-decision seam;
    uniqueDBName uses crypto/rand so parallel tests never collide.
  - internal/controlplane/testutil/fixtures.go (new): Factory builds
    deterministically-shaped, globally-unique in-memory fixtures. Typed structs
    Organization/User/APIKey/Project/Environment/Service with parent-linkage
    IDs; ServiceKind* constants. NewFactory(t) seeds a random namespace token;
    every builder takes its parent fixture so the hierarchy is compile-time
    enforced and child rows carry the right organization/project/environment
    IDs. Two factories (= two tests) never share an ID/slug/email/secret.
    APIKey.Secret is a fake plaintext credential for redaction tests. slugify/
    displayName/localPart normalise human labels.
  - internal/controlplane/testutil/redact.go (new): AssertRedacted(t, s,
    secrets...) and AssertRedactedValue(t, v, secrets...) — fail when a secret
    appears verbatim in output / in any rendering of a value (JSON + %+v + %#v).
    The failure message itself never prints the raw secret (shows length + a
    sentinel-redacted view). scanLeaks is the pure, white-box-tested seam.
  - internal/controlplane/testutil/internal_test.go (new, white-box): unit
    tests for lookupAdminDSN (set/trimmed/unset/blank), slugify, displayName,

    hierarchy, uniqueness within a factory (100 iterations), factories never
    collide (shared labels, 50 iterations each), empty/messy label handling,
    Service.Kind override.
  - internal/controlplane/testutil/redact_test.go (new, black-box):
    AssertRedacted/AssertRedactedValue do not false-positive on clean output;
    AssertRedacted guards a real factory-built API key secret.
  - internal/controlplane/testutil/database_test.go (new, black-box
    integration): isolated databases (table in db1 invisible from db2),
    parallel-safe (8 workers each create the same-named table), drops on
    cleanup (inner subtest then verify via pg_database), RequireMigratedDB
    applies migrations (schema_migrations + pgcrypto), migrated DBs isolated.
- Files changed: internal/controlplane/testutil/{database.go,fixtures.go,
  redact.go,internal_test.go,fixtures_test.go,redact_test.go} (new),
  internal/controlplane/testutil/doc.go (deleted),
  internal/controlplane/AGENTS.md, ralph/prd.json, ralph/progress.txt.
- Verification run: gofmt -w .; go mod tidy; go build ./...; go vet ./...;
  go test ./...; go test -race ./...; then with YALLA_TEST_DATABASE_URL
  pointing at a local docker-compose Postgres 16: go test -race -v
  ./internal/controlplane/testutil/... (all 9 integration tests RUN, not
  skipped, and PASS) and go test -race ./internal/controlplane/store/...
  (migrate suite still green).
- Results: all passed. goimports, golangci-lint, staticcheck, govulncheck not
  installed locally (documented). Integration tests skip cleanly without the
  env var so CI without Postgres stays green.
- Remaining risks / follow-ups:
  - The fixture Factory builds in-memory values only — there are no domain
    tables yet (Organization/User/etc. land in BE-0012/BE-0013). When the
    schema arrives, add repository-backed seeding (e.g. a Factory.Persist or a
    seeder bound to a *DB) that inserts these typed fixtures; the structs here
    are deliberately the shared shape that seeder will consume.
  - store/migrate/migrate_test.go still has its own local testPool. It cannot
    use testutil (testutil imports migrate -> import cycle). Leave it; it is
    the bootstrap layer. Every OTHER persistence package must use testutil.
  - Acceptance criterion 5 (stable JSON envelopes) is N/A: testutil is a
    test-support package with no HTTP surface. Criterion 6's "authorization
    failure" branch is also N/A (no auth surface); covered success +
    validation-style failures (empty/messy labels, blank DSN) + the
    not-found-style branch (DB dropped on cleanup -> not in pg_database).
- Learnings for future iterations:
  - testing.TB is a sealed interface (private method) — you CANNOT write a fake
    TB to assert "t.Errorf was called". Pattern: keep the decision logic in a
    pure unexported function (lookupAdminDSN, scanLeaks) and white-box unit-test
    THAT; the testing.TB wrapper is then trivial glue. White-box (package
    testutil) and black-box (package testutil_test) test files coexist fine in
    one directory.
  - A test helper taking testing.TB (not *testing.T) works in benchmarks and
    nested helpers too; t.Skip/Fatalf/Cleanup/Helper/Errorf are all on TB.
  - t.Run waits for a non-parallel subtest INCLUDING its t.Cleanup callbacks
    before returning — so "provision in an inner subtest, then assert it was
    cleaned up" is a reliable way to test cleanup behaviour.
  - Cleanup ordering is LIFO: register admin-pool Close first (runs last), the
    DROP DATABASE second, the test-pool Close last (runs first) — so the pool
    is closed, then the DB dropped while the admin pool is still open.
  - An import cycle is decided at the PACKAGE level: migrate's own _test.go
    files are package migrate, so they cannot import testutil even though
    they're "just tests". Bootstrap-layer packages keep local helpers.

## 2026-05-14 - BE-0012
- What was implemented: stable domain identity, slug, and Dokploy-naming
  primitives for every control-plane resource (new package
  internal/controlplane/domain).
  - domain/id.go: Kind type + the 8 canonical kinds (org/usr/key/proj/env/svc/
    dep/job) backed by a kinds set; ID type = "<kind>_<26-char crockford-base32
    suffix>" carrying 128 bits of crypto/rand entropy. NewID/MustNewID,
    ParseID/MustParseID (rejects missing separator, unknown kind, wrong-length
    suffix, non-Crockford chars — never echoes untrusted input), and
    ID.Valid/Kind/IsKind/String. Lowercase Crockford alphabet (i/l/o/u excluded)
    via base32.NewEncoding(...).WithPadding(NoPadding).
  - domain/slug.go: Slug type; NormalizeSlug (lenient: Unicode case-fold via
    cases.Fold so ß->ss, NFD + strip Mn marks so é->e, NFKC compatibility fold
    so ligatures/full-width -> ascii, lowercase, collapse non-[a-z0-9] runs to
    single hyphens, trim, truncate to MaxSlugLen=30 — idempotent, output always
    passes ValidateSlug); ValidateSlug (strict canonical check) + ParseSlug.
    The transform.Chain is stateful + not concurrency-safe, so it is pooled via
    sync.Pool (slugTransformerPool), one per in-flight call.
  - domain/dokployname.go: DokployName(label, id) = "<slug>-<id with _->->",
    deterministic + globally unique + Docker-safe (<=63 chars; MaxSlugLen 30
    chosen so slug + longest-prefixed ID fits). Empty/unusable label falls back
    to the resource kind. ValidateDokployName + DokployNameMaxLen const.
  - domain/errors.go: sentinel errors (ErrUnknownKind/ErrInvalidID/
    ErrInvalidSlug/ErrInvalidDokployName) with NO HTTP semantics — handler layer
    maps them to apierr.InvalidInput. domain imports neither apierr nor
    apienvelope.
  - Tests: id_test.go (kind validity, 4000-iteration NewID round-trip +
    uniqueness, ParseID table incl. excluded i/l/o/u + uppercase + bad length,
    zero-value, IsKind wrong-kind, error-does-not-echo-input, Must* panics);
    slug_test.go (normalize table incl. diacritics/ß/ligature/full-width/emoji,
    truncation never leaves trailing hyphen, empty-input errors, ValidateSlug +
    ParseSlug tables); dokployname_test.go (embeds id, deterministic across
    equivalent labels, empty-label kind fallback, invalid-id error, length stays
    <=63 for every kind, ValidateDokployName table); fuzz_test.go
    (FuzzNormalizeSlug invariants + idempotency, FuzzValidateSlug, FuzzParseID
    round-trip + Dokploy-safe).
  - go.mod: golang.org/x/text v0.29.0 promoted from indirect to direct (already
    in the module graph; no go.sum change).
  - AGENTS.md: added a domain bullet to internal/controlplane/AGENTS.md and a
    new internal/controlplane/domain/AGENTS.md.
- Files changed: internal/controlplane/domain/{id.go,slug.go,dokployname.go,
  errors.go,id_test.go,slug_test.go,dokployname_test.go,fuzz_test.go,AGENTS.md}
  (new), internal/controlplane/AGENTS.md, go.mod, ralph/prd.json,
  ralph/progress.txt.
- Verification run: gofmt -w .; go mod tidy; go build ./...; go vet ./...;
  go test ./...; go test -race ./...; plus 5s each of go test -fuzz for
  FuzzNormalizeSlug / FuzzValidateSlug / FuzzParseID.
- Results: all passed (fuzz: ~474k/33k/2.4M execs respectively, 0 failures, no
  testdata/fuzz crash files written). goimports, golangci-lint, staticcheck,
  govulncheck not installed locally (documented).
- Remaining risks / follow-ups:
  - testutil/fixtures.go builds FAKE deterministic IDs with its own prefixes
    (it uses "prj" where domain uses "proj") — deliberately independent of the
    domain package. When BE-0013 lands real domain tables, a repository-backed
    seeder should mint real domain.NewID values; the testutil structs are the
    shape that seeder will consume. Aligning testutil's fake prefixes with the
    domain kinds is optional cleanup, not required.
  - Slug uniqueness "within parent scope" (acceptance #2) is half here: domain
    guarantees well-formedness; the per-scope UNIQUE constraint lands with the
    BE-0013 tenant-hierarchy migration.
  - Acceptance #3 ("Dokploy names include deterministic Yalla IDs"): DokployName
    embeds the full ID. The actual Dokploy mapping (org->project->env->service)
    is consumed by the provisioning worker stories, not this one.
  - Acceptance #5-7 (JSON envelopes / authz / not-found / Postgres integration)
    are N/A for a pure domain-primitive package with no HTTP or DB surface;
    covered success + extensive validation-failure + fuzz instead. #8
    (redaction): IDs and slugs are not secrets — N/A, but error messages
    deliberately do not echo untrusted input (TestParseIDErrorDoesNotEchoInput).
- Learnings for future iterations:
  - golang.org/x/text/transform.Chain is STATEFUL and NOT safe for concurrent
    use. A package-level transform.Chain shared across t.Parallel() subtests
    panics in (*link).src. Pool it (sync.Pool) or build per-call. This will
    bite any future package doing Unicode normalisation.
  - cases.Fold() (golang.org/x/text/cases) does full Unicode case folding,
    which is what maps ß -> "ss"; strings.ToLower and NFKC do NOT. Put it first
    in the chain, before NFD/NFKC.
  - 16 random bytes encode to exactly 26 base32 chars with no padding (5 bytes
    -> 8 chars; 16 = 3*5 + 1 -> 24 + 2 = 26). Crockford lowercase alphabet is
    "0123456789abcdefghjkmnpqrstvwxyz" (i/l/o/u removed).
  - Go fuzz: f.Add seeds live in code; generated "interesting" corpus goes to
    the build cache, NOT testdata/ — testdata/fuzz/ files appear only on a
    failure. A clean fuzz run leaves the working tree untouched.
  - Keep domain primitives free of apierr/apienvelope imports — return plain
    sentinel errors and let the HTTP layer translate. This keeps the dependency
    arrow pointing one way (httpapi -> domain, never the reverse).

## 2026-05-14 - BE-0013
- What was implemented: the tenant hierarchy schema — organizations, users,
  memberships, projects, environments, services, and dokploy_refs — as
  migration 0002, plus schema-level integration tests.
  - internal/controlplane/store/migrate/migrations/0002_tenant_hierarchy.up.sql
    (new): the seven tables. organizations is the tenant root; users is a
    GLOBAL identity table linked to orgs only through memberships. projects/
    environments/services each carry organization_id (+ parent IDs) and are
    tenant-isolated by COMPOSITE foreign keys — environments reference
    projects(organization_id, id), services reference
    environments(organization_id, project_id, id) — so a child can never cross
    the tenant boundary. Each parent exposes the matching UNIQUE(organization_id,
    [project_id,] id) constraint the composite FK needs. Slugs are citext and
    unique within parent scope (org for projects, project for environments,
    environment for services), never globally. Every table has created_at/
    updated_at + a set_updated_at() BEFORE UPDATE trigger. memberships PK is
    (organization_id, user_id) with a role CHECK (owner/admin/member). services
    has a kind CHECK (application/database/compose). dokploy_refs is the
    polymorphic Yalla-resource -> Dokploy-object map (yalla_kind/yalla_id +
    dokploy_resource/dokploy_id covering organization/project/environment/
    application/compose/database/domain/backup); tenant-scoped via the
    organization_id FK, UNIQUE(dokploy_resource, dokploy_id) so an object is
    claimed once, UNIQUE(organization_id, yalla_id, dokploy_resource, dokploy_id)
    against duplicate rows while still allowing many domains/backups per
    resource. ON DELETE CASCADE everywhere from organizations down.
  - 0002_tenant_hierarchy.down.sql (new): drops the seven tables newest-first;
    DROP TABLE removes each table's indexes + trigger. set_updated_at() itself
    belongs to the baseline migration and is left in place.
  - internal/controlplane/store/schema_test.go (new, package store_test):
    integration tests via testutil.RequireMigratedDB + testutil.NewFactory —
    tables-exist, full-chain insert + hierarchy walk, FK enforcement (missing
    org, env/service crossing the tenant boundary via the composite FK), slug
    uniqueness within parent (incl. citext case-insensitivity + same slug
    allowed in a different org), cascade delete (descendants gone, global users
    row survives), cross-tenant isolation (org-scoped count), dokploy_refs
    constraints (object claimed once, duplicate rows rejected, bad
    dokploy_resource rejected, org FK), membership constraints (one per org,
    role CHECK, user FK). Constraint violations matched via *pgconn.PgError
    SQLSTATE class 23.
  - internal/controlplane/store/migrate/migrate_test.go: TestMigrationsUpIs
    Idempotent's Status().Current assertion now derives the expected version
    from m.Migrations() instead of the literal 1, so future migrations don't
    break it.
  - internal/controlplane/store/AGENTS.md: documented the composite-FK tenant
    boundary, global users table, dokploy_refs polymorphism, the no-ID-prefix-
    CHECK decision, and where schema integration tests live.
- Files changed: internal/controlplane/store/migrate/migrations/
  0002_tenant_hierarchy.{up,down}.sql (new), internal/controlplane/store/
  schema_test.go (new), internal/controlplane/store/migrate/migrate_test.go,
  internal/controlplane/store/AGENTS.md, ralph/prd.json, ralph/progress.txt.
- Verification run: gofmt -w .; go mod tidy; go build ./...; go vet ./...;
  go test ./...; go test -race ./... — the last two also run with
  YALLA_TEST_DATABASE_URL pointing at the docker-compose Postgres 16 (all 8
  schema integration tests RUN, not skipped, and PASS under -race).
- Results: all passed. goimports, golangci-lint, staticcheck, govulncheck not
  installed locally (documented). Integration tests skip cleanly without the
  env var so CI without Postgres stays green.
- Remaining risks / follow-ups:
  - No repository layer yet: BE-0014 (transaction pattern) + later repo stories
    add the tenant-scoped query methods. The testutil.Factory builds in-memory
    fixtures whose column shape matches these tables — a repository-backed
    seeder should consume them. Note Factory.User carries an OrganizationID but
    the users table is global; the seeder must create a separate membership row.
  - Acceptance criteria 5-6 (JSON envelopes / authorization-failure unit tests)
    are N/A for a pure schema migration with no HTTP surface; covered success +
    extensive constraint-violation (FK / unique / check) + not-found-style
    (cascade-deleted rows absent) instead.
  - Criterion 8 (redaction): no table stores secrets/tokens/env values — those
    land in dedicated later migrations; N/A here.
  - The migration runner is still not wired into cmd/yalla-api startup; that
    seam (runStartupChecks + runtime.Meta.SetMigrationVersion) is still open.
- Learnings for future iterations:
  - Composite foreign keys are the database-level tenant-isolation primitive:
    child FK (organization_id, parent_id) -> parent UNIQUE(organization_id, id)
    makes "child in a different org than its parent" unrepresentable. Every new
    hierarchy table should follow this and expose the UNIQUE its children need.
  - package migrate cannot import testutil (testutil imports migrate). Schema
    integration tests therefore live in a sibling package store_test, which CAN
    import testutil. This is the home for every future domain-table test.
  - pgx surfaces constraint violations as *pgconn.PgError; SQLSTATE class "23"
    (Code[:2] == "23") covers FK / unique / check / not-null uniformly — match
    on the class, not individual codes, for "the database rejected this" tests.
  - Adding a migration can break migrate_test.go assertions that hard-code the
    latest version. Only Status().Current was literal-bound; the rest already
    derive from len(m.Migrations()). Audit that file on every new migration.
  - citext columns give case-insensitive UNIQUE for free — 'shared-slug' and
    'SHARED-SLUG' collide within the same parent scope without a lower() index.

## 2026-05-14 - BE-0014
- What was implemented: the repository transaction pattern in
  internal/controlplane/store — the small persistence layer that makes
  transactional policy and quota checks structurally hard to bypass.
  - store.go: the Querier interface (Exec/Query/QueryRow, satisfied by pgx.Tx
    and *Tx); the *Tx handle (wraps pgx.Tx, unexported field); Store with
    New(pool, logger); Store.Write (read/write tx -> *Tx, commit on nil return,
    rollback on any error or panic via a deferred WithoutCancel rollback);
    Store.Read (read-only tx -> Querier); isConstraintViolation (SQLSTATE class
    23) and mapWriteError (constraint -> apierr.Conflict, else
    apierr.StoreUnavailable, raw cause wrapped for logging only).
  - project.go: Project struct + ProjectRepository — the reference repository.
    Insert requires *Tx (mutation), Get/CountByOrganization take Querier
    (reads). Every query is tenant-scoped (organization_id before id) and fully
    parameterized; pgx.ErrNoRows -> apierr.NotFound.
  - projectservice.go: the Authorizer / QuotaReserver / JobEnqueuer port
    interfaces (real engines land in BE-0018/BE-0022/BE-0024), CreateProjectInput,
    validateCreateProjectInput (pure, returns apierr.InvalidInput with field
    paths), and ProjectService.Create — the reference unit of work: validate,
    then inside one Store.Write compose authorize -> reserve quota -> insert ->
    enqueue job. Any step's failure rolls the whole transaction back.
  - doc.go rewritten to document the pattern.
- Files changed: internal/controlplane/store/{store,project,projectservice,doc}.go
  (new/rewritten), internal/controlplane/store/{internal_test,store_test,
  project_test,projectservice_test}.go (new), internal/controlplane/store/
  AGENTS.md, ralph/prd.json, ralph/progress.txt.
- Verification run: gofmt -w .; go build ./...; go vet ./...; go mod tidy;
  go test ./...; go test -race ./... — the last two with
  YALLA_TEST_DATABASE_URL pointing at the docker-compose Postgres 16, so all 18
  new store tests RUN (not skipped) and PASS, including under -race.
- Results: all passed. Unit tests in package store (internal_test.go) run
  without Postgres; the 12 integration tests in package store_test skip cleanly
  when YALLA_TEST_DATABASE_URL is unset. goimports / golangci-lint /
  staticcheck / govulncheck still not installed locally (documented in earlier
  entries).
- Acceptance criteria mapping:
  - "Repositories accept context and transaction handles explicitly" — every
    method takes ctx + a Querier or *Tx as its first/second parameter.
  - "Mutations combine authorization, quota, and write state in one
    transaction" — ProjectService.Create.
  - "Tests assert rollback on validation, quota, and Dokploy enqueue failures"
    — TestProjectServiceCreate{ValidationFailure,QuotaFailureRollsBack,
    EnqueueFailureRollsBack}, plus TestWriteRollsBackOn{Error,Panic}.
  - "No SQL string concatenation for user input" — all statements
    parameterized; the only `+` in SQL is the compile-time constant column list.
  - Criterion 5 (JSON envelopes) — no-op: this story has no HTTP surface; all
    errors are typed apierr values that already render through apienvelope.
  - "Unit tests cover success, validation failure, authorization failure,
    not-found" — covered across internal_test.go + the two _test.go suites.
  - "Integration tests run against isolated Postgres migrations, no live
    Dokploy" — RequireMigratedDB; the Dokploy dependency is a fake JobEnqueuer.
  - Redaction — no secrets are stored by this layer; TestMapWriteErrorDoesNotLeakCause
    proves a raw driver cause (DSN/credential) never reaches the error message.
- Remaining risks / follow-ups:
  - The port interfaces (Authorizer/QuotaReserver/JobEnqueuer) and the
    project.create / project.provision / projects constants are placeholders
    until BE-0018 (RBAC), BE-0022 (quota checker), and BE-0024 (job table)
    land. When they do, wire the real implementations into ProjectService and
    move ownership of the constants to those packages — store must not import
    them.
  - ProjectRepository is intentionally minimal (Insert/Get/CountByOrganization).
    Later hierarchy-CRUD stories extend it and add Environment/Service repos
    following the same *Tx-mutation / Querier-read split.
  - Store is not yet constructed at cmd/yalla-api startup; that wiring (pool +
    Store + migration runner) is still an open seam from BE-0010/BE-0013.
- Learnings for future iterations:
  - The "hard to bypass" mechanism is a TYPE, not a convention: *Tx has an
    unexported field and is only produced by Store.Write, so a repository
    mutation that takes *Tx literally cannot be called outside a transaction.
    Copy this split (mutations take *Tx, reads take Querier) for every repo.
  - testutil.Factory IDs are deliberately non-canonical (org_<16hex>0001), so
    domain.ParseID rejects them. Any test that feeds a service whose validation
    calls domain.ParseID/ParseSlug must seed rows with real domain.NewID(...)
    values — see seedDomainOrg in projectservice_test.go. Factory fixtures are
    still fine for direct repository/SQL tests that skip domain validation.
  - Store.Write needs no recover(): a deferred rollback runs during panic
    unwinding, and because the deferred func does not call recover() the panic
    keeps propagating. Use a `committed` bool (not the named return error) to
    decide whether the deferred rollback should fire.
  - Rollback uses context.WithoutCancel(ctx): the caller's context is often
    already cancelled (that is *why* we are rolling back), but the rollback
    must still reach the database. Ignore pgx.ErrTxClosed on rollback — it is
    expected after a failed commit.
  - apierr already keeps raw causes out of the user-facing Message
    (yerr.Error.Error() renders only "code: message"), so wrapping a driver
    error via .Wrap() is leak-safe and still errors.Is-traversable.
---

## 2026-05-14 - BE-0015
- What was implemented: API key hashing, generation, verification, and the

  - internal/controlplane/store/migrate/migrations/0003_api_keys.{up,down}.sql
    (new): the api_keys table. Stores ONLY secret_hash (one-way) + a public,
    globally-UNIQUE prefix — no column ever holds a usable credential.
    Tenant-scoped via organization_id FK (ON DELETE CASCADE). Lifecycle columns
    expires_at / revoked_at / last_used_at; created_by REFERENCES users ON
    DELETE SET NULL (nullable — keys survive creator deletion, lose
    attribution); scopes text[] NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'. created_at/updated_at +
    set_updated_at() trigger. Index on organization_id for tenant listing
    (the prefix lookup rides the UNIQUE index).
  - internal/controlplane/auth/apikey.go (new package): the credential
    primitives. Token shape "yk_<prefixRandom>_<secret>" (Crockford base32, no
    padding) is a public contract. Generate() -> GeneratedKey{Token, Prefix,
    SecretHash}; HashSecret = hex SHA-256 (fast unsalted hash is correct for
    192-bit-entropy secrets); VerifySecret uses subtle.ConstantTimeCompare;
    ParseToken validates shape and returns ErrMalformedToken (never echoes
    input). Token is a self-redacting type: String/GoString/LogValue/
    MarshalJSON all yield output.Sentinel; plaintext only via explicit
    Reveal().
  - internal/controlplane/store/apikey.go (new): APIKey row struct +
    lifecycle predicates (IsRevoked/IsExpired/IsUsable) + APIKeyRepository
    following the *Tx-mutation / Querier-read pattern. Insert/TouchLastUsed/
    Revoke take *Tx; FindByPrefix/Get/ListByOrganization take Querier.
    FindByPrefix is the deliberate non-tenant-scoped auth lookup (documented).
    Revoke is idempotent via COALESCE(revoked_at, $3). Zero-row mutations ->
    apierr.NotFound.
  - internal/controlplane/auth/apikey_test.go (new, package auth_test):
    generate->parse->verify round trip, uniqueness, deterministic hashing,
    constant-time verify (correct/wrong/empty/tampered), ParseToken malformed
    table, Token redaction across %v/%+v/%#v/%s/slog/JSON, and
    AssertRedactedValue over the full token + bare secret.
  - internal/controlplane/store/apikey_test.go (new, package store_test):
    insert+FindByPrefix, FindByPrefix not-found, tenant-scoped Get/List/
    TouchLastUsed/Revoke (cross-tenant -> NotFound), duplicate-prefix Conflict,
    nil-Tx guard, expiry round trip, the "plaintext is never persisted" test
    (concatenates every text column and asserts the token/secret are absent),
    and DB-free lifecycle-predicate unit tests. Adds seedUser helper.
  - internal/controlplane/auth/AGENTS.md (new); store/AGENTS.md: documented the
    api_keys table, the FindByPrefix non-tenant-scoped exception, and the
    idempotent zero-row-mutation -> NotFound convention.
- Files changed: 0003_api_keys.{up,down}.sql (new), auth/apikey.go (new),
  auth/apikey_test.go (new), auth/AGENTS.md (new), store/apikey.go (new),
  store/apikey_test.go (new), store/AGENTS.md, ralph/prd.json,
  ralph/progress.txt.
- Verification run: gofmt -w .; go mod tidy; go build ./...; go vet ./...;
  go test ./...; go test -race ./... — the last two with
  YALLA_TEST_DATABASE_URL pointing at the docker-compose Postgres 16, so all 10
  api_keys integration tests RUN (confirmed via -v, not skipped) and PASS,
  including under -race.
- Results: all passed (go test ./... and go test -race ./... exit 0, 24 ok
  packages; go vet exit 0). goimports/golangci-lint/staticcheck/govulncheck
  still not installed locally (documented in earlier entries).
- Acceptance criteria mapping:
  - "public prefix + secret body; only a hash is stored" — auth.Generate +
    api_keys (no plaintext column).
  - "lookup uses prefix ... constant-time hash comparison" —
    APIKeyRepository.FindByPrefix + auth.VerifySecret (subtle.ConstantTimeCompare).
  - "expiration, revocation, last_used_at, created_by, scope metadata" — all
    present as columns + APIKey fields + lifecycle predicates.
  - "tests prove plaintext keys are never persisted or logged" —
    TestAPIKeyPlaintextIsNeverPersisted + TestTokenRedactsItselfInEveryRendering
    + TestGeneratedKeyNeverLeaksTheSecret.
  - Criterion 5 (JSON envelopes) — N/A: this story has no HTTP surface; all
    errors are typed apierr values that already render through apienvelope.
  - "unit tests cover success/validation failure/authorization failure/
    not-found" — success + validation failure (ParseToken malformed table) +
    not-found (FindByPrefix/Get/TouchLastUsed/Revoke). Authorization-failure is
    N/A at this layer (the policy engine is BE-0018); the *Tx-only mutation
    surface is the structural hook the authorize step will share.
  - Integration tests use isolated Postgres migrations, no live Dokploy.
  - Redaction — Token self-redacts everywhere; the persisted-row test proves
    no plaintext reaches any column.
- Remaining risks / follow-ups:
  - No HTTP endpoint or auth middleware yet: the "create API key" endpoint and
    the request-authentication middleware (parse token -> FindByPrefix ->
    VerifySecret -> IsUsable -> TouchLastUsed -> Principal) are later stories.
    auth.VerifySecret + APIKeyRepository are the primitives they compose.
  - api_keys.scopes is an opaque text[] for now; the scope catalog / validation
    is a later policy story.
  - BE-0016 (service accounts) will need created_by to optionally point at a
    service account rather than a user; revisit the created_by FK then.
- Learnings for future iterations:
  - A self-redacting credential type needs FOUR methods to be leak-proof
    against testutil.AssertRedactedValue: String (covers %v/%+v/%s), GoString
    (covers %#v), LogValue (slog), MarshalJSON (JSON). Missing any one leaks.
  - testutil.Factory.APIKey only reads org.ID and user.ID off its args, so a
    test can pass partial fixtures: f.APIKey(testutil.Organization{ID: orgID},
    testutil.User{ID: uid}, label) just to borrow its globally-unique key ID.
  - For "plaintext never persisted" assertions, concatenate every text-bearing
    column in SQL (id||'|'||...||array_to_string(scopes,',')) and run
    testutil.AssertRedacted on the result — proves absence across the whole row
    in one shot.
  - Idempotent revoke without a Conflict: UPDATE ... SET col = COALESCE(col, $n)
    keeps the first timestamp and still counts the row as affected, so
    RowsAffected()==0 cleanly means "no such row" -> NotFound, never "already
    done".
  - base32.Encoding.EncodedLen() respects the WithPadding setting — compute
    token-part lengths from it rather than hard-coding, so the entropy budget
    and the ParseToken shape check stay in sync.

- Remaining risks / follow-ups:

    layer encodes the identity + ownership; the rule is documented in the migration and struct
    comments for the policy story to enforce.

    HTTP endpoint (same shape as BE-0013/BE-0014). The service-account HTTP surface lands later.

    independent — a human can mint a key owned by a service account — so there is no

- Learnings for future iterations:
  - A composite FK with a nullable child column is the clean tenant-isolation primitive for an
    OPTIONAL parent link: MATCH SIMPLE skips the check when the nullable column is NULL, and
    enforces the full (organization_id, parent_id) tuple when it is set. api_keys.service_account_id
    is the reference example; reuse this shape for any optional cross-table ownership link.

    (0003) migration file (ErrChecksumMismatch by design). apiKeyColumns is the single column
    list — updating that const + scanAPIKey + Insert keeps FindByPrefix/Get/List* consistent.
  - Not every unit-of-work needs a JobEnqueuer. ServiceAccountService composes only
    authorize -> quota -> write because a service account provisions nothing in Dokploy. Mirror
    ProjectService's shape but drop the steps that do not apply.
  - White-box store tests (package store) can freely reuse helpers across files — nopAuthorizer/
    nopQuotaReserver live in internal_test.go and serviceaccountservice_internal_test.go uses them.
    Likewise package store_test integration tests reuse recordingAuthorizer/recordingQuota,
    seedOrg/seedProject/seedDomainOrg, newStore, and insertAPIKey across files.
  - migrate.go embeds migrations/*.sql by glob, so a new NNNN_*.sql pair is picked up with no Go
    change; migrate_test.go already derives the latest version from m.Migrations() (no literal).

## 2026-05-14 - BE-0018
- What was implemented: the RBAC and scoped grants engine — internal/controlplane/policy, the
  authoritative authorization decision engine for the control plane.
  - internal/controlplane/policy/policy.go: package doc + core types. Role (six built-in
    constants: owner/admin/developer/viewer/ci/support); Capability (self/read/deploy/write/
    admin/owner/support); Action (dotted string); Scope (Organization->Project->Environment->
    Service, with unexported covers() containment); Grant (Role + Scope); Resource (domain.Kind
    + Scope); Principal (ID/Kind/OrganizationID/Role/Grants/Disabled — the single concrete type
    both API-key and session auth resolve to); Reason (stable allow/deny codes) + Decision.
  - internal/controlplane/policy/catalog.go: builtinRoleCaps (the authoritative role->capability
    matrix, each role listed in full — capabilities are NOT hierarchical); defaultActionCatalog
    (all 61 API-surface requiredActions -> one Capability each); BuiltinRoles()/Actions() stable
    sorted enumerators; CustomRoleResolver hook type; capSet helper.
  - internal/controlplane/policy/engine.go: Engine (immutable, concurrency-safe), NewEngine +
    Option (WithCustomRoles, WithAction). Decide(Principal, Action, Resource) -> Decision is a
    pure, total, panic-free function with a fixed deny-by-default order: no/disabled principal ->
    unknown action -> CapSelf bypass -> unresolvable org role -> cross-tenant (deny unless
    support read/break-glass) -> org-role capability -> covering-grant capability ->
    out_of_scope vs no_capability. Authorize/AuthorizeCtx map denials onto typed apierr
    (Unauthenticated for a missing principal, Forbidden otherwise; message carries only the
    action + reason code, never an ID). WithPrincipal/PrincipalFromContext carry the principal
    on the request context.
  - internal/controlplane/policy/policy_test.go (white-box, package policy): TestPolicyMatrix
    exhaustively covers all 6 built-in roles x all 61 actions (367 subtests) with verdicts
    derived from the matrix+catalog so drift fails CI; plus no-principal, disabled, unknown
    action, unknown role (and CapSelf bypassing the role check), custom roles (as org role and
    as grant role), grant narrowing/widening/out-of-scope/ignored-invalid (unknown role,
    foreign-org scope), cross-tenant deny + support read/break-glass allow, TestScopeCovers
    containment table, Authorize apierr-code mapping with a no-ID-leak assertion, the context
    carrier round trip, and a catalog/roles well-formedness guard.
  - Removed placeholder internal/controlplane/policy/doc.go (package doc now lives in policy.go).
  - internal/controlplane/AGENTS.md: documented the policy engine, the action catalog as the
    requiredAction source of truth, capability-based roles, scoped grants, and the context carrier.
- Files changed: internal/controlplane/policy/{policy.go,catalog.go,engine.go,policy_test.go}
  (new), internal/controlplane/policy/doc.go (deleted), internal/controlplane/AGENTS.md,
  ralph/prd.json, ralph/progress.txt.
- Verification run: gofmt -w .; go mod tidy; go build ./...; go vet ./...; go test ./...;
  go test -race ./....
- Results: all passed (TestPolicyMatrix = 367 passing subtests). goimports, golangci-lint,
  staticcheck, govulncheck not installed locally.
- Remaining risks / follow-ups:
  - The store.Authorizer port (Authorize(ctx, q Querier, action, organizationID)) is NOT yet
    wired to this engine — the existing services still use nopAuthorizer test doubles. A later
    story should add a store-port adapter that reads policy.PrincipalFromContext(ctx), builds an
    org-scoped Resource, and calls Engine.Authorize. It was left out here to keep policy from
    taking a build dependency on store and to keep BE-0018 to the engine itself.
  - Grants are in-memory on the Principal. The DB-backed grant rows the store.Authorizer comment
    anticipates ("a policy decision that reads grant rows") land with the grants-persistence
    story; the project.grants.* / environment.grants.* actions are already catalogued.
  - Postgres integration criterion is N/A (pure engine, no persistence). JSON-envelope criterion
    is N/A (no HTTP surface yet) — the engine emits typed apierr the apienvelope renderer maps.
  - "not-found behavior" maps to ReasonDeniedUnknownAction here (an uncatalogued action); there
    is no resource lookup in a pure engine.
- Learnings for future iterations:
  - Capability indirection keeps the role/action matrix small and TOTAL: roles confer a Capability
    set, actions require one Capability, and the per-action verdict is a set membership check.
    This made an exhaustive 6x61 table test derivable from the two source-of-truth maps rather
    than hand-enumerated, so the test fails the instant either map drifts.
  - CapSelf is checked BEFORE org-role resolution on purpose: auth.me/auth.orgs/organization.create
    must work for any authenticated principal even one with an unknown/empty role.
  - Cross-tenant is enforced structurally (resource.Scope.OrganizationID != principal.OrganizationID
    is the first thing checked after role resolution), and grants are filtered to the principal's
    own org — a foreign-org grant can never widen authority.
  - The 61 distinct requiredAction values come from prd.json apiSurface.endpoints; query them with
    ctx_execute_file rather than reading the 837KB PRD. defaultActionCatalog must stay in sync as
    later endpoint stories land — TestCatalogAndRolesAreWellFormed asserts the count (61).
  - Go 1.25 in this repo: t.Context() is available in tests; no need for context.Background().

## 2026-05-14 - BE-0019
- What was implemented: the action catalog as a typed, stable, compile-checked
  permission contract.
  - internal/controlplane/policy/actions.go (new): a `policy.Action…` constant
    for every one of the 61 catalogued backend actions (the dotted string value
    of each is public, stable API). `allActions` enumerates them as the single
    source of truth for "which actions exist"; `Actions()` returns a sorted
    stable copy (moved here from catalog.go); `Catalogued(Action) bool` is the
    membership check the BE-0020 authorization middleware will use to reject an
    endpoint wired to an unknown action.
  - internal/controlplane/policy/catalog.go: rewrote `defaultActionCatalog` to
    key on the `Action` constants instead of bare string literals, so an
    uncatalogued or mistyped action is now a compile error. Removed the
    duplicate `Actions()` (now in actions.go). builtinRoleCaps/BuiltinRoles
    unchanged.

    and `Operation.RequiredAction`, rendered as the `x-required-action` OpenAPI
    extension (omitempty — public endpoints omit it). operationFor wires it
    through. The published document is unchanged for the current public-only
    route table (omitempty), so no byte-stability regression.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/routes_test.go (new):
    TestEveryAuthenticatedRouteHasMappedAction iterates the served route table
    plus openAPIEndpoint and enforces the invariant — every RequiresAuth route
    must name a `policy.Catalogued` action; every public route must name none.
    The moment a future endpoint story registers an authenticated route without
    a mapped action, CI fails.
  - internal/controlplane/policy/actions_test.go (new): catalog<->allActions
    lockstep (no dups, no orphans, equal length), Actions() sorted + non-aliased,
    Catalogued known/unknown/empty, BE-0019 concept coverage

  - internal/controlplane/openapi/openapi_action_test.go (new): x-required-action
    renders for an authenticated operation and is omitted for a public one.
  - internal/controlplane/AGENTS.md: documented the action-constant convention
    and the new guard tests.
- Files changed: internal/controlplane/policy/{actions.go,actions_test.go} (new),
  internal/controlplane/policy/catalog.go,

  internal/controlplane/httpapi/routes_test.go (new),
  internal/controlplane/AGENTS.md, ralph/prd.json, ralph/progress.txt.

  - When you add an endpoint: add the `Action` constant in policy/actions.go,

## 2026-05-14 - BE-0017 - Implement user session token model
- Implemented human session token primitives in internal/controlplane/auth/session.go:
  compact HS256 JWT mint/verify, version-based revocation, redacted SessionToken
  credential type, and SessionClaims.Principal() -> policy.Principal so session
  auth and API-key auth resolve to one internal identity shape.

  - internal/controlplane/auth/session.go (new): SessionToken (credential type,
    String/GoString/LogValue/MarshalJSON -> output.Sentinel, Reveal escape hatch),
    SessionClaims (+ Principal()), MintSession/MintSessionParams,
    VerifySession/VerifySessionParams, sentinel errors (ErrTokenSignature,
    ErrTokenExpired, ErrTokenIssuer, ErrTokenAudience, ErrTokenRevoked,
    ErrUnknownSubject; structural failures reuse the existing ErrMalformedToken),
    ErrInvalidMintParams. Fixed base64url JWT header constant so "alg":"none"
    downgrades are unrepresentable. Signature check is constant-time hmac.Equal
    over every accepted key (active + rotation).
  - internal/controlplane/auth/session_test.go (new): round-trip + claim mapping,
    Principal() accepted by the real policy engine, expired/wrong-audience/
    wrong-issuer/bad-signature/tampered-payload, rotated-key acceptance, a table
    of malformed tokens (incl. alg:none, unknown payload field, exp<=iat),
    revocation-version matrix (match/stale/unknown-subject = not-found), mint
    param validation, and the full redaction contract over the token + signing key.
  - internal/controlplane/auth/AGENTS.md: added "Session token contract" section.
  - ralph/prd.json: BE-0017 passes -> true.

  go test ./...; go test -race ./internal/controlplane/....
- Results: all passed. goimports, golangci-lint, staticcheck, govulncheck not
  installed locally.
- Remaining risks / follow-ups:
  - VerifySession does no I/O: the CurrentRoleVersion lookup is injected. The
    BE-0020 auth middleware must wire it to the store (membership/role-version
    read) and own issuer/audience config — config.Config currently has
    SigningKeys but no Issuer/Audience fields; BE-0020 will need to add them.
  - All verification failures currently map to a single existing error code at
    the wire level once BE-0020 lands; the distinct E_AUTH_EXPIRED /
    E_AUTHENTICATION_REQUIRED / E_AUTH_INVALID codes are their own stories
    (BE-0513/0514/0515) and were intentionally not introduced here.
  - AC "JSON envelopes" and the Postgres-integration criterion are N/A for a
    pure crypto/value primitive (mirrors apikey.go); AC "authorization failure"
    is covered analogously by the revoked / unknown-subject paths.
- Learnings for future iterations:
  - auth may import policy + domain (no cycle: policy imports apierr+domain,
    neither imports auth). This is how a credential package can expose a typed
    policy.Principal without the store-style narrow-port indirection.
  - policy.Principal contains a []Grant slice, so it is NOT comparable with ==
    in tests — compare fields individually (vet will reject the == form).
  - Reuse ErrMalformedToken (already in apikey.go) for session structural
    failures so the auth middleware has one "bad credential shape" sentinel
    across both credential types.
  - The well-known base64url for {"alg":"HS256","typ":"JWT"} is
    eyJhbGciOiJIUzI1NiIsInR5cCI6IkpXVCJ9 — pinning it as a constant is both the
    alg-downgrade defence and cheaper than decoding+inspecting a header.
