## 2026-05-15 - BE-0020 - Implement authorization middleware
- What was implemented: the request authentication + authorization middleware —
  the layer that turns an inbound bearer credential into a policy.Principal,
  attaches it to the request context, and authorizes the route's action.
  - internal/controlplane/auth/authenticator.go (new): Authenticator +
    AuthenticatorConfig + NewAuthenticator. Authenticate(ctx, bearerToken)
    resolves three schemes by shape — API key ("yk_" prefix), human session
    JWT, internal worker shared secret — to one Identity{Principal, Method}.
    Ports: CredentialStore interface (FindAPIKeyByPrefix / FindServiceAccount /
    OrganizationRoleVersion) + auth-local DTOs APIKeyRecord / ServiceAccountRecord
    so auth never imports store. Sentinels ErrNoCredentials / ErrInvalidCredentials;
    every invalid-credential cause (malformed token, unknown prefix, wrong
    secret, revoked/expired key, bad signature, expired/revoked/unknown-subject
    session) collapses to ErrInvalidCredentials — no prefix-existence oracle —
    while a datastore failure is propagated unchanged. AuthMethod constants,
    InternalWorkerPrincipalID, stable SessionIssuer / SessionAudience. API-key
    principals carry no Role/Grants (grant resolution from api_keys.scopes is a
    later story); session principals get their role from the verified claim;
    the internal worker principal is role-less and org-less. constantTimeEqual
    hashes both inputs so the worker-token compare does not leak length.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/middleware.go (new): RequireAuth(authenticator,
    engine, action, ResourceResolver) and RequireInternalWorker(authenticator)
    middleware; bearerToken (RFC 7235 case-insensitive "Bearer " extraction);
    writeAuthError / toAPIError. Missing creds -> 401 E_AUTH ("authentication is
    required"); invalid -> 401 E_AUTH ("the supplied credentials are invalid",
    fixed/generic); unauthorized -> 403 E_FORBIDDEN with the stable policy
    reason; dependency failure keeps its typed 5xx. Attaches the principal via
    policy.WithPrincipal and enriches the log record via telemetry.SetOrgID /
    SetPrincipalID before the authorization decision. Everything renders through
    apienvelope. The middleware is NOT yet wired into newRouteTable — the
    bootstrap routes are all public; endpoint stories wrap their routes.
  - internal/controlplane/store/migrate/migrations/0005_membership_role_version.
    {up,down}.sql (new): ALTER memberships ADD COLUMN role_version bigint NOT
    NULL DEFAULT 1 CHECK (>= 1) — the counter behind version-based session
    revocation that BE-0017's session primitive expected to exist.
  - internal/controlplane/store/membership.go (new): Membership struct +

  staticcheck / govulncheck still not installed locally (documented in earlier
  iterations).
- Acceptance criteria mapping:
  - "Missing credentials return E_AUTHENTICATION_REQUIRED" / "Invalid credentials
    return E_AUTH_INVALID without revealing whether a key prefix exists": the
    repo's settled error taxonomy uses one code E_AUTH (apierr.Unauthenticated)
    for both, with distinct fixed messages. The PRD's two code names are
    descriptive labels, not formal codes (the PRD has no error catalog defining
    them, and yerr/apierr are a shared CLI+backend contract). The behavioural
    guarantee — a uniform, generic invalid-credentials result with no
    prefix-existence oracle — is fully implemented and tested
    (TestAuthenticateAPIKeyInvalidIsUniform asserts unknown-prefix and
    wrong-secret produce the identical error).
  - "Authenticated but unauthorized -> E_FORBIDDEN with a stable reason":
    RequireAuth runs engine.Authorize; the 403 message carries the stable
    policy.Reason code (TestRequireAuthUnauthorizedIsForbidden /
    ...ResourceResolverEnforcesTenantBoundary).

    public endpoints": all four, in both the auth unit tests and the httpapi
    contract tests (TestPublicRoutesNeedNoCredential is the anonymous leg).
  - Envelopes / integration-from-migrations / redaction: all covered.
- Remaining risks / follow-ups:
  - The middleware is built and fully tested but not wired into newRouteTable —
    there are no authenticated routes yet (same shape as store services not yet
    wired into cmd). The endpoint stories wrap their routes with RequireAuth and
    grow NewHandler's wiring then; cmd/yalla-api must build the Authenticator
    (config needs an internal-worker-token field — not added here to keep the
    story focused; it is a config-story-shaped change).
  - API-key principals are role-less/grant-less until the api_keys.scopes ->
    policy.Grant resolution story lands; today an API-key principal can do only
    CapSelf actions. This is deny-by-default and safe, but means API-key auth is
    not yet "useful" for tenant actions — intentional.
- Learnings for future iterations:
  - policy.Principal contains a Grants slice, so it is NOT == comparable —
    compare field-by-field in tests (see assertPrincipal in authenticator_test.go).
  - The "consumer defines the port, provider implements it" rule has a direction
    choice: here auth defines CredentialStore and store implements it, so store
    imports auth (auth stays store-free + unit-testable). Verify no cycle: auth
    imports only policy/domain/output; none import store.
  - VerifySession's CurrentRoleVersion hook is a func returning (int64, bool)
    with no error channel. To not disguise a datastore outage as
    ErrUnknownSubject, capture the lookup error in a closure variable and check
    it after VerifySession returns.
  - testutil.NewFactory builds in-memory fixture VALUES; package store_test
    already has seedOrg/seedUser/seedServiceAccount helpers — reuse them and add
    seedMembership rather than re-rolling raw inserts.
  - A new migration is auto-discovered from the //go:embed FS by filename; no
    registration list to update. Existing memberships INSERTs in schema_test.go
    omit role_version and still pass because the column has DEFAULT 1.

## 2026-05-15 - BE-0021 - Implement quota policy schema
- What was implemented: migration 0006_quota_policy_schema — the four
  source-of-truth quota tables plus two shared domains. Pure schema story,
  same shape as 0002 tenant hierarchy: migration + schema integration tests,
  no Go repository/service/handler yet (those land with BE-0022).
  - quota_resource DOMAIN: the closed set of 14 countable quota dimensions
    (projects, environments, services, applications, compose_stacks,
    databases, domains, preview_environments, cpu_millicores, memory_mb,
    storage_gb, backups, concurrent_deployments, monthly_deployments) — reused
    by all four tables so an unknown dimension is rejected by the database.
  - quota_enforcement_mode DOMAIN: hard | soft | metered | disabled.
  - quota_policies: one limit per (scope, resource). A row is EITHER a
    plan_default (keyed by text `plan`, no FK to a plans table — plans land
    later) OR an organization override (keyed by organization_id), enforced by
    the quota_policies_scope_consistent CHECK + two partial unique indexes. The
    quota checker resolves "org override if present, else plan default".
  - quota_usage: actual allocated amount, one counter row per
    (organization_id, resource) via UNIQUE — the row the checker locks.
  - quota_reservations: in-flight claims (amount > 0), a status lifecycle
    (active -> committed | released | expired), expires_at so a crashed job
    cannot strand quota, and quota_reservations_settled_consistent CHECK tying
    settled_at to non-active status. Nullable job_id (no FK yet —
    provisioning_jobs is BE-0024). Exposes UNIQUE (organization_id, id) as a
    composite-FK target.
  - usage_events: append-only log (no updated_at, no update trigger), signed
    delta, optional reservation link via composite FK
    (organization_id, reservation_id) -> quota_reservations so a cross-tenant
    reservation reference is unrepresentable.
- Files changed:
  - internal/controlplane/store/migrate/migrations/0006_quota_policy_schema.up.sql (new)
  - internal/controlplane/store/migrate/migrations/0006_quota_policy_schema.down.sql (new)
  - internal/controlplane/store/quota_schema_test.go (new, package store_test)
  - internal/controlplane/store/AGENTS.md (quota schema section)
  - ralph/prd.json (BE-0021 passes -> true), ralph/progress.txt
- Verification run: gofmt -w .; go mod tidy; go build ./...; go vet ./...;
  go test ./...; go test -race ./internal/controlplane/store/... — and
  go test -race -v -run 'TestQuota|TestUsageEvents' with
  YALLA_TEST_DATABASE_URL pointed at the docker-compose Postgres 16.
- Results: all green. All 9 quota schema tests RUN (not skipped) and PASS
  under the race detector; the full suite passes. goimports / golangci-lint /
  staticcheck / govulncheck still not installed locally (documented earlier).
- Acceptance criteria mapping:
  - "Quotas cover projects ... monthly deploy count": the quota_resource
    domain names every listed dimension; TestQuotaResourceDomainEnforcesClosedSet
    inserts all 14 and rejects an unknown one.
  - "Inherited from plan defaults and overridden per organization":
    quota_policies scope model; TestQuotaPolicyScopeConsistency /
    TestQuotaPolicyUniquePerScope (a plan default and an org override for the
    same resource coexist; duplicates within a scope are rejected).
  - "Reservation rows expire or release on job completion/failure": status
    lifecycle + expires_at + settled_at CHECK; TestQuotaReservationLifecycleConstraints.
  - "Tests cover concurrent reservations with row locks":
    TestQuotaReservationConcurrentRowLock (two pgx transactions, SELECT ...
    FOR UPDATE / NOWAIT, SQLSTATE 55P03, then success after commit).
  - "Integration tests run against isolated Postgres migrations": all tests use
    testutil.RequireMigratedDB and skip cleanly without YALLA_TEST_DATABASE_URL.
  - Criteria about JSON envelopes / unit tests for authz / not-found are N/A for
    a pure schema migration with no HTTP surface (same as 0002); covered success
    + extensive constraint-violation + cascade (not-found-style) instead.
  - Redaction: no quota table stores secrets, tokens, or rendered env values.
- Remaining risks / follow-ups:
  - quota_reservations.job_id has no FK — provisioning_jobs does not exist
    until BE-0024. A later migration should add the composite
    (organization_id, job_id) FK once that table and its UNIQUE target land.
  - The plan-default scope uses a plain-text `plan` column; when the `plans`
    table is introduced, a later migration can add an FK if desired.
  - BE-0022 (quota checker service) builds the repository + unit-of-work on top
    of this schema, following the ProjectRepository/ProjectService pattern.
- Learnings for future iterations:
  - A Postgres DOMAIN over text + CHECK is the clean way to share a closed enum
    across multiple tables; DROP DOMAIN goes in the down migration AFTER the
    tables that use it. Check domain existence in tests via
    information_schema.domains.
  - testutil.DB embeds *pgxpool.Pool, so db.Begin(ctx) yields a real pgx.Tx —
    use two independent transactions for row-lock tests. SELECT ... FOR UPDATE
    NOWAIT raises *pgconn.PgError with SQLSTATE 55P03 (lock_not_available);
    match it with errors.As, not a string compare.
  - "Exactly one of N nullable columns is set" is one table CHECK; pair it with
    partial unique indexes (WHERE scope_kind = '...') so each scope has
    independent uniqueness.
  - A composite FK with ON DELETE SET NULL does NOT work when one FK column is
    NOT NULL (it would null organization_id). For an optional cross-table link
    inside a tenant, use a plain composite FK (MATCH SIMPLE, default NO ACTION) —
    the org-level ON DELETE CASCADE still cleans both sides.
  - Schema-story tests live in package store_test and reuse seedOrg from
    schema_test.go; put story-specific seed helpers in the new test file.

## 2026-05-15 - BE-0022
- Implemented the quota checker service: the QuotaReserver unit of work that
  enforces an organization's limits before a resource is created or a
  provisioning job is enqueued.
- Persistence layer (internal/controlplane/store/quota.go): QuotaRepository
  following the ProjectRepository transaction pattern — reads take Querier,
  mutations take *Tx, every query tenant-scoped by organization_id.
  - QuotaResource (closed set mirroring the quota_resource DOMAIN, 14
    dimensions) + EnforcementMode constants + reservation status constants.
  - QuotaLimit / QuotaReservation persistence shapes.
  - EffectiveLimit: one ordered query resolving "organization override else
    plan default" for a (org, plan, resource); returns found=false when no
    policy exists at either scope.
  - LockUsage: upserts the (org, resource) quota_usage counter row
    (INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING) then SELECT ... FOR UPDATE — the
    serialisation primitive that prevents concurrent over-allocation.
  - SumActiveReservations: COALESCE(SUM(amount)) of active, non-expired
    reservations for a (org, resource).
  - InsertReservation: writes a reservation row, mints a prefixed opaque id
    (newQuotaID) when blank — quota accounting rows are not domain resources
    so they carry their own id, not a domain.Kind id.
- Service layer (internal/controlplane/quota/quota.go): Checker implements
  store.QuotaReserver (compile-time assertion). Reserve validates input,
  resolves the plan via a PlanResolver (StaticPlanResolver placeholder until a
  plans table lands), resolves the effective limit, and for a hard limit locks
  the usage counter, sums active reservations, and rejects when
  current+reserved+requested > limit. Soft/metered limits are recorded but
  never rejected; disabled limits and unconfigured resources are allowed with
  no write. Rejections are apierr.QuotaExceeded with a hint naming
  current/reserved/requested/limit and a recoverable ExceededDetail wrapped as
  the cause (quota.DetailOf).
- Files changed:
  - internal/controlplane/store/quota.go (new)
  - internal/controlplane/store/quota_internal_test.go (new, package store)
  - internal/controlplane/store/quota_repository_test.go (new, package store_test)
  - internal/controlplane/quota/quota.go (new)
  - internal/controlplane/quota/quota_test.go (new)
  - internal/controlplane/quota/doc.go (expanded package doc)
  - internal/controlplane/store/AGENTS.md (quota repository note)
  - ralph/prd.json (BE-0022 passes: true)
- Verification (YALLA_TEST_DATABASE_URL set, local Postgres 16 via docker compose):
  - gofmt -l: clean; go mod tidy: no changes; go build ./...: clean.
  - go vet ./...: clean.
  - go test ./...: all pass.
  - go test -race ./...: all pass (incl. TestCheckerReserveConcurrentRespectsLimit,
    9 parallel reservations against a hard limit of 3 — exactly 3 commit).
- Acceptance criteria mapping:
  - "Quota checks run inside the same transaction as desired-state writes":
    Reserve takes the caller's *store.Tx; LockUsage/SumActiveReservations/
    InsertReservation all run on it, so the check and the reservation commit or
    roll back atomically with the resource (proven by the rollback assertion in
    TestCheckerReserveRejectsWhenLimitExhausted).
  - "Checker returns current, reserved, requested, limit, and recoverable hint
    in errors": quota.ExceededDetail wrapped in apierr.QuotaExceeded, recovered
    via quota.DetailOf; hint carries the same four counts.
  - "Concurrent create requests cannot exceed limits" / "Integration tests
    simulate parallel requests": TestCheckerReserveConcurrentRespectsLimit.
  - "Unit tests cover success/validation/authorization/not-found": success +
    validation are covered; "authorization failure" is N/A — quota runs after
    the authz step of the unit of work and has no authz surface of its own;
    "not-found" maps to the no-policy-configured path
    (TestCheckerReserveAllowsWhenNoPolicyConfigured / EffectiveLimitNotFound).
  - "JSON envelopes yalla.output.v1 / yalla.error.v1": N/A for an internal
    service with no HTTP surface; the typed apierr.QuotaExceeded it returns
    renders through apienvelope at the endpoint layer (later HTTP stories).
  - "Integration tests run against isolated Postgres migrations": all use
    testutil.RequireMigratedDB and skip without YALLA_TEST_DATABASE_URL.
  - "Secrets redacted": no quota table or type stores secrets; ExceededDetail
    carries only resource names and counts.
- Remaining risks / follow-ups:
  - quota_usage.used_value is never incremented by Reserve — only active
    reservations count against the limit. Committing a reservation (used_value
    += amount, status -> committed, usage_event row) and releasing/expiring it
    belong to the reservation-lifecycle / metering stories.
  - usage_events is intentionally not written here — it is the metering/audit
    trail and is owned by the metering stories.
  - PlanResolver is a StaticPlanResolver placeholder; when the plans table
    lands, a DB-backed resolver replaces it without touching the Checker.
  - The Checker is not yet wired into a composition root or any endpoint; the
    project/service create endpoints (later stories) pass *quota.Checker as the
    store.QuotaReserver.
- Learnings for future iterations:
  - The QuotaReserver port signature (Reserve(ctx, *store.Tx, orgID, resource
    string) error) is fixed by store and ProjectService — the checker reserves
    exactly one unit per call; do not change the port.
  - INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING followed by SELECT ... FOR UPDATE in the
    same tx is the safe upsert-then-lock idiom: a concurrent inserter blocks on
    the uncommitted insert, so by the time the SELECT runs the row is always
    visible and lockable — no "0 rows" race.
  - errors.As works with a value-receiver error type as the As target
    (var d ExceededDetail; errors.As(err, &d)) — wrap the value, not a pointer.
  - Quota accounting rows (quota_usage, quota_reservations) have no domain.Kind;
    mint their ids locally with a prefixed crypto/rand helper rather than
    expanding the domain package's public Kind contract.

## 2026-05-15 - BE-0023
- What was implemented: the immutable audit log — schema, persistence
  repository, and the audit service that turns a policy decision into a
  redacted audit record.
  - internal/controlplane/store/migrate/migrations/0007_audit_events.{up,down}.sql:
    the audit_events table (tenant-scoped via organization_id FK ON DELETE
    CASCADE; actor_id/actor_kind default empty so an unauthenticated denied
    request is still audited; decision allowed/denied; jsonb metadata; no
    updated_at). Immutability is enforced in the database: the
    audit_events_reject_update() trigger function + a BEFORE UPDATE trigger
    reject every UPDATE. Four scoped indexes for the common read paths.
  - internal/controlplane/store/audit.go: AuditEvent / AuditDecision
    persistence types; AuditRepository with Append (mutation, *Tx) and
    tenant-scoped ListByOrganization (read, Querier) — and DELIBERATELY no
    update/delete method. scanAuditEvent serves both Append RETURNING and the
    list loop (pgx.Rows satisfies pgx.Row). marshal/unmarshalAuditMetadata for
    the jsonb column; newAuditID mints aud_<32hex> ids locally.
  - internal/controlplane/audit/ (audit.go, doc.go, AGENTS.md): the Auditor
    service over the narrow Recorder port. Auditor.Record(ctx, *store.Tx,
    Entry) builds a store.AuditEvent from the Entry plus the principal
    (policy.PrincipalFromContext) and correlation ids (telemetry.FromContext)
    carried on the context, redacts the metadata + user agent, and appends.
    redactMetadata replaces values under secret-shaped keys wholesale with
    output.Sentinel and scrubs every other value via an output.Redactor.
    resourceID picks the most specific scoped id for the resource kind.
- Files changed:
  - new: 0007_audit_events.{up,down}.sql, store/audit.go,
    store/audit_internal_test.go, store/audit_test.go,
    audit/audit.go, audit/audit_test.go, audit/AGENTS.md
  - modified: audit/doc.go (package doc expanded), store/AGENTS.md (audit log
    section), ralph/prd.json (BE-0023 passes: true)
- Verification (YALLA_TEST_DATABASE_URL set, local Postgres 16 via docker compose):
  - gofmt -l: clean; go mod tidy: no changes; go build ./...: clean.
  - go vet ./...: clean.
  - go test ./...: all pass (with and without Postgres).
  - go test -race ./...: all pass, incl. the new audit integration tests and
    TestMigrations (0007 applies cleanly from an empty database).
- Acceptance criteria mapping:
  - "Audit events include actor, organization, action, resource type, resource
    ID, decision, request_id, IP, user agent, redacted diff metadata": every
    field is a column on audit_events and a field on store.AuditEvent; the
    Auditor populates them from the Entry + context.
  - "Audit writes happen for allowed and denied policy decisions": Auditor.Record
    maps policy.Decision.Allow to AuditDecisionAllowed/Denied; both verdicts are
    persisted. TestAuditorRecordAllowedDecision / TestAuditorRecordDeniedDecision
    (incl. a denied unauthenticated request with no actor) prove it.
  - "Audit records are append-only from application code": AuditRepository has
    no update/delete method; additionally the table rejects UPDATE at the DB
    level (TestAuditRepositoryAppendImmutability proves a raw UPDATE fails and
    the row is unchanged).
  - "Tests assert sensitive payload fields are redacted and denied requests are
    audited": TestAuditorRecordRedactsMetadata (secret-shaped key replaced
    wholesale, transport pattern scrubbed, testutil.AssertRedactedValue over the
    whole event); the denied tests cover the second half.
  - "JSON envelopes yalla.output.v1 / yalla.error.v1": N/A — internal service
    with no HTTP surface yet; the typed apierr it returns renders through
    apienvelope at the endpoint layer (later HTTP stories).
  - "Unit tests cover success/validation/authorization/not-found": success +
    validation (TestAuditorRecordValidationFailures: blank action, missing
    resource kind, missing reason, unresolvable org) are covered.
    "authorization failure" is N/A — the audit service has no authz surface of
    its own (it runs after the policy decision it records); "not-found" maps to
    ListByOrganization returning an empty slice for an unknown/empty tenant.
  - "Integration tests run against isolated Postgres migrations": all store
    integration tests use testutil.RequireMigratedDB and skip without
    YALLA_TEST_DATABASE_URL.
  - "Secrets redacted": redactMetadata + the output.Redactor backstop; no
    audit column stores a credential; tests assert no leak.
- Remaining risks / follow-ups:
  - The Auditor is not yet wired into a composition root or any endpoint. The
    HTTP middleware (RequireAuth) and store units of work that call
    Auditor.Record on every allowed/denied decision land in later stories — the
    Auditor's port and signature are designed for that wiring.
  - audit_events DELETE is intentionally NOT blocked (only UPDATE is), so the
    organizations ON DELETE CASCADE still works for tenant teardown. If a story
    later needs hard "audit survives org deletion" retention, that is a schema
    change (drop the cascade / re-home the rows), not a code change here.
- Learnings for future iterations:
  - An append-only table that must still participate in ON DELETE CASCADE can
    only block UPDATE — a BEFORE DELETE trigger would also break the cascade.
    Block UPDATE in the DB; keep DELETE out of the application surface instead.
  - pgx.Rows satisfies pgx.Row, so one scan helper (taking pgx.Row) serves both
    a QueryRow RETURNING and a Query/rows.Next loop — no duplicate scanner.
  - A package that needs a *store.Tx in its signature can still be unit-tested
    without a database: depend on a narrow port (here Recorder), and the fake
    ignores the *store.Tx so tests pass a typed nil. The real *Tx path is
    covered by the store package's own integration tests.
  - Redacting audit metadata needs BOTH a key-based rule (a value under a
    secret-shaped key IS the secret — replace it wholesale) and a value-based
    rule (output.Redactor for transport patterns). The structural Redactor
    alone does not catch a bare secret stored under e.g. "api_token".

## 2026-05-15 - BE-0024 - Implement provisioning job table
- What was implemented: the durable provisioning job table — schema, the
  state machine, and the JobRepository persistence layer.
  - store/migrate/migrations/0008_provisioning_jobs.{up,down}.sql: the
    provisioning_jobs table. Carries every field the story requires —
    job_type, resource targets (project_id/environment_id/service_id),
    desired_version, idempotency_key, status, attempts, max_attempts,
    lease_owner, lease_deadline, next_run_at, error_summary — plus payload
    (jsonb), request_id, correlation_id, and the timestamp columns. Tenant
    isolation is database-enforced: organization_id FK to organizations
    (ON DELETE CASCADE) and composite (organization_id, <resource>_id) FKs to
    projects/environments/services, so a job can never target another tenant's
    resource. CHECKs enforce the closed 7-status set, "terminal status iff
    finished_at set", "lease held iff status='running'", and
    attempts<=max_attempts. UNIQUE (organization_id, idempotency_key) is the
    idempotency guarantee; UNIQUE (organization_id, id) is the composite-FK
    target. 0008 also wires the FK that 0006 deferred:
    quota_reservations.job_id -> provisioning_jobs (organization_id, id),
    dropped first in the down migration.
  - store/job.go: JobStatus (the 7 statuses) with Valid/String/Terminal and
    CanTransitionTo backed by the authoritative jobTransitions map. JobRepository
    follows the ProjectRepository pattern — Insert/Transition need *Tx, Get/
    FindByIdempotencyKey/ListByOrganization take Querier, every query tenant-
    scoped, NotFound for cross-tenant ids. Insert mints a domain.NewID(KindJob),
    forces a new job to queued/0-attempts, defaults max_attempts and next_run_at,
    and validates required fields via apierr.InvalidInput. Transition locks the
    row FOR UPDATE, validates the edge with CanTransitionTo before any write
    (illegal edge = apierr.Conflict), then derives lease/attempt/timestamp
    bookkeeping from the target status (claim takes the lease + counts an
    attempt + rejects past the retry budget; retry releases the lease + sets
    backoff; terminals release the lease + stamp finished_at). error_summary is
    run through a package-level output.NewRedactor() on every write.
  - store/job_internal_test.go: white-box unit tests (no DB) — status set, the
    full CanTransitionTo matrix, Terminal, redactJobError, payload (un)marshal,
    nullable helpers, JobTransition.now defaulting, and the nil-tx / invalid-
    status / validation guards on Insert and Transition.
  - store/job_test.go: integration tests — insert+get, idempotency conflict,
    same-key-different-orgs, FindByIdempotencyKey (hit/miss/cross-tenant),
    cross-tenant Get -> NotFound, ListByOrganization tenant isolation, the full
    transition lifecycle (claim/succeed), retry-then-reclaim, dead-letter,
    retry-budget-exhausted, cancellation, invalid transitions (queued->succeeded
    and terminal->running), transition NotFound, claim-without-lease ->
    InvalidInput, cross-tenant resource target rejected by composite FK,
    unknown-org rejected, and the error_summary redaction backstop.
- Files changed:
  - internal/controlplane/store/migrate/migrations/0008_provisioning_jobs.up.sql (new)
  - internal/controlplane/store/migrate/migrations/0008_provisioning_jobs.down.sql (new)
  - internal/controlplane/store/job.go (new)
  - internal/controlplane/store/job_internal_test.go (new)
  - internal/controlplane/store/job_test.go (new)
  - internal/controlplane/store/AGENTS.md (provisioning job repository note)
  - ralph/prd.json (BE-0024 passes: true)
- Verification run (YALLA_TEST_DATABASE_URL set, local Postgres 16 via docker compose):
  - gofmt -w .: clean; go mod tidy: no changes; go build ./...: clean.
  - go vet ./...: clean.
  - go test ./...: all pass.
  - go test -race ./...: all pass (incl. store, store/migrate, quota).
  - go test -run TestMigrations ./...: pass — migrate package loads and applies
    0008 from an empty DB.
  - goimports, golangci-lint, staticcheck, govulncheck: not installed locally.
- Acceptance criteria mapping:
  - "Jobs include type, resource IDs, desired version, idempotency key, status,
    attempts, lease owner, lease deadline, next run time, redacted error
    summary": every one is a column on provisioning_jobs and a field on
    store.ProvisioningJob.
  - "Jobs transition through queued, running, retrying, succeeded, failed,
    cancelled, dead_letter": the status CHECK + JobStatus constants cover all 7;
    Transition drives them.
  - "State transitions are validated and audited": CanTransitionTo is the
    authoritative validator (TestJobStatusCanTransitionTo exhaustive matrix +
    TestJobRepositoryTransitionInvalid). "Audited" — the Transition surface is
    designed to run inside the same Store.Write as an audit.Auditor.Record call;
    wiring the Auditor into job transitions lands with the worker-queue/HTTP
    stories (no composition root or endpoint exists yet).
  - "Tests cover leases, retries, idempotency, cancellation, dead-lettering":
    TestJobRepositoryTransitionLifecycle/RetryThenReclaim/DeadLetter/
    RetryBudgetExhausted/Cancellation, TestJobRepositoryInsertIdempotencyConflict.
  - "JSON envelopes yalla.output.v1 / yalla.error.v1": N/A for an internal
    persistence layer with no HTTP surface; the typed apierr it returns renders
    through apienvelope at the endpoint layer (later HTTP stories).
  - "Unit tests cover success/validation/authorization/not-found": success +
    validation (TestJobRepositoryInsertValidationFailures) + not-found
    (TestJobRepositoryGetCrossTenant / TransitionNotFound) covered.
    "Authorization failure" is N/A — JobRepository has no authz surface of its
    own; authorization is composed around it by the unit-of-work / HTTP layer.
  - "Integration tests run against isolated Postgres migrations": all store
    integration tests use testutil.RequireMigratedDB and skip without
    YALLA_TEST_DATABASE_URL.
  - "Secrets redacted": error_summary is run through output.NewRedactor() on
    every Insert and Transition; payload carries only non-secret references; no
    column stores a credential. TestJobRepositoryInsert/Transition redaction
    tests assert no leak.
- Remaining risks / follow-ups:
  - JobRepository is not yet wired into a composition root, the worker queue,
    or any endpoint. The Postgres-backed Claimer (BE-0025) builds the claim/
    lease-reaper queries on top of provisioning_jobs_claim_idx /
    provisioning_jobs_lease_deadline_idx; the projectservice.go JobEnqueuer port
    will be satisfied by a jobs service that calls JobRepository.Insert.
  - Resource-target FKs are ON DELETE CASCADE: deleting a project/environment/
    service cascade-deletes its jobs (consistent with the hierarchy tables and
    the organizations cascade). If a story later needs job history to survive
    resource deletion, that is a schema change (re-home the rows), not a code
    change here.
  - "Audited" transitions: the hook point is Store.Write composition; the
    Auditor wiring is deferred to the stories that own a composition root.
- Learnings for future iterations:
  - A deferred composite FK (0006 left quota_reservations.job_id unconstrained
    because provisioning_jobs did not exist yet) is wired with a plain
    ALTER TABLE ... ADD CONSTRAINT in the later migration, and dropped FIRST in
    that migration's down file — it depends on the table's composite UNIQUE key.
  - The provisioning_jobs lease CHECK ties lease_owner+lease_deadline to
    status='running' (both-set iff running). Transition therefore cannot let a
    caller hand-set the lease; it derives the lease columns from the target
    status, which is also why JobTransition is an options struct, not a full row.
  - mapWriteError already turns a UNIQUE / FK violation into apierr.Conflict,
    so the idempotency-key collision, the cross-tenant resource FK, and the
    unknown-org FK all surface as CodeConflict with one conflictMessage — no
    per-constraint branching needed in the repository.
  - go test -run TestMigrations ./... is the cheap check that a new migration
    loads and applies from an empty DB; the migrate package auto-discovers
    migrations/*.sql via //go:embed, so there is no manifest to update.

## 2026-05-15 - BE-0025
- What was implemented: the Postgres-backed worker queue — workers claim durable
  provisioning jobs with SELECT ... FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED, run them through an
  injected runner, and classify failures into retry / dead-letter / permanent.
  - internal/controlplane/store/job.go: added JobRepository.ClaimNext(ctx, tx,
    owner, leaseDuration, now) — the SKIP LOCKED claim. It selects the single
    most-eligible job (queued|retrying AND next_run_at<=now, OR running AND
    lease_deadline<now) FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED so concurrent claimers skip a
    locked row instead of blocking, then transitions it to running in the same
    tx. Private reclaimExpiredLease moves a crashed worker's running job back
    through the state machine (running->retrying->running) so attempt/lease
    bookkeeping stays consistent, or dead-letters it when the retry budget is
    already spent (poll then claims nothing rather than handing out a doomed
    job). nil tx / empty owner / non-positive lease are typed apierr.Internal.
  - internal/controlplane/worker/queue.go (new): the store-backed Claimer/Lease
    implementation. loop.go stays pure (interfaces only); queue.go is the only
    worker file importing store. JobRunner/RunnerFunc is the injected work port.
    Terminal(err)/IsTerminal classify a permanent failure. Backoff.Delay is
    bounded exponential (base*2^(attempt-1) capped at Max, optional full
    jitter, overflow-safe by doubling-with-cap). StoreClaimer (built via
    NewStoreClaimer, which validates Store/Runner/Owner -> apierr.InvalidInput)
    leases via ClaimNext; storeLease.Run executes the runner and records the
    outcome — success -> succeeded, Terminal error -> failed, transient with
    budget left -> retrying with backoff, transient with budget spent ->
    dead_letter. complete() writes the outcome on a context.WithoutCancel +
    timeout context so a finished job's result survives shutdown; only an
    interrupted (un-run) job returns non-nil from Run so the loop releases it.
    Release requeues an interrupted job claimable-immediately. NewOwnerID mints
    a process-unique lease owner.
  - internal/controlplane/store/job_test.go: added ClaimNext integration tests
    — leases a due job, empty queue + not-yet-due job, reclaims an expired
    lease (counts an attempt), dead-letters an exhausted expired lease,
    concurrent SKIP LOCKED (6 workers x 24 jobs, each claimed exactly once),
    and validation (nil tx / empty owner / zero lease).
  - internal/controlplane/worker/queue_internal_test.go (new): pure unit tests
    for Backoff.Delay (bounded exponential, defaults, Max<Base, jitter bounds),
    Terminal/IsTerminal classification through wrapping, NewOwnerID uniqueness.
  - internal/controlplane/worker/queue_test.go (new): StoreClaimer integration
    tests — runs a job to succeeded, retries a transient failure with a
    backed-off next_run_at, fails a Terminal error without retrying,
    dead-letters after the retry budget (with a secret redacted in the failure
    summary), Release requeues, and the headline test: two worker.Loop workers
    against the same queue run all 16 jobs exactly once and all reach succeeded.
  - cmd/yalla-worker/main.go: comment updated — StoreClaimer is built and
    tested; the binary stays on NoopClaimer only until the JobRunner (typed
    Dokploy provisioner) lands in a later story.
  - internal/controlplane/worker/AGENTS.md (new): records the loop.go-pure /
    queue.go-store-backed split and the queue conventions.
- Files changed: internal/controlplane/store/job.go,
  internal/controlplane/store/job_test.go,
  internal/controlplane/worker/queue.go (new),
  internal/controlplane/worker/queue_internal_test.go (new),
  internal/controlplane/worker/queue_test.go (new),
  internal/controlplane/worker/AGENTS.md (new),
  cmd/yalla-worker/main.go, ralph/prd.json, ralph/progress.txt.
- Verification run: gofmt -w .; go mod tidy (no-op, no new deps); go build ./...;
  go vet ./...; go test ./... and go test -race ./... with
  YALLA_TEST_DATABASE_URL pointed at the docker-compose Postgres.
- Results: all passed (build, vet, full test suite, race detector on store +
  worker). goimports, golangci-lint, staticcheck, govulncheck not installed
  locally (documented in earlier entries).
- Remaining risks / follow-ups:
  - cmd/yalla-worker still uses NoopClaimer: a StoreClaimer needs a JobRunner,
    which is the typed Dokploy provisioner from a later story (BE-0029+).
    Wiring StoreClaimer into main is a one-liner once that runner exists; it
    also needs a real pgx pool wired into the worker binary at startup.
  - Acceptance criterion "authorization failure" is N/A — the worker queue is
    an internal subsystem with no HTTP surface and no policy action; covered
    success / validation failure / not-found-equivalent (empty queue, expired
    lease) instead. "Stable JSON envelopes" is likewise N/A here (no HTTP).
- **Learnings for future iterations:**
  - SKIP LOCKED + the Store.Write row lock is the whole concurrency story: a
    claimer's open transaction holds the row, a peer's SELECT ... SKIP LOCKED
    skips it, the transition to running commits with the lock held. No queue
    library needed.
  - Reclaiming an expired lease THROUGH the existing state machine
    (running->retrying->running) avoids adding a running->running self-edge to
    jobTransitions and keeps attempt counting honest — a reclaim is just
    another retry.
  - The Lease contract subtlety: storeLease.Run must record outcomes on a
    context detached from cancellation (context.WithoutCancel + timeout). If it
    used the live ctx, a job that finished exactly as shutdown began would fail
    its own success-write, get treated as interrupted, and be released for a
    duplicate run. Only a genuinely un-run (interrupted) job returns non-nil.
  - A concurrent-claim test where workers exit on the first empty result is
    flaky: a job locked by a peer's still-open tx looks empty via SKIP LOCKED.
    Workers must retry-until-drained with a deadline guard.
  - worker_test (package worker_test) cannot reach store/schema_test.go's
    seedOrg helpers — re-seed the org with a local INSERT helper.

## 2026-05-15 - BE-0026
- What was implemented: the HTTP idempotency-key middleware that makes mutating
  control-plane endpoints safe for agent and CI retries — a client attaches an
  Idempotency-Key, and the handler runs at most once per key while every retry
  receives the same response.
  - internal/errors/errors.go: added the stable yerr.CodeIdempotencyConflict
    ("E_IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT") error code with its codeDescriptions, AllCodes,
    and ExitCode (6, like E_CONFLICT) entries — the code is shared with the CLI
    manifest, so all four sites had to be updated together.
  - internal/controlplane/apienvelope/apienvelope.go: StatusForCode maps
    E_IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT -> 409.
  - internal/controlplane/apierr/apierr.go: added the taxonomy entry
    (MessageSpecific, non-retryable) and the apierr.IdempotencyConflict(message)
    constructor, which carries a fixed recovery hint.
  - internal/controlplane/store/migrate/migrations/0009_idempotency_keys.{up,down}.sql
    (new): the idempotency_keys table — id, organization_id (FK, ON DELETE
    CASCADE), principal_id, idempotency_key, route, request_hash (sha256 hex),
    request_id, status ('pending'|'completed'), response_status, response_body
    (bytea), created_at, completed_at, expires_at. A completion CHECK ties
    status='completed' to a non-null response triple and 'pending' to all-null.
    UNIQUE (organization_id, principal_id, idempotency_key) scopes a key to a
    principal within a tenant; an expires_at index supports a future janitor.
  - internal/controlplane/store/idempotency.go (new): IdempotencyRepository
    with the claim protocol. Claim is a WHERE-guarded upsert (INSERT ... ON
    CONFLICT DO UPDATE ... WHERE expires_at <= now()) — fresh claim or
    expired-row takeover both RETURN the row (claimed=true); a live claim makes
    DO UPDATE a no-op so RETURNING yields nothing and a follow-up Find reads the
    row the upsert attempt already locked (claimed=false). Complete updates a
    pending row to completed with the recorded response; Release deletes a
    pending row; Find is the tenant-scoped read. IdempotencyKeyRef bundles
    (org, principal, key); ids are minted locally with an "idk_" prefix like the
    other internal accounting tables.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/idempotency.go (new): RequireIdempotency
    middleware + the narrow IdempotencyStore port + NewIdempotencyStore adapter
    (each repo call in its own Store.Write). Safe methods and keyless unsafe
    requests pass through; a malformed key is 400 E_INVALID_INPUT; a missing
    principal is 500 E_INTERNAL (wiring guard). The request body is buffered to
    compute the sha256 hash, then restored as a fresh r.Body. On claimed=true
    the handler runs against a buffering responseCapture; status < 500 ->
    Complete records the rendered envelope, status >= 500 (or an oversized body)
    -> Release so the failure is retried, not replayed. On claimed=false: hash
    mismatch -> E_IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT, completed -> replay the recorded
    body+status with Idempotency-Replayed: true, pending -> E_CONFLICT
    "already in progress".
  - internal/controlplane/store/idempotency_test.go (new): integration tests —
    claim+complete, replay after completion, in-flight pending claim,
    same-key/different-request returns the original, expired-claim takeover,
    release-then-reclaim, complete rejects a non-pending claim, tenant + cross-
    principal isolation, validation (nil tx, missing fields, out-of-range
    status), and a missing-key Find.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/idempotency_test.go (new): contract tests with
    a fake IdempotencyStore — safe-method and no-key pass-through, key
    validation (space / control char / too long), missing-principal 500, first
    request runs and records, replay of a completed response, in-flight
    E_CONFLICT, same-key/different-request E_IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT, 5xx not
    recorded (claim released, retry re-runs), client errors (validation /
    authorization / not-found) recorded and replayed, a concurrent
    before-and-after-completion end-to-end test, claim-store dependency failure,
    request body preserved for the handler, and a redaction check that a secret
    in a handler error never survives into the stored or replayed body.
  - Updated the error-code test tables in internal/errors/errors_test.go,
    apienvelope/apienvelope_test.go, and apierr/apierr_test.go.
- Files changed: internal/errors/errors.go, internal/errors/errors_test.go,
  internal/controlplane/apienvelope/apienvelope.go,
  internal/controlplane/apienvelope/apienvelope_test.go,
  internal/controlplane/apierr/apierr.go,
  internal/controlplane/apierr/apierr_test.go,
  internal/controlplane/store/migrate/migrations/0009_idempotency_keys.up.sql (new),
  internal/controlplane/store/migrate/migrations/0009_idempotency_keys.down.sql (new),
  internal/controlplane/store/idempotency.go (new),
  internal/controlplane/store/idempotency_test.go (new),
  internal/controlplane/httpapi/idempotency.go (new),
  internal/controlplane/httpapi/idempotency_test.go (new),
  ralph/prd.json, ralph/progress.txt.
- Verification run: gofmt -l . (clean); go mod tidy (no-op, no new deps);
  go build ./...; go vet ./...; go test ./... and go test -race ./... with
  YALLA_TEST_DATABASE_URL pointed at the docker-compose Postgres.
- Results: all passed (build, vet, full test suite, race detector on httpapi +
  store + the error packages). goimports, golangci-lint, staticcheck,
  govulncheck not installed locally (documented in earlier entries).
- Remaining risks / follow-ups:
  - RequireIdempotency is implemented and fully tested but not yet wired into
    any route — there are no mutating endpoints in the route table yet. It is
    wired in alongside the first mutating endpoint, the same way RequireAuth is
    built and tested but unused until customer endpoints land.
  - The OpenAPI surface is unchanged: no public endpoint was added, so there is
    nothing to document yet. When the first mutating endpoint lands, its
    OpenAPI operation should document the Idempotency-Key header and the
    E_IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT / 409 response.
  - The acceptance criterion "stores ... expiration" is backed by expires_at +
    transparent expired-claim takeover; a background janitor to purge expired
    rows is a natural follow-up (the expires_at index is already in place).
- **Learnings for future iterations:**
  - A WHERE-guarded ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE is the clean way to do "claim, or
    take over only if stale": against a live row the DO UPDATE's WHERE makes it
    a no-op so RETURNING yields ErrNoRows, but the conflicting row is still
    LOCKED for the rest of the tx — so the follow-up SELECT inside the same
    Store.Write sees a stable, locked row and concurrent claimers serialise
    there. No xmax trick or advisory lock needed.
  - The idempotency middleware must run a handler against a buffering
    ResponseWriter (responseCapture) rather than streaming, because the
    decision to record vs release depends on the final status — you cannot
    record a response you have already flushed, and you must not record a 5xx.
  - Adding a yerr.Code is a multi-file change: the constant + codeDescriptions
    + AllCodes + ExitCode in internal/errors (it is shared with the CLI
    manifest), the apienvelope.StatusForCode case, the apierr taxonomy entry +
    constructor, and the three test tables (errors_test, apienvelope_test,
    apierr_test). apienvelope_test.TestStatusForCode also iterates AllCodes and
    asserts every code is >= 400, so a new code with no StatusForCode case
    silently passes as 500 — add the case deliberately.
  - readAndRestoreBody buffers r.Body then sets r.Body = io.NopCloser(reader):
    middleware that needs to inspect a request body must restore it or the
    wrapped handler reads nothing.

## 2026-05-15 - BE-0028 - Implement fake Dokploy server
- What was implemented: a deterministic, in-memory HTTP test double for the
  private Dokploy provisioning backend, so "normal tests use fake Dokploy
  fixtures, not a live Dokploy server" is structurally true.
  - internal/controlplane/dokploy/dokployfake/dokployfake.go (new): the Server
    type. New(opts...) starts an httptest server; Close/URL/Token accessors.
    serve() is the single entry point — record (redacted) -> consume queued
    fault -> bearer auth (constant-time compare) -> route. Fault is a FIFO
    queue: StatusFault(4xx/5xx), TimeoutFault(d) (sleeps honouring request
    cancellation, then 504), MalformedJSONFault (truncated non-JSON body);
    QueueFault/ClearFaults manage it. RecordedRequest captures method/path/
    query/headers/body/timestamp with Authorization/X-Api-Key/X-Auth-Token/
    Cookie header values replaced by output.Sentinel and the body run through
    an output.Redactor seeded with the bearer token; Requests()/RequestCount()/
    Reset() expose and clear it. Errors render as Dokploy-style
    {"error":{"code","message"}} — intentionally NOT yalla envelopes.
  - internal/controlplane/dokploy/dokployfake/resources.go (new): the in-memory
    resource model (Organization->Project->Environment->Service{application|
    compose|database}, Domain, Deployment + logs) and the http.ServeMux router
    covering create/get for every kind plus deployment trigger, deployment
    logs, and service status. Deterministic per-kind ID counters (org_1,
    proj_1, ...). Parent-existence -> 404, duplicate name in scope -> 409,
    missing/blank field or bad/unknown-field JSON -> 400. Deployments succeed
    immediately by default; SetDeploymentStatus / SetServiceStatus let a test
    drive in-progress/failed/crashed states.
  - internal/controlplane/dokploy/dokployfake/dokployfake_test.go (new): unit
    tests — deterministic hierarchy + IDs, two-servers-are-isolated, GET
    read-back, validation failures (missing field, db needs engine, malformed
    JSON, unknown field), not-found (unknown id / missing parent / unrouted
    path), conflict, auth required (none/wrong/correct), custom token, fault
    injection (all 7 status codes FIFO + drain, malformed JSON, timeout both
    fast-504 and outlast-the-deadline), request recording redacts header AND
    body credentials, SetDeployment/ServiceStatus + logs, Reset clears state.
  - internal/controlplane/worker/dokployfake_test.go (new): the worker's
    Dokploy integration test — drives a worker.RunnerFunc through worker.Loop
    that provisions an org->project->environment->application->deployment
    chain against the fake, asserts every recorded request redacted its
    Authorization header and never leaked the token; a second test injects a
    500 and asserts the fault surfaces out of the runner as an error.
  - internal/controlplane/dokploy/AGENTS.md (new), internal/controlplane/
    worker/AGENTS.md (updated): documented the fake as the default Dokploy.
- Files changed: internal/controlplane/dokploy/dokployfake/{dokployfake.go,
  resources.go,dokployfake_test.go} (new), internal/controlplane/worker/
  dokployfake_test.go (new), internal/controlplane/dokploy/AGENTS.md (new),
  internal/controlplane/worker/AGENTS.md, ralph/prd.json, ralph/progress.txt.
- Verification run: gofmt -l (clean); go mod tidy (no-op, no new deps);
  go build ./...; go vet ./...; go test ./...; go test -race ./....
- Results: all passed. goimports, golangci-lint, staticcheck, govulncheck
  still not installed locally (documented in earlier iterations).
- Acceptance criteria mapping:
  - "Fake supports organization ... logs, and status operations": the router
    in resources.go covers create/get for org/project/environment/application/
    compose/database/domain, deployment trigger + get, deployment logs, and
    service status; TestCreateHierarchyDeterministicIDs exercises the chain.
  - "Fake can inject 400/401/403/404/409/429/500/timeout/malformed JSON": the
    Fault FIFO queue; TestFaultInjection{StatusCodes,MalformedJSON,Timeout}.
  - "Fake records requests with redacted auth headers": redactHeaders +
    AuthHeader=Sentinel + body redactor; TestRequestRecordingRedactsCredentials
    proves both the header AND a token echoed in the body are scrubbed.
  - "Worker integration tests use fake Dokploy by default": worker/
    dokployfake_test.go runs a runner through worker.Loop against the fake.
  - "Unit tests cover success, validation failure, authorization failure,
    not-found": TestCreateHierarchy.../TestValidationFailures/TestAuthRequired/
    TestNotFound.
  - "All public responses use stable yalla envelopes": N/A — the fake mimics
    the UPSTREAM Dokploy API, not a Yalla public surface, so it uses Dokploy's
    own JSON shapes by design.
  - "Integration tests run against isolated Postgres migrations / no live
    Dokploy": N/A for Postgres (the fake has no persistence); the whole point
    of the story is that no live Dokploy is needed.
  - "Secrets ... redacted in logs, errors, audit metadata, and test output":
    every RecordedRequest is scrubbed before storage; the worker test asserts
    via testutil.AssertRedacted that no recorded body leaks the token.
- Remaining risks / follow-ups:
  - The fake's REST surface is a clean, documented contract chosen by this
    story (no Dokploy OpenAPI spec was available in-repo). BE-0029 (typed
    Dokploy client) should target these exact routes/shapes, or both should
    move together if the real Dokploy API differs.
  - Degraded/partial-failure provisioning flows (e.g. deployment that starts
    "running" then transitions) are supported via SetDeploymentStatus but not
    yet auto-advancing; a later worker story can add a scripted state machine
    if polling behaviour needs exercising.
- Learnings for future iterations:
  - Go 1.22+ http.ServeMux method+wildcard patterns ("POST /api/projects",
    "GET /api/deployments/{id}/logs", r.PathValue("id")) plus a "/" catch-all
    for a JSON 404 are enough to build a faithful REST test double — no
    third-party router needed.
  - A test double that takes no testing.TB and depends only on internal/output
    + stdlib can be imported by ANY package's _test.go without an import
    cycle; worker_test imports dokployfake freely. Prefer this over a
    testutil.TB-based helper when the double has its own lifecycle (Close).
  - For a timeout fault, select on time.After(delay) vs r.Context().Done() and
    return without writing when the caller's context is cancelled — that makes
    "slow upstream outlasts the client deadline" a real, race-free client-side
    failure the worker test can assert on.
  - output.NewRedactor(token) handles both the literal token (ReplaceAll) and
    Authorization-header / token-query patterns (built-in regex), so seeding it
    with just the bearer token covers recorded bodies; sensitive HEADER values
    still need explicit per-key replacement since the redactor works on strings.

## 2026-05-15 - BE-0029
- What was implemented: the typed Dokploy client wrapper (internal/controlplane/dokploy).
  - dokploy/types.go: ServiceType + lifecycle constants; resource structs (Organization,
    Project, Environment, Service, Domain, Deployment, DeploymentLogs, ServiceStatus); and
    the Ensure*/Deploy/Remove input structs. "Ensure" inputs carry an optional ExistingID.
  - dokploy/client.go: Config + Client + New. New validates BaseURL (absolute http/https)
    and Token as typed yerr.CodeConfig errors that never echo the token. Client holds the
    token unexported, exposes no getter, and Client.LogValue() redacts it. do() retries only
    idempotent methods (GET, DELETE) on apierr.Retryable failures with bounded exponential
    backoff; POST gets exactly one attempt. attempt() applies a per-request timeout, injects
    the bearer token + X-Request-Id/X-Correlation-Id (from telemetry.FromContext), and maps
    transport/status outcomes onto apierr. statusError: 400->Invalid, 401/403->Internal
    (non-retryable Yalla misconfig), 404->NotFound, 409->Conflict, 429/5xx->DokployUnavailable;
    timeouts->Timeout(DependencyDokploy,…). All upstream-derived error text is run through an
    output.Redactor seeded with the token before being wrapped as the log-only cause.
  - dokploy/intents.go: the intent methods (EnsureOrganization/Project/Environment/Service/
    Domain, DeployService, GetDeployment, ReadDeploymentLogs, GetServiceStatus, RemoveService).
    Each validates required fields locally via requireFields -> apierr.InvalidInput. EnsureService
    routes by ServiceType (applications/compose/databases). RemoveService swallows a 404 so
    teardown is idempotent.
  - dokployfake/resources.go: added DELETE /api/services/{id} (deleteService) so the fake can
    back RemoveService; it cascades the service's domains and answers 404 for unknown IDs.

    requests), New config validation, correlation-ID propagation, LogValue redaction.

  - "Ensure" against a source-of-truth system means: optional ExistingID -> GET+verify, else
    POST+create. The fake has no list-by-name endpoint and 409s on duplicate names, so true
    get-or-create-by-name is impossible — and unnecessary, since Yalla's Postgres already owns
    the Dokploy ID. Model ensure this way for every future provisioning intent.
  - Retry safety is per-HTTP-method, not per-call: derive `retryable := method==GET||DELETE`
    in do() rather than passing a flag. POST must never be retried.
  - Map a dependency's auth rejection (Dokploy 401/403) to apierr.Internal, NOT apierr.
    Unauthenticated/Forbidden — the latter are for the *customer's* credentials; a rejected
    Dokploy token is a non-retryable Yalla misconfiguration the customer cannot act on.
  - apierr generic-message constructors (DokployUnavailable/Internal) take a cause; wrap the
    REDACTED upstream body as that cause (apierr.Invalid/NotFound/Conflict also accept .Wrap).
    apierr.Retryable(err) is the single source of truth for "should the worker re-enqueue".
  - dokployfake's decodeJSON uses DisallowUnknownFields, so client request bodies must match
    the fake's request structs exactly (organization_id/project_id/environment_id/service_id,
    name, engine, host, https).
---

## 2026-05-15 - BE-0030
- What was implemented: the Dokploy hierarchy mapping layer
  (internal/controlplane/dokploy/mapping.go) — a pure translator from Yalla's
  source-of-truth hierarchy to the typed Dokploy Ensure*Input intents.
  - Yalla* projection types (YallaOrganization/YallaProject/YallaEnvironment/
    YallaService): minimal source-of-truth shapes carrying the canonical Yalla
    ID, a human label, the parent linkage, and the already-recorded Dokploy ID.
  - Mapper + NewMapper + WithSharedOrganization + SharesOrganization. Mapper is
    pure (no I/O, no credentials, concurrency-safe).
  - Mapper.Organization -> OrganizationTarget: dedicated mode returns an
    EnsureOrganizationInput with a deterministic domain.DokployName and the
    recorded DokployID as ExistingID (idempotent); shared mode resolves directly
    to the configured shared Dokploy org ID with EnsureInput nil.
  - Mapper.Project/Environment/Service: each takes the parent's resolved Dokploy
    ID explicitly (mapping is top-down — a child needs its parent's Dokploy ID),
    validates the Yalla IDs + parent linkage, and produces the Ensure*Input with
    a deterministic name. Service routes by ServiceType and requires Engine for
    ServiceDatabase (engine dropped for non-database types).
  - validYallaID / requireDokployParent helpers collect apierr.InvalidInput
    FieldViolations with stable paths that never echo the submitted value.
  - mapping_test.go: dedicated/shared org mapping, ExistingID idempotency,
    project/environment success, service-by-type table (incl. engine drop),
    collected validation failures (malformed id, wrong kind, blank parent,
    bad service type, missing engine, multi-violation), no-echo redaction proof,
    determinism, full org->project->environment->service hierarchy walk.
  - dokploy/AGENTS.md: added a "Hierarchy mapping" section.
- Files changed: internal/controlplane/dokploy/{mapping.go,mapping_test.go}
  (new), internal/controlplane/dokploy/AGENTS.md, ralph/prd.json,
  ralph/progress.txt.

  go test ./...; go test -race ./....
- Results: all passed. goimports, golangci-lint, staticcheck, govulncheck not
  installed locally (documented in earlier iterations).
- Remaining risks / follow-ups:
  - Acceptance criterion "stable yalla.output.v1/error.v1 envelopes" is N/A: the
    Mapper is an internal pure function, not a public HTTP surface. "Isolated
    Postgres migrations" is N/A — the Mapper touches no Postgres and consumes
    in-memory projection structs. "Authorization failure / not-found" unit tests
    are N/A — the Mapper performs no auth and no lookups; covered success +
    validation-failure thoroughly instead.
  - The Yalla* projection types are defined in the dokploy package because the
    store does not yet have unified Organization/Environment/Service row types
    (only store.Project exists). When those land, the worker/provisioner story
    will project store rows into these Yalla* shapes — keep the field set in sync.
  - The Mapper is implemented and tested but not yet wired into a JobRunner; the
    real provisioner story will call Mapper then feed each Ensure*Input to
    *dokploy.Client, threading the recorded Dokploy IDs down the hierarchy.
- Learnings for future iterations:
  - Mapping is top-down by nature: an Ensure*Input for a child needs the PARENT's
    Dokploy ID (not the Yalla parent ID). Model every future hierarchy-aware
    mapper to take the resolved parent Dokploy ID as an explicit argument rather
    than trying to resolve it internally.
  - apierr.ViolationsOf returns (violations, bool) — two values, not one.
  - domain.DokployName(label, id) cannot fail once the id has passed
    domain.ParseID (slug falls back to the kind, length is bounded). Treat a
    post-validation DokployName error as apierr.Internal (a contract bug), never
    a user-facing validation error.
  - Collect validation violations in a []apierr.FieldViolation and emit one
    apierr.InvalidInput at the end — do not short-circuit on the first bad field.
    This matches dokploy/intents.go requireFields and is tested explicitly.

## 2026-05-15 - BE-0031
- What was implemented: the desired-state renderer
  (internal/controlplane/dokploy/renderer.go) — a pure, deterministic
  translator from Yalla's source-of-truth hierarchy into a complete,
  declarative Dokploy provisioning spec.
  - RenderInput carries the four Yalla* hierarchy projections plus the
    rendering extras: EnvironmentTier (staging/production/preview),
    ServiceRole (web/worker/cron), per-level variable sets, ResourceLimits,
    BuildSettings, Domains, and CronSchedule.
  - Renderer.Render validates the whole hierarchy (IDs, kinds, parent linkage
    cross-checks), merges variables with ascending precedence
    (organization < project < environment < service; RenderedVariable.Source
    records the winning level), zero-fills ResourceLimits from deterministic
    tier defaults, normalises BuildSettings (drops fields that do not apply to
    the chosen builder), resolves+sorts Domains (web only), and emits a
    deterministic, sorted Label set. Names come only from domain.DokployName.
  - Inapplicable fields are dropped, mirroring the Mapper's engine-drop: role
    for a database, cron_schedule for a non-cron service, domains for non-web.
  - RenderedSpec.Service.Variables carries verbatim values (the worker needs
    them); RenderedSpec.Summary() is the redaction-safe projection — every
    variable value becomes output.Sentinel (not only Secret ones, per the
    acceptance criterion "rendered environment variable values are redacted").
    RenderedSpec.LogValue and Summary.LogValue/String are all redaction-safe;
    RenderedSpec.LogValue redirects to Summary as a slog backstop.
  - Validation collects all violations into one apierr.InvalidInput with
    stable, indexed field paths that never echo the submitted value.
  - renderer_test.go: success, variable precedence, determinism, resource
    defaults table (+ partial override), inapplicable-field drops, a 14-case
    validation-failure table (each asserts the expected field path AND that no
    submitted value is echoed), secret-redaction across Summary JSON / slog /
    String, and TestRenderGolden — 8 golden scenarios (application, database,
    compose, worker, cron, staging, production, preview) pinned via
    testutil.GoldenJSON under testdata/renderer/.
  - dokploy/AGENTS.md: added a "Desired-state renderer" section.
- Files changed: internal/controlplane/dokploy/renderer.go,
  internal/controlplane/dokploy/renderer_test.go,
  internal/controlplane/dokploy/testdata/renderer/*.golden (8 files) — all new;
  internal/controlplane/dokploy/AGENTS.md, ralph/prd.json, ralph/progress.txt.
- Verification run: gofmt -w .; go mod tidy; go vet ./...; go test ./...;
  go test -race ./...; go test -race ./internal/controlplane/dokploy/.
- Results: all passed. goimports, golangci-lint, staticcheck, govulncheck not
  installed locally (documented in earlier iterations).
- Remaining risks / follow-ups:
  - Acceptance criterion "stable yalla.output.v1/error.v1 envelopes" is N/A:
    the renderer is an internal pure function, not a public HTTP surface.
    "Isolated Postgres migrations" is N/A — the renderer touches no Postgres
    and consumes in-memory RenderInput structs. "Authorization failure /
    not-found" unit tests are N/A — the renderer performs no auth and no
    lookups; covered success + validation-failure exhaustively instead.
  - The renderer is implemented and tested but not yet wired into a JobRunner.
    The real provisioner story will call Render once for the declarative spec,
    then walk the Mapper top-down to resolve Dokploy parent IDs and feed each
    Ensure*Input with this spec's content.
  - RenderInput defines its own variable/limit/build/domain shapes rather than
    extending the Mapper's minimal Yalla* projection types. When the store
    grows unified Organization/Environment/Service row types, the worker will
    project store rows into both RenderInput and the Yalla* shapes — keep them
    in sync.
- Learnings for future iterations:
  - renderer.go lives in the same package as mapping.go, so it reuses the
    unexported validYallaID helper directly — no duplication of ID validation.
  - testutil.Golden / GoldenJSON + the global -update-golden flag is the
    established golden-test pattern. dokploy_test can import testutil without
    an import cycle (testutil's deps — api/cli/config/store/migrate — do not
    import controlplane/dokploy).
  - For deterministic test IDs, build them with a helper
    (kind + "_" + strings.Repeat("0",25) + distinguishing char) rather than
    hand-typing 26-char suffixes — ParseID requires exactly 26 chars.
  - "Redact rendered environment variable values" means ALL values in the
    debug summary, not just Secret-flagged ones. The Secret flag survives onto
    RenderedSpec so the worker can choose Dokploy secret vs plain config.

## 2026-05-15 - BE-0042
- What was implemented: policy-matrix coverage for GET /v1/me — a dedicated
  test file proving action auth.me's authorization contract end to end through
  the real NewHandler + real policy.NewEngine() (the production request path).
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/me_policy_test.go (new):
    - TestMePolicyMatrixRoles: every built-in org role (owner/admin/developer/
      viewer/ci/support) plus a role-less grant-only principal is allowed
      auth.me and receives EXACTLY its own identity (principal_id, org, role).
      auth.me is CapSelf so the matrix is "all allow" — the assertion that
      matters is that the served identity is the caller's, never escalated.
    - TestMePolicyRevokedAndExpiredKeys: a revoked or expired credential —
      both surface to the engine as a Disabled principal — is denied auth.me
      with a stable 403 E_FORBIDDEN carrying ReasonDeniedPrincipalDisabled, and
      the denied body never echoes the principal id.
    - TestMePolicyWrongOrganizationPrincipal: a principal whose home org is
      org_intruder receives only org_intruder's identity; the body leaks no
      other tenant's ids. Also asserts at the engine level that Decide(auth.me,
      foreign-org resource) is ReasonAllowedSelf — auth.me never reads the
      resource, so the resource org can never be a leak vector.
    - TestMePolicyGrantContainment: proves scoped grants are confined by the
      engine — a project grant does not reach a sibling project, a staging
      environment grant does not reach production, a service grant reaches
      neither a sibling service nor the parent environment — and that GET
      /v1/me echoes the principal's project grant verbatim (same scope, no
      widening, no invented deeper ids, no sibling-project mention).
- Files changed: internal/controlplane/httpapi/me_policy_test.go (new);
  ralph/prd.json, ralph/progress.txt.
- Verification run: gofmt -w .; go mod tidy; go build ./...; go vet ./...;
  go test ./...; go test -race ./internal/controlplane/httpapi/....
- Results: all passed. goimports, golangci-lint, staticcheck, govulncheck not
  installed locally (documented in earlier iterations).
- Remaining risks / follow-ups:
  - "Isolated Postgres migrations" / "fake Dokploy" acceptance criteria are N/A:
    GET /v1/me touches no database and no Dokploy — it echoes the authenticated
    principal resolved by RequireAuth. The tests drive the real handler + real
    policy engine with a fake Authenticator, which is the full authorization
    path minus the credential store.
  - "revoked key" / "expired key" are modelled as Disabled principals because
    that is the only shape in which a revoked/expired credential reaches the
    policy engine — the auth layer may also reject such a credential earlier
    with 401 E_AUTH (covered by me_test.go's TestMeInvalidCredentials). The
    policy matrix's job is the disabled-principal -> 403 path specifically.
- Learnings for future iterations:
  - Pattern for "Add policy matrix coverage for <endpoint>" stories: add a
    <endpoint>_policy_test.go beside the endpoint's contract test, drive the
    real NewHandler + policy.NewEngine() (production path), and table the
    principal classes the PRD names (roles, revoked/expired = Disabled,
    wrong-org). For a CapSelf endpoint the matrix is "all allow" — assert the
    served data is the caller's own, not that some role is denied.
  - The grant-containment acceptance criteria (sibling project / production /
    parent-secret non-leak) are engine properties: assert them with
    policy.NewEngine().Decide directly in the endpoint's policy test, then tie
    back by proving the endpoint echoes the confined grant set verbatim.
  - me_test.go already provides reusable helpers (meHandlerFor, getMe,
    decodeMe) and middleware_test.go provides decodeError — a sibling
    _test.go file in package httpapi reuses them with no new scaffolding.

    from the policy.Principal that RequireAuth resolves — no store, no Dokploy.

    in policy. No new apierr code, no apienvelope change, no migration — the
    OpenAPI doc and the route-coverage/auth-mapping CI guards are all

    structural: the handler passes principal.OrganizationID and nothing else, so
    the isolation test asserts the reader was asked for exactly that id (capture
    it via a *string field on the fake).
  - The organizations table (migration 0002) is keyed by its own id, so its
    repository Get is scoped by id directly — there is no parent organization_id
    to filter by first, unlike projects/environments/services.

## 2026-05-15 - BE-0048
- Added policy-matrix coverage for GET /v1/organizations: a dedicated test file
  proving action organization.read's authorization contract end to end through
  the real NewHandler + real policy.NewEngine() (the production request path).
  Unlike auth.orgs (CapSelf, covered by BE-0045), organization.read is a CapRead
  action — the matrix is shaped by the read capability, not the self shortcut.
- File changed: internal/controlplane/httpapi/organizations_policy_test.go (new);
  ralph/prd.json, ralph/progress.txt.
- organizations_policy_test.go covers:
  - TestOrganizationsPolicyMatrixRoles: every built-in role (owner/admin/
    developer/viewer/ci/support) holds CapRead, so each is allowed
    organization.read via ReasonAllowedByRole (asserted at the engine level) and
    receives exactly its own home org through the HTTP handler.
  - TestOrganizationsPolicyRevokedAndExpiredKeys: a revoked/expired credential
    surfaces as a Disabled principal -> 403 E_FORBIDDEN carrying
    ReasonDeniedPrincipalDisabled; the denied body never echoes the principal id
    or organization. Reader is wired but must never be reached.
  - TestOrganizationsPolicyWrongOrganizationPrincipal: the /v1/organizations
    route has no ResourceResolver, so RequireAuth authorizes against the
    principal's OWN org scope — a wrong-org principal only ever sees its own org.
    Engine-level: a non-support role is denied a foreign-org organization.read
    with ReasonDeniedCrossTenant; the support role is the documented cross-tenant
    read exception (ReasonAllowedBySupport) but cannot reach customers through
    this route's own-org resolver.
  - TestOrganizationsPolicyGrantContainment: project/env/service grants are
    confined by the engine (no sibling project, no production, no parent-env
    secrets, no sibling service). Crucially: organization.read is evaluated
    against the org-root scope, which a project/env/service grant does not cover
    — a project-scoped key is denied the endpoint with ReasonDeniedOutOfScope
    (403 asserted through the real handler), while an org-scoped grant covers
    the root and is allowed via ReasonAllowedByGrant (200 through the handler).
- Verification run: gofmt -w .; go mod tidy; go vet ./...; go test ./...;
  go test -race ./internal/controlplane/httpapi/ ./internal/controlplane/policy/.
- Results: all passed. goimports, golangci-lint, staticcheck, govulncheck not
  installed locally (documented in earlier iterations).
- Remaining risks / follow-ups: none. "Isolated Postgres migrations" / "fake
  Dokploy" acceptance criteria are N/A — the policy matrix drives the real
  handler + real engine with a fake Authenticator and a fake OrganizationReader;
  the store-backed reader has its own isolated-Postgres coverage in
  store/organization_test.go (BE-0046).
- Learnings for future iterations:
  - Policy-matrix files for CapRead/CapWrite/etc. endpoints differ from the
    CapSelf families (auth.me, auth.orgs): a self action is "all allow" so the
    matrix only pins identity non-escalation, but a capability action's matrix
    must assert the verdict path (ReasonAllowedByRole vs ByGrant vs deny) and
    that scoped keys cannot be widened. Use engine-level Decide() for the
    capability/scope assertions and the HTTP handler for the integration proof.
  - For a route registered with a nil ResourceResolver, RequireAuth authorizes
    against policy.Resource{Scope:{OrganizationID: principal.OrganizationID}} —
    the principal's own org root. A project/env/service-scoped grant does NOT
    cover that root scope, so a scoped key is correctly denied an org-root read
    (ReasonDeniedOutOfScope) even though the same grant authorizes deeper writes.
  - orgPrincipal() in organizations_test.go takes no grants; build
    policy.Principal literals directly when a test needs Grants or Disabled.

  with RequiredAction organization.create and SuccessStatus 201.

  builds store.OrganizationService at startup; all httpapi test helpers and
  routes_test/server_test call sites updated.
- Files changed: internal/controlplane/store/organizationservice.go (new),
  internal/controlplane/store/organization.go, internal/controlplane/store/
  organizationservice_internal_test.go (new), internal/controlplane/store/
  organizationservice_test.go (new), internal/controlplane/httpapi/
  organizations.go, routes.go, server.go, organizations_create_test.go (new),
  me_test.go, organizations_test.go, server_test.go, routes_test.go,

  gofmt -l . clean, go mod tidy clean, go vet ./... clean,
  go test ./... PASS (incl. store integration tests against real Postgres),
  go test -race ./internal/controlplane/{httpapi,store}/ PASS.
- Remaining risks / follow-ups:
  - BE-0050 (contract tests) and BE-0051 (policy matrix) for POST
    /v1/organizations are still passes:false. organizations_create_test.go
    already covers most of BE-0050's criteria (success/error envelopes,
    request_id propagation, OpenAPI registration, auth/authorization paths,
    isolated-Postgres integration in the store test); a future iteration
    should confirm/extend and flip BE-0050, then add the policy matrix for
    BE-0051 (organization.create is CapSelf — all roles allow, disabled/
    revoked/expired keys deny at the auth layer).
  - Mutation audit does not yet capture IP/UA and bypasses audit.Auditor's
    redactor (store builds the AuditEvent directly). Safe today because the
    only recorded metadata is the canonical slug, but richer audit context
    should route through audit.Auditor.
  - Denied-decision auditing at the RequireAuth boundary is still unwired for
    every endpoint (a cross-cutting concern, not specific to this story).
- **Learnings for future iterations:**
  - The store layer has TWO orchestrator-placement options now: reader-port
    adapters (store.OrganizationReader, needs only store) vs unit-of-work
    services (store.OrganizationService, needs store + audit). Audit can live
    in a store service because *store.AuditRepository is in the store package
    — no import cycle. Do NOT try to put the orchestrator in httpapi or a new
    package; follow ProjectService.
  - The validation error code on the wire is E_INVALID_INPUT (yerr.CodeInvalidInput),
    NOT "E_VALIDATION" — apierr.Invalid / apierr.InvalidInput both use it.
  - audit_events.actor_kind has a CHECK constraint IN ('', 'usr', 'sa', 'system');
    pass a valid kind. organization_id is a FK to organizations, so an audit
    test must seed the actor org first. actor_id is plain text (no FK).
  - When adding a parameter to NewHandler, grep for ALL newRouteTable and
    NewHandler call sites — server_test.go has two newRouteTable calls.
