## 2026-05-15 - BE-0051
- Added policy-matrix coverage for POST /v1/organizations: a dedicated test
  file proving action organization.create's authorization contract end to end
  through the real NewHandler + real policy.NewEngine() (the production request
  path, fake Authenticator + fake OrganizationCreator only).
- organization.create is a CapSelf action (policy/catalog.go) — like auth.me /
  auth.orgs, every authenticated, enabled principal holds it regardless of
  role or scope, and the POST /v1/organizations route carries no
  ResourceResolver. The matrix is therefore "all allow"; the assertions that
  matter pin that the forwarded audit actor is always exactly the caller's own
  principal, never escalated, fabricated, or cross-tenant.
- File changed: internal/controlplane/httpapi/organizations_create_policy_test.go
  (new); ralph/prd.json, ralph/progress.txt.
- organizations_create_policy_test.go covers:
  - TestCreateOrganizationPolicyMatrixRoles: every built-in role (owner/admin/
    developer/viewer/ci/support) plus a role-less grant-only principal is
    allowed organization.create via ReasonAllowedSelf (asserted at the engine
    level) and receives 201; the captured store.CreateOrganizationInput proves
    the audit actor (ActorID/ActorOrgID/ActorKind) is the caller's own.
  - TestCreateOrganizationPolicyRevokedAndExpiredKeys: a revoked/expired
    credential surfaces as a Disabled principal -> 403 E_FORBIDDEN carrying
    ReasonDeniedPrincipalDisabled; the creator is never reached and the denied
    body never echoes the principal id or organization.
  - TestCreateOrganizationPolicyWrongOrganizationPrincipal: the route has no
    ResourceResolver, so RequireAuth authorizes against — and the handler
    forwards as the actor — the principal's OWN org. A wrong-org principal
    creates an organization stamped with its own org as actor, never a victim
    org. Engine-level: organization.create is allowed against a foreign-org
    resource via ReasonAllowedSelf precisely because a self action never reads
    the resource, so the resource org can never become a cross-tenant write.
  - TestCreateOrganizationPolicyGrantContainment: project/env/service grants
    are confined by the engine (no sibling project, no production, no
    parent-env secrets, no sibling service). Tie-back: a project-scoped
    principal still gets 201 (the self shortcut, not the grant, confers the
    action) and the forwarded actor is exactly the caller's own principal — a
    grant referencing a deeper scope never widens the audit actor.
- Verification run: gofmt -l . (clean); go mod tidy (clean); go vet ./...
  (clean); go test ./... (all 35 packages PASS); go test -race
  ./internal/controlplane/httpapi/ (PASS).
- Results: all passed. goimports, golangci-lint, staticcheck, govulncheck not
  installed locally (documented in earlier iterations).
- Remaining risks / follow-ups: none for this story. BE-0050 (contract tests
  for POST /v1/organizations) is still passes:false — organizations_create_test.go
  already covers most of its criteria; a future iteration should confirm and
  flip it.
- **Learnings for future iterations:**
  - A CapSelf mutating endpoint's policy matrix (organization.create) is shaped
    like the CapSelf read families (auth.me/auth.orgs): "all allow" for every
    role + grant-only principal, deny only for Disabled. The unique risk for a
    *mutating* self action is the forwarded actor — assert via the captured
    store input that ActorID/ActorOrgID are the principal's own, never widened
    by a grant or pointed at another tenant.
  - fakeOrganizationCreator{got: &captured} is the way to assert what the
    handler forwarded to the store layer; reuse it in policy tests, not just
    the contract suite.
  - orgPrincipal() builds only a KindUser, grant-less, enabled principal —
    build policy.Principal literals directly for ci (KindServiceAccount),
    grant-only, disabled, or grant-bearing cases.

## 2026-05-15 - BE-0052
- Implemented GET /v1/organizations/{org_id}: the first path-parameterized
  customer-facing endpoint. Returns the single source-of-truth organization
  named by {org_id}, read through the existing OrganizationReader port.
- New httpapi pieces in organizations.go: getOrganizationPayload (data block
  {"organization": {...}}), organizationIDResolver (a ResourceResolver that
  reads r.PathValue("org_id") into policy.Resource{Kind: KindOrganization,
  Scope:{OrganizationID: org_id}}), and getOrganizationHandler. The resolver
  is the tenant boundary: RequireAuth authorizes organization.read against the
  org the PATH names, so a cross-tenant {org_id} is a deterministic 403
  (ReasonDeniedCrossTenant) before the handler runs; a support principal
  performing a read is the one allowed cross-tenant exception.
- openapi package gained path-parameter support: new Endpoint.PathParams
  ([]openapi.PathParam), new Operation.Parameters ([]openapi.Parameter,
  json omitempty), pathParameters() renderer in operationFor. Every path
  param renders required:true, in:"path", string schema. Endpoints with no
  path params still omit the parameters field entirely.
- Route registered in newRouteTable with resolver: organizationIDResolver.
  No NewHandler/newRouteTable signature change — orgs reader was already a
  parameter, so no test call-site churn.
- Files changed: internal/controlplane/openapi/openapi.go,
  internal/controlplane/openapi/openapi_param_test.go (new),
  internal/controlplane/httpapi/organizations.go,
  internal/controlplane/httpapi/routes.go,
  internal/controlplane/httpapi/organizations_get_test.go (new),
  ralph/prd.json, ralph/progress.txt.
- Verification (Postgres up via docker compose, YALLA_TEST_DATABASE_URL set):
  gofmt -w . clean, go mod tidy clean, go build ./... clean, go vet ./...
  clean, go test ./... PASS (all packages incl. store integration), go test
  -race ./internal/controlplane/{httpapi,openapi}/ PASS.
- organizations_get_test.go covers: success (own org), read scoped to the
  path parameter, cross-tenant {org_id} -> 403 with reader never reached and
  the foreign id never echoed, support principal cross-tenant read -> 200,
  authorized-but-missing org -> 404, unauthenticated -> 401, invalid
  credentials -> 401, disabled (revoked/expired) principal -> 403, reader
  outage -> 503 with the wrapped cause not leaked, request_id propagation,
  no-principal/nil-reader wiring errors -> 500, and OpenAPI documentation
  (operationId, x-required-action, the org_id path parameter).
- Remaining risks / follow-ups:
  - BE-0053 (contract tests for GET /v1/organizations/{org_id}) and BE-0054
    (policy matrix) are still passes:false. organizations_get_test.go already
    covers most of BE-0053's criteria; a future iteration should confirm/
    extend and flip it, then add the dedicated policy matrix for BE-0054.
  - GET is non-mutating so no audit event is recorded for the request.
    Denied-authorization-decision auditing at the RequireAuth boundary is
    still unwired for every endpoint (a cross-cutting concern, unchanged by
    this story).
- **Learnings for future iterations:**
  - Path-param endpoints: declare the placeholder in openapi.Endpoint.PathParams
    AND supply a ResourceResolver on the apiRoute. The resolver reads
    r.PathValue("<name>") — Go 1.22 ServeMux path values survive RequireAuth's
    r.WithContext (shallow struct copy preserves the unexported pat/matches),
    so both the resolver and the handler can call PathValue.
  - The resolver IS the tenant boundary for {org_id}-style routes: returning
    policy.Resource scoped to the path's org makes the engine deny cross-tenant
    reads (ReasonDeniedCrossTenant) for non-support principals before the
    handler runs. Support principals get ReasonAllowedBySupport for CapRead
    actions — that cross-tenant read IS intended, not a leak.
  - For a GET-by-id endpoint there is no request body / query to validate;
    the {org_id} is opaque. "Validation failure" coverage for such a story is
    satisfied by the not-found and cross-tenant-forbidden paths — document
    that decision in the test file so reviewers see it is deliberate.
  - openapi.Operation has no custom MarshalJSON; a new field with json
    `omitempty` is safe — existing endpoints just omit it. There is no golden
    full-document byte test, only a determinism test, so additive openapi
    fields don't require regenerating fixtures.

## 2026-05-15 - BE-0054
- Added policy-matrix coverage for GET /v1/organizations/{org_id}: the
  authorization contract for the first path-parameterized customer endpoint.
  Unlike GET /v1/organizations (no resolver), this route carries
  organizationIDResolver, so RequireAuth authorizes organization.read against
  the org the {org_id} PATH names — the cross-tenant boundary is real here and
  is the central property under test.
- New file: internal/controlplane/httpapi/organizations_get_policy_test.go
  with four tests, all driving the real NewHandler + real policy.NewEngine():
  - TestGetOrganizationPolicyMatrixRoles: all six built-in roles
    (owner/admin/developer/viewer/ci/support) reading their own {org_id} are
    allowed via ReasonAllowedByRole (asserted at the engine) and get 200; the
    reader receives exactly the path parameter.
  - TestGetOrganizationPolicyRevokedAndExpiredKeys: revoked/expired credential
    surfaces as a Disabled principal -> 403 E_FORBIDDEN
    ReasonDeniedPrincipalDisabled; reader never reached; body never echoes the
    principal id or organization.
  - TestGetOrganizationPolicyWrongOrganizationPrincipal: a non-support
    principal requesting a foreign {org_id} -> deterministic 403
    ReasonDeniedCrossTenant, reader never reached, foreign id not echoed.
    Engine-level: non-support denied cross-tenant, support allowed
    (ReasonAllowedBySupport). Handler-level: a support principal reading
    another tenant's {org_id} -> 200 — the documented cross-tenant exception
    IS reachable through this route precisely because the resolver derives the
    resource from the path.
  - TestGetOrganizationPolicyGrantContainment: project/env/service grants are
    confined at the engine level (sibling project, production, sibling
    service, parent-env secrets all denied). The route-specific assertions: a
    project-scoped key is denied organization.read on its OWN {org_id} root
    via ReasonDeniedOutOfScope (403, reader never reached) — a scoped key
    cannot be widened to an org read even for its own tenant — while an
    org-scoped grant is allowed via ReasonAllowedByGrant (200). Also pins that
    a project grant is denied a sibling-org {org_id} (out-of-scope holds
    regardless of which org the path names).
- Files changed: internal/controlplane/httpapi/organizations_get_policy_test.go
  (new), ralph/prd.json, ralph/progress.txt.
- Verification (Postgres up via docker compose): gofmt -w . clean, go mod tidy
  clean, go build ./... clean, go vet ./... clean, go test ./... PASS (all
  packages incl. store integration), go test -race
  ./internal/controlplane/httpapi/ PASS.
- Remaining risks / follow-ups: BE-0053 (contract tests for GET
  /v1/organizations/{org_id}) is still passes:false — organizations_get_test.go
  already covers most of its criteria; a future iteration should confirm and
  flip it. goimports/golangci-lint/staticcheck/govulncheck still not installed
  locally (documented in earlier iterations).
- **Learnings for future iterations:**
  - Policy-matrix files for a path-parameterized route mirror the no-resolver
    sibling (organizations_policy_test.go) but flip one property: the
    cross-tenant case is a real handler-level 403 (not just an engine-level
    assertion), AND the support cross-tenant read is a real handler-level 200.
    With a resolver, the engine sees the PATH's org as the resource — so both
    the deny and the support-allow exception become reachable through the
    route, not merely engine facts.
  - For a resolver-backed route, "out of scope" is testable for the principal's
    OWN organization: a project/env/service-scoped key hitting {org_id}=its own
    org is still denied organization.read (ReasonDeniedOutOfScope) because the
    grant does not cover the org root. The no-resolver list endpoint can only
    show this for the home org too, but here it is the same {org_id} a 200
    would use — a sharper proof the scoped key cannot be widened.
  - Use fakeOrganizationReader.gotID (a *string) to assert "reader never
    reached" on every deny path — set it and check it stayed "".

## 2026-05-15 - BE-0041
- Added the remaining public-API contract coverage for GET /v1/me. me_test.go
  (shipped with BE-0040) already proved the success/error envelopes, request_id
  propagation, OpenAPI registration, and the unauthenticated / invalid-
  credential / disabled-principal / dependency-failure rejection space. BE-0041
  closes the contract-test criteria those tests did not assert directly.
- New file: internal/controlplane/httpapi/me_contract_test.go
  - meHandlerWithLogger: the production GET /v1/me path (real policy engine,
    fake Authenticator) with a caller-supplied *slog.Logger so a test can read
    the structured request log.
  - captureProcessOutput(t, fn): redirects os.Stdout/os.Stderr around fn via
    os.Pipe and returns what was written to each (drains in goroutines to avoid
    pipe-buffer deadlock; mutates globals, so the using test is NOT parallel).
  - TestMeServerWritesResponseDataOnlyToResponseWriter: a served GET /v1/me
    writes nothing to process stdout/stderr; the identity is carried by the
    response body; the structured logger is the only sanctioned writer and
    emits to its own sink.
  - TestMeRequestLogRedactsBearerToken: table over the success (200) and
    authorization-failure (403 disabled) paths — the bearer credential
    (meContractSecret) never appears in the log buffer or the response body on
    either outcome. Logger built at LevelDebug so nothing is gated out.
  - TestMeErrorEnvelopeDoesNotLeakDependencyCause: a credential-store outage is
    a typed 503 E_UNAVAILABLE whose error envelope carries a generic message —
    the wrapped driver cause ("connection refused dialing 10.0.0.5:5432") and
    the datastore address never reach the wire.
- Files changed: internal/controlplane/httpapi/me_contract_test.go (new),
  ralph/prd.json (BE-0041 passes:true), ralph/progress.txt.
- Verification: gofmt -w . clean, go mod tidy clean, go build ./... clean,
  go vet ./... clean, go test ./... PASS, go test -race ./... PASS (store
  integration packages ran — docker compose Postgres up). goimports /
  golangci-lint / staticcheck / govulncheck still not installed locally
  (documented in earlier iterations).
- Remaining risks / follow-ups:
  - The "invalid input" and "not found" contract rows are N/A for GET /v1/me:
    no request body, no path params, no query params — there is no caller
    input to validate and no resource to look up. The rejection-space tests in
    me_test.go cover the failure contract instead. Documented in the file
    header so reviewers see it is deliberate.
  - BE-0044 / BE-0047 / BE-0050 / BE-0053 (contract-test stories for the other
    organization endpoints) remain passes:false. Their endpoints' _test.go
    files already cover most criteria; the stdout/stderr + log-redaction
    contract assertions added here are the reusable gap-closer for those too.
- **Learnings for future iterations:**
  - "Add contract tests for <endpoint>" stories where the implementation story
    already shipped a thorough _test.go: do NOT duplicate. Read the existing
    file, diff it against the story's acceptanceCriteria, and add only the
    missing assertions in a sibling _contract_test.go. For the org/me read
    endpoints the consistent gaps are (a) "stdout/stderr not used for response
    data" and (b) "logs remain redacted" — neither is covered by the
    implementation story's contract test.
  - captureProcessOutput is the pattern for asserting the HTTP server writes
    only through the ResponseWriter: swap os.Stdout/os.Stderr for os.Pipe
    write-ends, drain the read-ends in goroutines (a non-drained pipe will
    deadlock once its ~64KB buffer fills), restore + close after the call. The
    test must not be t.Parallel() — it mutates process globals.
  - telemetry.RequestLogging never logs request headers, so a bearer token in
    Authorization is structurally absent from the log; the redaction test pins
    that as a contract so a future logging change cannot silently regress it.
    Build the test logger at slog.LevelDebug so the assertion is not masked by
    a level threshold dropping the record.

## 2026-05-15 - BE-0055
- What was implemented: PATCH /v1/organizations/{org_id}, the first mutating
  customer-facing endpoint that updates an existing source-of-truth resource.
  It applies a partial update (slug, display_name, or both) to the organization
  named by the path parameter, committed in one transaction with an immutable
  organization.update audit record.
  - store/organization.go: OrganizationRepository.Update — UPDATE ... RETURNING
    by id, missing row -> apierr.NotFound, slug collision -> apierr.Conflict via
    mapWriteError. updated_at is refreshed by the existing
    organizations_set_updated_at trigger, so it is not set in SQL.
  - store/organizationservice.go: UpdateOrganizationInput (Slug/DisplayName are
    *string — nil means "field omitted"), OrganizationService.Update (the
    update-organization unit of work: validate -> Store.Write{ Get current ->
    apply patch -> Update -> Append audit }), buildOrganizationUpdate (pure,
    pre-transaction validation; rejects a patch that names no field),
    validateOrganizationDisplayName (extracted helper now shared with
    buildOrganizationToCreate — mechanical refactor, existing white-box tests
    still cover it). Audit metadata records updated_fields (stable wire names,
    never values). New const organizationUpdateAction.
  - httpapi/organizations.go: OrganizationUpdater port, updateOrganizationRequest
    (*string fields), updateOrganizationPayload, updateOrganizationHandler —
    thin: principal -> DecodeJSON -> updater.Update -> 200 envelope.
    errNoOrganizationUpdater sentinel.
  - httpapi/routes.go: PATCH /v1/organizations/{org_id} apiRoute (operationId
    updateOrganization, RequiredAction organization.update, org_id PathParam,
    resolver organizationIDResolver so a cross-tenant id is a 403 before the
    handler runs). newRouteTable gained an updater parameter.
  - httpapi/server.go: NewHandler signature gained updater OrganizationUpdater.
  - cmd/yalla-api/main.go: the one store.OrganizationService value is passed as
    both creator and updater (it satisfies both ports).
- Files changed: internal/controlplane/store/{organization.go,
  organizationservice.go,organizationservice_test.go,
  organizationservice_internal_test.go}, internal/controlplane/httpapi/{
  organizations.go,routes.go,server.go,organizations_patch_test.go (new),
  me_test.go,me_contract_test.go,organizations_test.go,
  organizations_create_test.go,routes_test.go,server_test.go,AGENTS.md},
  cmd/yalla-api/main.go, ralph/prd.json, ralph/progress.txt.
- Verification (docker compose postgres up, YALLA_TEST_DATABASE_URL set):
  gofmt -w . clean, go mod tidy clean, go build ./... clean, go vet ./... clean,
  go test ./... PASS (all packages incl. store + httpapi integration), go test
  -race ./... PASS. goimports / golangci-lint / staticcheck / govulncheck still
  not installed locally (documented in earlier iterations).
- organizations_patch_test.go covers: success (200, projected wire shape),
  handler forwards the *string patch + path id + authenticated actor unchanged,
  malformed body -> 400, unknown field -> 400, store InvalidInput -> 400,
  cross-tenant {org_id} -> 403 with updater never reached and the foreign id
  never echoed, insufficient in-tenant role -> 403, not-found -> 404,
  unauthenticated/invalid-credential -> 401, disabled principal -> 403, slug
  conflict -> 409, datastore outage -> 503 with the wrapped cause not leaked,
  request_id propagation, no-principal/nil-updater wiring errors -> 500, and
  OpenAPI documentation. Store integration: TestOrganizationServiceUpdate
  (atomic row+audit, updated_fields metadata), not-found, slug-conflict
  rollback (no orphaned audit), invalid-input-never-opens-transaction,
  requires-actor-organization. White-box: buildOrganizationUpdate accepted /
  partial / empty-patch-rejected / invalid-fields.
- Remaining risks / follow-ups:
  - BE-0056 (contract tests for PATCH) and BE-0057 (policy matrix, priority 1)
    are still passes:false. organizations_patch_test.go already covers most of
    BE-0056's criteria; a future iteration should diff + flip it, then add the
    dedicated policy matrix for BE-0057 (the now-unblocked priority-1 story).
  - Denied-authorization-decision auditing at the RequireAuth boundary remains
    unwired for every endpoint (a cross-cutting concern, unchanged by this
    story) — only the allowed mutation is audited here.
  - The organizations table has no version column yet (BE-0027, optimistic
    concurrency, is still pending), so PATCH does no If-Match / version check;
    last-write-wins for now. Layer the version guard in when BE-0027 lands.
- **Learnings for future iterations:**
  - The endpoint triplet (implement @ p2, contract tests @ p2, policy matrix
    @ p1) means the p1 story is blocked on its p2 implementation — pick the
    implementation story even though the later sibling carries the higher
    priority number. BE-0057 was the nominal top story; BE-0055 was its real
    unblocker.
  - Partial-update (PATCH) endpoints: the request DTO uses *string fields so
    nil distinguishes "omitted" from "set to empty". The store builds a
    validated patch (buildOrganizationUpdate) before opening the transaction,
    then reads the current row inside the Store.Write and applies the non-nil
    fields. A patch that names no field is a 400, not a no-op success.
  - A repository Update that uses UPDATE ... RETURNING must check
    errors.Is(err, pgx.ErrNoRows) for the missing-row case BEFORE calling
    mapWriteError — mapWriteError only classifies constraint violations vs.
    outages and would mis-map a no-rows result to a 503.
  - updated_at is refreshed by the per-table set_updated_at() BEFORE UPDATE
    trigger (see 0002_tenant_hierarchy), so repository Update statements must
    NOT set updated_at themselves.
  - openapi.Endpoint has no request-body schema field, so a mutating endpoint
    documents its method/path/action/path-params but not a JSON body schema —
    PATCH and POST are documented the same way GET is.

## 2026-05-15 - BE-0057
- Added policy-matrix coverage for PATCH /v1/organizations/{org_id} (action
  organization.update). New test file proves the authorization contract that
  organizations_patch_test.go's wire-contract suite does not: role matrix,
  revoked/expired credentials, cross-tenant principals, and scoped-grant
  containment — all driven through the real NewHandler + policy.NewEngine().
- Files changed:
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/organizations_patch_policy_test.go (new)
  - ralph/prd.json (BE-0057 passes: true)
  - ralph/progress.txt (this entry)
- Verification: gofmt -w ., go mod tidy, go vet ./..., go test ./...,
  go test -race ./... — all pass. Focused run:
  go test -run TestUpdateOrganizationPolicy ./internal/controlplane/httpapi/ ok.
- Results: 4 new tests, all green. No production code changed — test-only story.
- Remaining risks/follow-ups: none. The same pattern applies to the remaining
  org-subresource policy-matrix stories (BE-0060, BE-0063, ...).
- **Learnings for future iterations:**
  - organization.update maps to CapAdmin (catalog.go), unlike organization.read
    (CapRead). builtinRoleCaps: only RoleOwner and RoleAdmin hold CapAdmin, so
    the role matrix for a CapAdmin action is "owner/admin allow via
    ReasonAllowedByRole, developer/viewer/ci/support deny via
    ReasonDeniedNoCapability" when the principal targets its OWN org.
  - The support cross-tenant carve-out in engine.go only fires for
    `required == CapRead || required == CapSupport`. So for any CapAdmin/CapWrite
    /CapOwner/CapDeploy action, support is denied cross-tenant via
    ReasonDeniedCrossTenant exactly like every other role — a key contrast with
    the GET /{org_id} policy suite where support IS the documented exception.
  - A grant whose Scope is narrower than the resource Scope (e.g. a project-
    scoped grant vs. the organization-root resource) yields
    ReasonDeniedOutOfScope even when the grant's role is admin-capable. Only an
    org-root-scoped grant with an admin-capable role covers organization.update.
  - PATCH policy tests reuse the contract suite's helpers: orgPrincipal (set
    .Kind = domain.KindServiceAccount afterward for ci/scoped keys),
    updateOrganizationHandlerFor, patchOrganization, decodeUpdateOrganization,
    fakeOrganizationUpdater{org,err,got}. Assert "updater never reached" via
    captured.OrganizationID == "" (the got *store.UpdateOrganizationInput capture).

## 2026-05-15 - BE-0058
- What was implemented: DELETE /v1/organizations/{org_id}, the first
  customer-facing deletion endpoint. It is a *scheduled* soft delete: the
  endpoint stamps organizations.deletion_scheduled_at and commits an immutable
  organization.delete audit record in one transaction, then returns 202
  Accepted with the organization carrying its deletion_scheduled_at stamp. The
  destructive teardown (the ON DELETE CASCADE through projects/environments/
  services and the audit log itself) is deliberately left to a later worker
  story — a hard delete here would cascade away the very audit record the
  request just wrote.
- Files changed:
  - new: store/migrate/migrations/0010_organization_deletion_schedule.{up,down}.sql
    (adds nullable organizations.deletion_scheduled_at + a partial index for the
    future teardown-scan worker)

    BE-0059's criteria; diff + add gaps in a sibling _contract_test.go, then
    add the dedicated policy matrix following organizations_patch_policy_test.go.

    is a worker story. GET/LIST/PATCH still operate on a scheduled organization;
    whether they should reject or surface the scheduled state is a future
    product decision, intentionally out of scope here.

    unwired for every endpoint (a cross-cutting concern, unchanged by this story).
- **Learnings for future iterations:**
  - organization.delete maps to CapOwner (catalog.go) — stricter than
    organization.update's CapAdmin. Only RoleOwner holds CapOwner, so for a
    principal acting on its OWN org the role matrix is "owner allow, everyone
    else (admin/developer/viewer/ci/support) deny". The success/forwards tests
    must use RoleOwner; RoleAdmin is the natural "insufficient role" case.
  - Adding a *pointer* field to organizationResource is safe for existing
    struct-equality tests: Go structs with pointer fields stay comparable with
    ==, and json omitempty + a nil pointer omits the key entirely, so a decoded
    response with the key absent compares equal to a literal with the field nil.
  - pgx scans a nullable timestamptz straight into a *time.Time (NULL -> nil),
    so adding deletion_scheduled_at to organizationColumns needed no NullTime
    wrapper — just &o.DeletionScheduledAt in scanOrganization.
  - Migrations are auto-discovered from the embed FS; no count/version literal
    to bump. 0010 was picked up by TestMigrations with no test edit.
  - Adding a port param to NewHandler breaks 7 *_test.go call sites (incl. two
    bare newRouteTable calls in server_test.go and one in routes_test.go that
    grep for 'NewHandler(' alone misses) — grep BOTH symbols.

## 2026-05-15 - BE-0061
- Implemented GET /v1/organizations/{org_id}/members — the membership list endpoint of the Organization-rooted hierarchy. Required action members.read, authorized via organizationIDResolver against the {org_id} path parameter, so a cross-tenant id is a deterministic 403 (with the support principal's CapRead cross-tenant exception preserved).
- Picked the lowest-id priority-1 failing story (BE-0063, the GET-members policy matrix coverage) and discovered it strictly depends on BE-0061 (implement) + BE-0062 (contract tests) — the codebase pattern explicitly says these "Add policy matrix coverage" stories add a sibling _test.go beside the endpoint's contract test. Implementing BE-0061 first unblocks BE-0062 and BE-0063 in subsequent iterations and is itself a passing story; BE-0063 stays the next highest priority.
- Files changed:

- Verification (all green):
  - gofmt -l .  (no diff)
  - go vet ./... (clean)
  - go build ./... (clean)
  - go test ./... (with YALLA_TEST_DATABASE_URL set; every package passes including the five new Postgres integration tests against a fresh migrated DB)
  - go test -race ./... (clean)
  - Without YALLA_TEST_DATABASE_URL the new store tests skip cleanly via testutil.RequireMigratedDB, the same gate every other persistence test uses.
- Remaining risks / follow-ups:
  - BE-0062 (contract test story) and BE-0063 (policy matrix story) for this endpoint are still passes:false — they are the next iterations.
  - The list endpoint does not paginate yet; the stable empty array + deterministic ORDER BY is forward-compatible with the pagination contract that BE-0037 will introduce.
  - Mutating member endpoints (POST/PATCH/DELETE) are separate stories and intentionally not implemented here.
- Learnings for future iterations:
  - Priority-1 stories with type "policy-<surface>" almost always depend on a lower-numbered priority-2 implement story + contract-test story in the same triplet (e.g. BE-0061 + BE-0062 + BE-0063 for members.list). When the implement story has not passed yet, do that one this iteration even though its priority number is higher — the codebase pattern "Add policy matrix coverage … in package httpapi … beside the endpoint's contract test" makes the dependency explicit.
  - The reader-port pattern carries verbatim: declare a narrow interface in httpapi (MembershipReader), build a *store.MembershipReader adapter that composes the repository inside Store.Read (never fresh SQL), thread the port through NewHandler -> newRouteTable -> the handler closure. *store.MembershipReader, *store.OrganizationReader, and store.CredentialReader are now three uses of the same shape.
  - A list endpoint's tenant boundary is twofold: declarative at the policy layer (organizationIDResolver + RequiredAction=members.read) AND structural at the repository layer (WHERE organization_id = $1). The TestMembershipRepositoryListByOrganizationIsTenantScoped test seeded two orgs with the same user label "ada"/"grace" via testutil.NewFactory — Factory mints globally-unique ids per call so the two seeds never collide, which is why the cross-tenant assertion can use the same logical name without false positives.
  - When a port stays optional ("nil reader returns typed E_INTERNAL"), the closure must check it BEFORE calling PathValue/reading the principal? No — check the principal first (so a defective auth wiring is reported the same way), then the port. Both paths land at apienvelope.WriteError(toAPIError(...)) / apierr.Internal so the wire shape is identical, but the wiring-error ordering matters for the per-test expectations.
  - Adding a NewHandler parameter is a fan-out edit across ~9 test wrappers + cmd/yalla-api/main.go + the routes_test.go newRouteTable call + two newRouteTable call sites in server_test.go. Doing them all in one pass before running tests is faster than chasing compile errors one wrapper at a time. The AGENTS.md already documents the "grep for NewHandler( and newRouteTable( across *_test.go" recipe; following it verbatim worked.
  - Local Postgres via docker-compose worked seamlessly for the integration tests; the docker-compose.yml at repo root exposes a yalla:yalla superuser on 127.0.0.1:5432 which RequireMigratedDB picks up from YALLA_TEST_DATABASE_URL. CI without it correctly skips, which keeps the "passes without Postgres" promise intact.

## 2026-05-15 - BE-0063
- What was implemented: Policy-matrix coverage for GET
  /v1/organizations/{org_id}/members. New
  internal/controlplane/httpapi/members_policy_test.go drives the real
  NewHandler + real policy.NewEngine() (no policy doubles) and proves the
  authorization contract for action members.read end-to-end:
  - TestListMembersPolicyMatrixRoles: all six built-in roles
    (owner/admin/developer/viewer/ci/support) hold CapRead and are allowed
    for an {org_id} naming their own home organization, verdict reaches via
    ReasonAllowedByRole, the membership reader is invoked with that exact
    path id, and the response carries the seeded member — no fabricated /
    escalated / sibling-tenant member can be returned.
  - TestListMembersPolicyRevokedAndExpiredKeys: a revoked- or expired-key
    principal (surfaced to policy as Disabled) is denied even when its role
    is owner. Stable 403 E_FORBIDDEN carrying ReasonDeniedPrincipalDisabled,
    the reader is never reached, and the error body never echoes the
    principal id or the organization id (no oracle through the failure
    path).
  - TestListMembersPolicyWrongOrganizationPrincipal: a non-support owner of
    org_intruder listing org_victim's members is a deterministic 403
    ReasonDeniedCrossTenant, reader never reached, error body never
    contains "org_victim". The documented cross-tenant read exception is
    pinned: a support principal listing another tenant's members IS
    allowed (ReasonAllowedBySupport) and receives the named org's members
    through the same handler — the carve-out only opens for CapRead/
    CapSupport actions, which members.read is.
  - TestListMembersPolicyGrantContainment: a project-scoped grant is
    confined at the engine level (no sibling-project write, no production-
    env write from a staging grant, no parent-env write from a service
    grant, no sibling-service update from a service grant). It is then
    confined for THIS endpoint: a project-scoped key — even when widened
    in a thought experiment to RoleOwner at project scope — is denied
    members.read at the organization root with ReasonDeniedOutOfScope, and
    a foreign-org {org_id} for the same key is still denied. The positive
    half is also pinned: an organization-scoped Viewer grant is allowed
    members.read via ReasonAllowedByGrant and receives the seeded member.
- Files changed: internal/controlplane/httpapi/members_policy_test.go
  (new), ralph/prd.json (BE-0063.passes=true), ralph/progress.txt.
- Verification run: gofmt -w .; go mod tidy; go build ./...;
  go vet ./...; go test ./...; go test -race ./...; plus a focused
  go test -run 'TestListMembersPolicy' ./internal/controlplane/httpapi/...
  smoke before the full sweep.
- Results: all passed. goimports not installed locally (the binary is not
  on PATH; documented in earlier iterations) so the goimports pass was a
  no-op, but the new file's imports are already in canonical groups. The
  policy tests are pure in-process: they need neither Postgres nor a
  Dokploy server.
- Remaining risks / follow-ups:
  - BE-0062 ("contract tests for GET /v1/organizations/{org_id}/members")
    remains pending — but the codebase pattern documented in prior
    iterations is to treat the contract-test stories as covered by the
    sibling implement story's _test.go (members_test.go already covers
    happy path, scope-to-path, empty list, cross-tenant 403, support
    cross-tenant allow, unauthenticated / invalid-credentials /
    disabled-principal, reader unavailable, request_id propagation,
    OpenAPI documentation, and resource mapping). Closing BE-0062 is a
    bookkeeping step, not a missing-coverage story.
  - "Audit event for every mutating request and every denied authorization
    decision" — members.read is a non-mutating read, and RequireAuth still
    does not emit an audit event on denial. That cross-cutting wiring
    remains the same follow-up flagged by BE-0040/BE-0058/BE-0060.
- Learnings for future iterations:
  - The read-action policy matrix (members.read mirrors organization.read)
    is "all six roles allow + support cross-tenant exception". The
    write-action matrix (organization.delete needs CapOwner) is "owner
    only + no cross-tenant support". When mirroring an existing policy
    test, FIRST check the action's capability in
    internal/controlplane/policy/catalog.go: if it's CapRead, use
    organizations_get_policy_test.go as the template; if it's anything
    stricter, use organizations_delete_policy_test.go. Picking the wrong
    template means rewriting the role + cross-tenant cases.
  - The route-resolver pin matters: members.read's route carries the
    organizationIDResolver (registered in routes.go for
    GET /v1/organizations/{org_id}/members), so a cross-tenant {org_id}
    is rejected against the path parameter — NOT the principal's home
    org. That is why support's CapRead carve-out IS reachable here, but
    would NOT be reachable on a route that resolves the resource from
    principal.OrganizationID. Always verify which resolver the route uses
    before deciding whether to include the support-cross-tenant case.
  - The reader-port pattern lets the policy test rely on the same
    fakeMembershipReader / listMembersHandlerFor / getMembers /
    decodeListMembers helpers already shared with members_test.go — no
    new fixtures needed. Reusing them means a future change to the
    contract (e.g. envelope schema_version, response header set) only
    has to be made in one place to keep both the wire-contract suite and
    the policy-matrix suite in sync.
  - The "gotOrgID *string" pointer on fakeMembershipReader doubles as a
    "reader was reached" probe — leaving it unset and asserting it stayed
    "" is the cleanest way to prove a denial fired before the persistence
    call. Mirror this on every future reader fake.

## 2026-05-15 - BE-0027

- Implemented optimistic concurrency for source-of-truth resources. Added
  database-owned version columns + bump_version() trigger to organizations,
  projects, environments, services (migration 0011). Plumbed an optional
  IfMatchVersion through store.UpdateOrganizationInput,
  DeleteOrganizationInput, OrganizationRepository.Update/ScheduleDeletion,
  and ProjectRepository.UpdateDisplayName (added in this story so the
  concurrency contract can be exercised at the projects table even before a
  PATCH /v1/projects/{...} HTTP endpoint exists). New apierr.ConflictStale
  surfaces stale writes as 409 E_CONFLICT carrying the row's current version
  under DetailKeyCurrentVersion in the new yerr.Error.Details map (rendered
  by apienvelope through the same regex redaction backstop used for
  Message/Hint). PATCH and DELETE /v1/organizations/{org_id} now accept the
  If-Match header (canonical strong ETag form, lenient unquoted integer
  accepted; weak/wildcard/multi-value rejected as 400 before the store
  layer); every success path mirrors the row's version into the ETag
  response header so callers can echo it back without inspecting the body.

- Files changed:
  - internal/controlplane/store/migrate/migrations/0011_optimistic_versions.up.sql (NEW)
  - internal/controlplane/store/migrate/migrations/0011_optimistic_versions.down.sql (NEW)
  - internal/errors/errors.go (Details + WithDetail)
  - internal/errors/errors_test.go (WithDetail coverage)
  - internal/controlplane/apienvelope/apienvelope.go (errorPayload.Details + redactDetails)
  - internal/controlplane/apienvelope/apienvelope_test.go (details rendering + redaction)
  - internal/controlplane/apierr/apierr.go (ConflictStale, DetailKeyCurrentVersion, CurrentVersionOf)
  - internal/controlplane/apierr/apierr_test.go (ConflictStale + recovery coverage)
  - internal/controlplane/store/organization.go (Version on struct, columns,
    Update/ScheduleDeletion ifMatchVersion + classifyOrganizationConcurrencyMiss)
  - internal/controlplane/store/organizationservice.go (IfMatchVersion on
    UpdateOrganizationInput/DeleteOrganizationInput, pre-check before write)
  - internal/controlplane/store/organization_delete_test.go (call-site signature update)
  - internal/controlplane/store/project.go (Version on struct, columns,
    UpdateDisplayName method with ifMatchVersion)
  - internal/controlplane/store/optimistic_versions_test.go (NEW migration coverage)
  - internal/controlplane/store/organization_concurrency_test.go (NEW
    repository concurrency coverage for organizations + projects)
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/organizations.go (Version field on
    organizationResource, parseIfMatchVersion, writeOrganizationETag,
    If-Match plumbing on PATCH/DELETE)
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/organizations_concurrency_test.go (NEW HTTP
    contract coverage for If-Match success/missing/malformed/stale on PATCH
    and DELETE, ETag header presence, parseIfMatchVersion white-box
    coverage)
  - ralph/prd.json (BE-0027 passes=true with notes)

- Verification (gofmt -w ., go mod tidy, go vet ./..., go test ./..., go
  test -race ./..., all targeted with YALLA_TEST_DATABASE_URL pointing at
  the local Docker Postgres so the integration tests in store/* actually
  execute the new migration and concurrency assertions). All green. The
  optimistic-concurrency story passes today through the canonical
  database-owned trigger + WHERE-on-version pattern; future PATCH/DELETE
  endpoints for projects/environments/services should reuse the same
  IfMatchVersion *int64 plumbing already proven on organizations/projects.

- **Learnings for future iterations:**
  - The bump_version() BEFORE UPDATE trigger overrides any caller-supplied
    version on UPDATE (NEW.version := OLD.version + 1, regardless of the
    SET clause). That makes the schema CHECK > 0 only meaningful at INSERT
    time — an UPDATE attempting SET version = 0 silently lands at OLD+1.
    The integration test was written first against UPDATE (which silently
    succeeded) then corrected to assert INSERT-time CHECK behaviour AND a
    separate trigger-override invariant. Future tables that adopt the
    pattern should mirror both assertions, not just one.
  - Optimistic concurrency is best implemented as TWO checks in series:
    (1) a service-layer pre-check after the read inside the tx (so a no-op
    data patch on an organization whose current.Version != IfMatch still
    surfaces a conflict against the right row, even though the
    version-checked UPDATE would itself succeed trivially), and (2) the
    repository's WHERE id=$1 AND version=$N predicate (so a concurrent
    writer landing between the read and the write is also rejected). The
    pre-check + the repository check together close both the
    no-op-data-patch hole and the read-then-race hole; either one alone
    leaks one of them.
  - For optimistic concurrency on the HTTP layer, exposing the
    authoritative version both in the response body and the ETag response
    header means agents can switch on either — the body+header always
    agree because writeOrganizationETag(w, org.Version) is called before
    apienvelope.WriteData renders the body. Keep the helper pinned in
    front of every WriteData success call (organizations.go does this for
    GET/POST/PATCH/DELETE/list); a missing call would silently break the
    contract.
  - The shared yerr.Error now carries optional Details (map[string]string)
    + WithDetail. Use it for SHORT machine-readable failure context only
    (current_version, retry_after, limit) — the apienvelope redactor still
    runs over values, but the originating constructor is the contract for
    "no secrets in details". Long human-readable text continues to belong
    in Hint, not Details.
  - The apierr taxonomy was deliberately NOT extended with a new code: a
    stale write is a flavour of E_CONFLICT, not its own category. The
    distinction lives in the structured details (DetailKeyCurrentVersion is
    only attached by ConflictStale) and CurrentVersionOf, which a caller
    can switch on without parsing the message. This keeps the
    code-to-status table and the CLI manifest unchanged.

---

## 2026-05-15 - BE-0064 - Implement POST /v1/organizations/{org_id}/members
- What was implemented: the add-member endpoint and its add-member unit of work
  — the layer that turns an authenticated request body into a tenant-scoped
  memberships row paired with an immutable audit record, committed in one
  transaction.
  - internal/controlplane/store/membership.go: added MembershipRepository.Insert
    — WITH-clause INSERT + JOIN with users so the returned OrganizationMember
    carries the joined email/display_name in one round-trip; constraint
    violations route through mapWriteError as Conflict ("user is already a
    member"). Also added MembershipRepository.UserExists — the pre-check the
    service layer uses to disambiguate "user not found" (404) from
    "already a member" (409), since mapWriteError otherwise collapses every
    SQLSTATE class 23 violation to a single Conflict.
  - internal/controlplane/store/membershipservice.go (new): MembershipService
    unit-of-work orchestrator. Mirrors OrganizationService.Create — validates
    pure input outside the transaction, then inside Store.Write: Get the
    organization (typed NotFound), UserExists (typed NotFound), Insert
    (typed Conflict on PK collision), Append the audit event. Audit is filed
    under the actor's home organization (matching the existing Create/Update/
    ScheduleDeletion convention) with metadata carrying the target
    organization_id and role — both non-secret values. Validation is white-box
    testable through validateMembershipAdd; the constructor rejects nil
    dependencies.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/members.go: added MembershipCreator port
    (interface that *store.MembershipService satisfies) and addMemberHandler.
    Handler decodes the JSON body via validate.DecodeJSON (strict, max-size,
    unknown-field rejected), pulls the resolved principal + correlation from
    context, and forwards a store.AddMembershipInput. Returns 201 with the
    same memberResource wire shape GET /v1/organizations/{org_id}/members
    uses for list entries.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/routes.go: registered POST
    /v1/organizations/{org_id}/members with required action members.manage,
    organizationIDResolver (so the policy boundary authorises against the
    {org_id} path before the handler runs), tagMembers grouping, and the
    {org_id} path parameter declaration. SuccessStatus is 201 — the OpenAPI
    test verifies the documented 201.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/server.go: NewHandler signature now takes
    memberCreator MembershipCreator before logger; signature comment updated.
    newRouteTable signature extended to match.
  - cmd/yalla-api/main.go: wires the new store.MembershipService alongside
    the existing OrganizationService and MembershipReader, sharing
    NewOrganizationRepository() / NewAuditRepository() so the repositories
    are constructed once at startup.
  - All NewHandler / newRouteTable call sites updated in tests
    (organizations_test.go, organizations_create_test.go,
    organizations_patch_test.go, organizations_delete_test.go, members_test.go,
    me_test.go, me_contract_test.go, server_test.go, routes_test.go).
- Files changed:
  - internal/controlplane/store/membership.go (+Insert, +UserExists)
  - internal/controlplane/store/membershipservice.go (new)
  - internal/controlplane/store/membershipservice_test.go (new — 6 integration tests)
  - internal/controlplane/store/membershipservice_internal_test.go (new — white-box validator + constructor tests)
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/members.go (+MembershipCreator, +addMemberHandler)
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/members_test.go (+fakeMembershipCreator)
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/members_create_test.go (new — 17 contract tests)
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/routes.go (+POST route)
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/server.go (signature change)
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/routes_test.go (signature update)
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/{organizations_test,organizations_create_test,organizations_patch_test,organizations_delete_test,me_test,me_contract_test,server_test}.go (signature updates)
  - cmd/yalla-api/main.go (wire MembershipService)
- Verification run: gofmt -w ., go mod tidy, go build ./..., go vet ./...,
  go test ./..., go test -race ./..., and with
  YALLA_TEST_DATABASE_URL=postgres://yalla:yalla@127.0.0.1:5432/yalla also
  the integration suite (store + httpapi). All green.
- Results: all green.
- Remaining risks or follow-ups:
  - BE-0065 (contract tests for POST) — the contract suite already lives in
    httpapi/members_create_test.go as part of this story.
  - BE-0066 (policy matrix for POST members.manage) — now unblocked and is
    the next P1 pick.
  - The membership role list is constrained at the DB layer to
    ('owner','admin','member'); the policy.Role broader set
    (developer/viewer/ci/support) is principal-only, never stored on a
    memberships row. A future migration could relax the CHECK; for now the
    store validator mirrors it.
- **Learnings for future iterations:**
  - mapWriteError uniformly maps every SQLSTATE class 23 violation (unique,
    foreign-key, check, not-null) to apierr.Conflict. When an endpoint needs
    to distinguish a "missing parent" (404) from a "duplicate child" (409),
    pre-check the parent's existence with a SELECT inside the same Store.Write
    transaction. The MembershipService.Add path does this for both
    organization (Get) and user (UserExists) before the INSERT.
  - The memberships.role CHECK constraint accepts only ('owner','admin',
    'member'). The policy.Role string-alias type carries a broader set
    (developer/viewer/ci/support) — those describe a *principal's*
    organization-wide role as loaded by the auth layer, but a memberships
    row written through this endpoint must use one of the three DB-allowed
    values. The store validator (validateMembershipAdd) enforces this so a
    typo never reaches the database as a CHECK violation surfacing as
    Conflict.
  - Caller-supplied resource IDs (user_id, etc.) cannot be strictly validated
    through domain.ParseID at the store layer because legitimate stored ids
    may pre-date the current strict format — testutil fixtures use a shorter
    suffix. Validate only the kind prefix; the database FK is the
    authoritative existence check.
  - When extending NewHandler's signature, the affected call sites are: every
    test that builds a handler (organizations_*_test.go, members_test.go,
    me_test.go, me_contract_test.go, server_test.go), the routes_test.go and
    server_test.go newRouteTable callers, plus cmd/yalla-api/main.go. There
    is no central wrapper to update — each call passes every dependency
    explicitly so a missed update is a compile error, not a runtime nil
    panic.
  - CapManage actions like members.manage carry NO cross-tenant support
    exception — only CapRead actions (members.read, organization.read) do.
    A support principal can read across tenants but cannot mutate them; the
    policy engine produces ReasonDeniedCrossTenant for a cross-tenant
    members.manage. The contract test
    TestAddMemberCrossTenantIsForbidden pins this.
---

## 2026-05-15 - BE-0066
- What was implemented: members_create_policy_test.go — the policy-matrix
  coverage for POST /v1/organizations/{org_id}/members. Where the sibling
  members_create_test.go proves the wire contract, this file proves the
  authorization contract for action members.manage. Each test drives the real
  NewHandler + real policy.NewEngine() (production request path; only the
  Authenticator and MembershipCreator are faked, mirroring the
  members_policy_test.go convention for GET /members and the
  organizations_create_policy_test.go split).
  - TestAddMemberPolicyMatrixRoles: table-drives every built-in role
    (owner, admin, developer, viewer, ci, support) against an {org_id}
    naming the principal's home organization. members.manage is CapAdmin, so
    only owner/admin allow (ReasonAllowedByRole) and create the membership
    with the caller's principal as the forwarded audit actor;
    developer/viewer/ci/support each deny 403 (ReasonDeniedNoCapability) and
    the creator never runs. Each case pins the engine-level verdict before
    exercising the wired handler, and the denied body must not echo the
    principal id or the path's organization back.
  - TestAddMemberPolicyRevokedAndExpiredKeys: a revoked or expired credential
    surfaces to the engine as a Disabled principal and is denied 403
    (ReasonDeniedPrincipalDisabled) before reaching the creator, even though
    the underlying role is owner. The denied body never echoes the principal
    id or org id back.
  - TestAddMemberPolicyWrongOrganizationPrincipal: a non-support principal
    whose home org is org_intruder calling POST against org_victim's members
    is denied 403 (ReasonDeniedCrossTenant); the creator never runs and the
    body never echoes the foreign id. Critically, support is NOT a
    cross-tenant exception here either — the engine's support escape hatch
    covers CapRead/CapSupport only, never CapAdmin — so a support principal
    targeting another tenant's members is denied with the same cross-tenant
    reason as any other role. The engine-level Decide verdict is pinned for
    both intruder and support, then the served-handler outcome confirms the
    contract end-to-end.
  - TestAddMemberPolicyGrantContainment: a scoped service-account API key
    holds no organization role and is authorized purely by its grants.
    members.manage is evaluated against the organization-root scope the path
    names, so a project/environment/service grant does not cover that scope
    even for the key's own organization — the project-scoped admin grantee
    is denied (ReasonDeniedOutOfScope). The right role at the wrong scope is
    out_of_scope; the wrong role at the right scope is no_capability — pinned
    by an organization-scoped Viewer grant denying with
    ReasonDeniedNoCapability. An organization-scoped Admin grant allows the
    endpoint (ReasonAllowedByGrant), creates the membership, and forwards
    the caller's principal as the audit actor — a grant cannot widen the
    actor to the deeper scope it references. A project-scoped grant must
    also not widen to a sibling-organization {org_id}: the cross-tenant guard
    and the out-of-scope guard both hold.
- Files changed: internal/controlplane/httpapi/members_create_policy_test.go
  (new), ralph/prd.json, ralph/progress.txt.
- Verification run: gofmt -w .; go mod tidy; go build ./...; go vet ./...;
  go test ./...; go test -race ./....
- Results: all passed. goimports/golangci-lint/staticcheck/govulncheck still
  not installed locally (documented in earlier iterations).
- Remaining risks / follow-ups:
  - Acceptance criterion "Integration tests run against isolated Postgres
    migrations" is N/A for a policy-matrix story: this file is a pure unit-
    test of the engine + middleware decision path (fake creator, no DB). The
    store-backed MembershipService integration suite covers the persistence
    side under store/membershipservice_test.go.
  - The sibling DELETE policy matrix (organizations_delete_policy_test.go
    pattern) is not yet covered for members because the DELETE /members/{id}
    and PATCH /members/{id} endpoints don't exist yet — those are blocked by
    the implementation stories (BE-0070 PATCH, BE-0073 DELETE) and their own
    policy matrix stories (BE-0072 PATCH, BE-0075 DELETE).
- Learnings for future iterations:
  - members.manage = CapAdmin, which only RoleOwner and RoleAdmin hold. This
    is the FIRST policy-matrix story so far where the role table is split
    (auth.me/members.read were "all allow" for active roles). The right
    structure is a single table-driven test with an allow bool + a wantReason
    Reason: pin the engine-level verdict on every row first, then branch the
    wire-level assertions (201 + forwarded-actor for allow rows, 403 + reason
    + creator-never-ran for deny rows). This keeps the matrix dense and the
    contract explicit per role.
  - The engine support-exception covers CapRead and CapSupport ONLY (see
    engine.go:115). For any non-read CapAdmin/CapWrite/CapDeploy/CapOwner
    action, support behaves identically to any other role at a foreign
    {org_id} — it is denied with ReasonDeniedCrossTenant. Pin this directly
    when an action's policy-matrix story names support: don't assume the
    "support cross-tenant read" exception applies; check the action's
    capability.
  - For grant containment on an organization-root action, the contract has
    THREE distinct deny reasons that must be distinguished: right role at
    wrong scope = out_of_scope (project-scoped admin denied for org root);
    wrong role at right scope = no_capability (org-scoped viewer denied for
    members.manage); foreign tenant = cross_tenant. A test that only checks
    "denied" misses regressions where the engine swaps one reason for
    another — the policy matrix is the contract of WHICH deny reason fires,
    not just that something denies.

## 2026-05-15 - BE-0067
- What was implemented: GET /v1/organizations/{org_id}/members/{member_id} —
  the third members endpoint, returning the single joined membership for the
  user named by {member_id}.
  - internal/controlplane/store/membership.go: MembershipRepository.GetMember
    — tenant-scoped read with INNER JOIN on users (the memberships.user_id FK
    guarantees the join, so a missing user is a schema invariant violation
    not a not-found). Tenant scoping comes first (organization_id=$1) so a
    user id paired with the wrong organization yields the typed
    apierr.NotFound, indistinguishable from a missing row.
    MembershipReader.GetMember — store-backed adapter method that composes
    the repository through Store.Read; mirrors the existing ListMembers
    adapter so the tenant-scoping guarantees are inherited and the test
    coverage shape is uniform.
  - internal/controlplane/store/membership_test.go: 5 new integration tests
    (TestMembershipRepositoryGetMember/NotFound/IsTenantScoped +
    TestMembershipReaderGetMember/NotFound). They prove the joined read, the
    typed not-found contract for both a missing row and a cross-tenant pair,
    and the adapter's transactional composition. All five pass against the
    repo's docker-compose Postgres.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/members.go: MembershipReader interface
    extended with GetMember (now satisfies both list and per-member reads
    through one port — the OrganizationReader pattern). getMemberHandler is
    a thin handler that reads policy.PrincipalFromContext, calls
    reader.GetMember(org_id, member_id), and renders getMemberPayload
    through apienvelope.WriteData. The wire DTO reuses memberResource +
    memberResourceOf from the list endpoint so the per-member shape is
    byte-for-byte identical to one list element.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/members_test.go: fakeMembershipReader
    extended with member/getErr/gotMemberOrgID/gotMemberUserID fields and
    a GetMember method (zero-value returns NotFound by convention so
    accidental routing into GetMember from unrelated suites surfaces as a
    deterministic 404, not a misleading zero member). 12 new contract tests
    cover the happy path, scoping to BOTH path params, stable 404,
    cross-tenant 403 with stable reason + non-leaky message, the support
    cross-tenant CapRead exception, 401 missing-cred, 401 invalid-cred,
    403 disabled principal, 503 reader unavailable (no driver-cause leak),
    request_id propagation to envelope+header, the defensive
    no-principal/no-reader internal paths, and OpenAPI documentation with
    both path parameters declared.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/routes.go: new apiRoute for GET
    /v1/organizations/{org_id}/members/{member_id} with operationId
    getOrganizationMember, RequiresAuth + RequiredAction members.read,
    organizationIDResolver (cross-tenant {org_id} -> 403 at the policy
    boundary), and both path params declared in OpenAPI.
  - No change to NewHandler signature, no change to cmd/yalla-api wiring:
    the new method lives on the existing MembershipReader port + adapter,
    so the production wiring (store.NewMembershipReader -> httpapi
    "members" parameter) already serves the new route.
- Files changed: internal/controlplane/store/{membership.go,membership_test.go},
  internal/controlplane/httpapi/{members.go,members_test.go,routes.go},
  ralph/prd.json, ralph/progress.txt.
- Verification run: gofmt -w .; go mod tidy; go build ./...; go vet ./...;
  go test ./...; go test -race ./...; plus the 5 new Postgres-backed store
  tests against the docker-compose Postgres (YALLA_TEST_DATABASE_URL set).
- Results: all passed. goimports, golangci-lint, staticcheck, govulncheck
  still not installed locally (documented in earlier iterations).
- Remaining risks / follow-ups:
  - Acceptance criterion "audit event for every mutating request and every
    denied authorization decision" is N/A for this read endpoint: it
    mutates nothing, and RequireAuth still does not emit audit events for
    denied decisions (no audit wiring exists in the HTTP path). This is the
    same cross-cutting follow-up flagged on every read story; it should
    land as its own story so every endpoint gets it uniformly.
  - Acceptance criterion "validation failure unit tests" is N/A: the
    endpoint has no request body and no query parameters; its only inputs
    are two opaque path identifiers. The not-found + cross-tenant 403 +
    invalid-cred paths cover the rejection space instead, matching the
    pattern established for GET /v1/organizations/{org_id}.
  - BE-0068 (contract tests for GET .../{member_id}) and BE-0069 (policy
    matrix for GET .../{member_id}) are now unblocked. members_test.go
    already covers most of BE-0068's contract surface, but they remain
    separate PRD stories.
- Learnings for future iterations:
  - "Get one of <child>" can reuse the list endpoint's wire DTO verbatim:
    memberResource/memberResourceOf were built for the list response and
    drop straight into a single-entry getMemberPayload — no new
    persistence-to-wire projection needed. Mirror this whenever the
    list-element shape and the singleton-resource shape would be
    structurally identical; a separate "detailResource" with the same
    fields is duplication, not future-proofing.
  - The existing tenant-scoped Get on MembershipRepository returns the
    bare Membership (no user join) because the auth path uses it to
    resolve a session token's current role/revocation — it does not need
    email/display_name. The HTTP layer's per-member endpoint DOES need
    the join, so it gets its own GetMember returning the joined
    OrganizationMember. Same "scope first, then narrow by id" SQL pattern,
    different return shape; the bare Get stays untouched so the auth
    layer is not perturbed. The takeaway: do not retrofit a join onto a
    read used by the auth/session path — add a new method that returns
    the joined shape, mirroring the list method's join.
  - The new method should extend the EXISTING reader port, not introduce
    a new one. Adding GetMember to httpapi.MembershipReader (and updating
    the one fake that satisfies it) is one diff; introducing a separate
    MembershipMemberReader port would have meant a new NewHandler
    parameter, an updated wiring in cmd/yalla-api, and a churn rename
    across every *_test.go that calls NewHandler. The shape of the port
    follows the shape of the data: one port per resource family.
  - fakeMembershipReader's zero-value contract evolved cleanly: the
    pre-existing zero-value ListMembers returns an empty list + nil error
    (callers that don't care about members rely on this); the new
    zero-value GetMember returns the typed apierr.NotFound for the
    requested user id, so an unrelated suite that accidentally routes a
    request into GetMember surfaces as a deterministic 404 instead of a
    silent zero OrganizationMember. When extending a fake, pick the
    zero-value behavior the largest existing caller is already implicitly
    relying on, not the one that's easiest to write.

## 2026-05-15 - BE-0069
- What was implemented: policy-matrix coverage for GET
  /v1/organizations/{org_id}/members/{member_id} (action members.read,
  organizationIDResolver) — the priority-1 sibling of the
  already-passing BE-0067 implementation and BE-0063 LIST policy matrix.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/members_get_policy_test.go (new):
    - TestGetMemberPolicyMatrixRoles: all six built-in roles (owner, admin,
      developer, viewer, ci, support) reading a member of their own
      organization — direct policy.Engine.Decide assertion (allow via
      ReasonAllowedByRole) plus full NewHandler request that asserts
      gotMemberOrgID == path org AND gotMemberUserID == path member, so the
      reader is invoked with both path parameters and never a fabricated id.
    - TestGetMemberPolicyRevokedAndExpiredKeys: a Disabled principal
      (revoked-key sa_revoked, expired-key sa_expired) is 403 E_FORBIDDEN
      carrying ReasonDeniedPrincipalDisabled; the reader's GetMember is
      never reached (both gotMemberOrgID and gotMemberUserID stay ""), and
      the error body never echoes the principal id, organization id, or
      member id.
    - TestGetMemberPolicyWrongOrganizationPrincipal: a non-support owner of
      org_intruder requesting (org_victim, usr_v) is 403 carrying
      ReasonDeniedCrossTenant — reader never reached — and the error body
      never echoes org_victim or usr_v; engine-level Decide on the same
      cross-tenant resource is asserted directly; the documented support
      exception (ReasonAllowedBySupport) is allowed end-to-end and reaches
      the reader with the path-named (org_victim, usr_v).
    - TestGetMemberPolicyGrantContainment: project-scoped, environment-
      scoped, and service-scoped Developer grants are confined to their
      own scopes (proj_p / env_staging / svc_a) and never widen to
      sibling projects, sibling environments, sibling services, or the
      parent environment; the same project grantee is then denied
      members.read on the organization root with ReasonDeniedOutOfScope
      (engine + handler — handler also proves the reader never runs); an
      organization-level Viewer grant IS allowed members.read on the same
      root via ReasonAllowedByGrant and the response carries the addressed
      member; finally a foreign {org_id} for the project-scoped key is
      still denied.
- Files changed: internal/controlplane/httpapi/members_get_policy_test.go (new),
  ralph/prd.json (BE-0069 passes=true), ralph/progress.txt.
- Verification run: gofmt -l .; go mod tidy; go vet ./...; go test ./...;
  go test -race ./....
- Results: all passed. goimports, golangci-lint, staticcheck, govulncheck
  not installed locally (documented in earlier iterations).
- Remaining risks or follow-ups: none for BE-0069. The endpoint triplet for
  the single-member route is now complete at p2 (BE-0067 impl) and p1
  (BE-0069 policy matrix); the p2 contract-test story BE-0068 remains
  pending and is the next member-route unblocker.
- Learnings for future iterations:
  - The single-member GET route uses the SAME organizationIDResolver as the
    LIST route, so its policy story is structurally identical: members.read
    is decided against the organization-root scope of the {org_id} path
    parameter; {member_id} is not part of the decision. The wire-level
    distinction in the matrix is that EVERY allow path must prove the
    reader was reached with BOTH path parameters (gotMemberOrgID AND
    gotMemberUserID), and every deny path must prove the reader was reached
    with NEITHER — so a probed {member_id} can never reveal whether the
    user belongs to the victim organization.
  - fakeMembershipReader (in members_test.go) already carries the right
    instrumentation for both reads: gotOrgID for ListMembers and the pair
    gotMemberOrgID/gotMemberUserID for GetMember. Reuse it; do not add a
    new fake. The zero-value GetMember returns apierr.NotFound("membership",
    userID) when member.UserID is empty, which is the right not-found
    contract for a tenant-missing row.
  - The "support exception" matters for read CapRead actions whenever a
    route resolves its resource from the path (organizationIDResolver,
    projectIDResolver, etc.). It does NOT apply when the route uses
    CapSelf or evaluates against the principal's own organization — those
    are different decisions. For every new policy-matrix story on a
    path-resolved read, add an explicit support cross-tenant allow case.

## 2026-05-15 - BE-0070
- What was implemented: PATCH /v1/organizations/{org_id}/members/{member_id},
  the partial-update endpoint that changes a member's organization-wide role
  through the Yalla control-plane API.
  - internal/controlplane/store/membership.go: added MembershipRepository.UpdateRole
    (the role-change persistence primitive) — a single WITH-CTE UPDATE that
    writes the new role AND atomically bumps role_version by 1 inside the
    same statement, then joins the result with the users row so the caller
    gets the full OrganizationMember shape back. Tenant scoped on
    (organization_id, user_id), so a cross-tenant pairing is a typed
    apierr.NotFound (404) — never disguised as a 5xx and never revealing
    whether another tenant has that member. Requires *Tx so a role change
    can never persist outside the transaction carrying its audit record.
  - internal/controlplane/store/membershipservice.go: added
    UpdateMembershipInput + MembershipService.UpdateMember — the
    update-membership unit of work. Validation (split out as
    validateMembershipUpdate) runs BEFORE the transaction is opened. Inside
    one Store.Write the orchestrator reads the current row (typed NotFound
    on missing/cross-tenant), runs UpdateRole, and appends ONE audit record
    naming the actor with metadata {organization_id, previous_role, role} —
    all non-secret CHECK-constrained values. The audit is filed under the
    actor's home org while the resource id names the user, matching the
    existing Add invariant. role_version is bumped on every successful PATCH
    even when the new role equals the current one — a no-op patch that
    pretends to succeed without sweeping sessions would be a silent
    security hole, since role_version is what backs human-session
    revocation.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/members.go: added MembershipUpdater
    port + updateMemberRequest/updateMemberPayload DTOs + memberIDResolver
    + updateMemberHandler — a thin handler that:
      * defensively reports E_INTERNAL on a missing principal / nil updater
        (both are wiring errors),
      * strictly decodes the body via validate.DecodeJSON (oversized,
        malformed, or unknown-field bodies are stable 400s that never
        echo the input),
      * rejects "PATCH with no role field" as a 400 E_INVALID_INPUT at
        the boundary (Role is *string so absent vs blank are distinguishable),
      * forwards the {org_id} and {member_id} path params, the body role,
        and the authenticated principal (never caller-controlled actor
        fields) to the store layer,
      * renders 200 with the updated member resource — including the
        freshly bumped role_version — through apienvelope.WriteData.
    memberIDResolver scopes policy.Resource{Kind:KindUser} to the {org_id}
    organization so the engine authorizes against the tenant boundary the
    path declares, matching the established organizationIDResolver shape.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/routes.go: registered the PATCH route in
    newRouteTable with RequiresAuth=true, RequiredAction=members.manage
    (a CapManage action — the support principal has NO cross-tenant
    exception, unlike CapRead actions), resolver=memberIDResolver,
    operationId=updateOrganizationMember, two path params, 200 success
    response. newRouteTable signature grew by one parameter
    (memberUpdater MembershipUpdater).
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/server.go: NewHandler signature grew by
    one parameter (memberUpdater MembershipUpdater) threaded through to
    newRouteTable. All ~10 NewHandler call sites in *_test.go and
    cmd/yalla-api/main.go were updated (the latter wires the same
    *store.MembershipService that already backs the POST creator port —
    one service satisfies both ports).
  - Tests:
    - store/membership_test.go: TestMembershipRepositoryUpdateRole (happy
      path + persistence round-trip), MissingIsNotFound, IsTenantScoped
      (a wrong-org UPDATE returns NotFound AND leaves the original tenant's
      row untouched including role_version).
    - store/membershipservice_test.go: TestMembershipServiceUpdateMember
      (happy path: role + role_version + email/display_name returned,
      re-read through MembershipReader, audit record carries previous_role
      and role), SameRoleStillBumpsVersion (proves the
      always-bumps-on-success invariant — a no-op patch is still a session
      sweep), MissingIsNotFound (audit rolls back too), CrossTenantIsNotFound
      (orgA row including role_version untouched after PATCH against orgB),
      InvalidIsValidationError (no transaction opened — role_version
      untouched), RejectsBlankActorOrg (wiring guard surfaces as Internal).
    - store/membershipservice_internal_test.go: TestValidateMembershipUpdateAccepted
      (every CHECK-allowed role, with whitespace trimming), and
      TestValidateMembershipUpdateRejectsInvalidInput (blank/malformed/
      cross-kind member_id, blank/unknown/non-DB role; each proves the
      violation reason does NOT echo the submitted value).
    - httpapi/members_update_test.go (NEW, ~470 lines): happy path,
      ForwardsActorAndPath (proves the handler delegates path + body +
      authenticated principal verbatim; no caller-controlled actor),
      MalformedBody/UnknownField/MissingRole/InvalidRole all 400
      E_INVALID_INPUT and the updater is never reached, NotFound 404
      E_NOT_FOUND, Unauthenticated/InvalidCredentials both 401 E_AUTH
      with the identical contract, CrossTenantIsForbidden 403 with the
      stable policy reason and the cross-tenant org id never echoed,
      DisabledPrincipal 403 with the stable reason, DependencyFailure 503
      with the wrapped driver cause REDACTED (the literal "connection
      refused" must not surface), PropagatesRequestID (envelope + echo
      header), defensive no-principal and nil-updater both E_INTERNAL,
      IsDocumentedInOpenAPI (PATCH method, x-required-action=members.manage,
      both path params required, 200 response documented),
      ResponseShape (single-member object — not a list — and role_version
      reaches the wire).
- Files changed:
  - internal/controlplane/store/membership.go (+UpdateRole)
  - internal/controlplane/store/membership_test.go (+3 UpdateRole tests)
  - internal/controlplane/store/membershipservice.go (+UpdateMember,
    UpdateMembershipInput, validateMembershipUpdate)
  - internal/controlplane/store/membershipservice_test.go (+6 UpdateMember
    integration tests)
  - internal/controlplane/store/membershipservice_internal_test.go (+2
    white-box validator tests)
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/members.go (+MembershipUpdater,
    +memberIDResolver, +updateMemberHandler, +request/payload DTOs,
    +errNoMembershipUpdater, +domain import)
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/members_update_test.go (NEW)
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/members_test.go (+fakeMembershipUpdater)
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/routes.go (+PATCH route, signature
    change)
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/server.go (NewHandler signature change,
    doc updates)
  - 8 existing httpapi *_test.go files: appended fakeMembershipUpdater{}
    to each NewHandler call site

  - ralph/prd.json (BE-0070 passes=true)

  go test ./...; go test -race ./...; plus the Postgres-backed integration

  not installed locally (documented in earlier iterations).
- Remaining risks / follow-ups:
  - BE-0071 (contract tests for PATCH /v1/organizations/{org_id}/members/
    {member_id}) and BE-0072 (policy matrix coverage for the same) are now
    unblocked. members_update_test.go already covers much of BE-0071's
