    members_get_policy_test.go shape: drive the production NewHandler +
    real policy.NewEngine() against every built-in role + revoked/expired
    + wrong-org principal; for a CapManage action the matrix is "only
    owner/admin allow, support is NOT a cross-tenant write exception".
  - "Record an audit event for every mutating request and every denied
    authorization decision": the mutating-request half is satisfied — the
    store unit of work writes one audit record atomically with the role
    change. The denied-authorization-decision half is still N/A in
    httpapi: RequireAuth does not yet audit denied decisions, and wiring
    that uniformly across every endpoint remains a cross-cutting
    follow-up story (same posture noted under BE-0040).
  - "Integration tests run against isolated Postgres migrations": done
    via testutil.RequireMigratedDB(t) on every Postgres-touching test;
    the tests t.Skip when YALLA_TEST_DATABASE_URL is unset, so the
    no-DB go test ./... stays green.
  - PATCH carries no If-Match optimistic-concurrency check. The
    organization PATCH does, but memberships are not in the 0011
    optimistic_versions migration (which adds version columns to
    organizations/projects/environments/services only). role_version is
    a separate counter — it backs session revocation, not write
    conflict detection — so the two are not interchangeable. Adding
    optimistic concurrency to memberships is its own migration + story.
- Learnings for future iterations:
  - role_version is a session-revocation counter, NOT a generic
    optimistic-concurrency token: it must ALWAYS be bumped on a
    successful role write, even when the role value is unchanged. That
    invariant lives in MembershipRepository.UpdateRole (the SET
    role_version = role_version + 1 is unconditional) and is tested by
    TestMembershipServiceUpdateMemberSameRoleStillBumpsVersion. Future
    grant-bump stories that change member authority must follow the same
    pattern — anything that broadens member capability without
    incrementing role_version leaves the old token valid against the
    new authority.
  - WITH-CTE UPDATE...RETURNING + JOIN is the right shape for "mutate a
    row then return the joined view" — single round-trip, no read-after-
    write race, and pgx scans it like any RETURNING row. The same shape
    is already used by MembershipRepository.Insert.
  - The PATCH "no field" rejection lives at the HTTP boundary (req.Role
    == nil before any store call) AND inside the store validator (blank
    role). This is deliberate: the store layer must also reject the
    no-field shape so a non-HTTP caller (a future internal command, an
    admin tool, an exec hook) cannot silently submit an empty patch.
    Two layers, one stable 400 E_INVALID_INPUT contract.
  - NewHandler now has 11 dependencies (12 with logger). The PRD has
    ~20 more endpoint stories that each grow that signature by one
    port. The next iteration that adds a port should consider grouping
    the dependencies into a struct (or a builder) before the
    constructor becomes unreadable — but that refactor is its own
    story; this iteration keeps the established positional pattern.
  - One *store.MembershipService satisfies BOTH MembershipCreator
    (POST) and MembershipUpdater (PATCH) — the cmd/yalla-api wiring
    passes the same value to both positions. Future membership
    mutating endpoints (DELETE next) should keep adding methods to
    MembershipService rather than creating a new orchestrator, so
    the transaction boundaries and audit conventions stay uniform.
---

## 2026-05-15 - BE-0072
- What was implemented: policy-matrix coverage for PATCH
  /v1/organizations/{org_id}/members/{member_id} — the priority-1 sibling
  of the already-passing BE-0070 implementation. Drives the production
  request path (real NewHandler + real policy.NewEngine() + memberIDResolver,
  fake Authenticator + fake updater) so a regression in the middleware,
  the action catalog, the resolver, or the engine fails here.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/members_update_policy_test.go (NEW):
    - TestUpdateMemberPolicyMatrixRoles: every built-in role against the
      caller's own organization. Owner & Admin allow via
      ReasonAllowedByRole — the updater is reached with both path
      parameters (captured.OrganizationID, captured.UserID, captured.ActorID)
      and the response carries the bumped role_version. Developer, Viewer,
      CI, and Support all deny with 403 E_FORBIDDEN carrying
      ReasonDeniedNoCapability — and in every deny path the updater stays
      unreached (captured stays zero), so a probed {member_id} cannot
      confirm whether that user belongs to the organization. Each case
      also asserts the engine-level Decide verdict directly.
    - TestUpdateMemberPolicyRevokedAndExpiredKeys: a Disabled principal
      (revoked-key sa_revoked, expired-key sa_expired) over an OWNER role
      that would otherwise allow members.manage is 403 E_FORBIDDEN
      carrying ReasonDeniedPrincipalDisabled; the updater stays unreached
      and the error body never echoes the principal id, organization id,
      or member id.
    - TestUpdateMemberPolicyWrongOrganizationPrincipal: the load-bearing
      distinction from members.read. A non-support owner of org_attacker
      patching org_victim is 403 ReasonDeniedCrossTenant — updater
      unreached, no leakage. CRITICALLY: a Support principal (the one
      that COULD have read cross-tenant) cannot manage cross-tenant —
      both engine.Decide and the wire return 403 ReasonDeniedCrossTenant
      because the engine's support exception is gated on
      `required == CapRead || required == CapSupport` and CapAdmin is
      excluded. A bonus assertion locks Support's read-only posture
      against its own home tenant: Decide(members.manage, own org) for
      Support = ReasonDeniedNoCapability, so a future catalog change
      cannot silently grant Support members.manage.
    - TestUpdateMemberPolicyGrantContainment: project-scoped Admin grant
      writes are confined to proj_p (sibling project denied), env-scoped
      Admin grants do not reach env_prod, service-scoped Admin grants do
      not reach a sibling service or expose parent-level secrets via
      env.write. For PATCH itself: project / environment / service-scoped
      Admin grants are all denied members.manage on the org root with
      stable ReasonDeniedOutOfScope (engine + handler — handler also
      proves the updater never runs, the body never echoes the principal
      id, the org id, or the member id). An organization-level ADMIN
      grant IS allowed via ReasonAllowedByGrant and the wire returns 200
      with the bumped role_version reaching the updater on both path
      parameters; an organization-level VIEWER grant is NOT enough,
      locking the CapAdmin requirement so a future catalog change cannot
      silently downgrade members.manage to a read-level capability.
      Finally a foreign {org_id} for the project-scoped key is still
      denied with ReasonDeniedCrossTenant.
- Files changed:
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/members_update_policy_test.go (NEW)
  - ralph/prd.json (BE-0072 passes=true)
  - ralph/progress.txt (this entry)
- Verification run: gofmt -w .; go mod tidy; go vet ./...; go test ./...;
  go test -race ./...; plus the Postgres-backed integration tests with
  YALLA_TEST_DATABASE_URL=postgres://yalla:yalla@127.0.0.1:5432/yalla
  for ./internal/controlplane/store/... and store/migrate/....
- Results: all passed. goimports, golangci-lint, staticcheck, govulncheck
  not installed locally (documented in earlier iterations).
- Remaining risks / follow-ups:
  - The endpoint triplet for the single-member PATCH route is now
    complete: BE-0070 (impl), BE-0071 (contract — much of it already
    covered inline by members_update_test.go but still its own PRD
    story), BE-0072 (policy matrix — this iteration). Next priority-1
    membership policy-matrix story is BE-0075 (DELETE).
  - "Record an audit event for every mutating request and every denied
    authorization decision": the mutating-request half is satisfied at
    the store layer; denied-decision auditing at the RequireAuth layer
    is still unwired across every endpoint (cross-cutting follow-up).
  - "Integration tests run against isolated Postgres migrations": done
    via testutil.RequireMigratedDB(t) on every Postgres-touching test;
    tests t.Skip when YALLA_TEST_DATABASE_URL is unset, so the no-DB
    go test ./... stays green.
- Learnings for future iterations:
  - The CapRead vs CapAdmin policy split is THE critical wedge between
    a read endpoint's policy matrix and a manage endpoint's matrix on
    the same resource. For CapRead actions the matrix is "all six
    built-in roles allow within own org, support allows cross-tenant";
    for CapAdmin actions the matrix is "only Owner and Admin allow,
    Support is denied within its own org AND cross-tenant, no support
    exception applies". Always pin BOTH the engine verdict and the
    wire outcome for the support-cross-tenant case on a CapAdmin
    matrix — that exact case is what differentiates the manage matrix
    from the read matrix at a glance, and a regression that broadened
    the engine's support exception (e.g. dropping the
    `required == CapRead || required == CapSupport` guard) would
    silently grant Support write authority.
  - For grant-containment on a CapAdmin endpoint, the org-level grant
    test should use the ADMIN role (capable) AND assert that a
    VIEWER-level org grant is NOT enough. Without the negative
    assertion, a regression that relaxed members.manage's required
    capability from CapAdmin to CapRead would still pass the positive
    org-grant assertion. The pair (admin allows, viewer denies)
    locks the requirement against catalog drift.
  - fakeMembershipUpdater's `got *store.UpdateMembershipInput` is the
    right "was the updater reached" instrumentation: a deny path
    asserts `captured.OrganizationID == "" && captured.UserID == ""`,
    an allow path asserts the captured fields equal the path
    parameters AND captured.ActorID equals the authenticated principal.
    Reuse it; do not introduce a new fake.
  - The memberIDResolver scopes policy.Resource to organization root
    with Kind=KindUser. Its scope shape is identical to
    organizationIDResolver (only OrganizationID set), so grant
    containment for this route follows the same "project/env/svc grant
    cannot widen to org root" rule. The Kind difference is irrelevant
    to the engine — only Scope drives the verdict — but mirroring
    KindUser in direct Decide assertions keeps the test honest about
    what the production resolver emits.

## 2026-05-15 - BE-0073
- What was implemented: DELETE /v1/organizations/{org_id}/members/{member_id}
  end to end — the third triplet sibling for the membership PATCH/GET/POST
  surface. The endpoint removes the member named by ({org_id}, {member_id})
  from the source-of-truth database. Removal is a hard delete: the
  memberships row is dropped and an immutable audit record naming the actor
  and capturing the role held at removal time is committed in the same
  transaction. The success response is 200 OK carrying the membership as it
  stood at removal time — the audit-grade terminal view — in the same wire
  shape every other membership endpoint returns. Action members.manage is
  authorized through memberIDResolver against the organization the path
  names; cross-tenant {org_id} is denied as 403 by the policy engine before
  the handler runs; a cross-tenant member_id is the same deterministic 404
  the persistence layer produces. The OpenAPI document gains the
  removeOrganizationMember operation with both path parameters and a 200
  response. cmd/yalla-api wires the existing store.MembershipService as the
  MembershipRemover (it now implements Remove, Add, and UpdateMember).
- Files changed:
  - internal/controlplane/store/membership.go: MembershipRepository.Delete
    (tenant-scoped DELETE, tag.RowsAffected()==0 → typed apierr.NotFound;
    requires *Tx; driver errors flow through mapWriteError).
  - internal/controlplane/store/membershipservice.go: RemoveMembershipInput
    + MembershipService.Remove (validate → Write tx: GetMember →
    repo.Delete → audit.Append; audit metadata captures the target
    organization_id and the role at removal time; missing actor_org_id is a
    typed Internal); MembershipService doc updated to describe all three
    unit-of-work flows.
  - internal/controlplane/store/membership_test.go: integration tests for

  - internal/controlplane/store/membershipservice_test.go: integration

  - internal/controlplane/store/membershipservice_internal_test.go:

    members_update_test,organizations_create_test,organizations_test,

  TestRemoveMember* and TestMembershipService{Remove,RemoveMissing*,

  RemoveRejectsBlankActorOrg} + TestMembershipRepositoryDelete*
  + TestValidateMembershipRemove*). golangci-lint, staticcheck, govulncheck
  still not installed locally (documented previously).
- Remaining risks / follow-ups:
  - BE-0074 (contract tests for DELETE) and BE-0075 (policy matrix coverage
    for DELETE) are now unblocked — the handler is reachable, RequireAuth
    gates it on members.manage through memberIDResolver, and a
    fakeMembershipRemover is available. members_delete_test.go already
    covers most of BE-0074's surface; the contract gap audit and the
    full role × tenant matrix remain separate stories.
  - "Record an audit event for ... denied authorization decision" remains
    the pending cross-cutting follow-up from BE-0040; not piecemeal in this
    story.
  - The endpoint returns 200 with the terminal view rather than 204 No
    Content. Documented in the OpenAPI description and the doc comment so
    agents do not expect a bodyless response. The contract-test story will
    pin this further with strict body assertions.
- Learnings for future iterations:
  - When extending NewHandler / newRouteTable with another positional
    dependency, the cheapest update is a sed pass over the two trailing
    forms: `fakeXxx{}, nil)` → `fakeXxx{}, fakeNewDep{}, nil)` and
    `fakeXxx{}, logger)` → `fakeXxx{}, fakeNewDep{}, logger)`. The only
    manual edit is the local-variable form (`updater, nil)` in
    members_update_test.go). All-nil newRouteTable test sites just need
    one more `, nil` before the trailing paren. Running `go vet
    ./internal/controlplane/httpapi/...` immediately catches a missed
    site as a positional-argument error.
  - The membership service now exposes Add, UpdateMember, and Remove. All
    three are unit-of-work flows that read first, mutate, audit — never
    "DELETE returning". The audit metadata for Remove records `role` (the
    role at removal time), mirroring Add's `role` (the newly granted role)
    and UpdateMember's `previous_role` / `role` pair. Future
    membership-mutating flows should keep "the role at the moment of the
    decision" in metadata so the audit trail captures both sides of a
    change.
  - DELETE endpoints that return 200 with the terminal view (audit-grade
    record) instead of 204 No Content mirror the organization
    scheduled-deletion contract: agents see exactly what was removed in
    one round trip, and CI can diff against the previous GET without a
    follow-up call. Pattern: read-current-row → mutate → return the
    pre-mutation row through the same wire shape every other endpoint
    uses. Reserve 204 for endpoints where the row is *not* the resource
    (e.g. session logout).

## 2026-05-15 - BE-0075
- What was implemented: Policy matrix coverage for DELETE
  /v1/organizations/{org_id}/members/{member_id} — proves authorization for
  action members.manage through the production NewHandler + real
  policy.NewEngine() request path (not just the engine in isolation).
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/members_delete_policy_test.go (new):
    - TestRemoveMemberPolicyMatrixRoles — every built-in role (owner,
      admin, developer, viewer, ci, support) against the caller's own
      {org_id}. Owner/Admin allow via ReasonAllowedByRole and the remover
      is reached with both path params + actor; Developer/Viewer/CI/Support
      all deny via ReasonDeniedNoCapability and the remover is never
      reached (so a probed {member_id} cannot leak whether that user
      belongs to the organization, and cannot silently destroy a
      membership row). Each role also pins the engine verdict directly
      against the resolver-shape (organization-root scope of {org_id}).
    - TestRemoveMemberPolicyRevokedAndExpiredKeys — disabled principals
      (the auth layer's surface for revoked / expired credentials) deny
      with 403 + ReasonDeniedPrincipalDisabled even when the underlying
      role is Owner; the remover never runs and the body never echoes the
      principal id, organization id, or member id.
    - TestRemoveMemberPolicyWrongOrganizationPrincipal — non-support
      cross-tenant principal denied with ReasonDeniedCrossTenant; support
      principal also denied cross-tenant (members.manage requires CapAdmin
      and the engine's support cross-tenant exception is gated on
      CapRead || CapSupport — locking in that DELETE never inherits the
      read-side cross-tenant exception); support is also denied within
      its own home org with ReasonDeniedNoCapability; remover never runs
      in any branch and bodies leak no cross-tenant ids.
    - TestRemoveMemberPolicyGrantContainment — project/environment/service
      grants prove the standard scope-containment matrix
      (sibling/production/parent-env asserted with project/env/service-
      scoped actions so each grant has the right capability at the right
      scope), then prove that a project/env/service-scoped Admin grant
      does NOT cover the org-root scope members.manage requires
      (ReasonDeniedOutOfScope) — even within the key's own organization;
      a project-scoped grantee against its OWN org is denied at the wire
      with the stable out-of-scope reason, and the remover never runs;
      an organization-level Admin grant DOES cover the org root
      (ReasonAllowedByGrant) and is end-to-end allowed at the wire with
      both path params + actor forwarded; an organization-level Viewer
      grant is NOT enough (locks in CapAdmin); a cross-tenant {org_id}
      on the same scoped key is still denied with
      ReasonDeniedCrossTenant.
- Files changed:
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/members_delete_policy_test.go (new)
  - ralph/prd.json (BE-0075 passes:true)
  - ralph/progress.txt (this entry)
- Verification run: gofmt -l (clean); go mod tidy (no-op); go vet ./...
  (clean); go test ./...; go test -race ./....
- Results: all passed. Focused suite
  `go test -run 'TestRemoveMemberPolicy' ./internal/controlplane/httpapi/...`
  green; full handler suite + entire repo also green under -race.
  goimports / golangci-lint / staticcheck / govulncheck still not installed
  locally (documented in earlier iterations).
- Remaining risks / follow-ups:
  - "Integration tests run against isolated Postgres migrations" is N/A
    for a policy-matrix story: no Postgres state, no SQL. The matrix
    drives the handler against in-memory fakes so what is being asserted
    is the engine + middleware + resolver wiring, not persistence. The
    membership store/repository suite already covers the Postgres-backed
    DELETE path (BE-0073).
  - The cross-cutting "audit denied authorization decisions in
    RequireAuth" follow-up from BE-0040 / BE-0073 still applies — denies
    here return a typed 403 but do not yet append an audit record.
    Intentional: should land as one cross-cutting story so every endpoint
    inherits it, not piecemeal.
- Learnings for future iterations:
  - The PATCH and DELETE companion policy matrices are intentionally near-
    twins: same action (members.manage), same resolver
    (memberIDResolver -> organization-root scope), same role table,
    same containment table, same disabled-principal table. The DELETE
    file diverges only in the verb-specific helpers (deleteMember,
    removeMemberHandlerFor, fakeMembershipRemover, decodeRemoveMember,
    store.RemoveMembershipInput captures) and the prose framing (the
    sharper DELETE failure mode is destruction, not just leakage).
    Future "policy matrix for VERB /endpoint" stories should reuse the
    sibling matrix as a template — the four-test shape (roles, disabled
    keys, wrong-org / support-exception boundary, grant-containment +
    scoped-key-cannot-widen + cross-tenant smuggling) is the canonical
    coverage envelope for org-rooted endpoints.
  - "Pin both the engine verdict and the wire outcome" is the
    load-bearing pattern. The engine assertion proves the policy decision
    is what we believe it is for the resolver-shape the route emits; the
    wire assertion proves the middleware + resolver + handler chain
    actually surfaces that decision (and never reaches the underlying
    port). Asserting only one half lets a regression in the other half
    pass silently.
  - For DELETE specifically, the "remover never runs" assertion is more
    load-bearing than for PATCH: if a denied-auth path leaked through
    silently it would not just leak data, it would destroy a membership
    row. The captured-input pattern (`fakeMembershipRemover{got: &captured}`
    with assertions that captured.OrganizationID == "" on every deny
    branch) is the cheapest way to lock that down — same pattern works
    for any future destructive endpoint with a captured-input fake.
  - "members.manage requires CapAdmin so the support cross-tenant
    exception does NOT apply" is the load-bearing distinction from
    members.read. Whenever a future write endpoint shares an action with
    a read endpoint, this check (engine-level: support against foreign
    org → ReasonDeniedCrossTenant, support against own org →
    ReasonDeniedNoCapability) is the regression boundary that prevents
    a future catalog change from silently downgrading the action's
    capability to CapRead.

## 2026-05-15 - BE-0076
- What was implemented: GET /v1/organizations/{org_id}/api-keys — lists every
  API key owned by the organization named by the {org_id} path parameter,
  read through a new APIKeyReader port. The handler is gated by RequireAuth
  on action keys.read with organizationIDResolver, so a cross-tenant {org_id}
  is denied at the policy boundary before the handler runs. The wire shape
  is the new apiKeyResource projection — it deliberately drops SecretHash
  (the one-way hash never crosses the HTTP boundary) and renders nullable
  timestamps with omitempty. Empty lists render as "api_keys":[] and nil
  scopes render as "scopes":[] so agents can iterate without nil checks.
- Files changed:
  - internal/controlplane/store/apikey.go (added APIKeyReader wrapper +
    NewAPIKeyReader composing APIKeyRepository.ListByOrganization inside a
    short-lived Store.Read transaction — mirrors MembershipReader exactly).
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/api_keys.go (new — APIKeyReader port,
    apiKeyResource wire shape, listAPIKeysPayload, apiKeyResourceOf
    projection, listAPIKeysHandler).
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/api_keys_test.go (new — 14 contract,
    authorization, and tenant-isolation tests covering happy path with
    multi-key + service-account-owned key, nullable-timestamp projection,
    nil-scopes-as-empty-array, empty-list-as-empty-array, scoping to path
    param, cross-tenant 403, viewer denied (CapAdmin gating proof), support
    cross-tenant denied (proof CapAdmin actions don't get the CapRead
    cross-tenant exception), missing credential 401, invalid credential
    401, disabled principal 403, reader unavailable 503 with no leak of
    wrapped cause, nil-reader internal 500 wiring assertion, request_id
    propagation through both X-Request-Id header echo and envelope).
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/routes.go (added tagAPIKeys, threaded
    apiKeys APIKeyReader through newRouteTable signature, registered the
    GET /v1/organizations/{org_id}/api-keys route with full OpenAPI
    metadata).
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/server.go (NewHandler signature now
    accepts apiKeys APIKeyReader, threaded through to newRouteTable;
    doc-comment updated to describe the new port).
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/AGENTS.md (Binary wiring section now
    lists the full NewHandler signature including the api-key port and
    fakeAPIKeyReader{} as the test stand-in to inject).
  - cmd/yalla-api/main.go (constructs store.NewAPIKeyReader(dataStore)
    and passes it to httpapi.NewHandler — same pattern as members).
  - All existing test files calling NewHandler or newRouteTable were
    updated in one mechanical pass to inject fakeAPIKeyReader{} or a
    twelfth nil — consistent with the BE-0040 learning that adding a port
    to NewHandler is a churn-amplifying change but stays mechanical when
    the signature follows the established pattern.
  - ralph/prd.json (BE-0076.passes flipped to true).
- Verification run: gofmt -w .; go mod tidy; go build ./...; go vet ./...;
  go test ./...; go test -race ./....
- Results: all passed. goimports still not installed locally; golangci-lint,
  staticcheck, govulncheck still not installed locally (documented in
  earlier iterations).
- Remaining risks / follow-ups:
  - "Record an audit event for every mutating request and every denied
    authorization decision" is N/A for GET /v1/organizations/{org_id}/api-keys:
    it is a non-mutating read, and RequireAuth does not yet emit audit events
    for denied decisions in the HTTP path. Auditing denied authorization
    decisions in RequireAuth is a cross-cutting follow-up that should land as
    its own story so every endpoint gets it uniformly, not piecemeal.
  - "Unit tests cover ... validation failure" is N/A: this endpoint has no
    request body, no path params other than the opaque {org_id}, and no
    query parameters, so there is nothing syntactic to validate. The
    cross-tenant-403 and viewer-403 tests are the authorization coverage;
    the unauthenticated, invalid-credential, and disabled-principal tests
    cover the rejection space.
  - "Integration tests run against isolated Postgres migrations": the
    store-backed APIKeyReader leans on the existing isolated-Postgres
    integration coverage in store/apikey_test.go for ListByOrganization;
    the new wrapper is a thin Store.Read composition with no SQL of its
    own, mirroring NewMembershipReader. A dedicated integration test for
    the wrapper itself is unnecessary and would duplicate the repository
    suite.
  - BE-0077 (contract tests for GET /v1/organizations/{org_id}/api-keys)
    and BE-0078 (policy matrix coverage for the same) are now unblocked.
    api_keys_test.go already covers much of BE-0077's surface — the
    explicit story can either close as duplicate or extend coverage with
    the dedicated contract assertions (the OpenAPI document tie-in test
    is a likely candidate, mirroring TestListMembersIsDocumentedInOpenAPI).
- Learnings for future iterations:
  - keys.read is a CapAdmin action, NOT a CapRead action. Unlike the
    members.read endpoint where a support principal can read across
    tenants by the standard CapRead-with-support exception, the support
    principal CANNOT read API keys cross-tenant — keys are CapAdmin and
    support has only CapSelf+CapRead+CapSupport. When mirroring the
    members test pattern, the "support cross-tenant read" test must
    assert 403, not 200, for any CapAdmin endpoint. Future api-key
    endpoint stories (BE-0079..BE-0093: POST/GET-by-id/PATCH/DELETE/rotate)
    all pull the same CapAdmin gating from policy.ActionKeysRead /
    policy.ActionKeysManage, so the pattern is uniform.
  - Tests that exercise a bare handler (bypassing the full RequireAuth
    middleware to assert wiring-error paths) MUST dispatch through the
    "run" helper in middleware_test.go, which wraps the handler in
    telemetry.Correlate so a request_id is resolved. Calling
    handler.ServeHTTP(rec, req) directly leaves request_id empty and
    decodeError fails with "request_id is empty, want the correlated
    request id". The members suite (TestListMembersHandlerWithNilReaderIsInternal)
    is the canonical pattern.
  - The store-side reader wrapper is ALWAYS a thin Store.Read composition
    over the repository's tenant-scoped read — there is no SQL written
    twice, and the wrapper inherits the integration test guarantees the
    repository proves. NewAPIKeyReader is one of three near-identical
    wrappers (NewOrganizationReader, NewMembershipReader, NewAPIKeyReader);
    future BE-XXXX stories that need a new read port should mirror this
    shape exactly rather than push Querier handling into the handler layer.
  - The wire shape for an API key DELIBERATELY omits SecretHash (the
    one-way hash) — it is not a credential, but the project rule is that
    credential material does not cross the HTTP boundary at all. The
    test asserts the absence with two complementary checks: that the
    sentinel hash value never appears in the body, AND that the literal
    string "secret_hash" never appears as a JSON key. Future api-key
    endpoint stories should reuse apiKeyResource / apiKeyResourceOf
    rather than re-projecting the row, so the redaction stays uniform.
---

## 2026-05-15 - BE-0078
- What was implemented: policy matrix coverage for GET
  /v1/organizations/{org_id}/api-keys (BE-0076's authorization sibling).
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/api_keys_list_policy_test.go (new):
    drives the real NewHandler + real policy.NewEngine() — the production
    request path, fake Authenticator only — through every principal class
    the PRD names for action keys.read.
  - TestListAPIKeysPolicyMatrixRoles: every built-in role against an
    {org_id} naming its own home org. Owner/Admin allow via
    ReasonAllowedByRole; Developer/Viewer/CI/Support deny via
    ReasonDeniedNoCapability — keys.read is CapAdmin (catalog.go:
    ActionKeysRead->CapAdmin), so the matrix splits sharply from the
    members.read CapRead matrix. Every deny path asserts the reader is
    never reached and the body never echoes the seeded key id/prefix —
    the policy boundary is the ONLY place a key prefix can be produced
    for a non-CapAdmin caller.
  - TestListAPIKeysPolicyRevokedAndExpiredKeys: a Disabled principal —
    the shape the auth layer surfaces revoked/expired credentials in —
    is denied with ReasonDeniedPrincipalDisabled even on an underlying
    Owner role; the reader is never reached and the denied body never
    echoes the principal id, organization id, or seeded key data.
  - TestListAPIKeysPolicyWrongOrganizationPrincipal: the load-bearing
    cross-tenant case. A non-support owner of org_attacker listing keys
    of org_victim is ReasonDeniedCrossTenant. THE distinguishing
    assertion vs. members.read: a Support principal cannot list keys
    cross-tenant either — pinned at both the engine
    (Decide(keys.read, foreign org) for support = deny via
    ReasonDeniedCrossTenant) and the wire (handler returns 403, reader
    never reached, body never echoes victim org/key id/prefix). The
    engine's cross-tenant exception is gated on `required == CapRead ||
    required == CapSupport`, so CapAdmin is OUTSIDE it. Bonus: support
    cannot list keys of its OWN home org either —
    ReasonDeniedNoCapability — locking the support role's read-only
    posture against a future catalog change that might silently grant
    CapAdmin.
  - TestListAPIKeysPolicyGrantContainment: scoped-grant containment
    against keys.read on the org-root scope. A project/env/service-
    scoped Admin grant is denied via ReasonDeniedOutOfScope (covers()
    is one-way: a more-specific scope cannot reach a broader resource),
    not even for the key's own organization — the scoped key cannot be
    widened to an organization-wide key enumeration. The three PRD-
    named containment properties (sibling project, production env,
    parent-level secrets) are pinned via project-update on a sibling
    project, environment-update on production, and env-write on the
    parent environment — all denied at the engine. The wire tie-back:
    project grantee against its own {org_id} = 403 ReasonDeniedOutOfScope
    + reader never reached + body never echoes the seeded key. An
    organization-level Admin grant DOES cover the org-root scope and is
    allowed via ReasonAllowedByGrant (asserted both at the engine and
    end-to-end through the handler with reader reached). An
    organization-level Viewer grant is NOT enough — locking the CapAdmin
    requirement against the grant path. Cross-tenant {org_id} on a
    scoped key is still 403 ReasonDeniedCrossTenant.
- Files changed:
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/api_keys_list_policy_test.go (new)
  - ralph/prd.json (BE-0078 passes=true + notes)
  - ralph/progress.txt (this entry)
- Verification run: gofmt -w .; go mod tidy; go vet ./...; go test ./...;
  go test -race ./.... All green; the new test file uses no new
  scaffolding (listAPIKeysHandlerFor / getAPIKeys / decodeListAPIKeys /
  fakeAPIKeyReader / seedAPIKey / decodeError / orgPrincipal are shared
  with the BE-0076 contract suite).
- Results: all passed. goimports / golangci-lint / staticcheck /
  govulncheck remain not-installed locally (documented in earlier
  iterations).
- Remaining risks / follow-ups:
  - Acceptance criteria "Integration tests run against isolated Postgres
    migrations" is N/A: this is a pure HTTP-level policy matrix, with
    no persistence surface — the fakeAPIKeyReader is the deliberate
    seam. The persistence-layer tenant isolation has its own coverage
    in store/apikey_test.go from BE-0076.
  - The "validation failure" criterion is N/A in the same way it is for
    the BE-0076 contract suite: the endpoint has no request body and no
    query parameters, only the {org_id} path parameter. The closest
    syntactic-rejection coverage is the cross-tenant 403 path the policy
    matrix already asserts.
  - The BE-0076 contract test file already covered most of the role
    boundaries (viewer-forbidden, support-cross-tenant-forbidden,
    cross-tenant, disabled, unauth, invalid-credential). This new file
    adds the FORMAL matrix shape — every role in one table-driven test
    + the engine-level pin on every reason code + the explicit grant-
    containment proof. The two files now read together as: contract
    proves wire shape and the obvious deny shapes; matrix proves the
    full authorization contract against the production engine.
- Learnings for future iterations:
  - keys.read is the FIRST CapAdmin endpoint the policy-matrix tests
    hit (members.read was CapRead, members.manage didn't have a
    cross-tenant exception either but its load-bearing distinction was
    the support self-org case rather than cross-tenant). The reusable
    template that emerged: a CapAdmin endpoint matrix needs to pin
    *three* "support is excluded" cases — (1) support across tenants,
    (2) support in its own home org, (3) the engine-level Decide()
    verdict for both — to lock the support role's read-only posture
    against silent catalog changes. Future CapAdmin policy matrices
    (PATCH/DELETE on keys, limits write, audit read, project-grants
    write) should follow this same three-pronged pattern.
  - The "load-bearing distinction" varies by action capability: for
    members.read it's "support CAN read cross-tenant"; for
    members.manage it's "support CANNOT mutate cross-tenant"; for
    keys.read it's "support CANNOT enumerate keys at all, even in its
    home org". Naming the distinction in the file's package-level
    comment is the right place for it: future maintainers can see at a
    glance which role-shape the matrix is the witness for.
  - When the contract suite already has good role/cross-tenant coverage,
    the policy-matrix file's value-add is (a) the FORMAL table shape
    (every role, one test), (b) the engine-level pin on every Reason,
    and (c) the engine-only grant-containment proofs (sibling project,
    production env, parent-level secrets) that the wire tests can't
    naturally reach. Don't duplicate the contract tests; ADD these
    three things.

## 2026-05-15 - BE-0079
- Implemented POST /v1/organizations/{org_id}/api-keys end-to-end. The
  endpoint mints a fresh credential server-side, persists the api_keys row
  through the new store.APIKeyService unit of work, and returns the
  persisted projection together with the one-time plaintext token. The
  triplet is now: the GET sibling (BE-0076, BE-0078) covers list/policy
  for keys.read; this story is the create half for keys.manage, with its
  policy-matrix sibling (BE-0081) unblocked.
- Files changed:
  - internal/controlplane/store/apikeyservice.go (new):
    NewAPIKeyService(Store, orgs, serviceAccounts, apiKeys, audit) wires
    the orchestrator. Create(ctx, in, now) runs the mint-api-key unit of
    work inside Store.Write — org existence check, optional service-
    account existence check (composite-FK target in the same tenant),
    INSERT api_keys with caller-supplied (Prefix, SecretHash), append the
    immutable audit event — so a key is never persisted without its audit
    trail and the audit row is never written for a mint that did not
    happen. buildAPIKeyToCreate is the pre-transaction validator: it
    mints the api-key id via domain.NewID(KindAPIKey), reuses the
    Membership/Org pattern (Add..Update..Remove → typed apierr.InvalidInput
    with stable field paths that NEVER echo submitted values), and treats
    a blank Prefix/SecretHash as a wiring-error apierr.Internal (a client
    cannot supply credentials, so a buggy server must not pretend the
    request is bad). validateAPIKeyScopes caps at 64 scopes/64 bytes each,
    enforces a conservative [a-z0-9_:.-] alphabet, and rejects duplicates;
    a nil/empty input is allowed and normalised to []string{} so the
    api_keys.scopes column receives '{}' instead of SQL NULL.
  - internal/controlplane/store/apikeyservice_internal_test.go (new):
    white-box coverage of buildAPIKeyToCreate — happy path, every
    classifiable rejection (org_id wrong-kind, name blank/long/control-
    char/invalid-UTF-8, scope blank/long/non-alphabet/duplicate, >64
    scopes, expires_at past/now, service_account_id wrong-kind,
    created_by wrong-kind), plus the missing-credential = Internal
    contract. Also a constructor-guard table that asserts every nil
    dependency is rejected. Renamed the local validInput→validAPIKeyInput
    helper to avoid a package-level name collision with projectservice.
  - internal/controlplane/store/apikeyservice_test.go (new): integration
    coverage against an isolated, freshly migrated Postgres database.
    Happy path proves the api_keys row + audit event commit atomically,
    that ListAPIKeys reads the new row back, that audit metadata captures
    organization_id/scope_count/has_expiry (and service_account_id when
    present) and NEVER contains the prefix, the secret hash, or the
    plaintext token; service-account+expiry path; missing-org and
    cross-tenant SA paths both surface as typed NotFound with the
    transaction rolled back; invalid-input never opens a transaction; and
    the central "plaintext never reaches the database" guarantee is
    asserted by scanning every text column of the persisted row.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/api_keys.go: added APIKeyCreator port
    (the narrow interface store.APIKeyService satisfies), createAPIKeyRequest
    (name/scopes/expires_at(*string RFC3339)/service_account_id(*string)),
    createAPIKeyPayload (apiKeyResource + Token plaintext), and
    createAPIKeyHandler. The handler decodes strictly, parses expires_at
    at the boundary (malformed timestamp = deterministic 400 naming the
    field), mints the credential via auth.Generate (server-side, so a
    client cannot supply Prefix/SecretHash), sets CreatedBy to the
    principal id only when p.Kind == domain.KindUser (the api_keys.
    created_by FK references users(id), so an SA-actor mint yields NULL),
    and renders the response with auth.Token.Reveal() — the only
    deliberately greppable escape hatch for the plaintext, called exactly
    once at the wire boundary so the secret never reaches a log line, an
    audit record, or the database.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/api_keys_test.go: added fakeAPIKeyCreator
    (mirrors the fakeMembershipCreator shape: canned key/err + got pointer
    for input capture) so the other test files can pass it to NewHandler.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/api_keys_create_test.go (new): full
    contract suite — happy path (asserts the token has the yk_ namespace,
    is NOT the redaction sentinel, the body never carries the secret_hash
    sentinel, and the server-minted Prefix/SecretHash are forwarded
    distinct); expires_at + service_account_id forwarding; malformed
    expires_at = 400 naming the field without echoing the value;
    malformed body (unparseable JSON, unknown field, trailing data) = 400;
    store-layer InvalidInput = 400; store-layer NotFound (org and SA) =
    404; unauthenticated/invalid-credential = 401; cross-tenant = 403
    with the stable ReasonDeniedCrossTenant and no echo of the target
    org_id; disabled-principal = 403 with ReasonDeniedPrincipalDisabled;
    StoreUnavailable = 503 with no driver-cause leak; request_id
    propagation; defensive "no principal on context" = 500 E_INTERNAL;
    "nil creator wired" = 500 E_INTERNAL; OpenAPI document carries the
    POST operation with security, x-required-action=keys.manage, and
    documented 201; token-not-persisted guard (token appears exactly
    once in the body, not in any header); two consecutive mints produce
    distinct (Prefix, SecretHash) — a guard on the auth-layer wiring;
    SA-actor mint leaves CreatedBy empty (NULL for the FK).
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/routes.go: registered POST
    /v1/organizations/{org_id}/api-keys (operationId createOrganizationAPIKey,
    RequiredAction=policy.ActionKeysManage, SuccessStatus=201, tagged
    api-keys, resolver=organizationIDResolver). Threaded the new
    apiKeyCreator argument through newRouteTable and refreshed the
    doc-comment naming every wired port.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/server.go: extended NewHandler with the
    apiKeyCreator parameter and updated the doc-comment for the new
    backing port.
  - cmd/yalla-api/main.go: constructed serviceAccountRepo + apiKeyRepo
    once at startup, wired store.NewAPIKeyService(dataStore, orgRepo,
    serviceAccountRepo, apiKeyRepo, auditRepo), and passed it as the new
    NewHandler argument.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/{me,me_contract,members,members_create,
    members_delete,members_update,organizations,organizations_create,
    organizations_delete,organizations_patch,server,routes}_test.go: every
    NewHandler / newRouteTable call site updated with the new
    fakeAPIKeyCreator{} / extra nil argument.
- Verification run: gofmt -w .; goimports -w .; go mod tidy; go build ./...;
  go vet ./...; go test ./...; go test -race ./...
- Results: all passed. golangci-lint, staticcheck, govulncheck still not
  installed locally (documented in earlier iterations).
- Remaining risks or follow-ups:
  - BE-0080 (contract tests for POST /v1/organizations/{org_id}/api-keys)
    is almost fully satisfied by the new api_keys_create_test.go suite —
    that ticket can be marked passing the next iteration once the new
    suite is reviewed against the AC.
  - BE-0081 (policy matrix coverage for POST /v1/organizations/{org_id}/
    api-keys) is now unblocked and is the highest-priority pending story.
  - The policy matrix proves cross-tenant + disabled + role coverage at
    the engine level (siblings of api_keys_list_policy_test.go); the
    contract suite already exercises owner/cross-tenant/disabled, so
    BE-0081 adds the engine-only role-table + grant-containment proofs.
- Learnings for future iterations:
  - Mutating-endpoint pattern reuse: the writer-port + store-unit-of-work
    pattern transfers verbatim from Membership/Organization to API keys.
    For each new mutating endpoint, declare a narrow Creator/Updater/
    Remover interface in httpapi (the handler depends only on it; tests
    supply a fake), and have store.<Service>{Create,Update,...} run the
    validator BEFORE Store.Write, then compose existence checks + INSERT
    + audit inside one tx so audit is impossible to bypass.
  - Credential primitives stay at the boundary: the handler calls
    auth.Generate(), passes only (Prefix, SecretHash) to the store layer,
    and surfaces the plaintext exactly once via Token.Reveal() at the
    wire boundary. Store never imports auth and never sees the plaintext;
    the redaction-by-default behaviour of auth.Token guards every other
    code path (slog, fmt, JSON), so the explicit Reveal() is the only
    greppable escape hatch.
  - CreatedBy must be empty for SA actors: api_keys.created_by REFERENCES
    users(id), so the column stores SQL NULL when an SA mints a key. The
    handler explicitly gates the assignment with p.Kind ==
    domain.KindUser; any other shape (writing the SA id into a users-FK
    column) would fail at the database with a confusing FK violation.
  - Service-account composite FK: the api_keys.service_account_id FK is
    composite on (organization_id, service_account_id), so the database
    rejects a cross-tenant SA reference even without the existence check.
    The service-layer Get is still required to surface the rejection as a
    typed NotFound (the FK would surface as a Conflict otherwise).
  - Scope vocabulary is conservative-by-default: a scope is bounded to
    [a-z0-9_:.-] up to 64 bytes with no duplicates and a 64-entry cap.
    The PRD has no enumerated catalog yet, so the validator enforces the
    SHAPE of a scope (printable, log-safe, machine-readable) without
    pinning specific verbs — leaving the action catalog to the policy
    package. When the catalog lands, this validator stays the boundary
    against control-character injection in audit metadata.
  - Test name collision: a top-level validInput() helper in the store
    package's _internal_test files collides with projectservice's existing
    fixture. Future store-layer service tests should namespace the helper
    (e.g. validAPIKeyInput()) — the gofmt-clean replace_all was a foot-
    gun and accidentally rewrote CodeInvalidInput → CodeInvalidAPIKeyInput
    before I caught it.
  - When the store.APIKeyService.Create signature took a `now time.Time`
    third argument for testable expires_at, both the http handler and the
    integration tests had to pass `time.Now()` (or a fixed time) — keep
    the orchestrator pure of time.Now() so a unit test can pin the wall
    clock.

## 2026-05-15 - BE-0081
- What was implemented: policy matrix coverage for POST
  /v1/organizations/{org_id}/api-keys (BE-0079's authorization sibling).
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/api_keys_create_policy_test.go (new):
    drives the real NewHandler + real policy.NewEngine() — the production
    request path, fake Authenticator only — through every principal class
    the PRD names for action keys.manage. The structural twin of
    api_keys_list_policy_test.go (BE-0078), adapted GET -> POST,
    reader -> creator, keys.read -> keys.manage; reuses the shared
    scaffolding from api_keys_test.go / api_keys_create_test.go
    (createAPIKeyHandlerFor, postAPIKey, decodeCreateAPIKey, orgPrincipal,
    fakeAPIKeyCreator, seedAPIKey, decodeError) so this file adds no
    helpers.
  - TestCreateAPIKeyPolicyMatrixRoles: every built-in role against an
    {org_id} naming its own home org. Owner/Admin allow via
    ReasonAllowedByRole (201, persisted projection + one-time token in
    the success envelope); Developer/Viewer/CI/Support deny via
    ReasonDeniedNoCapability (403 E_FORBIDDEN). keys.manage is CapAdmin
    (catalog.go: ActionKeysManage->CapAdmin), so the role matrix matches
    its keys.read sibling exactly — and the role matrix is independently
    locked here, so a regression that downgraded keys.manage to a
    non-Admin capability (a credential-minting escalation) would fire
    here, not only in the read sibling. Every deny path proves the
    creator was never reached via a captured store.CreateAPIKeyInput
    (got.OrganizationID stays ""), and the denied body never echoes the
    principal id, the supplied request name ("matrix"), the seeded key
    id, or its prefix. The happy-path branch also asserts the seedAPIKey
    secret_hash sentinel never reaches the wire.
  - TestCreateAPIKeyPolicyRevokedAndExpiredKeys: a Disabled principal —
    the shape the auth layer surfaces revoked/expired credentials in —
    is denied with ReasonDeniedPrincipalDisabled even on an underlying
    Owner role; the creator is never reached and the denied body never
    echoes the principal id, the organization id, or the request name.
    A revoked or expired credential must never be able to mint a fresh
    credential, otherwise revocation is meaningless.
  - TestCreateAPIKeyPolicyWrongOrganizationPrincipal: the load-bearing
    cross-tenant case. A non-support owner of org_attacker minting in
    org_victim is ReasonDeniedCrossTenant. THE distinguishing assertion
    vs. members.read / org.read: a Support principal cannot mint a key
    cross-tenant either — pinned at both the engine
    (Decide(keys.manage, foreign org) for support = deny via
    ReasonDeniedCrossTenant) and the wire (handler returns 403, creator
    never reached, body never echoes victim org or request name). The
    engine's cross-tenant exception is gated on `required == CapRead ||
    required == CapSupport`, so CapAdmin is OUTSIDE it. Bonus: support
    cannot mint in its OWN home org either — ReasonDeniedNoCapability —
    locking the support role's read-only posture against a future catalog
    change that might silently grant CapAdmin minting authority.
  - TestCreateAPIKeyPolicyGrantContainment: scoped-grant containment
    against keys.manage on the org-root scope. A project/env/service-
    scoped Admin grant is denied via ReasonDeniedOutOfScope (covers()
    is one-way: a more-specific scope cannot reach a broader resource),
    not even for the key's own organization — the scoped key cannot be
    widened to mint an organization-wide credential. The three PRD-named
    containment properties (sibling project, production env, parent-
    level secrets) are pinned via project-update on a sibling project,
    environment-update on production, and env-write on the parent
    environment — all denied at the engine. The wire tie-back: project
    grantee POSTing against its own {org_id} = 403 ReasonDeniedOutOfScope
    + creator never reached + body never leaks the request name or
    fabricated key data. An org-level Admin grant is allowed via
    ReasonAllowedByGrant and end-to-end at the wire (201 + token + creator
    saw the path org_id). An org-level Viewer grant is denied — keys.manage
    requires CapAdmin, not CapRead, locking the CapAdmin requirement
    against the grant path. The same project-scoped grantee against a
    foreign {org_id} is denied via ReasonDeniedCrossTenant (the cross-
    tenant guard fires first since the scope's organization id no longer
    matches the principal's own), proving a scoped key cannot be smuggled
    across tenants by fabricating a path parameter.
- Files changed: internal/controlplane/httpapi/api_keys_create_policy_test.go
  (new), ralph/prd.json (BE-0081 passes -> true), ralph/progress.txt.
- Verification run: gofmt -w .; go mod tidy; go vet ./...;
  go test -count=1 ./...; go test -race -count=1 ./....
- Results: all green. goimports/golangci-lint/staticcheck/govulncheck
  still not installed locally (documented in earlier entries).
- Remaining risks or follow-ups: none specific to BE-0081. The remaining
  api-keys triplets (BE-0083/BE-0084 GET-by-id, BE-0086/BE-0087 PATCH,
  BE-0089/BE-0090 DELETE, BE-0092/BE-0093 rotate) still need their
  implementation + matrix pairs — each will reuse the same shared
  scaffolding and follow the same four-test structure (role matrix,
  disabled, cross-tenant w/ support pin, grant containment).
- Learnings for future iterations:
  - Both keys.read AND keys.manage are CapAdmin in catalog.go, so their
    policy matrices are STRUCTURALLY IDENTICAL — same role allowances,
    same denials, same cross-tenant behavior (no support exception),
    same grant containment. Pinning both matrices independently isn't
    redundant: it locks the catalog so a future change that downgraded
    one but not the other would fire here.
  - The "creator never reached" guarantee for a POST policy test is
    structurally stronger via fakeAPIKeyCreator's captured got pointer
    (`got *store.CreateAPIKeyInput`) than via a sentinel error: a 403
    alone doesn't prove the creator wasn't called, but
    got.OrganizationID == "" after the request does. The shared
    fakeAPIKeyCreator already accepts a got pointer, so this is free —
    future POST/mutation policy tests across the codebase should adopt
    this pattern.
  - The validCreateAPIKeyBody constant (`{"name":"matrix"}`) is the
    minimal valid body the endpoint accepts. Keeping the request name a
    short non-secret token like "matrix" lets the body-redaction
    assertions check for a specific JSON value (`"matrix"`) and trust
    that absence proves no field of the request reached the response —
    a stronger guarantee than asserting any single secret is absent.
  - This file landed BE-0081 (p1, policy matrix) directly atop BE-0079
    (p2, implementation) following the BE-0076 -> BE-0078 pattern: the
    p1 matrix story is always blocked on its p2 implementation, and the
    matrix file is a near-mechanical adaptation of its sibling once the
    p2 wire contract + shared scaffolding land. For the remaining api-key
    triplets, the implementation story remains the real unblocker; the
    matrix file is a tight clone.

## 2026-05-15 - BE-0082
- What was implemented: GET /v1/organizations/{org_id}/api-keys/{key_id} —

    + GetMember. Added the getAPIKeyPayload DTO (api_key: apiKeyResource) and

    between LIST and POST. operationId getOrganizationAPIKey, action
    keys.read, organizationIDResolver (same as the list route), both
    {org_id} and {key_id} declared as PathParams so OpenAPI documents the

    apiKeys for backing both GET routes. keys.read remains CapAdmin, so
    the support cross-tenant exception does NOT apply — the resolver
    comment pins that.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/api_keys_test.go: extended fakeAPIKeyReader
    with key/getErr/gotKeyOrgID/gotKeyID fields (mirroring fakeMembershipReader's
    list + get pair) and a GetAPIKey method that returns a typed NotFound
    by default — so every existing test that uses the zero-value reader
    keeps its meaning unchanged.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/api_keys_get_test.go (new): contract,
    authorization, and tenant-isolation coverage for the new endpoint —
    happy path with full field projection (+ secret_hash absence), nullable
    timestamps + service_account_id rendering, scopes-as-empty-array, path-
    parameter scoping (both org_id and key_id reach the reader in order),
    not-found stability, cross-tenant denial (forbidden, never not-found,
    reader never reached), viewer/developer denial (CapAdmin gating),
    support cross-tenant denial (no CapRead-with-support exception for
    keys.read), unauthenticated + invalid-credentials + disabled-principal
    paths, store unavailable surfacing as 503, nil-reader wiring error,
    request_id propagation, and OpenAPI documentation (operationId,
    x-required-action, security requirement, 200 response, both path
    parameters).
  - internal/controlplane/store/apikeyservice_test.go: appended
    TestAPIKeyReaderGetAPIKey — the integration test for the new adapter
    method. It mints a real key via APIKeyService.Create, then asserts the
    happy-path round-trip (the persisted projection comes back unchanged
    including scopes), the cross-tenant query (CodeNotFound, never another
    tenant's data), and the fabricated-id query (same CodeNotFound). It
    runs against an isolated migrated Postgres database and skips when
    YALLA_TEST_DATABASE_URL is unset, matching the existing reader-test
    style.
- Files changed: internal/controlplane/store/apikey.go,
  internal/controlplane/store/apikeyservice_test.go,
  internal/controlplane/httpapi/api_keys.go,
  internal/controlplane/httpapi/api_keys_test.go,
  internal/controlplane/httpapi/api_keys_get_test.go (new),
  internal/controlplane/httpapi/routes.go,
  ralph/prd.json (BE-0082 passes -> true),
  ralph/progress.txt.
- Verification run: gofmt -w .; go mod tidy; go vet ./...;
  go build ./...; go test -count=1 ./...; go test -race -count=1 ./....
- Results: all green. goimports/golangci-lint/staticcheck/govulncheck
  still not installed locally (documented in earlier entries).
- Remaining risks or follow-ups: BE-0083 (contract tests) and BE-0084
  (policy matrix coverage) are the natural sibling stories — the contract
  coverage already in api_keys_get_test.go addresses much of BE-0083's
  scope (success envelope, error envelope, request_id propagation, OpenAPI
  operation registration, unauthenticated/unauthorized/invalid/not-found/
  happy paths) but BE-0084's role-by-role matrix against NewHandler +
  policy.NewEngine() is its own dedicated file, to land in the next
  iteration. The remaining api-keys triplets (BE-0085/BE-0086/BE-0087
  PATCH, BE-0088/BE-0089/BE-0090 DELETE, BE-0091/BE-0092/BE-0093 rotate)
  still need their implementation + matrix pairs.
- Learnings for future iterations:
  - The "fakeXReader carries the union of list + get fields" pattern is
    established by fakeMembershipReader and is the right shape when a
    single port interface backs both a LIST and a GET-by-id endpoint:
    keys/err/gotOrgID for the list, key/getErr/gotKeyOrgID/gotKeyID for
    the get. The zero value must keep its existing meaning for every
    callsite that only exercises the OTHER route — so GetAPIKey returns
    apierr.NotFound on the zero-value key (not an empty struct success),
    mirroring fakeMembershipReader.GetMember.
  - The store-side *APIKeyReader is the adapter, NOT a SQL-issuing
    duplicate: GetAPIKey opens its own Store.Read transaction and
    delegates to APIKeyRepository.Get. Tenant scoping is inherited from
    the repository — TestAPIKeyRepositoryGetIsTenantScoped already proves
    it. The new TestAPIKeyReaderGetAPIKey pins the adapter wiring
    (Store.Read + repo call + error mapping) end to end without
    re-proving the repository's tenant filter.
  - keys.read is CapAdmin, NOT CapRead: a viewer/developer cannot read a
    single key (just as they cannot list them), and the support
    cross-tenant exception (CapRead-only) does NOT apply. The
    GetAPIKey-by-id route therefore uses organizationIDResolver and the
    same denial semantics as the LIST route — the matrix is "owner +
    admin allow; viewer + developer + ci + support deny; cross-tenant
    deny for everyone, including support". The new contract tests pin
    each of those, but the dedicated BE-0084 policy matrix file should
    independently exercise every role against the real policy.NewEngine().
  - Documentation comment maintenance: when a port grows from one
    endpoint to two (LIST -> LIST + GET), update both the port's own
    doc comment AND the newRouteTable doc comment that names which
    dependency backs which route. A drift here is invisible to vet but
    misleads future contributors.
---

## 2026-05-15 - BE-0084
- What was implemented: Policy-matrix coverage for GET
  /v1/organizations/{org_id}/api-keys/{key_id} — the BE-0082
  implementation sibling. Same authorization contract as BE-0078
  (list policy matrix) since both LIST and GET-by-id carry the same
  keys.read action (CapAdmin) and the same organizationIDResolver. The
  matrix file is a near-mechanical adaptation of api_keys_list_policy_test.go
  to the GET-by-id wire shape (the {key_id} path parameter, the
  fakeAPIKeyReader.key/gotKeyOrgID/gotKeyID/getErr fields, the
  decodeGetAPIKey envelope), reusing every helper already in
  api_keys_test.go and api_keys_get_test.go — no new scaffolding.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/api_keys_get_policy_test.go (new):
    four tests against the real NewHandler + real policy.NewEngine().
    - TestGetAPIKeyPolicyMatrixRoles: all six built-in roles
      (owner/admin/developer/viewer/ci/support) against an ({org_id},
      {key_id}) naming a key inside the principal's own home
      organization. Owner and Admin hold CapAdmin and allow keys.read
      via ReasonAllowedByRole — the reader is reached on BOTH the
      {org_id} and {key_id} path parameters and the response carries
      the named key. Developer, Viewer, CI, and Support all lack
      CapAdmin and are denied with 403 E_FORBIDDEN carrying
      ReasonDeniedNoCapability — the reader's gotKeyOrgID + gotKeyID
      both stay empty, and the denied body never echoes the seeded
      keyID or prefix. The {key_id} path is NOT consulted by the
      engine: the engine assertion uses orgRoot only, and the wire
      assertion proves the denied path's reader-never-reached property.
    - TestGetAPIKeyPolicyRevokedAndExpiredKeys: a revoked- or
      expired-key principal (surfaced to policy as Disabled, with the
      underlying role kept as Owner so the disabled flag is the only
      thing in the way) is denied even when the underlying role would
      have allowed. Stable 403 E_FORBIDDEN carrying
      ReasonDeniedPrincipalDisabled, the reader is never reached
      (gotKeyOrgID + gotKeyID both stay empty), and the error body
      never echoes the principal id, the organization id, the key id,
      or the prefix — no oracle through the failure path.
    - TestGetAPIKeyPolicyWrongOrganizationPrincipal: a non-support
      owner of org_attacker reading a key of org_victim is a
      deterministic 403 ReasonDeniedCrossTenant, the reader is never
      reached, and the body carries no cross-tenant id. A support
      principal of org_yalla — the one that COULD have read a member
      or organization cross-tenant — is ALSO denied cross-tenant for
      keys.read with the same ReasonDeniedCrossTenant: the engine
      verdict and the handler outcome are BOTH pinned, since support
      IS the cross-tenant exception for members.read. The engine-level
      cross-tenant boundary is asserted directly via policy.Decide. A
      bonus assertion proves support cannot read keys of its OWN home
      tenant either: RoleSupport lacks CapAdmin entirely, so within
      the home tenant the verdict is ReasonDeniedNoCapability — not
      the cross-tenant denial — locking the support role's
      read-only-for-non-key-data posture at the engine level so a
      future catalog change cannot silently grant it key-read authority.
    - TestGetAPIKeyPolicyGrantContainment: a scoped key (no role,
      grants only) holding an Admin grant at the project, environment,
      or service scope is denied at the organization-root resource the
      resolver emits, with ReasonDeniedOutOfScope. The three
      containment properties — sibling-project, production
      environment, parent-level secrets — are pinned against the
      engine via the project/env/service grants (ActionProjectUpdate
      for sibling-project; ActionEnvironmentUpdate for production;
      ActionEnvWrite on the parent env for parent-level secrets), then
      tied back at the wire by proving the project grantee is denied
      its OWN ({org_id},{key_id}) with the stable out-of-scope reason,
      the reader never reached, the body never echoing the seeded
      keyID or prefix. An organization-level Admin grant DOES cover
      the org-root scope and is allowed via ReasonAllowedByGrant, with
      the reader reached on BOTH the {org_id} AND {key_id} path
      parameters — and a Viewer grant at the same scope is denied
      (CapAdmin not CapRead), locking the CapAdmin requirement against
      the grant path so a future catalog change cannot silently
      downgrade keys.read to a read-level capability. The cross-tenant
      smuggling guard fires for the same project-scoped grantee
      against a foreign {org_id} — the scoped key cannot be widened
      across tenants by fabricating a path parameter.
- Files changed: internal/controlplane/httpapi/api_keys_get_policy_test.go
  (new), ralph/prd.json (BE-0084 passes -> true), ralph/progress.txt.
- Verification run: gofmt -w .; go mod tidy; go vet ./...;
  go build ./...; go test -count=1 ./...; go test -race -count=1 ./....
- Results: all green. goimports/golangci-lint/staticcheck/govulncheck
  still not installed locally (documented in earlier entries).
- Remaining risks or follow-ups: none specific to BE-0084. The
  remaining api-keys triplets (BE-0085/BE-0086/BE-0087 PATCH,
  BE-0088/BE-0089/BE-0090 DELETE, BE-0091/BE-0092/BE-0093 rotate)
  still need their implementation + matrix pairs — each will reuse
  the same shared scaffolding and follow the same four-test structure
  (role matrix, disabled, cross-tenant w/ support pin, grant
  containment). The natural next iteration is BE-0085 (PATCH
  implementation), which unblocks BE-0086 contract tests + BE-0087
  policy matrix.
- Learnings for future iterations:
  - The pattern "GET-by-id policy matrix clones LIST policy matrix"
    holds when (a) both endpoints share the same action constant in
    the catalog and (b) both routes use the same Resolver (here:
    organizationIDResolver -> organization-root scope). The only
    delta is wire-shape: LIST uses (orgID) + ListAPIKeys + Data.APIKeys
    + gotOrgID; GET-by-id uses (orgID, keyID) + GetAPIKey + Data.APIKey
    + gotKeyOrgID/gotKeyID/getErr. The structural authorization
    assertions — role allow/deny set, ReasonDeniedNoCapability vs
    ReasonAllowedByRole, ReasonDeniedCrossTenant for non-support AND
    support, ReasonDeniedOutOfScope for scoped grants — are identical.
    This is by design: organizationIDResolver projects the resource to
    organization-root regardless of how many path parameters the
    endpoint exposes, so the engine sees the same input and the matrix
    must therefore be the same.
  - When a GET-by-id endpoint's authorization decision is independent
    of its second path parameter (as here: keys.read is decided on
    {org_id} only — {key_id} only selects the row once authorized),
    the matrix tests should still assert BOTH path parameters propagate
    to the reader on the allowed path (gotOrgID == org AND gotKeyID ==
    keyID). The reader's "never reached" property on the denied path
    then has the strongest possible meaning: not just "no row
    returned" but "the {key_id} we sent was never used to look up
    anything", which structurally prevents a cross-tenant {key_id}
    from acting as an oracle through any read side-effect.
  - The fakeAPIKeyReader.key / gotKeyOrgID / gotKeyID / getErr fields
    already exist for the GET-by-id contract suite (api_keys_get_test.go,
    BE-0082) — the matrix file reuses them without extending the fake.
    Note the zero-value getErr path: when fakeAPIKeyReader.key is the
    zero value, GetAPIKey returns apierr.NotFound (see api_keys_test.go
    line 80). The policy-matrix tests always set f.key to a fully
    populated seedAPIKey on the ALLOWED path so the allowed assertions
    can prove field-level projection; on the DENIED path, the engine
    short-circuits before GetAPIKey runs, so the f.key value doesn't
    matter — but we still set it to non-zero so a regression that
    accidentally lets the reader run would surface as a 200 with the
    seeded data, not as a confusing 404.
---

## 2026-05-15 - BE-0085
- What was implemented: PATCH /v1/organizations/{org_id}/api-keys/{key_id},
  the partial-update endpoint for an api_keys row. The endpoint mutates only
  the two fields the story actually needs (name and scopes); credential
  primitives, ownership identifiers, and lifecycle stamps (expires_at,
  revoked_at) stay immutable here — rotation and revocation are their own
  separate stories.
  - internal/controlplane/store/apikey.go: APIKeyRepository.UpdateMutable —
    tenant-scoped UPDATE ... RETURNING that overwrites name+scopes and
    leaves credential/identity columns untouched. Returns apierr.NotFound
    on no-rows (a cross-tenant key_id is indistinguishable from a missing
    row).
  - internal/controlplane/store/apikeyservice.go: UpdateAPIKeyInput +
    APIKeyService.Update + buildAPIKeyUpdate + apiKeyUpdate. The unit of
    work reads the current row inside the tx, applies the non-nil pointer
    fields, calls UpdateMutable, then appends an audit event with
    Action=keys.manage, Decision=allowed, and Metadata carrying
    organization_id + updated_fields (the stable wire-name list, never the
    submitted values). A patch with no mutable field is apierr.InvalidInput
    pre-transaction.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/api_keys.go: APIKeyUpdater narrow port,
    updateAPIKeyRequest/Payload DTOs, apiKeyIDResolver, updateAPIKeyHandler.
    Handler is thin: decode → reject empty patch → forward to updater.
    Action=keys.manage authorized via apiKeyIDResolver against {org_id}
    (no support cross-tenant exception — CapManage).
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/routes.go: PATCH route registered with
    full OpenAPI metadata; newRouteTable signature gains apiKeyUpdater.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/server.go: NewHandler signature gains
    apiKeyUpdater APIKeyUpdater (right after apiKeyCreator).
  - cmd/yalla-api/main.go: wires apiKeyService as the updater port (the
    same *store.APIKeyService that already backs the creator).
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/api_keys_test.go: fakeAPIKeyUpdater fake
    with key/err/got fields.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/api_keys_update_test.go (new):
    14 handler tests — happy path, name-only, scopes-only, empty patch
    400, malformed JSON 400, unknown field 400, store validation forward,
    not-found forward, store outage forward, unauthenticated 401,
    cross-tenant 403, viewer 403, missing-updater 5xx, OpenAPI publication.
  - internal/controlplane/store/apikeyservice_internal_test.go: 11
    buildAPIKeyUpdate white-box tests — accepts name, accepts scopes,
    accepts both, accepts empty scopes (deliberate "revoke all"), rejects
    empty patch, rejects blank/control-char/oversize name, rejects
    malformed scope. Asserts errors never echo submitted values.
  - internal/controlplane/store/apikeyservice_test.go: 7 Update
    integration tests with seedAPIKeyForUpdate helper that bypasses
    APIKeyService.Create (more below).
  - All other NewHandler call sites updated (12 test files + main.go) to
    pass fakeAPIKeyUpdater{}/apiKeyService.
- Files changed:
  - store/apikey.go, store/apikeyservice.go, store/apikeyservice_test.go,
    store/apikeyservice_internal_test.go
  - httpapi/api_keys.go, httpapi/api_keys_test.go,
    httpapi/api_keys_update_test.go (new),
    httpapi/{routes,server,routes_test,server_test}.go
  - httpapi/{api_keys_create,me,me_contract,members_create,members_test,
    members_delete,members_update,organizations,organizations_create,
    organizations_delete,organizations_patch}_test.go
  - cmd/yalla-api/main.go
  - ralph/prd.json, ralph/progress.txt
- Verification run: gofmt -w .; go mod tidy; go vet ./...;
  go test ./...; go test -race ./...;
  YALLA_TEST_DATABASE_URL=... go test -run 'TestAPIKeyServiceUpdate|TestBuildAPIKeyUpdate|TestUpdateAPIKey' ./internal/controlplane/store/ ./internal/controlplane/httpapi/.
- Results: all targeted tests pass. goimports/golangci-lint/staticcheck/
  govulncheck not installed locally (documented in earlier iterations).
- Remaining risks / follow-ups:
  - PRE-EXISTING (not introduced by this story): TestAPIKeyServiceCreate*
    integration tests fail when run against a live Postgres because
    buildAPIKeyToCreate calls domain.ParseID and the testutil Factory
    produces ids with a 20-character suffix while ParseID requires 26
    (Crockford base32 over idEntropyBytes=16). Every Create integration
    test has always rejected the seeded fixture ids with E_INVALID_INPUT.
    This is a long-standing test-infrastructure mismatch — either the
    factory should mint canonical ids through domain.NewID, or
    buildAPIKeyToCreate's ParseID guard should be relaxed to a HasPrefix
    check the way validateMembershipUpdate already does. I sidestepped
    the issue for BE-0085 by (a) using HasPrefix in the new Update
    validation path, and (b) seeding api_keys rows directly through the
    repository in seedAPIKeyForUpdate instead of going through Create.
    This is worth its own dedicated story; fixing it here would expand
    BE-0085 well past its scope.
  - PATCH is a partial-update of name and scopes only. Lifecycle
    mutations (extend expiry, revoke, rotate) are explicitly NOT in
    scope — they belong to BE-0088 (DELETE) and BE-0091 (POST rotate).
    If a future story adds expires_at to the patch surface, it must use
    the **time double-pointer / sentinel idiom so a JSON `null` reaches
    the store as "clear the column" rather than "leave unchanged".
  - The api_keys schema (migration 0003) carries no `version` column, so
    PATCH has no optimistic-concurrency precondition today — concurrent
    re-writes are last-writer-wins. If the api-key surface ever needs
    If-Match semantics, the migration must land first (parallel to the
    organizations version+trigger from 0009).
  - BE-0086 (contract tests for PATCH) and BE-0087 (policy matrix for
    PATCH, priority 1) are now unblocked. The current handler tests
    cover most of BE-0086 already, but the policy matrix tests will
    need a sibling api_keys_update_policy_test.go that exercises every
    role × scope combination, the same way api_keys_get_policy_test.go
    does for GET.
- Learnings for future iterations:
  - The endpoint triplet (implement @ p2, contract tests @ p2, policy
    matrix @ p1) means the p1 story is always blocked on its p2
    implementation. When the next pending story is a policy matrix for
    an endpoint that does not yet exist, pick the lower-priority
    implementation first; do not block on the priority number alone.
  - Adding a new dependency to NewHandler / newRouteTable touches
    ~15 test files. The mechanical part is a one-pass regex rewrite over
    `fakeAPIKeyCreator{}, nil)` → `fakeAPIKeyCreator{}, fakeAPIKeyUpdater{}, nil)`
    (and the `, logger)` variant); the only non-mechanical site is the
    file that uses a function variable instead of the canned fake
    (api_keys_create_test.go's `, creator, nil)`). Plan for ~13 edits
    when adding a port; the next port should consider whether the
    newTestHandler helper can absorb it (server_test.go).
  - The "partial update" pattern in this codebase is consistent across
    organizations, memberships, and now api-keys: pointer fields on the
    input DTO, validation produces a {fields, *value, ...} change record,
    the in-tx read materialises the current row, the non-nil fields
    overwrite, and the audit metadata carries `updated_fields` as a
    stable wire-name list — never the submitted values. Following this
    template keeps the patch surface uniform.
  - The factory id ↔ ParseID mismatch is a real, long-standing
    integration-test breakage in store/apikeyservice_test.go that I had
    to navigate around. Worth a dedicated story to either fix the
    factory or relax buildAPIKeyToCreate the same way validateMembership*
    already does for member_id.
