## 2026-05-15 - BE-0087
- What was implemented: api_keys_update_policy_test.go — the
  authorization-matrix coverage for PATCH
  /v1/organizations/{org_id}/api-keys/{key_id}, sibling to the contract
  test in api_keys_update_test.go. Drives NewHandler + the real
  policy.NewEngine() (production request path, fake Authenticator and
  fake APIKeyUpdater only).
  - TestUpdateAPIKeyPolicyMatrixRoles: every built-in role
    (owner/admin/developer/viewer/ci/support) against the api-key scope
    apiKeyIDResolver emits for the principal's own org. Owner+Admin
    allow via ReasonAllowedByRole — proves the updater is reached with
    BOTH the {org_id} AND {key_id} path params and the response carries
    the mutated key. The other four deny with the stable
    ReasonDeniedNoCapability (CapAdmin > CapRead/CapDeploy/CapSupport),
    return 403 E_FORBIDDEN, the updater never runs, and the denied body
    never echoes the seeded key_id or prefix. Support is the
    load-bearing case shared with BE-0084: CapAdmin is excluded from
    the support cross-tenant exception, so support cannot mutate keys
    of its own home tenant either.
  - TestUpdateAPIKeyPolicyRevokedAndExpiredKeys: a Disabled principal
    (revoked or expired credential) whose underlying role would have
    allowed keys.manage is denied with ReasonDeniedPrincipalDisabled —
    the updater never runs and the body leaks neither the principal
    id, the organization id, nor the seeded key data.
  - TestUpdateAPIKeyPolicyWrongOrganizationPrincipal: a non-support
    cross-tenant owner is denied with ReasonDeniedCrossTenant; a
    Yalla-internal support principal is ALSO denied cross-tenant
    (CapAdmin excludes the support exception); engine verdicts are
    pinned directly against an api-key Resource so the resolver-shape
    is exactly what the engine sees. Bonus assertion: a support
    principal cannot mutate keys of its OWN home org either — the
    home-tenant verdict is ReasonDeniedNoCapability, not the
    cross-tenant denial. This locks the support role's posture so a
    future catalog edit cannot silently grant it key-management
    authority.
  - TestUpdateAPIKeyPolicyGrantContainment: the three grant-containment
    invariants the PRD names are pinned engine-level against
    project/env/service-scoped admin grants (sibling project, staging
    vs production, service vs parent-env-write), then tied back to the
    wire by proving a project-scoped grantee is denied PATCH on its
    own ({org_id},{key_id}) with ReasonDeniedOutOfScope, body free of
    key_id/prefix. An organization-level Admin grant DOES allow
    (ReasonAllowedByGrant) and the same patch end-to-end succeeds with
    the updater reached on both path params; an organization-level
    Viewer grant does NOT allow (locks CapAdmin against a downgrade to
    CapRead). A foreign {org_id} on a scoped grantee still surfaces
    ReasonDeniedCrossTenant — a scoped key cannot be smuggled across
    tenants by fabricating a path parameter.
- Files changed:
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/api_keys_update_policy_test.go (new).
  - ralph/prd.json (BE-0087 passes=true).
  - ralph/progress.txt (this entry).
- Verification run: gofmt -w .; go mod tidy; go vet ./...;
  go build ./...; go test -run 'TestUpdateAPIKeyPolicy' -race -v
  ./internal/controlplane/httpapi/...; go test ./...;
  go test -race ./....
- Results: all passed. The four new tests pass under -race in ~1.5s
  total. goimports/golangci-lint/staticcheck/govulncheck still not
  installed locally (documented in earlier iterations).
- Remaining risks / follow-ups:
  - Acceptance criteria "stable yalla.output.v1/error.v1 envelopes",
    "integration tests against isolated Postgres migrations", and
    "secrets/tokens/keys/cookies/env values redacted" are N/A for a
    policy-matrix test: no Postgres surface (the updater is faked) and
    no secret material crosses the boundary. The contract sibling
    (BE-0086) already pins envelope shape and the secret_hash sentinel
    is asserted out of the wire; here the structural guard is "denied
    response never echoes the seeded key_id or prefix", which it does.
  - BE-0086 (contract tests for PATCH ... api-keys/{key_id}) is
    already passes=true, so this story has no further blocker. Next
    open p1 in the queue is BE-0090 (policy matrix for DELETE
    /v1/organizations/{org_id}/api-keys/{key_id}), still gated on its
    p2 implementation story BE-0088.
- Learnings for future iterations:
  - The keys.read (BE-0084) and keys.manage (BE-0087) matrices are
    structurally identical because the catalog maps BOTH to CapAdmin
    (catalog.go:120-121). The wire-contract differences are: GET reads
    via APIKeyReader.GetAPIKey and asserts gotKeyOrgID/gotKeyID;
    PATCH writes via APIKeyUpdater.Update and asserts
    gotInput.OrganizationID/gotInput.KeyID. Use seedAPIKey for the
    happy-path response value either way — it is the credential
    helper, not the reader-vs-updater distinction.
  - apiKeyIDResolver emits Kind=domain.KindAPIKey, NOT
    KindOrganization like organizationIDResolver. The engine's
    cross-tenant clause cares about Scope.OrganizationID, not Kind,
    so the verdict shape is the same — but engine-level assertions in
    policy_test files must use the matching Kind to pin the exact
    resolver shape on the wire path.
  - The handler-comment claim in api_keys.go that "keys.manage is a
    CapManage action" is misleading commentary — there is no
    CapManage in capabilities.go. catalog.go is authoritative:
    ActionKeysManage requires CapAdmin. Trust catalog.go over
    handler-comment when they disagree.
  - For a denied-policy case driven through the wire, the request
    body must still be at least a structurally-valid PATCH ({"name":
    "renamed"}) so that the policy boundary is provably the only
    thing rejecting it — an empty {} body would hit the handler's
    "patch with no field" 400 if the policy ever let it through. The
    minimal-valid-body convention makes a future regression where
    the policy allows a denied caller surface as a 200, not a 400.
---

## 2026-05-15 - BE-0088
- What was implemented: DELETE /v1/organizations/{org_id}/api-keys/{key_id},
  the revoke-api-key customer-facing endpoint. Revocation is the customer-
  facing soft delete for an API key: the api_keys row stays in the database
  so the audit trail of who minted it remains linked to a live row, but the
  credential is permanently unusable from that moment on. Every subsequent
  authentication attempt fails through the uniform invalid-credentials path
  (the existing prefix-lookup -> IsUsable check rejects a revoked row).
  - internal/controlplane/store/apikey.go: changed APIKeyRepository.Revoke
    to return (APIKey, error) using UPDATE ... RETURNING so a caller can
    see the row Postgres just stamped (revoked_at + trigger-refreshed
    updated_at) without a follow-up Get. SQL-level idempotency preserved
    via COALESCE(revoked_at, $now); the no-row case maps to apierr.NotFound
    via errors.Is(pgx.ErrNoRows). The single existing test of the
    repository was updated; one other caller (credentials_test.go) was
    updated to discard the new row return value.
  - internal/controlplane/store/apikeyservice.go: added RevokeAPIKeyInput
    (OrganizationID, KeyID, plus the Actor*/RequestID/CorrelationID
    correlation set that the handler fills in from policy.PrincipalFromContext
    + telemetry.FromContext, same pattern as the create/update inputs).
    Added APIKeyService.Revoke(ctx, in, now): id-shape pre-check (kind-
    prefix guard mirroring Update); in tx — Get current row (tenant-
    scoped, so cross-tenant key_id is the same typed NotFound as a missing
    row), conflict early if current.IsRevoked() (apierr.Conflict, so a
    re-revoke is never a silent success that would write a misleading
    audit record), call repo.Revoke, append audit event with metadata
    {organization_id, revoked_at=RFC3339Nano} — no credential material,
    just non-secret structural values. now is plumbed in so tests pin
    time without monkey-patching time.Now.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/api_keys.go: added the APIKeyRevoker port
    (interface depends on store.RevokeAPIKeyInput so the handler stays
    unit-testable with a fake), revokeAPIKeyPayload wire DTO (single
    apiKeyResource — same stable wire shape as every other api-key
    endpoint, the secret hash is never projected), errNoAPIKeyRevoker
    typed-internal sentinel for the wiring-error path, and
    revokeAPIKeyHandler — a thin handler that reads
    policy.PrincipalFromContext + telemetry.FromContext, forwards to the
    revoker with the actor + correlation set, and renders 200 OK carrying
    the revoked row (mirrors members.Remove's terminal-view convention).
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/routes.go: registered the DELETE route
    in the data-driven route table — RequiresAuth=true,
    RequiredAction=keys.manage (CapManage — no cross-tenant support
    exception, mirror of PATCH semantics), resolver=apiKeyIDResolver (the
    same resolver PATCH uses, scopes the policy decision to the {org_id}
    path parameter), Method=DELETE, OperationID=revokeOrganizationAPIKey,
    tags=[api-keys], 200 default success status. newRouteTable signature
    gained apiKeyRevoker APIKeyRevoker.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/server.go: NewHandler signature gained
    apiKeyRevoker APIKeyRevoker (16th positional arg, last before logger),
    forwarded into newRouteTable. The "registering an authenticated route
    without authenticator/engine panics at startup" fail-fast posture
    naturally covers the new route too.
  - cmd/yalla-api/main.go: wires apiKeyService (same *store.APIKeyService
    that already backs creator + updater) into the new revoker slot — one
    APIKeyService satisfies APIKeyCreator + APIKeyUpdater + APIKeyRevoker.
  - Tests: 15 handler unit tests in httpapi/api_keys_delete_test.go drive
    the real NewHandler with a fake APIKeyRevoker — happy path (200 +
    revoked_at projected onto the wire + secret-hash sentinel proof),
    request/correlation id forwarding, not-found 404, already-revoked 409,
    store-validation 400, store-outage 5xx with cause redaction,
    unauthenticated 401, invalid-credentials 401, cross-tenant 403,
    viewer-denied 403, disabled-principal 403, nil-revoker internal,
    request-id propagation, OpenAPI publication invariant. 6 service
    integration tests in store/apikeyservice_test.go drive
    APIKeyService.Revoke against real Postgres — happy path (revoked_at
    stamped + audit event with non-leaking metadata + credential
    primitives untouched + name unchanged), already-revoked Conflict (no
    second audit row, row revoked_at unchanged), missing-key NotFound (no
    audit row), cross-tenant NotFound (target row untouched, neither
    tenant's audit log written), invalid-id-shape rejection (no
    transaction opens), and missing-actor-org Internal (not InvalidInput
    — a wiring error, never client input). The existing
    TestAPIKeyRepositoryRevokeIsIdempotentAndTenantScoped test was
    updated to consume the new (APIKey, error) signature and assert the
    returned row's revoked_at matches the first revocation timestamp on
    re-revoke.
  - All NewHandler callers (16 test sites + cmd/yalla-api/main.go +
    newRouteTable test sites) updated for the new positional arg; every
    existing test now passes the canned fakeAPIKeyRevoker{}.
- Files changed: internal/controlplane/store/{apikey.go,apikeyservice.go,
  apikey_test.go,apikeyservice_test.go,credentials_test.go},
  internal/controlplane/httpapi/{api_keys.go,api_keys_delete_test.go (new),

  me_test.go,me_contract_test.go,members_test.go,members_create_test.go,

  go test ./...; go test -race ./...; integration tests for the touched
  surface (TestAPIKeyServiceRevoke*, TestAPIKeyServiceUpdate*,
  TestAPIKeyRepositoryRevoke*, TestAuthenticator*) under
  YALLA_TEST_DATABASE_URL=postgres://yalla:yalla@127.0.0.1:5432/yalla.
- Results: all passed locally. goimports/golangci-lint/staticcheck/
  govulncheck still not installed; documented in earlier iterations. The
  TestAPIKeyServiceCreate* integration tests fail on this branch
  ALREADY (verified via `git stash` + re-run against parent commit) —
  pre-existing failure unrelated to BE-0088, the Create validator
  rejects something the test fixtures supply. Out of scope for this
  story; it should be tracked as its own bug.
- Remaining risks / follow-ups:
  - BE-0089 (contract tests for DELETE /v1/organizations/{org_id}/api-
    keys/{key_id}) and BE-0090 (policy matrix coverage for the same) are
    now unblocked; api_keys_delete_test.go already covers a good chunk of
    BE-0089's surface but those remain separate PRD stories.
  - Pre-existing TestAPIKeyServiceCreate* failures should be filed and
    fixed — they break the integration suite for everyone touching the
    api-key store.
  - "Record an audit event for every ... denied authorization decision"
    is still N/A at the per-endpoint level: RequireAuth does not yet
    emit audit events for denied decisions for any endpoint. The
    cross-cutting follow-up that lands denied-decision auditing in
    RequireAuth will pick up DELETE /v1/organizations/{org_id}/api-
    keys/{key_id} along with every other authenticated route.
- **Learnings for future iterations:**
  - When changing a repository method's signature (here Revoke's `error`
    return became `(APIKey, error)`), greppping ALL callers is mandatory
    — even test-only ones, because go test compiles them. The
    apikey_test.go + credentials_test.go updates were needed before
    `go build` would pass.
  - The "soft delete" surface for an API key is a one-way revocation,
    NOT the scheduled-deletion-with-deletion_scheduled_at pattern used by
    organization.delete. An API key's revoked_at column already exists
    and the auth path already enforces "revoked => unauthenticated", so
    revocation is the natural and immediate customer-facing soft delete.
    Return 200 OK with the revoked snapshot (mirror members.Remove), not
    202 Accepted (which is for asynchronous teardown like
    organization.delete schedules).
  - SQL idempotency is fine for the repository, but the customer
    endpoint should NOT silently succeed on a re-revoke — the caller's
    view of the lifecycle is stale, and a silent success would write a
    misleading audit record. Pre-check the current state in the service
    layer (Get -> isRevoked() -> Conflict) and let the repository remain
    silently idempotent for non-customer callers (workers, admin jobs).
    This mirrors organization.delete's scheduling pre-check exactly.
  - "Add policy matrix coverage" and "Add contract tests" stories
    skipped on the PRD don't necessarily mean the endpoint is
    under-tested. The BE-008x cycle pattern in this repo has been
    "implement + happy/edge tests in one commit, policy matrix in a
    follow-up". BE-0083/BE-0086/BE-0089 in particular cover OpenAPI/
    contract assertions that overlap heavily with the implementation
    commit's own tests.

## 2026-05-15 - BE-0090
- What was implemented: api_keys_delete_policy_test.go — policy-matrix
  coverage for DELETE /v1/organizations/{org_id}/api-keys/{key_id}.
  Mirrors the BE-0087 sibling (PATCH api_keys_update_policy_test.go) since
  both endpoints share action keys.manage + apiKeyIDResolver, only
  swapping the handler/helper/fake/input triplet:
    revokeAPIKeyHandlerFor / deleteAPIKey / decodeRevokeAPIKey /
    fakeAPIKeyRevoker / store.RevokeAPIKeyInput.
  Three Test functions, no new scaffolding:
    1. TestRevokeAPIKeyPolicyMatrixRoles — every built-in role
       (owner/admin/developer/viewer/ci/support) drives both the engine
       Decide call and the wired handler against ({org_id},{key_id})
       inside the principal's home org. CapAdmin holders allow via
       ReasonAllowedByRole; the rest deny via ReasonDeniedNoCapability,
       the revoker is never reached on a deny, and the denied body
       cannot echo the seeded key id or prefix.
    2. TestRevokeAPIKeyPolicyRevokedAndExpiredKeys — a Disabled principal
       (the auth-layer surface for both revoked and expired credentials)
       whose underlying role is Owner is denied via
       ReasonDeniedPrincipalDisabled; the revoker is never reached and
       the body never echoes principal id, organization, key id, or
       prefix. The "stale credential scorched-earths the tenant's CI
       keys" abuse is the load-bearing motivation.
    3. TestRevokeAPIKeyPolicyWrongOrganizationPrincipal — a non-support
       cross-tenant owner AND a Yalla support principal both deny against
       a foreign org with ReasonDeniedCrossTenant; engine verdict pinned
       directly. Bonus: support cannot revoke its OWN home-tenant keys
       either (ReasonDeniedNoCapability — RoleSupport lacks CapAdmin),
       which locks the support role's read-only-for-key-data posture
       against silent catalog drift.
    4. TestRevokeAPIKeyPolicyGrantContainment — sibling project,
       production environment, and parent-level secrets pinned via the
       project/env/service grant Decide calls; then the load-bearing
       extension: a project/env/service-scoped Admin grant does NOT
       cover the api-key scope of its OWN org (ReasonDeniedOutOfScope),
       so a leaked scoped CI key cannot be weaponised to revoke
       organization-wide owner keys. Tied back at the wire: project
       grantee gets 403 OutOfScope at its own ({org_id},{key_id}); an
       org-level Admin grant gets 200 with the revoker reached on both
       parameters; an org-level Viewer grant is denied (CapAdmin not
       CapRead); the same project grantee against a foreign {org_id} is
       still denied via the cross-tenant guard.
- Files changed: internal/controlplane/httpapi/api_keys_delete_policy_test.go
  (new), ralph/prd.json (passes: true), ralph/progress.txt.
- Verification run: gofmt -w .; go mod tidy; go build ./...; go vet ./...;
  go test -run 'TestRevokeAPIKeyPolicy' ./internal/controlplane/httpapi/;
  go test ./...; go test -race ./....
- Results: all passed. goimports not installed locally (PATH); gofmt is
  the source of truth and ran clean. golangci-lint, staticcheck, and
  govulncheck still not installed locally (documented in earlier
  iterations).
- Remaining risks / follow-ups: none for BE-0090. The same load-bearing
  property (a scoped key cannot widen to org-wide key.* operations)
  applies identically to keys.manage on POST /v1/organizations/{org_id}/
  api-keys/{key_id}/rotate (BE-0093, the next p1 in the queue), and the
  matrix structure here transplants cleanly with no new helpers.
- Learnings for future iterations:
  - The "Add policy matrix coverage" stories for the api-keys endpoints
    that share action keys.manage (PATCH/DELETE/rotate) are pure mirrors
    of one another — only the handler/helper/fake/input quadruple
    differs. Once BE-0087's structure existed, BE-0090 was a sub-200
    line edit-with-substitutions; rotate (BE-0093) will be the same.
  - DELETE has no body, so the "minimal valid body keeps the deny path
    indistinguishable from a would-have-succeeded request" caveat the
    PATCH file's preamble carries does NOT apply here. Replaced with a
    one-sentence note that the policy boundary fires purely off the
    {org_id} path parameter — keep this distinction when mirroring to
    other body-less mutating endpoints (POST .../rotate also has no
    body, so the same simplification applies there).
  - The "revoker received the path's key_id verbatim" assertion only
    holds for ALLOW cases — gotInput must be tested for empty fields on
    DENY cases (proves the policy boundary stopped the request before
    it reached the persistence layer). Mirror this everywhere:
    `gotInput.OrganizationID != "" || gotInput.KeyID != ""` is the
    structural anti-bypass invariant.
  - seedAPIKey returns a store.APIKey whose RevokedAt is nil by default;
    for the success-path assertions in a DELETE test the seeded row has
    to mimic the post-revoke snapshot, so the test stamps a deterministic
    revokedAt and assigns persisted.RevokedAt = &revokedAt before handing
    it to the fake. The PATCH equivalent did not need this because the
    success snapshot it returns is just the renamed row.

## 2026-05-15 - BE-0032
- What was implemented: internal/controlplane/reconcile, the pure
  reconciliation engine that classifies drift between Yalla's source-of-truth
  hierarchy and Dokploy's actual state into safe, dangerous, or unmanaged
  buckets and routes each through the matching port (Repairer / ReviewRecorder
  / UnmanagedRecorder).
  - doc.go: package overview that names the three drift kinds, the stable
    DriftReason vocabulary, and the Plan/Apply lifecycle the worker drives.
  - types.go: the DriftKind / ActionType / DriftReason / ResourceLevel
    enums; the Desired*/Actual* in-memory snapshot shapes (Organization ->
    Project -> Environment -> Service, with EnvVars and Domains on services);
    ServiceRef and DomainRef (resolved Yalla+Dokploy locators); UnmanagedResource
    and ReviewEvent payload structs. Closed-set Valid() guards on DriftKind
    so a future bug can't smuggle in a new kind silently. Value-free design:
    Reason/Level/Kind never carry env-var values or hostnames a customer
    submitted.
  - diff.go: the pure Diff(desired, actual) Plan function. Walks every level
    top-down with Dokploy-ID-keyed lookup tables, classifies divergence, and
    emits a stable sorted Plan. Safe drift (env_var_changed, env_var_missing,
    env_var_extra, domain_missing) -> Repairer; dangerous drift
    (service_missing / database_missing on a recorded Dokploy ID,
    domain_renamed, service_type_changed) -> Reviewer; everything in actual
    without a desired counterpart (project / environment / service / domain
    level) -> Unmanaged. The DesiredValue field is the only path an env-var
    value reaches an Action, and only ActionUpdateEnvVar consumes it.
  - reconcile.go: the Reconciler engine and its ports
    (DesiredStateReader, ActualStateReader, Repairer, ReviewRecorder,
    UnmanagedRecorder). New() fails fast with apierr.Internal on any missing
    port. Plan() validates the org ID (apierr.InvalidInput on blank /
    malformed / wrong-kind), preserves typed apierr from adapters, and
    upgrades untyped errors to apierr.StoreUnavailable (desired reader) or
    apierr.DokployUnavailable (actual reader). An organization whose
    DokployID is empty short-circuits to an empty plan — pre-provisioned
    state is not drift. Apply() is best-effort: every per-action failure is
    captured in Result.Failures with the error string scrubbed through
    output.Redactor; the loop only stops when ctx is cancelled. Reconcile()
    is the Plan+Apply convenience wrapper that emits one structured tick log.
  - AGENTS.md: documents the "value-free classification", safe-vs-dangerous
    boundary, deterministic-order, best-effort Apply, and "quarantine never
    delete" conventions for future adapters and follow-up reviewers.
  - Tests: 21 unit tests across diff_test.go and reconcile_test.go covering
    every acceptance test case explicitly — TestDiff_DeletedApp (dangerous,
    service_missing), TestDiff_RenamedDomain (dangerous, domain_renamed),
    TestDiff_ChangedEnvVar (safe, env_var_changed, with a "no value leaks
    into the classification fields" assertion), TestDiff_MissingDatabase
    (dangerous, database_missing — separate code from missing application),
    TestDiff_UnmanagedResource (unmanaged at service level). Plus
    no-drift baseline, missing-env-var safe ensure, extra-env-var safe
    removal, service-type change, unprovisioned-desired ignored, and
    deterministic ordering. Engine-level: TestNew_RequiresPorts (each missing
    port yields apierr.Internal), TestPlan_ValidationFailure (blank,
    malformed, wrong-kind org IDs return apierr.InvalidInput with typed
    FieldViolations, no port is called), TestPlan_NotFoundFromDesired
    (typed apierr.NotFound preserved, actual reader not called),
    TestPlan_AuthorizationFailureFromDesired (apierr.Forbidden preserved),
    TestPlan_UntypedDesiredErrorBecomesStoreUnavailable +
    TestPlan_UntypedActualErrorBecomesDokployUnavailable (untyped errors
    upgraded, token strings scrubbed), TestPlan_UnprovisionedOrganizationReturnsEmptyPlan,
    TestApply_RepairsSafeDrift (env-var Repairer call carries Secret=true,
    Reviewer not called, secret never reaches captured log buffer),
    TestApply_RecordsDangerousDrift (deleted app -> Reviewer, Repairer
    untouched), TestApply_MarksUnmanagedResource (rogue service -> Unmanaged
    only, locates parent without exposing it), TestApply_BestEffortContinuesPastFailure
    (one Repairer error doesn't stop the rest of the plan, Failure.Err is
    scrubbed through Redactor with no upstream token leak),
    TestApply_CancelledContextStopsLoop (ctx.Err() returned mid-loop),
    TestApply_UnmanagedActionWithoutPayloadIsCaptured (malformed-internal
    plan captured as Failure, not panic), TestApply_RedactsSensitiveErrorsInResult
    (Reviewer's upstream "Authorization: Bearer ..." string scrubbed in
    Failure.Err and in the captured log).
- Files changed: internal/controlplane/reconcile/{doc.go,types.go,diff.go,
  reconcile.go,diff_test.go,reconcile_test.go,AGENTS.md} (all new),
  ralph/prd.json, ralph/progress.txt.
- Verification run: gofmt -w .; go mod tidy; go build ./...; go vet ./...;
  go test ./...; go test -race ./....
- Results: all passed. goimports binary not installed locally (documented in
  earlier iterations); golangci-lint, staticcheck, govulncheck still not
  installed locally.
- Remaining risks / follow-ups:
  - The acceptance criterion "stable yalla.output.v1 / yalla.error.v1
    envelopes" is N/A: reconcile is a pure service package with no HTTP
    surface. The taxonomy is enforced at the apierr boundary instead —
    every error the engine returns is either a typed apierr.* preserved
    from an adapter or a typed apierr.* produced by the engine
    (Invalid / Internal / StoreUnavailable / DokployUnavailable).
  - "Integration tests run against isolated Postgres migrations" is N/A
    here for the same reason: reconcile depends on no database. The
    store-backed DesiredStateReader and the store-backed Review/Unmanaged
    recorders will land as their own follow-up adapter stories once the
    service/application/domain persistence schema (currently
    not on disk past migration 0011) ships.
  - The Dokploy-backed ActualStateReader adapter is also a follow-up: the
    typed dokploy.Client today only exposes Ensure*/Get*/Remove operations,
    not bulk List operations needed to enumerate actual state. When that
    adapter lands it will add the List intents (or paginated reads) to
    dokploy/intents.go alongside the engine adapter.
  - The five "test cases" enumerated in the PRD acceptance criteria are all
    covered by named tests (deleted app, renamed domain, changed env var,
    missing database, unmanaged resource). Each test asserts both the
    classification (Kind/Reason) and the no-value-leak property, so a
    regression that started routing values into Reason or EnvVarKey would
    fail loudly.
- Learnings for future iterations:
  - Crockford Base32 (lowercase) excludes i / l / o / u — hand-rolled test
    IDs that contain "kvl" / "demo" / "old" / "ufo" fail domain.ParseID().
    Use domain.MustNewID(domain.KindX) in tests (the existing convention in
    internal/controlplane/dokploy/mapping_test.go and others) rather than
    string-literal IDs; this also future-proofs tests against ID-format
    changes. A test failure like
    "error code = E_INVALID_INPUT; want E_NOT_FOUND" where every port-side
    test fails with the same code is almost always the org ID validator
    rejecting the fixture before the port is reached.
  - apierr.Internal(cause).Error() returns the fixed Message string
    "an internal error occurred" only — the wrapped cause is reachable via
    errors.Unwrap, not via Error(). When a test wants to assert a redacted
    upstream string is captured, walk the chain with errors.Unwrap (see
    errChainString in reconcile_test.go) instead of inspecting
    err.Error() directly.
  - apierr.Forbidden/NotFound from an adapter must be PRESERVED, not
    re-wrapped — the engine's wrapDesiredErr / wrapActualErr only upgrade
    untyped errors and short-circuit on a *yerr.Error via errors.As.
    Otherwise the typed taxonomy collapses to "E_UNAVAILABLE" everywhere
    and callers lose the ability to act on the real reason.
  - For a Reviewer port, the action's Reason carries the classification
    code but the engine still needs to choose a Level for the ReviewEvent:
    LevelDomain for ActionReviewRenamedDomain, LevelService for everything
    else dangerous. Hard-code that mapping inside reviewEventFor; if it
    drifts, the review queue UX (not yet built) will route to the wrong
    triage view.
  - When the planner inserts a "service type changed" review action it
    still scans the service's env-var and domain children. Suppressing the
    children would lose drift signal the operator may want to see before
    deciding whether to adopt or destroy the type-changed service; emitting
    review-only actions for the service body keeps the deeper diff
    available without auto-repairing through a wrong-typed service.
---

## 2026-05-15 - BE-0091
- Implemented POST /v1/organizations/{org_id}/api-keys/{key_id}/rotate
  (Rotate API key) end to end through the same vertical the rest of the
  api-key endpoints follow: store-layer unit of work, narrow handler port,
  thin handler, route + OpenAPI metadata folded into newRouteTable, full
  test surface (contract/wire + policy matrix at handler, integration at
  store).
- Store layer:
  - apikey.go: APIKeyRepository.RotateCredential — `UPDATE api_keys SET
    prefix=$3, secret_hash=$4 WHERE organization_id=$1 AND id=$2 RETURNING ...`,
    tenant-scoped (a cross-tenant key_id matches no rows → typed NotFound,
    never a 5xx and never disguised as another tenant's key), nil-tx
    guarded, mapWriteError funnels a prefix-collision into the same typed
    Conflict the other writes produce.
  - apikeyservice.go: RotateAPIKeyInput + APIKeyService.Rotate. Validates
    id shape first (so an invalid id never opens a transaction), demands
    server-minted Prefix and SecretHash as a wiring-error Internal (the
    handler is responsible for minting through auth.Generate before
    delegating), then runs read-current-row → reject revoked/expired as
    typed Conflict → repository.RotateCredential → audit.Append, all in
    one transaction. The audit metadata carries the new public prefix
    (non-secret) as rotated_prefix; the secret hash is never recorded.
- HTTP layer:
  - api_keys.go: APIKeyRotator interface + rotateAPIKeyPayload (api_key +
    one-time plaintext token) + rotateAPIKeyHandler. The handler mints
    the new credential through auth.Generate BEFORE the store call so a
    crypto failure cannot leave the row in a half-rotated state, projects
    only the public Prefix on the response (no secret_hash on the wire),
    and surfaces the plaintext token through auth.Token.Reveal() exactly
    once — every subsequent read returns the projection without the
    token, so a token not captured at rotation time is unrecoverable.
  - routes.go: POST /v1/organizations/{org_id}/api-keys/{key_id}/rotate
    route entry, RequiresAuth=true, RequiredAction=keys.manage, resolver
    = apiKeyIDResolver. OpenAPI Description spells out the lifecycle
    contract (rotated credential body invalidates the old prefix lookup
    the moment the row is committed, revoked/expired is 409, cross-tenant
    is 403/404, no cross-tenant support exception for CapAdmin).
  - server.go: NewHandler takes apiKeyRotator APIKeyRotator. Threaded
    through newRouteTable to the new route.
- Binary wiring:
  - cmd/yalla-api/main.go: existing apiKeyService value satisfies the
    new APIKeyRotator port too, so the binary wiring is a single new
    positional argument (`apiKeyService` reused for revoker AND rotator).
- Tests:
  - httpapi/api_keys_rotate_test.go (BE-0091 contract + BE-0092 surface
    coverage at the wire): happy path projects the NEW prefix and surfaces
    the plaintext token exactly once, the handler mints the credential
    pair and forwards it verbatim (token prefix matches Reveal()), the
    secret_hash sentinel never reaches the wire and `secret_hash` is not
    even a field on the response, request_id propagation + envelope
    schema + OpenAPI registration with x-required-action=keys.manage,
    plus the full negative matrix (NotFound, revoked Conflict, expired
    Conflict, store validation 400, store outage 5xx with no driver
    cause leak, unauthenticated 401, invalid-credentials 401, viewer 403,
    disabled-principal 403, cross-tenant 403, nil rotator 5xx, bare-handler
    nil-rotator 500).
  - httpapi/api_keys_rotate_policy_test.go (BE-0093 authorization matrix
    @ p1): the production request path is driven by NewHandler + a real
    policy.NewEngine; tests pin the verdict at BOTH the engine layer
    (Decide) AND the wire (handler response + rotator-never-reached).
    Matrix covers owner/admin allow via ReasonAllowedByRole, viewer/
    developer/CI/support deny via ReasonDeniedNoCapability (with the
    explicit "support cannot rotate even cross-tenant nor own-tenant
    keys" property that locks CapAdmin out of the support cross-tenant
    exception), disabled principals (revoked/expired credential) deny
    via ReasonDeniedPrincipalDisabled, cross-tenant deny via
    ReasonDeniedCrossTenant, grant containment for project/environment/
    service scope (each denies the api-key scope via ReasonDeniedOutOfScope
    while an org-level Admin grant allows via ReasonAllowedByGrant; a
    Viewer grant at org level still denies because keys.manage is
    CapAdmin not CapRead). Every denied response is asserted not to leak
    the seeded key's id or prefix.
  - store/apikeyservice_test.go: 8 new isolated-Postgres integration
    tests for APIKeyService.Rotate — happy path (credential swapped in
    place, identity/ownership/scopes preserved, updated_at advances,
    audit record under the actor's home org with rotated_prefix as
    non-secret metadata and no secret_hash anywhere in metadata),
    missing key NotFound, cross-tenant key_id NotFound with the target
    row untouched and no audit trail on either tenant, revoked key
    Conflict (and the rejected rotate leaves the row's prefix
    untouched), expired key Conflict (the row's expires_at is stamped
    via direct UPDATE so this property is decoupled from Create's
    input validator — see the gotcha below), invalid id shape rejected
    as InvalidInput before any transaction, missing credential
    primitives rejected as Internal (wiring error, never InvalidInput),
    missing actor org rejected as Internal, and an integration property
    test proving FindByPrefix(old prefix) → NotFound and
    FindByPrefix(new prefix) → the same key id+org after rotation
    (auth-time lookup follows the new credential immediately).
- All existing httpapi tests updated to thread the new fakeAPIKeyRotator{}
  argument through NewHandler / newRouteTable. The fake is a shared
  zero-value type in api_keys_test.go next to fakeAPIKeyCreator/
  fakeAPIKeyUpdater/fakeAPIKeyRevoker — same idiom, same Got pointer for
  call-site verification.
- Files changed:
  - internal/controlplane/store/apikey.go (+RotateCredential repository method)
  - internal/controlplane/store/apikeyservice.go (+RotateAPIKeyInput type
    and +APIKeyService.Rotate orchestrator)
  - internal/controlplane/store/apikeyservice_test.go (+8 integration tests)
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/api_keys.go (+errNoAPIKeyRotator,

  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/server.go (NewHandler signature param)

    matrix suite)

    internal/controlplane/httpapi/server_test.go (newRouteTable call sites)

  go test ./... ; go test -race ./...;
  YALLA_TEST_DATABASE_URL=postgres://yalla:yalla@127.0.0.1:5432/yalla
  go test ./internal/controlplane/store/ -run Rotate -race ;
  go test ./internal/controlplane/httpapi/ -race.
- Results: all passed. goimports binary not installed locally
  (documented in earlier iterations); golangci-lint / staticcheck /
  govulncheck not available in this environment either.
- Remaining risks / follow-ups:
  - BE-0092 (contract tests for POST .../rotate) and BE-0093 (policy
    matrix coverage for POST .../rotate) are now unblocked — the new
    api_keys_rotate_test.go and api_keys_rotate_policy_test.go already
    cover much of those stories' acceptance surface, but they remain
    separate PRD stories.
  - There is a pre-existing factory/validator mismatch in the store
    layer: testutil.NewFactory produces ids with a 20-character suffix
    (e.g. `org_12be7c0a4209ea0b0001`), while domain.ParseID requires a
    26-character Crockford-base32 suffix. TestAPIKeyServiceCreateWith
    ServiceAccountAndExpiry already fails against a live Postgres for
    this reason (Create's validator calls domain.ParseID for org_id and
    created_by). All factory-id integration tests that bypass Create
    (using seedAPIKeyForUpdate's direct Insert) are unaffected. This is
    NOT introduced by BE-0091 — my Rotate tests deliberately seed the
    expired key via direct UPDATE on api_keys to be decoupled from the
    Create validator and demonstrate the Rotate path's own lifecycle
    precondition. Fixing the factory/validator mismatch is its own
    story.
- Learnings for future iterations:
  - The single-value APIKeyService satisfies four ports now
    (Creator/Updater/Revoker/Rotator). Adding a fifth verb is just a
    new method on APIKeyService, a new interface in api_keys.go, two
    new positional args (NewHandler + newRouteTable), one new route
    entry, and a single new positional arg in cmd/yalla-api/main.go
    that reuses the same `apiKeyService` value. Don't multiply
    services — the orchestrator is the unit of work boundary, not the
    handler-facing port.
  - "Rotate" is credential-body in place: identity, ownership, scopes,
    and lifecycle stamps are immutable across the call. The plaintext
    token is shown exactly once (mirrors Create), and the old prefix
    is destroyed for authentication purposes the moment the row
    commits — proved end to end via FindByPrefix(old) → NotFound and
    FindByPrefix(new) → the same key id. This is the property the
    BE-0091 acceptance contract is built on; if a future change adds
    soft-rotate semantics (keep both prefixes for a grace period),
    that property would have to be relaxed deliberately.
  - When seeding an expired key for integration tests, stamp the row's
    expires_at via a direct `UPDATE api_keys` SQL rather than going
    through APIKeyService.Create with ExpiresAt set. Create validates
    its input ids through domain.ParseID, which currently disagrees
    with testutil.NewFactory's id shape (see remaining risks above);
    seedAPIKeyForUpdate uses APIKeyRepository.Insert directly and
    bypasses that validator, so an in-place UPDATE on the seeded row
    is the closest equivalent for adding lifecycle stamps without
    couplng the test to a separate latent bug.
  - When `NewHandler` and `newRouteTable` grow a port parameter, the
    grep target is wider than just `*_test.go`: a few files use the
    full-signature `NewHandler(...)` directly (organizations_create_test,
    organizations_test, me_test) and others use the multi-line form
    (api_keys_*_test, members_*_test, organizations_*_test) — and the
    *production* `newRouteTable` call sites (routes_test.go,
    server_test.go) need updating too. The four routes_test/server_test
    call sites use named arg-count placeholders like `nil, nil, nil, nil,
    nil, nil, nil, nil, nil, nil, nil, nil, nil, nil, nil` so they don't
    match the "fakeAPIKeyRevoker{}, nil)" pattern — a follow-up grep
    pass for "newRouteTable\\(" catches them.

## 2026-05-15 - BE-0093
- What was implemented: BE-0093 (policy matrix coverage for POST
  /v1/organizations/{org_id}/api-keys/{key_id}/rotate) was already landed
  alongside BE-0091 in commit 6aae878 as
  internal/controlplane/httpapi/api_keys_rotate_policy_test.go. This pass
  verifies the file fully satisfies the acceptance criteria and flips the
  PRD flag to passes: true; no source code changes were needed.
- Acceptance audit (file: internal/controlplane/httpapi/api_keys_rotate_policy_test.go):
  - TestRotateAPIKeyPolicyMatrixRoles drives every built-in role (owner,
    admin, developer, viewer, ci, support) through the production
    NewHandler + real policy.NewEngine() path. Owner/Admin allow via
    ReasonAllowedByRole; Developer/Viewer/CI/Support deny with
    ReasonDeniedNoCapability (keys.manage is a CapAdmin action, OUTSIDE
    the support cross-tenant exception). Denied responses are checked for
    leakage of the seeded key id / prefix.
  - TestRotateAPIKeyPolicyRevokedAndExpiredKeys covers Disabled principals
    surfacing as the "revoked key" / "expired key" auth states — both
    return 403 with ReasonDeniedPrincipalDisabled and never reach the
    rotator; the body is asserted not to leak principal id, org id, or
    seeded key id/prefix.
  - TestRotateAPIKeyPolicyWrongOrganizationPrincipal pins the cross-tenant
    boundary: a non-support owner of org_attacker rotating a key in
    org_victim is a deterministic 403 (ReasonDeniedCrossTenant); the
    rotator is never reached and the body never echoes the victim org or
    its seeded key data. Support is also pinned — both the engine verdict
    and the handler outcome — to confirm CapAdmin is excluded from the
    support cross-tenant exception (cross-tenant denial), and support in
    its OWN org is denied via ReasonDeniedNoCapability so a future catalog
    change cannot silently grant support key-rotation authority.
  - TestRotateAPIKeyPolicyGrantContainment proves grant containment
    (sibling project, production environment, parent-level secrets,
    sibling service) via project/env/service-scoped Admin grants — none
    of those scoped grants covers the api-key resource scope, so each is
    denied keys.manage with ReasonDeniedOutOfScope. The project-scoped
    grantee is then tied back to the wire: 403 with the stable
    out-of-scope reason, rotator never invoked, denied body never echoes
    the seeded key. The complementary positive lock — an
    organization-level Admin grant allows via ReasonAllowedByGrant while
    an organization-level Viewer grant denies — pins the CapAdmin
    requirement against the grant path. A final cross-tenant {org_id}
    case on the same scoped key confirms the cross-tenant guard fires
    over the out-of-scope guard.
- Files changed:
  - ralph/prd.json (BE-0093 passes: false -> true).
  - ralph/progress.txt (this entry).
- Verification run from repository root:
  - gofmt -l . -> clean
  - go mod tidy -> clean
  - go vet ./... -> clean
  - go build ./... -> clean
  - go test ./internal/controlplane/httpapi/ -run TestRotateAPIKeyPolicy
    -count=1 -> ok (0.4s)
  - go test -race ./internal/controlplane/httpapi/ -run
    TestRotateAPIKeyPolicy -count=1 -> ok (1.5s)
  - go test ./... -> ok
  - go test -race ./... -> ok
- Remaining risks or follow-ups: none for BE-0093. The full role + grant +
  cross-tenant + disabled-principal matrix is now locked behind the
  production request path for the rotate endpoint, mirroring the sibling
  BE-0090 (DELETE) matrix.
- Learnings for future iterations:
  - Policy-matrix-coverage stories for endpoints that ship paired with
    their authorization tests (e.g. BE-0091 landed both files in one
    commit) may already be implemented before their own iteration runs;
    before opening a new editor, grep for an existing
    <endpoint>_policy_test.go and verify it covers each acceptance
    criterion. If it does, the work is to flip the PRD flag, document the
    verification, and commit, not to rewrite the suite.
  - The keys.manage CapAdmin requirement is the lock that prevents support
    cross-tenant key mutation. Mirror it in any future CapManage action's
    policy matrix: assert support is denied cross-tenant AND in its own
    home org, since CapAdmin is excluded by construction from the engine's
    `required == CapRead || required == CapSupport` cross-tenant clause.

## 2026-05-15 - BE-0094
- What was implemented: GET /v1/organizations/{org_id}/limits, the quota
  surface's first customer-facing read endpoint. Returns the effective quota
  limits configured for the organization named by the {org_id} path
  parameter, in deterministic resource order. Each entry on the wire carries
  resource, limit_value, enforcement_mode, and source ('organization' for an
  override or 'plan_default' for an inherited plan default). Resources with
  no policy at either scope are unconstrained and omitted from the list.
  - internal/controlplane/store/quota.go: added QuotaScope (string values
    match quota_policies.scope_kind verbatim — QuotaScopePlanDefault,
    QuotaScopeOrganization), EffectiveQuotaLimit (Resource, LimitValue,
    EnforcementMode, Scope), and QuotaRepository.ListEffectiveLimits — a
    single DISTINCT ON (resource) query that returns the resolved limit per
    resource preferring the organization override over the plan default.
    Tenant scoped: only organization-row overrides for organizationID and
    plan-row defaults for the named plan are considered.
  - internal/controlplane/store/limits.go: new file with DefaultPlan,
    PlanLookup function type (same signature as quota.PlanResolver.Plan so
    placeholders are interchangeable without a cross-package dep), and
    LimitsReader — the store-backed adapter that composes QuotaRepository
    through Store.Read. NewLimitsReader rejects a nil store; a nil
    PlanLookup or an empty string from a configured lookup falls back to
    DefaultPlan so a misconfigured resolver cannot erase the floor every
    tenant is meant to inherit. ListEffectiveLimits resolves the plan and
    the limits inside one read-only transaction.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/limits.go: new file with errNoLimitsReader,
    LimitsReader port, listLimitsPayload + limitResource wire types,
    limitResourceOf projection, and listLimitsHandler. Wires action
    limits.read through organizationIDResolver (the standard reader-port
    pattern for organization-rooted GET endpoints).
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/routes.go: added tagLimits ("limits"),
    threaded limits LimitsReader through newRouteTable's signature between
    memberRemover and apiKeys, and registered the GET
    /v1/organizations/{org_id}/limits route entry with full OpenAPI
    metadata (operationId getOrganizationLimits, the deterministic-order
    contract, the source-field semantics, the omitted-on-no-policy
    contract, and the CapRead support-cross-tenant exception that policy
    BE-0096 will pin down).
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/server.go: threaded limits LimitsReader
    through NewHandler's signature between memberRemover and apiKeys, and
    updated the package doc comment.
  - cmd/yalla-api/main.go: wired store.NewLimitsReader(dataStore, nil) at
    startup and passed the result to httpapi.NewHandler.
  - internal/controlplane/store/limits_test.go: new integration tests
    against an isolated, freshly migrated Postgres — precedence resolution
    (org override beats plan default), empty result when no policy is
    configured, tenant scoping (org A's override never leaks into org B),
    LimitsReader composing the repository through Store.Read with the
    DefaultPlan fallback, custom PlanLookup picking the right plan default,
    and NewLimitsReader rejecting a nil store.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/limits_test.go: new contract tests with a
    fakeLimitsReader fake — stable yalla.output.v1 envelope (schema_version,
    request_id, deterministic field order/names), empty-list-not-null
    response, path-{org_id} forwarded to the reader verbatim, 401 from the
    auth middleware before the reader is touched, 403 from
    organizationIDResolver for a cross-tenant {org_id} before the reader is
    touched, 503 mapped from store.StoreUnavailable without leaking the raw
    cause, and 500 internal error when the handler reaches with a nil
    reader without leaking internal detail.
  - Every other test wrapper that calls NewHandler / newRouteTable was
    updated to thread fakeLimitsReader{} (or one more nil for the route
    table calls) between the membership remover and the api-key reader
    slots: me_test.go, me_contract_test.go, organizations_test.go,
    organizations_create_test.go, organizations_patch_test.go,
    organizations_delete_test.go, members_test.go, members_create_test.go,
    members_update_test.go, members_delete_test.go, api_keys_test.go,
    api_keys_create_test.go, api_keys_update_test.go, api_keys_delete_test.go,
    api_keys_rotate_test.go, server_test.go, routes_test.go.
- Files changed:
  - new: internal/controlplane/store/limits.go,
         internal/controlplane/store/limits_test.go,
         internal/controlplane/httpapi/limits.go,
         internal/controlplane/httpapi/limits_test.go
  - modified: internal/controlplane/store/quota.go,
              internal/controlplane/httpapi/routes.go,
              internal/controlplane/httpapi/server.go,
              cmd/yalla-api/main.go,
              17 httpapi test wrappers,
              ralph/prd.json, ralph/progress.txt.
- Verification run: gofmt -w .; go vet ./... (clean);
  go build ./... (clean); go test ./... (clean without Postgres);
  YALLA_TEST_DATABASE_URL=postgres://yalla:yalla@127.0.0.1:5432/yalla
    go test -count=1 -race -timeout=300s ./... — every package green
    except the pre-existing TestAPIKeyServiceCreate* / TestAPIKeyReaderGetAPIKey
    failures (documented in BE-0085's progress as the testutil.Factory
    20-char id vs domain.ParseID 26-char strict-check mismatch); verified by
    stashing my changes and reproducing the same failures on the baseline
    commit. The new BE-0094 tests pass against the same Postgres image.
- Results: all targeted tests green. goimports / golangci-lint / staticcheck /
  govulncheck not installed locally (documented in earlier iterations).
- Remaining risks / follow-ups:
  - BE-0095 (contract tests for GET /limits) and BE-0096 (policy matrix
    for GET /limits) are still passes:false and are the natural next two
    iterations. limits_test.go already covers a large chunk of BE-0095's
    criteria; BE-0096 is the dedicated cross-tenant / role-matrix file
    (the same pattern members_get_policy_test.go follows for members.read).
  - PlanLookup is wired with nil at the API binary today, so every tenant
    resolves to DefaultPlan = "default". When the plans table lands, the
    cmd/yalla-api wiring should pass a real PlanLookup (e.g.
    PlanRepository.GetPlanName) and the existing limits surface continues
    to work without an API contract change — the response field names,
    the source values, and the resource ordering are stable.
  - The pre-existing TestAPIKeyService* failure is still open. It is
    documented in BE-0085 as worth its own dedicated story; sidestepping
    is the correct local choice for BE-0094.
- Learnings for future iterations:
  - quota_policies.scope_kind takes literal string values 'plan_default'
    and 'organization' — I mirrored those values verbatim into the
    QuotaScope type and the wire field 'source', so projecting database
    -> wire is a single string cast. Using the same literal across layers
    avoids a translation table and a divergence opportunity.
  - DISTINCT ON (resource) ... ORDER BY resource, (scope_kind = 'organization') DESC
    is the cleanest one-query resolver for "org override beats plan
    default, one row per resource, in deterministic resource order". The
    same shape generalises to any (scope, override) precedence resolution
    where the two scopes share a discriminator column.
  - The reader-port plumbing carries verbatim from BE-0061 (members.list):
    declare a narrow interface in httpapi (LimitsReader), build a
    *store.LimitsReader adapter that composes the repository through
    Store.Read (never fresh SQL), thread the port through
    NewHandler -> newRouteTable -> the handler closure. A new port adds ~13
    NewHandler call-site edits across the httpapi test wrappers (each
    test file that builds a handler), main.go, and server.go's package
    doc. perl -i -pe substitution over the canonical
    "fakeMembershipRemover{}, fakeAPIKeyReader{}" landmark is the
    least-error-prone way to apply a uniform insertion across all 17
    sites.
  - When the next port lands, consider whether newTestHandler in
    server_test.go could absorb its fake — the public-surface tests
    (~13 sites) all rely on the canned-fake pattern but don't need
    different fakes per file.
  - Plans table is still placeholder. The PlanLookup function type
    (signature mirroring quota.PlanResolver.Plan) lets a real plans
    table land later without touching the limits API contract or the
    LimitsReader adapter — the binary just wires a different lookup at
    construction time. Same idea applies to any "this column has no
    foreign key yet" placeholder.
  - apierr.AuthRequired does not exist; the auth-required factory is
    apierr.Unauthenticated and the resulting error code is "E_AUTH" (not
    "E_AUTH_REQUIRED"). The full code catalogue is in
    internal/errors/errors.go and the constructors are in
    internal/controlplane/apierr/apierr.go.
  - go test integration suites for store package are easy to verify
    locally: docker compose up -d postgres and
    export YALLA_TEST_DATABASE_URL=postgres://yalla:yalla@127.0.0.1:5432/yalla
    runs the migrated-DB skip gate. The pre-existing TestAPIKeyService*
    failure is reproducible on the baseline commit (git stash, run, pop)
    — that is the right way to disambiguate "introduced by my story" from
    "long-standing test infra mismatch".
