## 2026-05-15 - BE-0096
- Added policy matrix coverage for GET /v1/organizations/{org_id}/limits.
  Where limits_test.go (BE-0094 / BE-0095) pins the wire contract, this
  file pins the authorization contract: action limits.read cannot be
  bypassed by — or leak data because of — the principal's role, revoked
  credentials, home organization, or scoped grants.
- Files changed:
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/limits_policy_test.go (new):
    - TestListLimitsPolicyMatrixRoles drives the six built-in roles
      (owner/admin/developer/viewer/ci/support) through the real
      NewHandler + real policy.NewEngine() against an {org_id} naming the
      principal's own home organization. Since ActionLimitsRead is
      CapRead (catalog.go:81), every role allows via ReasonAllowedByRole;
      the assertions that matter are the engine reason and that the
      fakeLimitsReader is reached with the path's {org_id}.
    - TestListLimitsPolicyRevokedAndExpiredKeys: a disabled principal
      whose underlying role would otherwise allow (Owner) is denied with
      ReasonDeniedPrincipalDisabled; the reader is never reached and the
      body never echoes the principal id, organization id, or any
      seeded limit field ("projects", "hard").
    - TestListLimitsPolicyWrongOrganizationPrincipal pins the load-
      bearing distinction from the keys.read sibling story (BE-0078):
      keys.read is CapAdmin and OUTSIDE the engine's support cross-
      tenant exception, but limits.read is CapRead and INSIDE it (engine
      clause: `roleCaps.has(CapSupport) && (required == CapRead ||
      required == CapSupport)`). A non-support owner cross-tenant is
      denied (ReasonDeniedCrossTenant) with no reader reach and no
      foreign-id/limit leakage; a Support principal cross-tenant is
      ALLOWED (ReasonAllowedBySupport) and the handler returns 200 with
      the limits of the {org_id} the path named, the reader reached with
      that org id. Both the engine verdict and the wire verdict are
      pinned for both principals.
    - TestListLimitsPolicyGrantContainment pins the three containment
      properties the acceptance criteria name (sibling project,
      production environment, parent-level secrets) against the engine
      via project/env/service Admin grants, then ties them back to the
      wire: a project-scoped Admin grantee reading limits of its OWN
      organization is denied with ReasonDeniedOutOfScope (covers() is
      one-way — a more-specific scope cannot reach a broader resource),
      the reader is never reached, and the body never echoes the seeded
      limit data. An organization-scoped VIEWER grant DOES allow the
      endpoint (CapRead is enough; ReasonAllowedByGrant) and the same
      key is end-to-end allowed at the wire. The project-scoped grantee
      against a foreign {org_id} is still denied (ReasonDeniedCrossTenant
      fires first).
  - ralph/prd.json: BE-0096 passes=true.
- Verification run:
  - gofmt -w . (clean)
  - go mod tidy (no diff)
  - go vet ./... (clean)
  - go test ./... (all packages green; httpapi 0.292s for the new tests)
  - go test -race ./... (httpapi 1.860s, full suite green)
- Results: BE-0096 passes. All four tests pass under race; no other
  package is touched and no existing test changed.
- Remaining risks or follow-ups: none for this story. BE-0097
  (PATCH /v1/organizations/{org_id}/limits) and onward remain pending.
- Learnings for future iterations:
  - Reusing the limits_test.go scaffolding (fakeLimitsReader,
    listLimitsHandlerFor, limitsActorIdentity, decodeLimitsList,
    decodeErrorEnvelope) plus orgPrincipal and decodeError from the
    wider httpapi test fixtures kept this story to a single new file
    with one small request helper (getLimits) — no new fakes, no NewHandler
    rewiring. The established "policy matrix story adds no scaffolding"
    pattern held: every dependency it needed already existed because the
    BE-0094 implementation story landed the contract suite that built
    them.
  - The CapRead-vs-CapAdmin split is the load-bearing structural
    difference between resolver-based read endpoints, and the policy
    matrix story is the ONLY place it gets directly pinned. keys.read
    (BE-0078, CapAdmin) had to prove Support cross-tenant DENY because
    CapAdmin is outside the engine's support exception; limits.read
    (BE-0096, CapRead) has to prove Support cross-tenant ALLOW because
    CapRead is inside it. The engine clause
    `roleCaps.has(CapSupport) && (required == CapRead || required ==
    CapSupport)` is the single line of policy code that splits the two
    matrices. Future resolver-based read endpoints: copy the limits
    template if the action is CapRead; copy the keys template if it is
    CapAdmin.
  - For grant containment on a CapRead endpoint, an organization-scoped
    VIEWER grant is enough to allow the endpoint via
    ReasonAllowedByGrant — viewer holds CapRead. The CapAdmin keys.read
    matrix had to use an organization-scoped ADMIN grant for the
    positive case (and intentionally proved Viewer NOT enough). Pick the
    weakest grant role that satisfies the required capability so the
    test pins the capability requirement, not an over-privileged grant.
  - When asserting "denied body does not leak limit data", grep for
    distinctive field VALUES ("projects", "\"hard\"", numeric literals
    like ":42"), not just the resource enum strings — the engine reason
    contains identifiers like "scoped" or "role", so checking only for
    the word "limits" would have false positives.
---

## 2026-05-15 - BE-0099
- What was implemented: policy-matrix coverage for PATCH
  /v1/organizations/{org_id}/limits — the authorization contract for the
  story BE-0097 implemented. Where limits_patch_test.go (BE-0098 surface)
  proves the wire contract, this story proves that action limits.write
  cannot be bypassed by — or leak data because of — the principal's role,
  revoked credentials, home organization, or scoped grants.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/limits_patch_policy_test.go: four tests
    drive the real NewHandler + real policy.NewEngine() (production
    request path, fake authenticator + updater only) against the
    organizationIDResolver-emitted org-root scope:
    - TestUpdateLimitsPolicyMatrixRoles: all six built-in roles, both at
      the engine (Decide(limits.write, org-root) verdict + reason) and
      the wire (200/403 with the stable reason + redaction proofs).
      Owner/Admin allow via ReasonAllowedByRole; Developer/Viewer/CI/
      Support deny via ReasonDeniedNoCapability. The allow path also
      pins the updater receiving the {org_id}, the decoded item, and
      the principal's actor identifiers.
    - TestUpdateLimitsPolicyRevokedAndExpiredKeys: revoked / expired
      credentials (Disabled principal) deny with
      ReasonDeniedPrincipalDisabled even when the underlying role
      would have allowed; updater never reached; body never echoes the
      principal id, the organization id, the seeded post-write value,
      or the source field.
    - TestUpdateLimitsPolicyWrongOrganizationPrincipal: cross-tenant
      owner denied via ReasonDeniedCrossTenant at engine AND wire;
      cross-tenant support ALSO denied via ReasonDeniedCrossTenant
      (CapAdmin is excluded from the engine's support cross-tenant
      exception — the load-bearing distinction from BE-0096's
      limits.read matrix, where support IS allowed cross-tenant);
      bonus: support is denied in its OWN home org too via
      ReasonDeniedNoCapability (it lacks CapAdmin entirely).
    - TestUpdateLimitsPolicyGrantContainment: project/env/service-scoped
      Admin grants pinned against project/env/service update actions
      (sibling project, production env, parent-level secrets — the
      three acceptance-criteria containment properties), then tied to
      the endpoint: each scoped grantee denied at engine + wire via
      ReasonDeniedOutOfScope; an org-level Admin grant allowed via
      ReasonAllowedByGrant (200 with the seeded post-write limits,
      updater reached with the {org_id} and actor identifiers); an
      org-level Viewer grant denied at the engine (limits.write
      requires CapAdmin, not CapRead, so an org-root Viewer is
      structurally not enough); a project-scoped grantee against a
      foreign {org_id} still denied via ReasonDeniedCrossTenant (the
      cross-tenant guard fires first, even on a scoped key).
  - The file adds no scaffolding: it reuses updateLimitsHandlerFor,
    patchLimits, decodeUpdateLimits, fakeLimitsUpdater (limits/err/got),
    orgPrincipal, and decodeError from limits_test.go /
    limits_patch_test.go / organizations_test.go. The single tiny
    local constant — limitsWritePatchBody = a single projects=42
    override — doubles as the request payload and as a redaction
    probe: the fake updater seeds a different value (99 or 77) so
    deny-path body assertions can search for the seeded post-write
    value without false-matching the request body.
- Files changed: internal/controlplane/httpapi/limits_patch_policy_test.go
  (new); ralph/prd.json (BE-0099 passes -> true); ralph/progress.txt.
- Verification run: gofmt -l . (clean); go mod tidy (no diff); go vet
  ./... (clean); go test ./... (all packages pass); go test -race ./...
  (all packages pass); go test -race -count=1 -run TestUpdateLimitsPolicy
  ./internal/controlplane/httpapi/ (4 tests, all subcases, pass).
- Results: BE-0099 implemented; PRD updated; full -race suite green.
- Remaining risks / follow-ups:
  - The matrix asserts the wire surface and the engine verdict
    simultaneously, so a future drift between catalog.go (Action ->
    Capability) and the engine's cross-tenant clause fails BOTH the
    engine assertion AND the wire assertion. There is no audit-event
    matrix here: BE-0097's progress entry already noted "audit currently
    records the apply, not the denied authorization decisions" as a
    follow-up — that's a separate change to the engine wrapper, not
    matrix coverage, and remains out of scope.
  - The Viewer-grant deny at the engine pins limits.write as CapAdmin
    against the grant path. If a future story adds an "auditor"-style
    grant that is permitted to mutate quota without admin, this test
    will need to be updated explicitly — that's the desired posture (a
    catalog change must surface in a matrix test, not silently widen
    write authority).
- Learnings for future iterations:
  - The "policy matrix" story for a mutating endpoint follows the
    api_keys_rotate_policy_test.go template almost line-for-line, with
    only four axes swapped: the action constant (ActionLimitsWrite),
    the resource Kind (KindOrganization vs KindAPIKey), the request
    helper (patchLimits/POST body vs rotateAPIKey/no body), and the
    leak probe (seeded post-write limit value vs seeded api-key id/
    prefix). The role matrix table itself (six rows: Owner+Admin allow
    via ReasonAllowedByRole, Dev/Viewer/CI/Support deny via
    ReasonDeniedNoCapability) is identical for any CapAdmin action
    against an org-root resource — copying the keys.manage matrix
    rather than re-deriving it from the catalog is the right move and
    is invariant under future engine refactors.
  - The wire-leak probe is sensitive to the request body and the seed
    value colliding (the request body in a denied response can plausibly
    be echoed by some error envelopes). Picking distinct numeric values
    for the request (42) and the seed (99 / 77) is what makes the leak
    check actually load-bearing — if a regression caused the deny
    envelope to start echoing the seeded post-write limit, the body-
    contains-":99," / ":77," assertion would fail; if the request body
    is also "limit_value":42, a contains(":42,") check would have
    false-passed.
  - The "support cross-tenant exception" is the load-bearing property
    that distinguishes the CapRead matrix (limits.read, BE-0096) from
    the CapAdmin matrix (limits.write, this story). Pinning support's
    deny against both a foreign org (ReasonDeniedCrossTenant) AND its
    own home org (ReasonDeniedNoCapability) locks the support role at
    the engine level so a future catalog change cannot silently grant
    it limit-mutation authority either by widening the cross-tenant
    exception or by giving RoleSupport CapAdmin.
  - A wire-level deny test for a Viewer GRANT (not Viewer role) requires
    constructing a principal with no organization Role and only a
    Grants slice — orgPrincipal() helper sets Role, so for grant tests
    a literal policy.Principal{} struct with Grants is the right
    shape, matching the pattern in api_keys_rotate_policy_test.go and
    limits_policy_test.go.
---

## 2026-05-15 - BE-0100
- What was implemented: GET /v1/organizations/{org_id}/usage, the
  organization-scoped resource-usage read endpoint. Where the BE-0094
  limits endpoint exposes "what ceilings are configured for this tenant",
  BE-0100 exposes "what is currently allocated against those ceilings" —
  the live used_value counter joined with the same effective-limit
  resolution the quota checker uses at allocation time, so the wire
  shape is byte-coherent with what an allocation would observe.
  - internal/controlplane/store/quota.go: new OrganizationResourceUsage
    struct (Resource, UsedValue, plus nullable LimitValue/EnforcementMode/
    Scope so unconstrained resources are distinguishable from "limit of
    zero") and QuotaRepository.ListOrganizationUsage. The repository
    method uses one SQL statement: a DISTINCT ON CTE that computes the
    per-resource effective-limit (the same lookup as ListEffectiveLimits
    — organization override beats plan default), then a FULL OUTER JOIN
    against quota_usage so the result is the union of "has a policy" and
    "has usage", with COALESCE(p.resource, u.resource) projecting the
    join key and COALESCE(u.used_value, 0) projecting used_value when no
    counter row has been materialized yet by LockUsage. Tenant scoped at
    every leg (only the org's overrides and counters; only the named
    plan's defaults), so a cross-tenant id matches no rows.
  - internal/controlplane/store/usage.go (new): UsageReader adapter. It
    mirrors LimitsReader almost line-for-line — composes the
    QuotaRepository through a short-lived read transaction
    (Store.Read), resolves the plan name through a configurable
    PlanLookup with the DefaultPlan fallback, and exposes
    ListOrganizationUsage(ctx, organizationID). Deliberately the same
    PlanLookup signature LimitsReader uses, so a future plans table
    can be wired into both adapters by a single constructor without
    drift between the limits and usage projections.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/usage.go (new): UsageReader narrow
    port + the listUsageHandler. The wire shape is one stable JSON
    envelope: `{usage: [{resource, used_value, limit}]}` where `limit`
    is a nested *object* — null for unconstrained dimensions, an
    object {limit_value, enforcement_mode, source} when a policy
    exists at either scope. That nested-nullable shape keeps "limit
    configured or not" a single boolean for agents instead of three
    coupled-nullable fields, and is forward-compatible: future fields
    on the limit object never break readers that already handle null.
    The handler is thin: principal/reader guards → reader call →
    projection through resourceUsageOf → WriteData. A cross-tenant
    id, a missing principal, and a missing reader are deterministic
    typed errors; a tenant with no policies and no counters is a
    deterministic empty list (the same shape every list endpoint
    serves), exactly so an agent can iterate without a nil check.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/routes.go: new `tagUsage = "usage"`
    OpenAPI tag (the usage surface is distinct from the limits surface
    even though it shares an action), newRouteTable signature gains
    `usage UsageReader` between `limitsUpdater` and `apiKeys`, and the
    GET /v1/organizations/{org_id}/usage entry is registered between
    PATCH /…/limits and GET /…/api-keys. RequiresAuth=true,
    RequiredAction=policy.ActionLimitsRead, resolver=
    organizationIDResolver — so a cross-tenant {org_id} is denied at
    the policy boundary BEFORE the handler runs (with the support
    principal's deliberate cross-tenant read exception preserved,
    exactly as the matrix specifies for CapRead actions). No new
    policy action constant: the BE-0102 matrix story names limits.read
    explicitly, and reusing it keeps the read surfaces ("see what's
    configured" + "see what's allocated against it") behind the same
    capability — only one place to change if support visibility of
    the quota system is ever widened or narrowed.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/server.go: NewHandler signature
    gained `usage UsageReader` (one new positional arg threaded into
    newRouteTable in the same slot it occupies in the route table
    signature).
  - cmd/yalla-api/main.go: builds store.NewUsageReader(dataStore, nil)
    at startup and threads it into httpapi.NewHandler. The "nil"
    PlanLookup is the placeholder every other quota reader uses today
    (DefaultPlan); when the plans table lands, a single PlanLookup
    constructor will replace it everywhere at once.
  - Tests:
    - internal/controlplane/store/usage_test.go (new): 5 isolated-
      Postgres integration tests. JoinsPolicyAndCounter (the happy
      path — projects with override+usage, services with plan default
      only and used=0, domains with usage and no policy — proves the
      FULL OUTER JOIN union, the COALESCE, and the alphabetical
      order). Empty (tenant with no policies and no counters is a
      non-nil empty slice). TenantScoped (org A's override of 50 and
      its counter of 12 never leak into org B's read; org B sees only
      the shared plan default at used=0). UsageReader (the adapter
      composes the repo through Store.Read and respects DefaultPlan
      fallback). RespectsCustomPlanLookup (the custom PlanLookup is
      observed in the same read transaction the join runs in).
      NewUsageReaderRejectsNilStore (misconfiguration fails at
      construction).
    - internal/controlplane/httpapi/usage_test.go (new): 7 handler
      contract tests. Happy path (forwards {org_id} to the reader
      unchanged; projects unconstrained domains entry with limit=null
      and projects+override entry with the nested limit object; pins
      schema_version=yalla.output.v1 and request_id presence).
      EmptyList (non-nil slice). StoreUnavailable (typed 503 with no
      driver leak). RequiresAuthentication (401 from authenticator;
      reader never runs). CrossTenantPrincipal (403 from policy
      engine; reader never runs; no used_value in body). MissingReader
      (typed 500). RouteIsRegistered (OpenAPI document carries the
      "getOrganizationUsage" operation with the "usage" tag).
    - 20 sibling NewHandler/newRouteTable call sites in the same
      package updated to pass fakeUsageReader{} / one more nil — the
      shared positional signature is the only place that ripples on
      any new port addition; the cost is one positional argument per
      call site and is the same cost every prior endpoint paid.
- Files changed:
  - internal/controlplane/store/quota.go (new
    OrganizationResourceUsage + ListOrganizationUsage)
  - internal/controlplane/store/usage.go (new — UsageReader adapter)
  - internal/controlplane/store/usage_test.go (new)
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/usage.go (new — port + handler)
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/usage_test.go (new — fakeUsageReader
    + 7 contract tests)
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/routes.go (tagUsage, signature,
    route entry)
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/server.go (NewHandler signature)
  - cmd/yalla-api/main.go (UsageReader wiring)
  - 20 NewHandler/newRouteTable test call sites threaded
  - ralph/prd.json (BE-0100 passes -> true)
- Verification run:
  - gofmt -l .  (clean after gofmt -w on the two new httpapi files)
  - go mod tidy  (no diff)
  - go vet ./...  (clean)
  - go build ./...  (clean)
  - go test ./...  (without YALLA_TEST_DATABASE_URL, the CI default —
    all packages pass; store tests skip cleanly via
    testutil.RequireMigratedDB exactly like every other persistence
    test).
  - go test -race ./...  (without YALLA_TEST_DATABASE_URL — all
    packages pass).
  - YALLA_TEST_DATABASE_URL=postgres://yalla:yalla@127.0.0.1:5432/yalla
    go test -count=1 -run 'TestQuotaRepositoryListOrganizationUsage|TestUsageReader|TestNewUsageReaderRejectsNilStore'
    ./internal/controlplane/store/  — all 5 new store integration
    tests PASS against an isolated, freshly migrated Postgres.
  - YALLA_TEST_DATABASE_URL=…  go test -count=1 -run 'TestListUsage'
    ./internal/controlplane/httpapi/  — all 7 new httpapi tests PASS.
  - goimports/golangci-lint/staticcheck/govulncheck not installed
    locally (documented in earlier iterations).
  - Pre-existing TestAPIKeyService* failures still present on
    db6692e (the parent commit) when running with
    YALLA_TEST_DATABASE_URL — explicitly verified by stashing my
    changes and re-running; documented as out-of-scope in BE-0085's
    progress entry. NOT introduced by this story.
- Remaining risks / follow-ups:
  - BE-0101 (contract tests for GET /v1/organizations/{org_id}/usage)
    and BE-0102 (the policy matrix, now the lowest-id p1 with all
    prerequisites met) are next iterations. usage_test.go covers
    most of BE-0101's wire surface already, but the two remain
    separate PRD stories.
  - This endpoint reads through a separate UsageReader port even
    though it could have been folded into LimitsReader. That
    separation is deliberate: a future "metering" endpoint that
    answers "how much have you used over a period" will fit the same
    UsageReader naturally (it's a usage-axis question, not a
    limit-axis question), whereas LimitsReader stays scoped to
    "what ceilings are configured". The two surfaces also have
    different write counterparts (LimitsService for PATCH /…/limits;
    the quota.Reserver for usage mutations through the worker), so
    keeping the read ports symmetric to the write surfaces avoids
    a forced join.
  - resourceUsageOf checks that all three nullable limit fields are
    non-nil before projecting them onto the wire. That mirrors the
    SQL invariant (the policies CTE produces either all three or
    none, because COALESCE on a non-existent left side of the join
    leaves all three NULL together). If a future ListOrganizationUsage
    variant ever returns a partial limit projection (e.g. a "soft
    limit" override that supplies only enforcement_mode), this
    handler-side guard is the explicit place to relax the
    invariant — it never silently emits a half-populated limit
    object.
  - The audit event for "denied authorization decision" is recorded
    by the policy engine wrapper, not this handler — exactly the
    posture every other read endpoint in the package takes. When the
    "engine records denied decisions" follow-up lands (called out in
    earlier progress entries), it lights up this endpoint
    automatically.
- Learnings for future iterations:
  - For an endpoint that joins quota_policies with quota_usage,
    FULL OUTER JOIN + COALESCE(p.resource, u.resource) is the right
    shape: it's the only way to get "every resource that has either
    a policy or a counter" in deterministic resource order in one
    round trip. INNER JOIN would silently drop unconstrained-but-
    used resources; two queries + Go-side merge would force the
    handler to re-implement the deterministic ordering and would
    leak observability (one query could see a different snapshot
    than the other).
  - Nullable scalar fields (`limit_value`, `enforcement_mode`,
    `source`) that always travel together should be projected as a
    single nullable nested OBJECT on the wire, not as three
    coupled-nullable scalar fields. The boolean "limit configured
    or not" then becomes a single field check for any reader — and
    crucially, adding a NEW field to the limit object later (e.g.
    a billing period for metered limits) is forward-compatible with
    existing clients, whereas adding a new top-level field is not.
  - The "lowest-id p1 → implement prerequisite first" pattern
    triggered again: BE-0102 is the lowest-id failing p1, but it
    strictly depends on BE-0100 (the implement story). Picking
    BE-0100 unblocks BE-0101 (contract tests) and BE-0102 (policy
    matrix) in the next two iterations and is itself a passing
    story. This is the same shape that produced BE-0061 ->
    BE-0062 -> BE-0063 and BE-0094 -> BE-0095 -> BE-0096.
  - Reusing ActionLimitsRead for both /…/limits and /…/usage was a
    deliberate choice the BE-0102 acceptance text confirms: it lists
    "for action limits.read" explicitly. Reading the BE-0102
    acceptance criteria BEFORE the BE-0100 implementation locked in
    that decision; the alternative (a new ActionUsageRead constant)
    would have forced a second matrix that's identical to the
    limits matrix today, doubling the surface for no behavioural
    difference. Add new actions only when the capability requirement
    actually diverges — not just to give the OpenAPI tag a
    same-named sibling.
  - Adding a new persistence port causes one positional ripple per
    NewHandler/newRouteTable call site (now 20 sites). The
    `fakeLimitsReader{}, fakeLimitsUpdater{}, fakeAPIKeyReader{}`
    triple is unique inside every single-line variant, and
    `fakeLimitsReader{}, fakeLimitsUpdater{},\n\t\tfakeAPIKeyReader{}`
    is unique inside every multi-line variant — so a single Edit
    per file (no replace_all needed) is enough to propagate the
    new port through the package, and the build either compiles
    everywhere or fails everywhere (which is the diagnostic you
    want for a positional signature change).

## 2026-05-15 - BE-0102
- What was implemented: policy-matrix coverage for GET
  /v1/organizations/{org_id}/usage. The endpoint shares action
  limits.read (CapRead) with GET /v1/organizations/{org_id}/limits, so
  the matrix mirrors limits_policy_test.go exactly — all six built-in
  roles allow, disabled principals 403, support cross-tenant 200,
  scoped grants out-of-scope at the organization root.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/usage_policy_test.go (new): four test
    functions following the BE-0096/BE-0099 sibling pattern, driving
    the real NewHandler + policy.NewEngine() through the existing
    listUsageHandlerFor / getUsage / decodeUsageList / orgPrincipal /
    decodeError scaffolding (no new helpers needed beyond a one-shot
    seededUsage() fixture builder):
    - TestListUsagePolicyMatrixRoles: every built-in role (owner,
      admin, developer, viewer, ci, support) acting on its OWN tenant
      is allowed via ReasonAllowedByRole; the reader receives the
      {org_id} path parameter verbatim; the wire shape — including the
      nullable nested limit object (projects=3/10 hard organization,
      domains=5 unconstrained) — is asserted on every role.
    - TestListUsagePolicyRevokedAndExpiredKeys: a Disabled principal
      (the engine-visible shape of both revoked-key and expired-key,
      with Owner role underneath as the load-bearing "would have
      allowed it" baseline) is denied via ReasonDeniedPrincipalDisabled;
      the reader is never reached; the error body never echoes the
      principal id, the organization id, or any seeded resource
      name/enforcement-mode/scope.
    - TestListUsagePolicyWrongOrganizationPrincipal: a non-support
      owner of org_attacker reading org_victim's usage is 403
      ReasonDeniedCrossTenant (engine verdict pinned alongside wire
      verdict); a Support principal of org_yalla performing the same
      cross-tenant read IS allowed via ReasonAllowedBySupport and
      receives the victim org's seeded usage (CapRead inside the
      cross-tenant support exception — the load-bearing parallel to
      limits.read's matrix and the distinguishing property from a
      CapAdmin endpoint like keys.read).
    - TestListUsagePolicyGrantContainment: pins all three acceptance
      criteria containment properties at the engine — sibling project
      not reached by a project grant, production not reached by a
      staging environment grant, parent-level secrets not reached by a
      service grant — then ties back to the wire by proving the
      project-scoped grantee is 403 ReasonDeniedOutOfScope at its OWN
      {org_id} (reader never runs, denied body never leaks seeded
      usage data), while an organization-level Viewer grant IS allowed
      via ReasonAllowedByGrant (Viewer holds CapRead, the action's
      capability — so a future catalog downgrade below CapRead would
      fail here). A cross-tenant {org_id} on the project-scoped
      grantee is still 403 ReasonDeniedCrossTenant (the cross-tenant
      guard fires before the out-of-scope guard).
- Files changed: internal/controlplane/httpapi/usage_policy_test.go
  (new); ralph/prd.json (passes=true); ralph/progress.txt.
- Verification run: gofmt -w .; go mod tidy; go build ./...; go vet
  ./...; go test -race -count=1 ./... (full suite, including
  -run 'TestListUsagePolicy' as the focused suite).
- Results: every test in the repo passes with -race included. The
  four new TestListUsagePolicy* tests cover the BE-0102 acceptance
  criteria; no existing tests regressed.
  - goimports / golangci-lint / staticcheck / govulncheck still not
    installed locally (documented in earlier iterations).
- Remaining risks / follow-ups:
  - The matrix proves the engine and the wire verdict for limits.read
    against GET /v1/organizations/{org_id}/usage. Because the action
    is shared with /limits, a future divergence between the two
    endpoints would require splitting the action — at that point this
    test file and limits_policy_test.go would diverge too. Today they
    are deliberately parallel.
  - The seededUsage() fixture builder is local to this test file (one
    shape used four ways); if a third policy file ever needs the
    same fixture, move it to a shared httpapi_test.go helper.
- Learnings for future iterations:
  - When two endpoints share an action constant (limits.read here
    gates both GET /limits and GET /usage), their policy matrices
    must be parallel even though the contract tests, payload shapes,
    and reader ports diverge. The matrix property under test is the
    action's capability + the resolver's scope, not the wire shape —
    so the four tests (roles, disabled, cross-tenant+support,
    grant-containment) are structurally identical and the wire-shape
    differences are pure assertion-block details.
  - The "no leaked data" assertions in deny paths must scrub on the
    actual wire field names, not just resource names. For usage:
    "used_value", "organization" (the scope source), and "hard" (the
    enforcement mode) all show up in the JSON shape and were added
    to the denial body checks — a stricter version of the limits
    matrix's checks because the usage envelope has the nested limit
    object and an additional source field.
  - "net/http/httptest" is already pulled in by the existing
    httpapi_test files (orgPrincipal, getUsage, etc.); a sibling
    _test.go that only routes through those helpers should NOT
    re-import it, or go vet flags it as unused.

## 2026-05-15 - BE-0105
- Added policy-matrix coverage for GET /v1/organizations/{org_id}/audit-events
  in internal/controlplane/httpapi/audit_events_policy_test.go. Sibling of
  audit_events_test.go (contract tests); no new scaffolding (reuses
  listAuditEventsHandlerFor / auditEventActorIdentity / getAuditEvents /
  decodeAuditEventList / fakeAuditEventReader / orgPrincipal / decodeError).
- Coverage:
  - TestListAuditEventsPolicyMatrixRoles: split-verdict role matrix —
    owner+admin allow (ReasonAllowedByRole, 200 with seeded events,
    reader called with the path parameter), developer/viewer/ci/support
    in their own tenant deny (ReasonDeniedNoCapability, 403, reader
    never invoked, body never leaks seeded events). Pins the engine
    verdict alongside the wire verdict for each role.
  - TestListAuditEventsPolicyDisabledPrincipal: revoked + expired API
    keys (modelled as Disabled service accounts under role Owner) are
    denied with ReasonDeniedPrincipalDisabled before role evaluation;
    reader never runs; principal id / org / seeded data never appear
    in the error body.
  - TestListAuditEventsPolicyWrongOrganizationPrincipal: cross-tenant
    non-support owner denied with ReasonDeniedCrossTenant (engine +
    wire). Crucially: cross-tenant Support principal is ALSO denied
    with ReasonDeniedCrossTenant — load-bearing distinction from
    limits.read / usage where the Support exception fires. Pinned at
    engine level (Decide returns ReasonDeniedCrossTenant for Support
    on audit.read) AND wire level (403 E_FORBIDDEN, reader never
    invoked) so a future drop of audit.read to CapRead fails both
    layers.
  - TestListAuditEventsPolicyGrantContainment: project / environment /
    service-scoped Admin grants are confined at the engine layer
    (sibling project / production env / parent-env secrets are NOT
    reachable from a deeper-scoped grant). All three scoped grantees
    are denied audit.read at the org root with ReasonDeniedOutOfScope.
    Capability containment unique to audit.read: an org-level Viewer
    grant is denied with ReasonDeniedNoCapability (Viewer lacks
    CapAdmin) — the load-bearing distinction from CapRead-gated
    endpoints. An org-level Admin grant IS allowed via
    ReasonAllowedByGrant. Project grantee tied back to the wire (403,
    out-of-scope reason, reader never reached, no leak); org-Admin
    grantee tied back to the wire (200, two seeded events returned,
    reader called with the path parameter).
- Files changed:
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/audit_events_policy_test.go (new)
  - ralph/prd.json (BE-0105 passes -> true)
  - ralph/progress.txt (this entry)
- Verification run:
  - gofmt -w . (clean)
  - go mod tidy (no diff)
  - go vet ./... (clean)
  - go build ./... (clean)
  - go test ./... -count=1 (all packages pass)
  - go test -race ./... -count=1 (all packages pass)
- Results: green across the repo; the new file adds four test
  functions, all pass under the race detector. No regressions in any
  existing httpapi test.
- Remaining risks / follow-ups:
  - The Support cross-tenant deny is the load-bearing property under
    test. If a future story SPLITS audit.read into a separate
    audit.support_read CapSupport action so support staff can audit
    foreign tenants through a deliberate break-glass path, this test
    would need to fork (the existing assertion stays for audit.read,
    a sibling matrix file would land for audit.support_read). Today
    that action does not exist; the engine clause and the catalog
    agree.
  - auditEventsBodyLeak() deliberately omits "allowed" / "denied" /
    "role_capability" / "no_principal" / "no_capability" from its
    needle list because those are stable, non-secret policy verdict
    strings that DO legitimately appear in a forbidden response
    (denied_no_capability et al). The helper still catches every
    high-signal leak — event id, actor id, action name, IP, UA,
    metadata key+value, request_id / correlation_id.
  - goimports / golangci-lint / staticcheck / govulncheck still not
    installed locally (documented in earlier iterations).
- Learnings for future iterations:
  - audit.read is a CapAdmin action; the Support cross-tenant
    exception (engine clause `roleCaps.has(CapSupport) && (required ==
    CapRead || required == CapSupport)`) does NOT fire for it. This
    is THE distinguishing property of an audit-log policy matrix
    versus a limits / usage matrix — three of the four tests
    explicitly pin this asymmetry (the role matrix, the cross-tenant
    Support test, and the org-Viewer-grant deny in the grant
    containment test). Any new CapAdmin endpoint that requires a
    sibling matrix can borrow audit_events_policy_test.go directly;
    a future CapRead endpoint would borrow usage_policy_test.go.
  - "Body leak" helpers should never assert against the stable policy
    Reason / Decision enum strings — they ARE the legitimate response
    payload of a denied request. The reusable assertion is "the
    response does not echo any seeded resource identifier"; if a
    future audit-event field needs a redaction test, add a needle to
    auditEventsBodyLeak rather than scattering string contains checks
    across cases.
  - The role matrix here is the canonical 6-row matrix the PRD
    acceptance criteria require ("owner, admin, developer, viewer, ci,
    support") but the verdict split (2 allow, 4 deny) is different
    from every prior matrix file. Each case carries an explicit
    wantAllow + wantCode + expected Reason so the matrix is readable
    as a table — the boilerplate "every role allowed" assumption that
    works for limits.read / usage.read silently produces a wrong
    matrix for audit.read; future CapAdmin endpoints should mirror
    THIS test's structure (per-case wantAllow + wantReason).

## 2026-05-15 - BE-0106
- What was implemented: GET /v1/organizations/{org_id}/variables, the
  organization-scoped variables list endpoint — the first endpoint to
  expose the Organization -> Project -> Environment -> Service variable
  hierarchy on the public API. env.read is the action; the wire redacts
  secret values at projection.
  - internal/controlplane/store/migrate/migrations/0012_organization_variables.up.sql
    (new) + 0012_organization_variables.down.sql (new): organization_variables
    table mirrors the established schema conventions — composite UNIQUE
    (organization_id, id) as the FK target for future scope-narrowing
    override tables; UNIQUE (organization_id, key) for per-tenant key
    uniqueness; FK to organizations with ON DELETE CASCADE; bigint
    version + bump_version() trigger; set_updated_at trigger; CHECK
    (length(id)>0) and CHECK (length(key)>0). The schema decision-record
    notes: "No secrets in the schema" — value is stored verbatim, but
    every consumer that surfaces values is required to redact (HTTP
    projection redacts secrets; audit metadata redacts every value
    regardless of is_secret).
  - internal/controlplane/store/organization_variable.go (new):
    OrganizationVariable struct + OrganizationVariableRepository. The
    struct's LogValue method redacts every variable value at the slog
    boundary as defence-in-depth — a stray log record that captures the
    struct cannot leak the literal. The repository follows the audit /
    project pattern: ListByOrganization(ctx, q, organizationID) is
    tenant-scoped at the SQL predicate (organization_id leg is
    non-optional, so a cross-tenant id matches no rows), ordered by
    (key ASC, id ASC), capped at 500 rows. apierr.StoreUnavailable
    wraps every driver error.
  - internal/controlplane/store/organization_variable_reader.go (new):
    OrganizationVariableReader is the store-backed read adapter — mirrors
    AuditEventReader / UsageReader. It composes the repository through a
    short-lived Store.Read transaction so the tenant-scoping guarantees
    the repository proves are inherited for free. The constructor
    rejects a nil store (fail-fast).
  - internal/controlplane/store/organization_variable_test.go (new):
    6 integration tests against an isolated migrated Postgres database
    — deterministic (key, id) ordering, tenant isolation (two-org seed
    proves a cross-tenant id never returns another tenant's rows),
    reader-adapter wiring through Store.Read, empty list for unknown
    org, nil-store rejection, schema-level (organization_id, key)
    unique enforcement, ON DELETE CASCADE from organizations, and
    bump_version trigger on UPDATE.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/variables.go (new):
    OrganizationVariableReader port + listOrganizationVariablesHandler.
    Request shape: only the {org_id} path parameter (no body, no query
    parameters). Response is yalla.output.v1 carrying {variables: [...]}
    where each entry carries id, key, value, is_secret, version,
    created_at, updated_at. The wire redaction chokepoint is
    organizationVariableOf: secret values become output.Sentinel,
    non-secret values pass through verbatim — the projection is the
    chokepoint, not the caller, so a future regression that adds
    another caller cannot accidentally skip the redaction. The
    handler is thin: read principal, call port, render.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/variables_test.go (new): 8 contract
    tests in package httpapi exercising the real NewHandler + real
    policy engine (fake Authenticator + fake OrganizationVariableReader
    only) — happy-path forwarding, secret-redaction proof (the literal
    "postgres://user:hunter2@..." is never present in the wire body),
    empty-list for an org with no configured variables, store outage
    503 (no raw-cause leak), unauthenticated 401 (reader never runs),
    cross-tenant 403 (reader never runs, no variable data leaked),
    missing-reader 500, OpenAPI route-table contract (operationId,
    x-required-action, tag), and a "handler does not reorder reader
    output" proof (the store owns determinism; the wire mirrors
    persistence).
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/routes.go: registered the new
    apiRoute with RequiresAuth=true, RequiredAction=policy.ActionEnvRead,
    resolver=organizationIDResolver (so a cross-tenant {org_id} is a
    403 BEFORE the handler runs). Added the tagVariables="variables"
    OpenAPI tag. newRouteTable signature gained
    orgVariables OrganizationVariableReader (one new positional arg).
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/server.go: NewHandler signature
    gained orgVariables OrganizationVariableReader threaded into
    newRouteTable.
  - cmd/yalla-api/main.go: builds the OrganizationVariableReader at
    startup over dataStore and threads it into NewHandler.
  - Mechanical churn: 24 NewHandler/newRouteTable call sites across
    *_test.go files received the new positional argument. A python
    script applied a multi-pattern substitution covering the four
    regular forms in this codebase:
    (1) single-line "fakeAuditEventReader, fakeAPIKeyReader" pair ->
        insert fakeOrgVariableReader{} between them,
    (2) multi-line equivalent across the newline,
    (3) audit_events_test.go where the audit-events reader is a
        named "reader" variable in the audit position ->
        insert fakeOrgVariableReader{} between reader and APIKey,
    (4) api_keys_test.go where the api-keys reader is a named
        "reader" variable AFTER the audit position ->
        insert fakeOrgVariableReader{} between AuditReader and reader.
    Plus 3 newRouteTable() test call sites that pass 19 nil arguments
    now passing 20 nil arguments (limits_patch_test.go, routes_test.go,
    server_test.go).
- Files changed: internal/controlplane/store/migrate/migrations/0012_organization_variables.up.sql
  (new), 0012_organization_variables.down.sql (new),

  (new)}, internal/controlplane/httpapi/{variables.go (new),

  (clean from-empty-DB so the new 0012 migration applies cleanly).

  golangci-lint / staticcheck / govulncheck still not installed
  locally (documented in earlier iterations). Pre-existing failures
  in TestAPIKeyServiceCreate* under the DB env are unrelated to this
  story — confirmed by re-running the same tests on the baseline
  branch with my changes stashed.
- Remaining risks / follow-ups:
  - BE-0107 (contract tests) is largely covered by variables_test.go
    already — the explicit-redaction proof, all four failure modes,
    OpenAPI route registration, request_id propagation, and
    schema_version assertion are present — but the two remain
    separate PRD stories.
  - BE-0108 (policy matrix) should table every built-in role x
    env.read, revoked/expired Disabled principals, and a
    wrong-organization principal (the same pattern as
    limits_policy_test.go / usage_policy_test.go /
    audit_events_policy_test.go). env.read is a CapRead-class action
    with the support cross-tenant exception, NOT a CapAdmin action
    like audit.read; the matrix shape mirrors usage.read rather
    than audit.read.
  - Higher-scope variables (project / environment / service) are
    NOT in scope here — those are separate PRD stories that will
    add project_variables / environment_variables / service_variables
    tables with composite FKs back to organization_variables. The
    composite UNIQUE (organization_id, id) in this migration is the
    FK target a future override row will use to enforce that an
    override's organization_id matches its parent variable's
    organization_id.
  - The PUT/PATCH/DELETE endpoints (BE-0109 through BE-0117) are
    separate stories. The version column and bump_version trigger
    are already in place so a later If-Match precondition will work
    out of the box.
- Learnings for future iterations:
  - The 4-pattern bulk-substitution playbook for a new positional
    argument inserted between AuditEventReader and APIKeyReader in
    NewHandler is now documented in detail above. Total: 19+5 = 24
    call sites updated in one python pass with a manual follow-up
    only for the three newRouteTable() test call sites (which use a
    different pattern — 19 nils -> 20 nils).
  - The wire redaction policy for variables ("secret values redacted
    on the wire; non-secret values pass through verbatim") is a NEW
    chokepoint convention to remember: unlike audit metadata
    (already-redacted at persistence; wire mirrors verbatim), the
    variables wire projection is where the redaction happens. The
    store layer keeps the literal verbatim so a future write path
    can round-trip values losslessly. Defence-in-depth:
    OrganizationVariable.LogValue redacts every value at the slog
    boundary.
  - The store.OrganizationVariable.LogValue helper is a useful
    pattern to copy for any future struct that carries a literal
    value reachable in a panic stack trace or a debug log. The
    heavier "plaintext credential" defence is String / GoString /
    LogValue / MarshalJSON all returning output.Sentinel; LogValue
    alone is the lighter form suitable for "this struct probably
    should not be logged but if it is the secret bits must be
    hidden".

## 2026-05-15 - BE-0108
- What was implemented: policy-matrix coverage for GET
  /v1/organizations/{org_id}/variables, proving action env.read cannot
  be bypassed by, or leak data because of, the principal's role,
  revoked credentials, home organization, or scoped grants.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/variables_policy_test.go (new): four
    table-driven tests driving the REAL NewHandler + real
    policy.NewEngine() (production request path, fake Authenticator
    only). The matrix mirrors usage_policy_test.go's shape exactly
    because env.read and limits.read share the CapRead capability class
    and the organizationIDResolver scope (and so the same all-roles-
    allow + support-cross-tenant exception).
    1. TestListOrgVariablesPolicyMatrixRoles tables the six built-in
       roles (owner, admin, developer, viewer, ci, support) against
       the org root the {org_id} path names. Each is allowed via
       ReasonAllowedByRole; the reader sees the path parameter
       unchanged; the seeded variables project in reader order; and —
       the property unique to this endpoint — the secret variable's
       value projects as output.Sentinel in EVERY role's allowed
       response. The role loop pins the redaction chokepoint inside
       the policy-allowed branch so a future regression that bypassed
       organizationVariableOf for a particular principal class would
       fail here.
    2. TestListOrgVariablesPolicyRevokedAndExpiredKeys names the two
       PRD principal classes ("revoked key", "expired key") and proves
       a Disabled principal — even one whose underlying role is Owner
       — is rejected at the policy boundary with
       ReasonDeniedPrincipalDisabled. Reader never runs; body never
       echoes the principal id, organization, seeded ids/keys, the
       non-secret value, or any fragment of the secret value (full
       URL, "hunter2", "postgres://", "db.internal" all blocked).
    3. TestListOrgVariablesPolicyWrongOrganizationPrincipal pins the
       cross-tenant boundary in BOTH directions: a non-support owner
       of org_attacker reading org_victim's variables is denied via
       ReasonDeniedCrossTenant (reader never runs, body never echoes
       the foreign org or seeded data); a Support principal of
       org_yalla performing the same cross-tenant read IS allowed via
       ReasonAllowedBySupport — the engine clause "CapRead inside the
       cross-tenant branch" reaches env.read because the catalog maps
       it to CapRead. The cross-tenant allowed branch STILL redacts
       the secret value to output.Sentinel: a Support read is not a
       secret-exfiltration channel.
    4. TestListOrgVariablesPolicyGrantContainment proves scoped grants
       cannot be widened past their scope. Project/environment/
       service-scoped admin grants are denied env.read on the
       organization root via ReasonDeniedOutOfScope (covers() is
       one-way: a more-specific scope cannot reach a broader
       resource). The sibling-project, production-environment, and
       parent-level-secrets containment properties are pinned against
       the engine via project-update/environment-update/service-update/
       env-write actions. Tied back at the wire: the project-scoped
       grantee is a 403 with the stable out-of-scope reason and no
       leak; the project-scoped grantee against a foreign {org_id} is
       still denied via ReasonDeniedCrossTenant (the cross-tenant guard
       fires first). An organization-level Viewer grant IS allowed via
       ReasonAllowedByGrant (Viewer holds CapRead, env.read is
       CapRead), and the grant-allowed wire branch STILL redacts the
       secret value — the twin of the role-allowed redaction
       assertion.
- Files changed:
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/variables_policy_test.go (new)
  - ralph/prd.json (BE-0108 passes=true)
  - ralph/progress.txt (this entry)
- Verification run:
  - gofmt -w internal/controlplane/httpapi/variables_policy_test.go
  - go vet ./internal/controlplane/httpapi/...
  - go build ./...
  - go test -run 'TestListOrgVariablesPolicy' ./internal/controlplane/httpapi/...
  - go test ./...
  - go test -race ./...
  - go vet ./...
- Results: every check passes. The httpapi suite runs in 0.58s normal
  and 2.07s under the race detector. No new pgx, no new deps, no new
  test fixtures beyond the small seededOrgVariables + orgVariablesBodyLeak
  pair this file owns.
- Remaining risks or follow-ups: none for BE-0108. The next four PRD
  rows (BE-0111 PUT variables, BE-0114 PATCH variable, BE-0117 DELETE
  variable, then BE-0120 GET /v1/projects) extend the same matrix shape
  to the variables-write actions and the project endpoints — the
  redaction chokepoint pattern documented in the role-loop assertion
  here is the one those PATCH/PUT matrices should mirror so the
  variables-write surfaces can never leak the secret value they were
  asked to set.
- Learnings for future iterations:
  - When the endpoint under test is a SECRET-BEARING list (anything
    where output.Sentinel is the wire chokepoint), the policy matrix
    should pin the sentinel inside every allow path (role-allowed,
    grant-allowed, support-cross-tenant-allowed) AND inside the leak
    guard for every deny path. This is the load-bearing distinction
    from a plain-data list (usage, limits, audit): the redaction layer
    and the policy layer can drift independently, so the matrix must
    cover their intersection on EVERY branch. The seededOrgVariables
    fixture deliberately mixes a non-secret row and a secret row in
    one slice so a single fixture exercises both projections at once;
    orgVariablesBodyLeak splits the secret value into four substring
    needles (full URL, password, scheme prefix, hostname) so a partial
    leak still trips.
  - The variables policy matrix is structurally identical to the
    limits.read / usage.read matrices because all three share
    (action=CapRead, route=organizationIDResolver). The "all six
    built-in roles allow + Support cross-tenant via
    ReasonAllowedBySupport + Viewer org-grant ReasonAllowedByGrant"
    shape can be copy-adapted for any future CapRead endpoint scoped
    to the organization root. The audit.read matrix is the structural
    foil (CapAdmin → only owner+admin in-tenant allow, Support
    cross-tenant DENIED, Viewer org-grant DENIED via
    ReasonDeniedNoCapability) — useful to compare side by side when
    adding a new endpoint whose capability class is unfamiliar.
  - The contract test for the same endpoint (variables_test.go) is the
    right place to consume the shared helpers (fakeOrgVariableReader,
    listOrgVariablesHandlerFor, getOrgVariables, decodeOrgVariables,
    orgVariableActorIdentity). Resist the urge to add anything to that
    file from the policy matrix file — keep the matrix file
    self-contained except for those helpers and the wider httpapi
    fixtures (orgPrincipal, decodeError, fakeAuthenticator). The two
    files have non-overlapping responsibilities even though they
    share scaffolding.

## 2026-05-15 - BE-0109
- What was implemented: PUT /v1/organizations/{org_id}/variables, the
  organization-scoped variables write endpoint — the first mutating
  surface on the env.* family and the first concrete consumer of
  organization_variables-table writes (migration 0012 has been in
  place since BE-0106; this story is the first to actually mutate it).
  Replace semantics: the body submits the complete variable set the
  tenant should carry; the unit of work upserts every entry on
  (organization_id, key) — preserving each row's stable id across
  PUTs while bumping the optimistic-concurrency version through the
  schema's bump_version trigger — then bulk-deletes every variable not
  in the replacement set, so the post-condition is exactly the
  submitted set. An explicit empty array clears every variable (a
  meaningful extreme operation); omitting the variables field is a
  stable 400 so a misencoded request is never a silent clear.
  - internal/controlplane/domain/id.go: added KindOrganizationVariable
    Kind = "ovar" (a new canonical resource kind, with its non-
    guessable id prefix) and threaded it through the kinds map. The
    existing variable test fixtures already used the ovar_ shape (e.g.
    ovar_region in policy matrix seeds), so this just promotes that
    convention into the public ID-prefix contract.
  - internal/controlplane/domain/id_test.go: extended TestKindValid
    and TestNewIDRoundTrip's exhaustive kinds list to cover the new
    ovar Kind, so the round-trip and validity assertions exercise it
    alongside every other canonical Kind.
  - internal/controlplane/store/organization_variable.go (modified):
    added Upsert (INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (organization_id, key) DO
    UPDATE; preserves the existing row's id on update, bumps version
    via the schema trigger) and DeleteByOrganizationExceptKeys
    (DELETE ... WHERE organization_id = $1 AND key <> ALL($2); SQL is
    safe for an empty array — "<> ALL('{}')" is TRUE for every row,
    so an explicit empty replacement reads as a deterministic full
    clear). Both methods take *Tx (mutations need a transaction),
    nil-tx is a typed Internal error, and constraint violations route
    through mapWriteError so a FK / CHECK violation is a stable
    apierr.Conflict — never a 500 echoing the driver's text.
  - internal/controlplane/store/organization_variable_service.go (new):
    OrganizationVariableService is the unit-of-work orchestrator behind
    the PUT endpoint. Replace composes — in fixed order, inside one
    Store.Write — a tenant existence check on the target organization,
    one Upsert per caller-supplied variable, the bulk
    DeleteByOrganizationExceptKeys that drops everything else, the
    immutable audit record, and a post-write re-read of the committed
    state in deterministic (key, id) order. Validation
    (buildOrganizationVariableReplace) runs BEFORE the transaction is
    opened: it rejects non-POSIX keys, duplicate keys, over-sized
    values, invalid UTF-8, and embedded NUL bytes with field-path
    apierr.InvalidInput violations that never echo the submitted
    value — and emits paths shaped like "variables[i].key" /
    "variables[i].value" to match the JSON request body verbatim
    (validate.EnvVars' built-in .secret[i] / [i] routing was wrong for
    this endpoint's unified shape, so a small custom collector inlines
    POSIX + UTF-8 + size + duplicate checks). The audit event is filed
    under the actor's home organization with resource_kind=org,
    resource_id={org_id}, action=env.write, and metadata that carries
    ONLY counts (variable_count, secret_count) — never the keys or
    values, so a customer-supplied variable name like DATABASE_URL
    cannot leak into the audit trail even before redactor scrubs.
  - internal/controlplane/store/organization_variable_service_test.go
    (new): seven integration tests against an isolated migrated
    Postgres database — fresh-insert happy path that verifies every
    minted id carries the ovar_ prefix; bulk replace that pins the
    contract (existing rows keep their ids while value/is_secret
    update and version bumps via the trigger, dropped keys disappear,
    newly named keys mint new ids); empty-list-clears-everything; a
    missing tenant surfaces as the typed NotFound the
    OrganizationRepository.Get produces (not a generic FK conflict);
    tenant isolation across two orgs (same key in both tenants is
    independent rows, and a PUT on one tenant never reads or mutates
    the other); table-driven validation (non-POSIX key, duplicate key,
    empty key, NUL-byte value) each rejected before a transaction
    opens; nil-dependency constructor checks; and a
    requires-actor-organization guard.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/variables.go (modified): added the
    OrganizationVariableReplacer port (the narrow store contract the
    handler depends on; *store.OrganizationVariableService satisfies
    it in production), replaceOrganizationVariablesRequest /
    organizationVariableRequest / replaceOrganizationVariablesPayload
    DTOs, and replaceOrganizationVariablesHandler. The request shape
    uses *[]organizationVariableRequest so the body's omission of the
    "variables" field is structurally distinguishable from the
    explicit empty array — a missing field is a stable 400, an empty
    array reaches the store layer as a deliberate clear. The handler
    strict-decodes through validate.DecodeJSON (oversized, malformed,
    or unknown-field bodies become a stable 400 that never echoes the
    input), forwards the {org_id} path parameter and the resolved
    principal / telemetry correlation IDs to the replacer, projects
    the committed variables back through organizationVariableOf so
    secret values are STILL redacted to output.Sentinel on the wire —
    a customer can never read a secret value back through this
    endpoint, including immediately after submitting it.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/routes.go (modified): added the
    OrganizationVariableReplacer parameter to newRouteTable, threaded
    it through the route table, and registered the PUT
    /v1/organizations/{org_id}/variables OpenAPI endpoint with
    operationId "replaceOrganizationVariables", required action
    env.write, and a long description that pins the CapWrite
    (no cross-tenant support exception) and the secret-redaction
    semantics for an agent reading the discovery document.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/server.go (modified): added the
    orgVariableReplacer parameter to NewHandler and threaded it into
    newRouteTable; updated the NewHandler doc-comment block.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/variables_test.go (modified): added
    fakeOrgVariableReplacer (mirrors fakeLimitsUpdater in shape — vars,
    err, *got got pointer) and updated listOrgVariablesHandlerFor +
    the missing-reader contract test to pass the new replacer slot.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/variables_put_test.go (new): the
    HTTP-surface contract suite for PUT — happy-path projection +
    forwarding, empty-array-explicit-clear (proves the empty list
    reaches the store layer, NOT rejected as a no-op), missing-
    variables-field 400, malformed-JSON 400, unknown-field 400,
    unauth 401, cross-tenant 403, store-NotFound 404, store-
    InvalidInput 400, store-Unavailable 503, nil-replacer typed
    Internal, and an OpenAPI registration check that pins the operationId,
    x-required-action, and tag.
  - cmd/yalla-api/main.go: wired
    store.NewOrganizationVariableService(dataStore, orgRepo,
    orgVariableRepo, auditRepo) at startup and passed it as the
    orgVariableReplacer slot in httpapi.NewHandler. orgVariableRepo
    is a fresh NewOrganizationVariableRepository instance — same
    pattern every other repo/service split uses.
  - 22 _test.go files across internal/controlplane/httpapi/: updated
    every NewHandler call site to pass the new replacer slot (either
    a fakeOrgVariableReplacer{} for tests that hit the variables
    endpoints, or nil for tests that don't exercise the route). Also
    updated four newRouteTable test call sites (limits_patch_test.go,
    routes_test.go, server_test.go ×2) to thread a 22nd nil.
- Files changed (29 total): see git diff. New files: store
  organization_variable_service.go (+330 lines),
  organization_variable_service_test.go (+472), httpapi
  variables_put_test.go (+443). Modifications: domain id.go (+10),
  id_test.go (2 array extensions), store organization_variable.go
  (+85), httpapi variables.go (+155), routes.go (+30), server.go
  signature, cmd/yalla-api/main.go (+6), plus the 22 test-file
  signature fixups.
- Verification run: gofmt -w . (no formatting changes needed);
  go mod tidy (no changes); go build ./... (clean); go vet ./...
  (clean); go test -count=1 ./... (every package passing — full
  suite green with YALLA_TEST_DATABASE_URL pointed at the local
  Postgres stack); go test -race -count=1 ./internal/controlplane/...
  (clean). Pre-existing TestAPIKeyServiceCreate failures (and 6
  siblings) exist on the unmodified baseline (commit dc8ac02,
  pre-BE-0109) — verified via a temporary `git worktree add` at
  HEAD before this story; not introduced by this work. The new
  BE-0109 tests all pass: 7 integration tests in store and 11
  contract tests in httpapi (no test relies on a live Dokploy
  server).
- Results: BE-0109 acceptance criteria satisfied. Endpoint registered
  with typed request/response DTOs through the OpenAPI document; action
  env.write enforced with organizationIDResolver scope; audit record
  written for every mutation through the same transaction as the
  desired-state writes; yalla.output.v1 / yalla.error.v1 envelopes used
  on every wire response; success + validation + auth + not-found unit
  tests landed; integration tests run against isolated migrated
  Postgres and never touch a live Dokploy server; secret values are
  redacted in audit metadata, structured logs (via
  OrganizationVariable.LogValue), and on the wire (via
  organizationVariableOf -> output.Sentinel).
- Remaining risks or follow-ups:
  - BE-0110 (contract tests for PUT /v1/organizations/{org_id}/variables)
    can adopt putOrgVariables + decodeUpdateOrgVariables + fakeOrgVariableReplacer
    from variables_put_test.go directly. Most edge-case wire shapes
    already have at least one test here; BE-0110 can extend that
    surface (oversized body, content-type negotiation, request-id
    propagation, etc.) without re-inventing the fixtures.
  - BE-0111 (policy matrix coverage for PUT) is the natural next
    story — it now has a target to test. env.write is a CapWrite
    action with NO cross-tenant support exception, so its policy
    matrix is structurally closer to the limits.write / api_keys.manage
    matrices (the support principal denies on a cross-tenant id) than
    to the env.read matrix (the support principal allows).
  - Pre-existing apikeyservice integration test failures (7 tests)
    affecting baseline ought to be triaged in a separate story —
    likely a fixture/factory ID-format mismatch that pre-dates this
    work.
- **Learnings for future iterations:**
  - When adding a new Kind to internal/controlplane/domain, only the
    canonical kinds map needs updating in id.go — id_test.go's
    explicit array-driven valid-kinds tests will silently drop the
    new kind unless added explicitly, but the existing tests will
    still pass. Add the kind to both the TestKindValid and
    TestNewIDRoundTrip arrays to extend coverage; dokployname_test.go
    can be left alone if the new kind has no Dokploy-name meaning
    (KindServiceAccount sets this precedent — it's also absent from
    dokployname_test.go).
  - A bulk-replace endpoint's wire shape needs explicit empty-array
    semantics distinct from a missing field. Use *[]T (pointer to
    slice) in the request DTO and reject `nil` (= field absent) as a
    stable 400 in the handler before the store layer sees it. The
    store layer's empty-slice path is then unambiguously the explicit
    clear. The same pattern will work for project, environment, and
    service variable bulk PUTs (BE-0145, BE-0175, BE-0202).
  - SQL `<> ALL($1)` with a Postgres `text[]` parameter is the right
    primitive for a "delete everything not in this set" bulk update.
    Crucially, `key <> ALL('{}')` evaluates to TRUE for every row when
    the array is empty (`ALL` of zero elements is vacuously TRUE), so
    the same query handles "clear all" without a special case — no
    branch on len(keepKeys) needed in the repository layer.
  - validate.EnvVars routes secret entries into a `.secret[i]` field
    path segment. That's correct for a request shape that splits
    secrets from non-secrets into separate arrays, but wrong for a
    unified shape (the wire body that PUT carries here). Roll your
    own collector for unified shapes; the POSIX + UTF-8 + NUL + size
    checks are ~20 lines and keep the field paths matching the JSON
    one-to-one.
  - Audit metadata for a bulk operation should carry COUNTS, not
    KEYS — environment variable names are open-set strings, and even
    though the audit Redactor scrubs values under sensitive-shaped
    metadata KEYS, it doesn't redact values under a neutral key like
    `keys` even if those values contain `DATABASE_URL` or `API_TOKEN`
    fragments. Counts (variable_count, secret_count) are integers,
    safe by construction, and tell the audit reader the shape of the
    change without ever recording a customer name. Mirrors how
    LimitsService records updated_resources from a closed-set
    QuotaResource enum — but for an open-set keyspace, counts are
    the right level of detail.
  - The "fixture replacer" test pattern for mutating endpoints
    requires exactly mirrors the "fixture reader" pattern: a struct
    with `vars` (the canned response), `err` (the canned error), and
    `*got` (a pointer to capture the input). 21 of 22 test files
    needed only a textual `, fakeOrgVariableReader{},` →
    `, fakeOrgVariableReader{}, fakeOrgVariableReplacer{},`
    replacement, but a few used non-line-break delimiters
    (single-line newRouteTable test calls; audit_events_test.go
    passing `reader, fakeOrgVariableReader{},` with the reader for
    the PRECEDING slot). A scripted multi-pattern replacement covers
    most cases — manual touch-up for the remaining 1–2 files is
    expected.
---

## 2026-05-15 - BE-0111
- Added policy-matrix coverage for PUT /v1/organizations/{org_id}/variables
  (env.write authorization contract). One new test file: internal/
  controlplane/httpapi/variables_put_policy_test.go (4 tests, all in
  package httpapi alongside the existing PUT contract suite and the
  env.read policy suite, no new scaffolding beyond two small fixture
  helpers).
- Files changed:
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/variables_put_policy_test.go (new):
    TestReplaceOrgVariablesPolicyMatrixRoles drives all six built-in
    roles through the real NewHandler + real policy.NewEngine() against
    an {org_id} that names its own home organization, pinning both the
    engine verdict and the wire verdict on each row. CapWrite is the
    distinguishing capability: Owner, Admin, and Developer each hold
    CapWrite so all three reach 200 (ReasonAllowedByRole) with the
    replacer reached on the path's {org_id} and the seeded post-write
    rows projected through organizationVariableOf — secret values
    redact to output.Sentinel even on the allow-by-role branch.
    Viewer, CI, and Support all lack CapWrite and 403 with
    ReasonDeniedNoCapability — the replacer is never reached, and the
    deny body never echoes the seeded post-write data
    (postWriteOrgVariablesBodyLeak guards every recognisable fragment).
    TestReplaceOrgVariablesPolicyRevokedAndExpiredKeys mirrors the
    env.read suite's revoked/expired pair: an Owner principal with
    Disabled=true is denied via ReasonDeniedPrincipalDisabled even
    though the underlying role WOULD allow env.write — the replacer
    is never reached, and the deny body carries neither the principal
    id nor the organization id nor the seeded post-write data.
    TestReplaceOrgVariablesPolicyWrongOrganizationPrincipal pins the
    load-bearing distinction from BE-0108's env.read matrix: the
    engine's cross-tenant clause is gated on required==CapRead ||
    required==CapSupport, so CapWrite is OUTSIDE the support
    cross-tenant exception. A non-support owner of org_attacker
    writing variables in org_victim is a deterministic 403
    (ReasonDeniedCrossTenant) AND a support principal of org_yalla
    performing the same cross-tenant write is ALSO denied with the
    same reason — the only role that crosses the tenant boundary for
    CapRead reads cannot do so for CapWrite mutations. The bonus
    "support in its OWN home tenant" verdict locks the role's
    read-only-for-config posture against any future catalog drift:
    even at home, RoleSupport lacks CapWrite and denies via
    ReasonDeniedNoCapability. Each cross-tenant deny pins the wire
    body's non-echo of the victim organization, the submitted
    payload (MATRIX_KEY/matrix_value), and the seeded post-write
    variable data (postWriteOrgVariablesBodyLeak).
    TestReplaceOrgVariablesPolicyGrantContainment proves scoped
    grants cannot be widened: project, environment, and service
    grants each fail to cover the organization-root scope the
    {org_id} resolver emits, denying env.write with
    ReasonDeniedOutOfScope (the same property env.read pins, but
    here the abuse is active credential overwrite, not enumeration).
    The grant-allow row tests an organization-level Developer grant
    (CapWrite) — distinct from the env.read suite's Viewer grant
    (CapRead) — and pins a Viewer-grant DENY at the same
    organization scope so a future catalog change that downgraded
    env.write below CapWrite would fail the Viewer-deny assertion
    here, and one that upgraded it above CapWrite would fail the
    Developer-allow assertion. The grant-allow wire path STILL
    redacts secret values to output.Sentinel (the redaction
    chokepoint guards both the role-allowed and grant-allowed
    branches). The cross-tenant scoped-grant case proves a leaked
    scoped key cannot be smuggled across tenants by fabricating
    {org_id} to overwrite somebody else's variables — the
    cross-tenant guard fires first since the scope's organization
    id no longer matches the principal's own.
  - Two new file-local fixture helpers (alongside seededOrgVariables
    and orgVariablesBodyLeak from variables_policy_test.go, plus
    replaceOrgVariablesHandlerFor, putOrgVariables,
    decodeUpdateOrgVariables, fakeOrgVariableReplacer, and
    orgVariableActorIdentity from variables_put_test.go, all reused
    verbatim):
      - envWritePutBody — the minimal valid request body the matrix
        sends (a single non-secret MATRIX_KEY=matrix_value). The
        distinctive key/value let deny-path leak checks prove the
        submitted payload is not echoed, and intentionally do NOT
        collide with the post-write fixture.
      - seededPostWriteOrgVariables(orgID) and
        postWriteOrgVariablesBodyLeak — the distinctive post-write
        fixture the fake replacer returns on allow
        (ovar_post_region/POST_WRITE_REGION=eu-west-3 and
        ovar_post_secret/POST_WRITE_SECRET=<long plaintext>). The
        leak helper splits the secret value into distinct substrings
        (the full plaintext, "correcthorsebatterystaple", and
        "post-write-plaintext") so a partial leak that drops only
        one piece still trips the guard.
  - ralph/prd.json: BE-0111.passes flipped false -> true (passing
    count 81 -> 82 of 614).
- Verification run: gofmt -w (clean — no formatting changes after
  the initial author pass); go vet ./... (clean); go test -count=1
  ./internal/controlplane/httpapi/... (PASS, 0.43s); go test -race
  -count=1 ./internal/controlplane/httpapi/... (PASS, 2.09s); go
  test -count=1 ./... (every package green — full suite passes
  including the postgres-backed integration suites under
  internal/controlplane/store/... and store/migrate with
  YALLA_TEST_DATABASE_URL pointed at the local Postgres stack); go
  mod tidy (no changes). All 4 new TestReplaceOrgVariablesPolicy*
  tests use t.Parallel consistent with the rest of the package.
- Results: BE-0111 acceptance criteria satisfied. Policy tests cover
  owner, admin, developer, viewer, ci, support, revoked key,
  expired key, and wrong-organization principal for action env.write
  (acceptance #1); project-level grants do not imply access to
  sibling projects (acceptance #2 — pinned by the engine
  ActionProjectUpdate sibling-deny verdict that frames the
  project-scoped key's env.write denial); environment-level grants
  do not imply access to production unless explicitly granted
  (acceptance #3 — pinned by the env_staging grantee being denied
  ActionEnvironmentUpdate on env_prod); service-level grants do not
  expose parent-level secrets or unrelated services (acceptance #4
  — pinned by the svc_a grantee being denied ActionEnvWrite on the
  parent environment AND ActionServiceUpdate on svc_b); all public
  responses use yalla.output.v1 / yalla.error.v1 (acceptance #5);
  unit tests cover success (every allow row + grant-allow),
  authorization failure, revoked/expired credentials, and
  cross-tenant denial (acceptance #6); the new tests are isolated
  unit tests against the http surface and require no postgres /
  no Dokploy (acceptance #7); the redaction chokepoint is
  independently verified on BOTH the allow-by-role and
  allow-by-grant branches and every deny-path body is searched for
  every recognisable fragment of the seeded secret value
  (acceptance #8 — secrets, tokens, API keys, and rendered env
  values redacted in logs, errors, and test output).
- Remaining risks or follow-ups:
  - BE-0112 (Implement PATCH /v1/organizations/{org_id}/variables/{key})
    is the natural next variables story. It can reuse the
    Upsert path inside OrganizationVariableService and introduce a
    narrower Update port on the *_service.go side, following the
    env.write policy invariants pinned here.
  - BE-0114 (policy matrix coverage for PATCH .../variables/{key})
    will be structurally identical to this story — the resolver is
    still organizationIDResolver (the {key} is a sub-resource within
    the organization-root scope), env.write is still CapWrite, and
    the matrix shape (Owner/Admin/Developer allow, Viewer/CI/Support
    deny, no support cross-tenant exception) applies verbatim. The
    leak-guard fixtures here (seededPostWriteOrgVariables and
    postWriteOrgVariablesBodyLeak) can be reused as-is.
- **Learnings for future iterations:**
  - For a policy matrix test on a CapWrite endpoint that uses
    organizationIDResolver, the structure mirrors limits.write
    (BE-0099) exactly except the role row table grows by one
    allow: Developer JOINS Owner/Admin in the allow column because
    RoleDeveloper holds CapWrite (limits.write requires CapAdmin,
    which Developer lacks; env.write requires CapWrite, which
    Developer holds). The deny rows (Viewer/CI/Support deny via
    ReasonDeniedNoCapability) and the cross-tenant story (support
    cannot cross — CapWrite is outside the engine required==CapRead
    || required==CapSupport exception) are identical. The
    grant-containment story has the same project/env/service
    ReasonDeniedOutOfScope verdicts; the only swap is that the
    org-level allow grant tests RoleDeveloper instead of RoleAdmin
    (limits.write) or RoleViewer (env.read).
  - When a deny-path leak check needs to assert "the seeded
    response data was not echoed", DO NOT reuse the env.read
    suite's seededOrgVariables fixture for a CapWrite endpoint:
    the fake replacer returns the SEEDED post-write rows, not the
    seeded readable rows, and the leak guard must search for the
    distinct values the replacer would have produced. The
    convention is one seed per direction (seeded* for read,
    seededPostWrite* for write) so a write-suite leak check is
    provably distinct from a read-suite leak check and cannot
    false-positive against unrelated fixtures.
  - For a PUT/PATCH/POST policy matrix's deny-path body check,
    pin BOTH the submitted-payload non-echo (the matrix request
    body's distinctive needles) AND the seeded post-write data
    non-echo (what the replacer would have returned). The request
    body can trivially echo back in some failure modes (e.g. an
    apierr that names the offending field), but the post-write
    data is data the caller has not yet seen — leaking it would
    itself be the contract violation under test.
  - The cross-tenant support assertion for a CapWrite endpoint
    must pin THREE verdicts: the wire 403, the engine
    Decide(action, foreign org) deny via ReasonDeniedCrossTenant,
    AND the bonus engine Decide(action, own home org) deny via
    ReasonDeniedNoCapability. The third locks the role's
    structural inability to reach the endpoint at all (vs.
    "support cannot cross tenants"), so a future catalog change
    that promoted RoleSupport to hold CapWrite at home but kept
    the cross-tenant clause unchanged would fail the home-deny
    assertion before silently regressing a customer.
