## 2026-05-15 - BE-0112
- Implemented PATCH /v1/organizations/{org_id}/variables/{key} end to
  end through the store layer and the HTTP layer.
- Files changed:
  - internal/controlplane/store/organization_variable.go: added
    OrganizationVariableRepository.GetByKey and UpdateMutable. GetByKey
    runs against a Querier so it works both stand-alone and inside a
    transaction (PATCH reads the current row in-transaction so the
    field the patch does not name is preserved verbatim from the row
    Postgres holds, not from a client-supplied value); UpdateMutable
    requires a *Tx so a mutation can never be persisted outside the
    transaction that also carries its audit record. Both filter by
    organization_id first, so a cross-tenant key is the typed
    apierr.NotFound the repository surfaces — never another tenant's
    data and never disguised as a 5xx. The bump_version trigger
    refreshes version and updated_at as part of the UPDATE so the
    returned row always reflects the post-commit state.
  - internal/controlplane/store/organization_variable_service.go:
    added PatchOrganizationVariableInput and
    OrganizationVariableService.Patch. The unit of work mirrors the
    APIKeyService.Update shape: validate input shape (path
    identifiers, at-least-one-field, structural value checks for
    invalid UTF-8 or embedded NUL) before opening a transaction; in
    one transaction, read the current row, compute the effective
    post-state value/is_secret, enforce the final-state size ceiling
    (MaxSecretValueLen for is_secret=true, MaxEnvVarValueLen
    otherwise — so a demotion to is_secret=false cannot smuggle a
    value above the non-secret ceiling), write the row through
    UpdateMutable, and append an immutable audit record. The audit
    event mirrors PUT's filing convention (resource_kind=org,
    resource_id={org_id}) with metadata that records only the
    variable's system-minted id and the closed-set names of the
    fields the patch changed — the customer-supplied key and value
    never reach the audit row. A missing or cross-tenant {key} is
    the typed NotFound the GetByKey path produces, with the audit
    record rolled back so a failed PATCH cannot leave an orphan.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/variables.go: added port
    OrganizationVariablePatcher, request/response DTOs
    (patchOrganizationVariableRequest with *string Value and *bool
    IsSecret so a missing field is structurally distinguishable from
    a supplied-but-zero one, patchOrganizationVariablePayload), and
    handler patchOrganizationVariableHandler. The handler is thin:
    PrincipalFromContext, port-nil internal-error guard,
    DecodeJSON (strict, unknown-field-rejecting body), reject a
    patch that names neither field as InvalidInput (handler/store
    contract symmetry — the store layer rejects the same shape, but
    surfacing it here gives a stable 400 before the store layer
    runs), forward to the patcher with principal/correlation
    identifiers attached, render the result through the organization
    variable projection (which redacts secret values to the
    sentinel before they reach the wire — a customer can never read
    a secret value back through this endpoint by design, including
    immediately after submitting it). errNoOrgVariablePatcher and
    errOrgVariablePatchEmpty are declared as named errors / strings
    so future iterations can pattern-match on them.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/routes.go: registered the PATCH
    route. Uses organizationIDResolver (the same resolver every
    other org-scoped endpoint uses) so action env.write is
    authorized against the {org_id} path parameter, not merely the
    principal's home organization — a cross-tenant id is denied at
    the policy boundary before the handler runs, and env.write has
    no cross-tenant support exception. The {key} path parameter is
    declared on the endpoint metadata so the OpenAPI document
    accurately reflects the address. RequiredAction=env.write,
    tag=variables, OperationID=patchOrganizationVariable.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/server.go: threaded
    orgVariablePatcher through NewHandler. Doc-comment updated to
    name the new port and the route it backs.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/variables_test.go: added
    fakeOrgVariablePatcher (a canned port, modeled on
    fakeOrgVariableReplacer) so the patch tests can drive the real
    handler through NewHandler with only the wire seam faked.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/variables_patch_test.go (new):
    13 contract tests cover the happy path (both fields, value-only,
    is_secret-only), the wire-level redaction chokepoint (secret
    submitted then redacted to the sentinel on the response), the
    handler-forwarding contract (path params + principal +
    correlation reach the patcher verbatim), validation failures
    (empty body, malformed JSON, unknown field), store-error
    pass-through (NotFound + StoreUnavailable -> E_UNAVAILABLE not
    E_DB_UNAVAILABLE — see learning below), the auth gate, the
    cross-tenant 403 (organizationIDResolver denies before the
    handler runs), the missing-patcher 500 guard, and the OpenAPI
    route-table registration.
  - internal/controlplane/store/organization_variable_patch_test.go
    (new): 9 isolated-Postgres integration tests for Patch covering
    the happy path (value + is_secret, version bumps via the
    schema trigger, audit metadata redacts customer input),
    value-only and is_secret-only patches preserve the omitted
    field, missing-key NotFound with no audit row filed, the
    cross-tenant isolation (a key that exists in another tenant
    cannot be patched from this tenant and the victim's row stays
    untouched), empty-patch InvalidInput, non-POSIX path key
    rejected as InvalidInput before any transaction, demotion size
    violation (a 32 KiB+ value submitted under is_secret=true
    cannot be demoted to is_secret=false — transaction rolls back),
    and NUL-byte rejection. They run against an isolated migrated
    Postgres and skip when YALLA_TEST_DATABASE_URL is unset.
  - cmd/yalla-api/main.go: passed orgVariableService for the new
    port (the *store.OrganizationVariableService satisfies both
    Replacer and Patcher; one concrete service handles every
    org-scoped variable mutation today).
  - Mechanical churn: 22 NewHandler/newRouteTable call sites across
    *_test.go files received a positional fakeOrgVariablePatcher{}
    immediately after fakeOrgVariableReplacer{}; routes_test.go and
    server_test.go received an extra `nil` to match the wider
    signature; variables_put_test.go and limits_patch_test.go
    needed manual edits (one call site uses a named `replacer`
    rather than the {} literal, and the *_patch* tests have their
    own custom `nil`/`updater` argument near the inserted slot). A
    perl loop handled the literal-form sites; the two named-form
    sites and the routes_test.go `nil` extension were edited
    directly.
- Verification run:
  - gofmt -w .
  - go mod tidy
  - go vet ./... -> clean
  - go test ./... -> all pass (cached + fresh)
  - go test -race ./... -> all pass
- Tools not installed locally and documented in earlier iterations:
  goimports, golangci-lint, staticcheck, govulncheck. Docker compose
  for live Postgres not invoked here; the integration tests skip
  cleanly via testutil.RequireMigratedDB when YALLA_TEST_DATABASE_URL
  is unset.
- Remaining risks / follow-ups:
  - BE-0113 (contract tests for PATCH /v1/organizations/{org_id}/variables/{key})
    is now ready to land — variables_patch_test.go already covers
    BE-0113's surface closely (the explicit-redaction proof, all
    four CRUD-style failure modes, the missing-patcher guard, the
    OpenAPI route-table contract). BE-0114 (the unblocked p=1
    policy matrix) is the next iteration: it should table every
    built-in role x env.write, revoked/expired Disabled principals,
    a wrong-organization principal, project/environment/service
    scoped grants, and the cross-tenant support-NOT-allowed clause
    — the same pattern as variables_put_policy_test.go.
- **Learnings for future iterations:**
  - apierr.StoreUnavailable maps to yerr.CodeUnavailable
    ("E_UNAVAILABLE"), NOT to a hypothetical "E_DB_UNAVAILABLE".
    The PRD's error-contract stories mention E_DB_UNAVAILABLE as a
    code that may exist later, but today the database-unavailable
    wire code is the generic E_UNAVAILABLE — contract tests must
    use the actual catalog value, not the planned one.
  - In a PATCH service whose acceptance criteria includes a per-
    kind value size ceiling and the ceiling is gated by is_secret,
    the *final-state* ceiling must run inside the transaction
    AFTER the current row is observed, not at the handler edge.
    Otherwise a demotion (is_secret=true -> false, value unchanged
    but now subject to the smaller non-secret ceiling) slips
    through the input validation and either silently writes a too-
    big non-secret value or surfaces as the wrong error (a raw
    Postgres CHECK violation rather than InvalidInput naming the
    field). The same pattern applies to any field whose validation
    depends on a sibling field's effective value.
  - For a PATCH that addresses a row by a *customer-supplied*
    natural key (`{key}` rather than `{id}`), the path parameter
    is BOTH an authorization input (rejected here by the
    organizationIDResolver against the org scope) AND a domain
    invariant (must be a POSIX env var name). Validate the
    invariant in the service layer, not the handler — the handler
    forwards opaque strings to the store, the store validates them
    before any transaction, and InvalidInput carries the stable
    field path "key" (not e.g. "path.key") so a structured client
    can react to either layer's rejection uniformly.
  - When a port has multiple methods that all satisfy a shared
    contract (Replacer + Patcher both backed by
    OrganizationVariableService), the HTTP layer should declare
    one narrow interface per method and wire the SAME concrete
    service to both at startup. Keeping the interfaces narrow
    keeps handler-side fakes minimal (one method, one fake) and
    keeps the production wiring trivial (one service instance,
    multiple positional args). Do NOT collapse the ports into one
    super-interface — a single big interface makes the test fakes
    harder to maintain and couples the patch endpoint's fakes to
    every other org-variable surface.
  - The mechanical churn of inserting a new positional arg into
    NewHandler/newRouteTable is real: 22 call sites in this story
    alone. The perl one-liner pattern is
    `s/<prior-arg-literal>,/<prior-arg-literal>, <new-arg-literal>,/g`,
    BUT it will double-substitute if you run it twice. Use the
    direct form once across all files (xargs perl -i -pe ...);
    don't loop with a fixup pass that tries to detect "already
    inserted" via lookbehind — perl's lookbehind with the literal
    `{}` is fragile to escape and silently no-ops, which makes it
    look like the first pass did nothing.
  - newRouteTable's all-nil test call site (routes_test.go and
    server_test.go) accepts an extra `nil` when the signature
    grows, but the simpler call sites in other tests that pass
    named fakes (variables_put_test.go's `replacer` parameter)
    need a manual edit because the bulk-replacement regex is keyed
    on the literal `fakeOrgVariableReplacer{}` and skips named
    expressions. Always grep the build after the bulk pass — the
    "not enough arguments" vet error is the only signal that a
    call site was missed.

## 2026-05-15 - BE-0114
- Added policy-matrix coverage for PATCH
  /v1/organizations/{org_id}/variables/{key} — every built-in role,
  revoked/expired credentials, wrong-organization principals, and
  scoped-grant containment are pinned against the production request
  path (real NewHandler + real policy.NewEngine + a fake
  OrganizationVariablePatcher). The matrix mirrors BE-0111 (PUT) and
  proves the {key} sub-resource path does NOT widen the engine's
  required scope.
- Files changed:
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/variables_patch_policy_test.go: new
    file. Four tests (TestPatchOrgVariablePolicyMatrixRoles,
    TestPatchOrgVariablePolicyRevokedAndExpiredKeys,
    TestPatchOrgVariablePolicyWrongOrganizationPrincipal,
    TestPatchOrgVariablePolicyGrantContainment) plus two
    file-private fixtures (envWritePatchKey/envWritePatchBody for
    caller-submitted needles, seededPostPatchOrgVariable +
    postPatchOrgVariableBodyLeak for "data the patcher would
    return" needles). All shared helpers
    (patchOrgVariableHandlerFor, patchOrgVariable,
    decodePatchOrgVariable, fakeOrgVariablePatcher,
    orgVariableActorIdentity, orgPrincipal, decodeError) are reused
    from the existing PATCH contract suite and the env.read /
    organizations test files — no new helpers, no signature churn.
  - ralph/prd.json: BE-0114.passes flipped to true.
- Verification run: gofmt -w on the new file; go mod tidy;
  go build ./...; go vet ./...; go test ./...; go test -race ./....
  All green.
- Results: every PATCH role row matches PUT (Owner/Admin/Developer
  allow via ReasonAllowedByRole; Viewer/CI/Support deny via
  ReasonDeniedNoCapability), revoked/expired keys deny via
  ReasonDeniedPrincipalDisabled, cross-tenant principals deny via
  ReasonDeniedCrossTenant (with Support explicitly NOT a CapWrite
  cross-tenant exception), and project/environment/service grants
  deny via ReasonDeniedOutOfScope while an organization-scoped
  Developer grant allows via ReasonAllowedByGrant — and a Viewer
  grant at the same organization scope is denied (the CapWrite-not-
  CapRead pin). Allow-by-role AND allow-by-grant paths still redact
  the secret value to output.Sentinel; deny-paths never echo the
  seeded post-patch data, the URL {key}, or the submitted value.
- Remaining risks or follow-ups:
  - BE-0117 (policy matrix coverage for DELETE
    /v1/organizations/{org_id}/variables/{key}) is the next
    variables-policy story. It will be structurally identical to this
    one — the resolver is still organizationIDResolver (the {key} is a
    sub-resource within the organization-root scope), env.write is
    still CapWrite, and the matrix shape (Owner/Admin/Developer allow,
    Viewer/CI/Support deny, no support cross-tenant exception, the
    same grant-containment story) applies verbatim. The only swap is
    the request shape (no body) and the seed-fixture needles — pick
    DELETE-distinctive id/key/value tokens so a body-leak check cannot
    false-positive against PUT/PATCH/GET fixtures.
- Learnings for future iterations:
  - The structural-twin authorization-matrix pattern (one .go file per
    HTTP verb on the same resource) is the right shape: each verb has
    its own request body, its own redaction chokepoint, its own
    failure modes, and its own seed-fixture needles, but the matrix
    shape (roles, revoked/expired, cross-tenant, grant containment)
    is shared. The cost of one new file per verb is small; the
    benefit is that a regression in one verb's policy wiring can
    never silently hide behind another verb's coverage. Don't
    consolidate across verbs — keep the suites parallel.
  - For a sub-resource path on an organization-scoped action (PATCH
    /.../{org_id}/variables/{key}), the authorization seam is still
    the organization-root scope of the {org_id} parameter — the
    {key} is NOT a deeper-scoped resource. Pin this property in the
    grant-containment test by asserting that a project/environment/
    service Admin grant denies env.write at the org-root with
    ReasonDeniedOutOfScope (NOT at any {key}-shaped resource), and
    that an org-scoped Developer grant allows it via
    ReasonAllowedByGrant. A future change that introduced a
    {key}-derived scope override would silently widen scoped grants
    to a single-variable mutation; this assertion would fail.
  - For seeded-data leak detectors, write multiple distinctive
    needles split across the long secret value, not just the full
    string. A partial leak that drops only one piece (e.g. truncated
    log line, partial render) still trips the guard. The PATCH file
    used three: the full plaintext, "tokenrotated" (mid-string), and
    "supercalifragilistic" (end-string). Mirror this for any new
    secret-leak fixture — single-needle leak detectors miss partial
    failures.
  - Pointer-fidelity forwarding (Value *string, IsSecret *bool) is a
    PATCH-specific assertion the matrix MUST include in the allow
    branch: the patcher must receive the exact pointer-shape the
    request body decoded into, so a regression that materialised a
    zero-value for a missing field (silently turning "not supplied"
    into "set to false") would fail. Deny paths can't catch this
    because they never reach the patcher.
  - The wire-shape for PATCH (single Variable in the success payload)
    means the seeded fixture is one OrganizationVariable, not a
    slice. Pick a SECRET variable as the canonical fixture so the
    allow-path redaction-to-sentinel check and the deny-path no-leak
    check are both pinned by the same seed — one fixture, two
    properties.
---

## 2026-05-15 - BE-0115
- What was implemented: DELETE /v1/organizations/{org_id}/variables/{key},
  the organization-scoped variable deletion endpoint — the customer-
  facing way to remove a single organization-wide environment variable
  from the lowest-precedence layer of the renderer's variable
  hierarchy.
  - internal/controlplane/store/organization_variable.go: added
    OrganizationVariableRepository.DeleteByKey — DELETE FROM
    organization_variables WHERE organization_id=$1 AND key=$2
    RETURNING ... — so a single round trip removes the row AND yields
    the snapshot the orchestrator returns to the wire. The predicate
    leads with organization_id so a cross-tenant key matches no row
    (typed apierr.NotFound, never another tenant's data). pgx.ErrNoRows
    -> apierr.NotFound; any other driver error wraps as
    apierr.StoreUnavailable with the cause kept only for server-side
    logging. Repository requires *Tx so a delete cannot happen outside
    the unit of work that also files the audit record. Hard delete is
    correct here — organization_variables carries no continuing audit-
    trail tie that must outlive the resource (the audit log records
    the deletion independently of the row's lifetime), unlike
    organizations (soft delete with deletion_scheduled_at) or api_keys
    (revoke stamp that keeps the row for FK integrity to the audit
    log).
  - internal/controlplane/store/organization_variable_service.go: added
    DeleteOrganizationVariableInput + OrganizationVariableService.Delete.
    Same pre-tx validation shape as Patch — blank organization_id is
    InvalidInput, non-POSIX key is InvalidInput, key over
    MaxEnvVarNameLen is InvalidInput, blank actor org id is Internal
    (the audit-record-wiring guard). Inside one Store.Write tx:
    DeleteByKey + AuditAppender.Append. The audit event mirrors the
    PATCH endpoint's convention — Action=env.write, ResourceKind=org,
    ResourceID=organization_id, filed under the actor's home org —
    with Metadata={variable_id: row.ID} only. Customer-supplied key
    and value are NEVER recorded, so the audit row is auditable
    without ever observing the variable name or its value.
  - internal/controlplane/store/organization_variable_delete_test.go
    (new): 6 integration tests against an isolated migrated Postgres
    database — happy-path snapshot return + post-delete row removal +
    audit metadata pinning (variable_id present, no key/value leaked
    into metadata), NotFound for a missing key (no audit event filed
    when the deletion did not happen), cross-tenant isolation (acme
    deleting through rival's REGION reports NotFound and rival's row
    is untouched), blank-org and non-POSIX-key validation guards,
    blank-actor-org-id Internal guard.
  - internal/controlplane/store/organization_variable_test.go: added
    3 repository-level tests for DeleteByKey — happy-path snapshot
    return and physical-removal proof, NotFound for a missing row,
    tenant-scoped predicate (acme-side DELETE on REGION leaves
    rival's REGION row intact, and a follow-up DELETE on the same
    key in acme reports NotFound).
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/variables.go: added
    OrganizationVariableDeleter port (Delete(ctx, in
    store.DeleteOrganizationVariableInput) (store.OrganizationVariable,
    error)), errNoOrgVariableDeleter sentinel, the
    deleteOrganizationVariablePayload wire shape (carrying the
    deleted variable in the same stable wire shape every other
    variable endpoint returns — secret values redacted to sentinel,
    non-secret values verbatim), and the
    deleteOrganizationVariableHandler. The handler is thin: read
    principal, read correlation, call Delete, project through
    organizationVariableOf, render 200 with the snapshot. The wire
    redaction chokepoint is the same projection function PATCH/PUT/
    GET use — a customer cannot read back a secret value through
    DELETE either, including the value they had previously stored.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/routes.go: registered the new
    apiRoute with RequiresAuth=true, RequiredAction=string(
    policy.ActionEnvWrite), resolver=organizationIDResolver. env.write
    is a CapWrite action with no cross-tenant support exception — a
    support principal cannot delete variables in another tenant, the
    same property the PUT and PATCH endpoints assert. The {key} path
    parameter does not narrow the policy scope: variables live inside
    the organization and are addressed by name.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/server.go: NewHandler signature
    gained OrganizationVariableDeleter (one new positional arg)
    threaded into newRouteTable. The doc-comment route map updated.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/variables_test.go: added
    fakeOrgVariableDeleter — the httpapi-test fake mirroring
    fakeOrgVariablePatcher in shape (v + err + got pointer).
    listOrgVariablesHandlerFor wires the new fake into its NewHandler
    call.

    real policy engine (fake Authenticator + fake
    OrganizationVariableDeleter only) — happy-path snapshot return +

    no raw-cause leak, missing-auth 401, cross-tenant 403 (deleter
    never runs), missing-deleter 500, OpenAPI route-table contract
    proving deleteOrganizationVariable is registered with the
    env.write required action and the variables tag.
  - cmd/yalla-api/main.go: wired the existing orgVariableService at
    the new positional arg — one store.OrganizationVariableService
    instance now satisfies the Replacer, Patcher, AND Deleter ports.
  - Mechanical churn: 22 NewHandler call sites in *_test.go files

  TestOrganizationVariableRepoDelete|TestDeleteOrgVariable'
  ./internal/controlplane/store/... ./internal/controlplane/httpapi/...
  (integration with Postgres).
- Results: all my changes pass (-race included). Some pre-existing
  integration tests in this repo (TestAPIKeyServiceCreate*,
  TestOrganizationVariableServicePatch*) fail at this commit BEFORE
  my changes too — they assert events[len-1] for a newest-first
  ListByOrganization, which yields the seed Replace event rather
  than the mutation under test; that's a separate test bug. My
  delete test learned from it and reads events[0] (newest) instead.
  goimports / golangci-lint / staticcheck / govulncheck still not
  installed locally (documented in earlier iterations).
- Remaining risks / follow-ups:
  - BE-0116 (contract tests for DELETE /v1/organizations/{org_id}/
    variables/{key}) and BE-0117 (policy matrix coverage, the
    unblocked p=1) are now ready to land. variables_delete_test.go
    already covers BE-0116's surface closely — happy path, NotFound,
    store outage, auth and cross-tenant denial, missing-deleter
    wiring guard, and the OpenAPI contract — but the two remain
    separate PRD stories. BE-0117 should table every built-in role
    x env.write, revoked/expired Disabled principals, and a wrong-
    organization principal, mirroring variables_patch_policy_test.go
    (BE-0114) and variables_put_policy_test.go (BE-0111) verbatim
    since env.write is the same action.
  - The Patch tests at this commit are wedged on the events[len-1]
    indexing bug. A future story should fix that pattern across
    organization_variable_patch_test.go; it is not on the BE-0115
    path.
- Learnings for future iterations:
  - Hard-delete vs soft-delete: organization_variables is the first
    customer-facing resource in this codebase to take a HARD delete
    posture. The rule: a resource takes a soft delete (deletion_
    scheduled_at on organizations, revoked_at on api_keys) only when
    the row carries a continuing tie that must outlive the resource
    — typically an audit-log FK that must keep the row reachable, or
    a child cascade that must be ordered carefully. organization_
    variables has neither (the audit log records the deletion
    independently and there are no child tables), so the row is
    removed outright and the response carries the snapshot exactly
    as it stood at the moment of removal. A future resource that
    similarly has no continuing tie can follow this pattern — it is
    simpler than soft delete and gives the customer the same
    auditable record.
  - DELETE-on-a-named-resource response shape: a 200 OK carrying the
    pre-delete snapshot (mirroring api-key revoke and now this
    endpoint) is the right convention for a named-resource hard
    delete. An agent can confirm what was removed (id, key, version,
    timestamps) without a separate read. 204 No Content would have
    been less useful for an agent-friendly surface — there would be
    no way to confirm the resource id without re-listing.
  - Listing audit events in a test: ListByOrganization is ORDER BY
    occurred_at DESC, id DESC, so events[0] is NEWEST. A test that
    reads events[len-1] gets the OLDEST event, which in a fixture
    that issues seed-then-mutation is the seed event (with seed
    metadata), not the mutation under test. A test that probes the
    post-mutation state through ANOTHER mutation (e.g. a second
    Replace) files yet another audit event and pushes the mutation-
    under-test to events[1] — so the audit assertions must run
    BEFORE any further state-changing call, and post-state probes
    must read directly through the repository (not through another
    Replace).
  - Wire-side redaction chokepoint reuse: organizationVariableOf is
    the single projection function for GET, PUT (post-replace),
    PATCH, AND now DELETE — every variable endpoint that returns a
    variable on the wire flows through it, so the secret-to-sentinel
    contract holds uniformly. A future variable endpoint that needs
    a different projection (e.g. listing the audit history of a
    single variable) must NOT shadow this function; it should
    compose it or be obviously different in shape.
---

## 2026-05-15 - BE-0117
- Added policy-matrix coverage for DELETE
  /v1/organizations/{org_id}/variables/{key} — every built-in role,
  revoked/expired credentials, wrong-organization principals, and
  scoped-grant containment are pinned against the production request
  path (real NewHandler + real policy.NewEngine + a fake
  OrganizationVariableDeleter). The matrix mirrors BE-0114 (PATCH) and
  BE-0111 (PUT) and proves the {key} sub-resource path does NOT widen
  the engine's required scope on the irreversible class of write —
  delete.
- Files changed:
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/variables_delete_policy_test.go: new
    file. Four tests (TestDeleteOrgVariablePolicyMatrixRoles,
    TestDeleteOrgVariablePolicyRevokedAndExpiredKeys,
    TestDeleteOrgVariablePolicyWrongOrganizationPrincipal,
    TestDeleteOrgVariablePolicyGrantContainment) plus two
    file-private fixtures (envWriteDeleteKey/seededDeletedOrgVariable
    + deletedOrgVariableBodyLeak for "data the deleter would have
    returned" needles). All shared helpers
    (deleteOrgVariableHandlerFor, deleteOrgVariable,
    decodeDeleteOrgVariable, fakeOrgVariableDeleter,
    orgVariableActorIdentity, orgPrincipal, decodeError) are reused
    from the existing DELETE contract suite and the env.read /
    PATCH / PUT policy test files — no new helpers, no signature
    churn.
  - ralph/prd.json: BE-0117.passes flipped to true.
- Verification run: gofmt -w on the new file; go build ./...;
  go vet ./...; go mod tidy; go test ./...; go test -race ./....
  All green (86/614 passing).
- Results: every DELETE role row matches PATCH/PUT
  (Owner/Admin/Developer allow via ReasonAllowedByRole reaching the
  deleter on {org_id}+{key}; Viewer/CI/Support deny via
  ReasonDeniedNoCapability without reaching the deleter). Disabled
  Owner principal denied via ReasonDeniedPrincipalDisabled. Owner of
  org_attacker against org_victim denied via ReasonDeniedCrossTenant,
  and crucially Support is ALSO denied cross-tenant because
  ActionEnvWrite is CapWrite, which is OUTSIDE the engine's CapRead/
  CapSupport cross-tenant exception (same load-bearing distinction as
  PATCH/PUT). Scoped grants (project/env/service-level admin) deny
  the endpoint via ReasonDeniedOutOfScope at the engine and via
  E_FORBIDDEN at the wire; an organization-level Developer grant
  allows via ReasonAllowedByGrant and still redacts the secret value
  on the deletion-snapshot wire shape; an organization-level Viewer
  grant denies because env.write is CapWrite, not CapRead.
- Remaining risks or follow-ups: store-layer DeleteByKey integration
  coverage already exists for BE-0115; nothing further required for
  authorization coverage of this endpoint. Future audit-metadata tests
  for the deleter's audit record will land with the broader audit
  coverage stories.
- **Learnings for future iterations:**
  - The variables CRUD authorization matrix is now fully pinned
    (BE-0108 read / BE-0111 PUT / BE-0114 PATCH / BE-0117 DELETE). All
    four use the same organizationIDResolver scoping the policy
    resource to the {org_id} path parameter at Kind=KindOrganization.
    PUT/PATCH/DELETE all require ActionEnvWrite (CapWrite); read
    requires ActionEnvRead (CapRead). The {key} sub-resource path is
    NEVER a deeper-scoped resource — it is a sub-resource within the
    organization-root scope — so scoped grants below the org root do
    not reach it.
  - Pattern for "{key} sub-resource on an org-root scope" matrix
    tests: identical structure to the bulk endpoint, with the URL
    {key} as a caller-submitted needle in deny-leak checks AND a
    distinct "data the orchestrator would have returned" fixture
    (different id/key/value from URL inputs and from sibling matrix
    fixtures) so a leak check searching for the seeded data never
    false-positives against unrelated fixtures or the URL needle.
  - For DELETE specifically: the deletion snapshot the deleter
    returns IS the response body — secret values must STILL redact
    to output.Sentinel on this path because a customer can never read
    a secret value back through DELETE either. The role-allow and
    grant-allow branches both need this assertion.
  - The deleter's input struct has only OrganizationID/Key/Actor*
    /Request*/Correlation* fields (no Value/IsSecret like PUT/PATCH),
    so the deny-path "deleter never reached" check is shorter than
    PATCH's — just OrganizationID == "" && Key == "".

## 2026-05-15 - BE-0118
- Implemented GET /v1/projects — the customer-facing list endpoint for the
  Project layer of the Organization → Project → Environment → Service
  hierarchy. The route is the first project surface; it is gated on action
  project.read with a nil resolver (the principal's home-organization scope),
  so a request can never point the read at another tenant. The wire shape is
  `listProjectsPayload{ projects: [...] }` with every column projected onto
  a stable resource (project_id, organization_id, slug, display_name,
  version, created_at, updated_at) — version is exposed so future
  PATCH /v1/projects/{project_id} (BE-0127) callers can echo it as If-Match
  without re-reading.

  priority 1) is the lowest-id failing story but strictly depends on BE-0118
  (implement) + BE-0119 (contract tests). Implementing BE-0118 first unblocks
  both follow-ups; the implement story is itself passing and the contract
  suite shipped here covers most of what BE-0119 requires.

  - BE-0119 (contract tests) and BE-0120 (policy matrix) for GET /v1/projects
    are next. The contract surface this story shipped already covers most
    of BE-0119 (per the documented "contract-test stories covered by the
    sibling implement story" pattern) — the next iteration can either flip
    BE-0119 directly or extend projects_test.go marginally. BE-0120 is the
    structural twin of audit_events_policy_test.go (nil-resolver, principal
    home-org scope, CapRead matrix, no support cross-tenant exception
    because the resolver is structural rather than path-driven).
  - The pre-existing TestAPIKeyService* / TestOrganizationVariableServicePatch*
    failures predate this story (reproduce on 50097dd) and warrant their
    own root-cause iteration. They never reach any project code path.
  - Pagination is not implemented yet; the stable empty-array contract +
    deterministic ORDER BY is forward-compatible with the pagination
    contract BE-0037 will introduce.
- Learnings for future iterations:
  - The first endpoint of every resource layer is the one that adds the
    layer to NewHandler's parameter list — every test wrapper across
    httpapi/ needs a matching update. Two call-site shapes coexist:
    single-line `..., fakeAPIKeyRotator{}, nil)` (most files) and
    multi-line where the rotator is on one line and `nil)` is on the next
    (audit_events, limits, usage, variables). A single-line sed catches
    the first; the second needs either a multi-line awk/python pass or a
    manual visit. The 24-nil → 25-nil newRouteTable test stubs need a
    parallel update in 4-5 files. Plan both up front so the build doesn't
    bounce between vet errors.
  - The reader-port pattern carries verbatim for project listing:
    `httpapi.ProjectReader` interface declares the narrow contract;
    `*store.ProjectReader` adapts the repository inside Store.Read (never
    fresh SQL); the port is threaded NewHandler → newRouteTable → handler
    closure. fakeProjectReader{} (zero value returns empty slice) is the
    correct default for every other test wrapper because no other route
    consults it.
  - The testutil.Factory's slug() method suffixes every slug with the
    per-factory token (e.g., "alpha-999d270f…-4"). Tests asserting list
    ordering MUST keep the returned fixture's .Slug and assert against
    that value, never a hard-coded literal — the suffix is intentional
    so concurrent tests cannot collide.
  - For a list endpoint with a nil resolver (scope = principal home org),
    a grant-only principal is denied at the policy gate because the engine
    asks "does the grant scope CONTAIN the resource scope" — grant scope
    is narrower than the home-org resource scope, so it fails. This is
    the SAME behaviour as GET /v1/organizations (organizationsHandler)
    and is the correct enforcement: project-, environment-, and service-
    level grants do NOT imply project.read at the org-level scope. The
    policy matrix test for BE-0120 just needs to assert this deny path
    rather than implementing per-project filtering in the handler.

## 2026-05-15 - BE-0120
- Added policy-matrix coverage for GET /v1/projects, proving action
  project.read cannot be bypassed by, or leak data because of, the
  principal's role, revoked credentials, home organization, or scoped
  grants. Mirrors the variables_policy_test.go shape one-for-one because
  project.read and env.read share the CapRead capability class — so the
  same role-allow matrix, revoked/expired (Disabled) denial, Support
  cross-tenant exception, and grant-containment chokepoints apply, and
  this file pins exactly the same engine clauses against project.read.
- File changed:
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/projects_policy_test.go (new)
  - ralph/prd.json (BE-0120 passes=true)
  - ralph/progress.txt (this entry)
- The route uses a NIL ResourceResolver (routes.go), so RequireAuth
  authorizes against the principal's own home organization scope — there
  is NO path parameter that a foreign principal could fabricate to point
  the read at another tenant. The test file documents this structural
  difference vs. variables (which carries organizationIDResolver), and
  the wrong-organization principal test pins the cross-tenant boundary
  in two layers:
  1. Wire: an attacker in org_attacker hitting /v1/projects has the
     reader called with the attacker's home org id — never with a
     fabricated or caller-controlled value. The response body never
     echoes the victim's org id. This is the structural property that
     makes the endpoint cross-tenant-safe.
  2. Engine defence-in-depth: Decide(intruder, project.read,
     foreign_org_resource) -> ReasonDeniedCrossTenant; and
     Decide(support, project.read, foreign_org_resource) ->
     ReasonAllowedBySupport. These are engine-only pins today (no route
     constructs a foreign resource) so a future {org_id}-scoped project
     endpoint inherits a working cross-tenant deny + the support
     exception without re-deriving them.
- projects_policy_test.go covers:
  - TestListProjectsPolicyMatrixRoles: every built-in role
    (owner/admin/developer/viewer/ci/support) is allowed project.read
    via ReasonAllowedByRole (asserted at the engine level) and receives
    200 with the seeded two-project fixture in stable yalla.output.v1
    shape. Reader is reached with the principal's home org id, every
    column of the wire shape (project_id, organization_id, slug,
    display_name, version, created_at, updated_at) survives, and the
    fixture has two distinct versions (1, 2) so a future regression
    that hard-coded version=1 in the projection would fail here.
  - TestListProjectsPolicyRevokedAndExpiredKeys: a Disabled principal —
    even one whose underlying role is Owner — is rejected at the
    policy boundary with E_FORBIDDEN + ReasonDeniedPrincipalDisabled.
    Reader never runs; body never echoes the principal id, the org, or
    the seeded project ids/slugs/display names. Case names map 1:1 to
    the PRD principal classes ("revoked key", "expired key").
  - TestListProjectsPolicyWrongOrganizationPrincipal: the structural
    cross-tenant test described above. Wire-level: attacker sees only
    their own org via seenAttacker == ownOrg and body !~ victimOrg.
    Engine-level: ReasonDeniedCrossTenant for non-support owner of a
    foreign org, ReasonAllowedBySupport for Support of yalla on a
    foreign org. The Support engine clause is the same CapRead branch
    that fires for env.read / limits.read / usage.read.
  - TestListProjectsPolicyGrantContainment: all three PRD grant-
    containment properties pinned at the engine — sibling project
    (project grant does not reach proj_q via project.update),
    production environment (staging grant does not reach env_prod via
    environment.update), parent-level secrets (service grant does not
    reach env.write on the parent environment, and does not reach a
    sibling service). Tied back at the wire: the project-scoped key
    hitting /v1/projects at its OWN home org is 403 with
    ReasonDeniedOutOfScope (the scoped key cannot be widened to an
    organization-wide list — covers() is one-way), reader never reached,
    body does not leak the seeded project data. The org-level Viewer
    grant DOES cover the org-root scope (ReasonAllowedByGrant) and is
    allowed at the wire — this locks the CapRead requirement against
    the grant path so a future catalog change that upgraded project.read
    above CapRead would fail here (silently denying every org-level
    Viewer grantee) before it could regress a real customer. A final
    cross-tenant grant pin: the same project-grant carried by a
    principal whose home org is foreign is denied at the engine — a
    stolen scoped key cannot be smuggled across tenants by changing the
    principal's home org.
- Verification run:
  - gofmt -w internal/controlplane/httpapi/projects_policy_test.go
  - go vet ./internal/controlplane/httpapi/...
  - go test -run 'TestListProjectsPolicy' ./internal/controlplane/httpapi/...
  - go test ./...
  - go test -race ./...
  - go vet ./...
- Results: all green. Initial wire-level cross-tenant assertion failed
  because the canonical fakeProjectReader (from projects_test.go)
  returns its seeded projects unconditionally regardless of the org id
  it was called with — so seeding it with the victim's projects caused
  them to surface in the attacker's 200 OK body. Fixed by seeding the
  attacker reader with the attacker's OWN projects and asserting
  structural isolation differently: the reader is hardcoded by the
  handler to use principal.OrganizationID (seenAttacker == ownOrg), and
  the response body never echoes the victim's org id literal. This is
  the correct way to assert the property against a non-content-keyed
  fake — the production ProjectReader.ListByOrganization tenant-scopes
  by definition, so the handler-side contract under test is which org
  id the handler asks for, not what the fake returns.
- Remaining risks or follow-ups:
  - The CapRead role matrix here is identical to env.read / limits.read
    / usage.read in shape but with different fixtures. A future
    refactor could lift the matrix into a shared table-driven helper
    parameterised on (action, fixture, decoder) so a new CapRead
    endpoint can adopt the matrix in five lines; intentionally not
    done in this story to keep the change focused.
  - BE-0123 (POST /v1/projects policy matrix) is priority 1 but
    depends on BE-0121 (POST /v1/projects implement) — which is
    priority 2 and not yet implemented. The next iteration should
    pick BE-0121 to unblock BE-0122 (contract tests) + BE-0123
    (policy matrix). The shape of BE-0123 will diverge from this
    file: project.create is CapWrite (not CapRead), so the role
    matrix splits along the write capability class (viewer denies,
    Support denies cross-tenant), and the grant containment test
    must prove an organization-level Viewer grant is DENIED
    project.create (because the action requires CapWrite, which
    Viewer does not hold) — the inverse of the CapRead-allowed
    assertion in this file.
- **Learnings for future iterations:**
  - For nil-resolver list endpoints (organization-root scope of the
    principal's home org), the cross-tenant property is STRUCTURAL —
    there is no path parameter to forge. The policy matrix test still
    needs to pin (a) that the reader is hardcoded to the principal's
    home org id and (b) the engine-level cross-tenant verdict
    defence-in-depth, so a future {org_id}-scoped variant inherits a
    working cross-tenant deny. Do not try to assert a wire-level 403
    for a wrong-organization principal on a nil-resolver route — the
    route returns 200 with the principal's own org's projects, which
    is correct.
  - When asserting tenant isolation against a fake reader that returns
    its seeded fixture unconditionally (the canonical pattern in
    projects_test.go and variables_test.go for keyed-by-orgID fakes
    that only echo the call's org id), do not seed the fake with the
    victim's data — that data WILL surface in the body because the
    fake does not enforce the production tenant-scope. Instead seed
    with the principal's OWN org's data and prove (i) the reader was
    called with the right org id (seenSomething == expectedOrg) and
    (ii) the body does not echo the victim's org id literal. The
    production ProjectReader.ListByOrganization is tenant-scoped at
    the SQL layer; the handler-side contract under test is which org
    id the handler asks for, not what the fake returns.
  - The engine's same-tenant role-cap check fires BEFORE the
    cross-tenant Support exception, so a Support principal reading its
    OWN organization is allowed via ReasonAllowedByRole — NOT
    ReasonAllowedBySupport. The Support exception only applies when
    principal.OrganizationID != resource.Scope.OrganizationID. This
    matters for the matrix-roles test (which asserts
    ReasonAllowedByRole for support same-tenant) and for the
    wrong-organization test (which asserts ReasonAllowedBySupport for
    support cross-tenant). Asserting ReasonAllowedBySupport for support
    same-tenant would silently fail.
  - Mirror an existing policy matrix file exactly when adding the next
    one — variables_policy_test.go is the canonical CapRead matrix
    shape, projects_policy_test.go is now the canonical CapRead matrix
    shape for nil-resolver list endpoints. Future CapRead nil-resolver
    list endpoints (members, api-keys, etc.) should be derived from
    projects_policy_test.go's structure; future CapRead resolver-bound
    endpoints (variables, limits, usage) from variables_policy_test.go.
---

## 2026-05-15 - BE-0121
- What was implemented: POST /v1/projects, the create-project endpoint —
  the second customer-facing mutating endpoint in Yalla's
  Organization -> Project -> Environment -> Service hierarchy, and the
  unblock for BE-0122 (contract tests) + BE-0123 (policy matrix, priority 1).
  - internal/controlplane/store/projectservice.go: extended ProjectService
    to take an AuditAppender dependency and to file an immutable audit
    record inside the same transaction as the desired-state write. The
    fixed-order unit of work is now: authorize, reserve quota, INSERT
    project row, enqueue provisioning job, append audit. Every failure
    after validation rolls the whole transaction back, so a created
    project can never exist without its job AND audit trail. Added
    Actor*/Request*/Correlation* fields to CreateProjectInput so the
    httpapi handler can pass the resolved principal and request
    correlation through. NewProjectService rejects a nil audit appender at
    construction.
  - internal/controlplane/store/projectservice_test.go: extended the
    success test to assert the audit row commits with the right
    action/actor/correlation/metadata; added
    TestProjectServiceCreateRequiresActorOrg covering the wiring guard
    (an authenticated request that reaches the service with no actor
    organization is Internal before any database work runs). Threaded
    store.NewAuditRepository() through every existing NewProjectService
    call; added Actor*/correlation fields to the shared newCreateInput
    fixture; added listProjectAuditEvents helper mirroring the
    organizationservice_test.go pattern.
  - internal/controlplane/store/internal_test.go: added nopAuditAppender
    and extended TestNewProjectServiceRejectsNilDependencies to cover the
    new nil-audit guard.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/projects.go: added the ProjectCreator
    port, createProjectRequest/createProjectPayload DTOs,
    createProjectHandler, and errNoProjectCreator. The handler is thin —
    decode (strict, unknown-field rejected), read principal off context,
    forward to ProjectCreator with the principal's home org as both
    OrganizationID (the create target) and ActorOrgID (the audit tenant).
    A nil principal or nil creator surfaces as Internal; a missing creator
    can only happen through a wiring error. The request body intentionally
    does NOT include organization_id — the strict decoder rejects it as
    unknown-field, so a caller cannot point the write at another tenant.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/routes.go: registered POST /v1/projects
    with RequiresAuth=true, RequiredAction=policy.ActionProjectCreate
    (CapWrite), SuccessStatus=201, tagProjects, and a nil resolver — the
    created project does not exist yet, so the resource scope authorized
    against is the principal's home organization. Extended newRouteTable
    with a projectCreator ProjectCreator parameter.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/server.go: extended NewHandler with the
    projectCreator parameter, threaded into newRouteTable.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/projects_create_test.go (new): 11
    contract tests exercising the full NewHandler + real policy.NewEngine()
    (fake Authenticator + fake ProjectCreator only) — happy path 201 with
    every column projected onto the wire shape, principal scope forwarding
    (the creator is always called with the principal's home org and actor
    fields), unknown-field rejection of a smuggled organization_id (the
    structural tenant boundary), malformed JSON rejection, unauthenticated
    401, viewer-CapWrite 403 (the load-bearing distinction from CapRead:
    project.read is allowed for viewer but project.create is not),
    InvalidInput propagation, Conflict propagation, StoreUnavailable
    propagation (no driver-string leak), nil-creator wiring error, and
    OpenAPI route-table contract (operation_id=createProject,
    tag=projects, RequiredAction=project.create, RequiresAuth=true,
    SuccessStatus=201). Also added the canonical fakeProjectCreator.
  - 25 *_test.go files in internal/controlplane/httpapi/ received the
    mechanical churn: every NewHandler call gained a trailing
    fakeProjectCreator{} arg before its logger arg, and every
    newRouteTable call gained a trailing nil. Done by a one-shot Node.js
    rewriter against textual signatures; one site (projects_test.go
    listProjectsHandlerFor) used a named reader parameter rather than a
    literal fakeProjectReader{} and was patched by hand.
  - cmd/yalla-api/main.go: constructed the production *store.ProjectService
    and threaded it into httpapi.NewHandler. The Authorizer/QuotaReserver/
    JobEnqueuer ports are wired with documented placeholder adapters
    (alwaysAllowAuthorizer, noopQuotaReserver, noopJobEnqueuer) — the HTTP
    RequireAuth middleware is the authoritative gate for project.create
    today, and the real adapters land with their respective stories
    (project-quota plan resolver and project provisioning worker).
    store.NewProjectService still rejects nil dependencies, so the
    placeholders are explicit, not silently zero-valued.
- Files changed:

- Results: all green. goimports is not installed on this machine; gofmt
  and go vet are both clean.
- Remaining risks or follow-ups:
  - BE-0123 (priority 1, POST /v1/projects policy matrix) is now
    unblocked. The matrix shape diverges from BE-0120's nil-resolver list
    matrix: project.create is CapWrite, so the role matrix splits along
    the write capability class — viewer denies (the inverse of BE-0120's
    viewer-allows assertion), support denies even same-tenant, and a
    grant-only principal whose grants do not contain the home org denies.
    The principal classes the PRD names are owner/admin/developer/viewer/
    ci/support + revoked/expired keys + wrong-organization principal.
  - BE-0122 (contract tests) and BE-0123 (policy matrix) can be picked
    in either order; the contract tests are easier and the policy matrix
    benefits from the contract tests' fixtures.
  - The Authorizer/QuotaReserver/JobEnqueuer placeholders in main.go are
    correct-but-empty — they need real adapters when (a) the project
    quota plan resolver story lands and (b) the provisioning worker
    story lands. The HTTP RequireAuth middleware is the authoritative
    gate today; production traffic cannot bypass the policy.
- **Learnings for future iterations:**
  - When extending a unit-of-work service to record an audit event, add
    Actor{ID,Kind,OrgID} + RequestID/CorrelationID fields to the input
    struct; the httpapi handler fills them from
    policy.PrincipalFromContext + telemetry.FromContext. The audit row's
    OrganizationID is the actor's home org (the tenant the audit log
    lives under), while ResourceID is the resource being created. Reject
    a blank ActorOrgID as Internal — it is a wiring error, never a
    client error — before opening the transaction.
  - Existing test files with NewHandler call sites all share one
    signature, but a small minority pass named parameter variables (e.g.
    "reader" instead of "fakeProjectReader{}") in the last positional
    slot. A regex pass on literal "fakeProjectReader{}" handles 35/36
    sites; the holdout (listProjectsHandlerFor) needs a manual patch.
    Future additions to NewHandler's signature will face the same
    pattern.
  - The route table arity is now 26 (build + 25 readers/services). New
    newRouteTable test sites in newly-added test files must use 25 nils
    after BuildInfo{}. A one-line audit (counting nils in the source) is
    the fastest way to verify.
  - The strict JSON decoder (validate.DecodeJSON with disallowUnknownFields)
    is the load-bearing piece of structural tenant isolation for POST
    endpoints: the request body has no organization_id field, and a body
    that smuggles one is rejected as a 400 before the handler even reads
    the principal. The handler builds CreateProjectInput.OrganizationID
    from p.OrganizationID, never from the body, so a caller cannot point
    the write at another tenant even if the decoder were to accept the
    field. Both layers are required — defense-in-depth.
  - Placeholder Authorizer/Quota/Jobs adapters in main.go are correct as
    long as they are: (a) explicitly typed and named with a TODO-bearing
    comment, (b) constructed at startup so store.NewProjectService's nil
    guards still fire on a misconfigured binary, (c) never silently
    zero-valued (a nil dependency must remain a startup error). The
    pattern matches the way runtime.NewMeta is wired before the real
    migration probe lands.
