## 2026-05-15 - BE-0123
- What was implemented: policy matrix coverage for POST /v1/projects,
  the second policy-matrix file in the project family (after BE-0120's
  project.read matrix), and the first CapWrite matrix on a nil-resolver
  route (every prior CapWrite matrix — variables put/patch/delete —
  uses organizationIDResolver). project.create is CapWrite, so the
  matrix splits along the write capability class: Owner/Admin/Developer
  allow in own org (ReasonAllowedByRole); Viewer/CI/Support deny in own
  org (ReasonDeniedNoCapability); EVERY role denies cross-tenant
  (ReasonDeniedCrossTenant — CapWrite is outside the engine's support
  cross-tenant exception, unlike CapRead). Privileged Yalla support
  that needs to create a project on a customer's behalf goes through
  explicit break-glass admin tooling, never this customer-facing route.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/projects_create_policy_test.go (new):
    four tests drive the real NewHandler + real policy.NewEngine() —
    the production request path — so a regression in the middleware,
    the action catalog, or the engine fails here. The file adds two
    small fixtures (canonicalCreateProjectInput, canonicalCreatedProject)
    and one deny-leak guard (createProjectDenyBodyLeak) and otherwise
    consumes the shared scaffolding from projects_create_test.go
    (createProjectHandlerFor, postProjects, createProjectRequestBody,
    decodeCreateProject, decodeCreateProjectError, fakeProjectCreator)
    and the wider httpapi test fixtures (orgPrincipal, decodeError).
  - TestCreateProjectPolicyMatrixRoles is the role matrix proper. It
    runs every built-in role (owner/admin/developer/viewer/ci/support)
    through the full request path. Allow rows pin: 201 yalla.output.v1,
    creator reached with input.OrganizationID == principal home org,
    input.ActorOrgID == principal home org, input.ActorID == principal
    id (so the audit row names the actor verbatim), and the canned
    created project echoed back. Deny rows pin: 403 yalla.error.v1,
    error message contains the stable reason, creator MUST NOT run
    (gotInput.OrganizationID and gotInput.ActorID both unset), and the
    body never echoes any of the recognisable request-body or canned-
    project fields. Both the engine verdict and the wire verdict are
    asserted side by side so a future drift between the two fails here.
  - TestCreateProjectPolicyRevokedAndExpiredKeys covers the two PRD-
    named credential-disabled cases ("revoked key", "expired key").
    Both surface to the policy engine as Disabled=true; underlying role
    is Owner so a working credential WOULD allow it, ensuring Disabled
    is load-bearing. Asserts: 403, ReasonDeniedPrincipalDisabled in the
    message, creator never reached, body never echoes the principal id,
    the org id, or the request data.
  - TestCreateProjectPolicyWrongOrganizationPrincipal pins the cross-
    tenant boundary. The route uses a nil resolver and the strict JSON
    decoder rejects organization_id in the body, so the wire surface
    offers no path for a foreign principal to point the write at
    another tenant — tenant isolation is structural and the creator is
    ALWAYS called with the principal's home org id. The wire half
    proves an attacker still creates in its OWN org and that the
    response body never echoes the victim's org id. The engine half is
    a sub-test loop (cross_tenant_owner/admin/developer/viewer/ci/
    support) authorizing project.create against a foreign org Resource;
    every role — INCLUDING Support — denies via
    ReasonDeniedCrossTenant, because CapWrite is outside the engine's
    `CapSupport && (CapRead || CapSupport)` exception. This is the
    load-bearing distinction from BE-0120 (project.read), where the
    same Support cross-tenant probe is the documented ALLOW exception.
  - TestCreateProjectPolicyGrantContainment pins the three PRD-named
    grant-containment properties (sibling project, production
    environment, parent-level secrets) at the engine via project/env/
    service-scoped admin grants, then ties the property back to the
    wire by proving a project-scoped grantee is denied POST /v1/projects
    at its own home org with ReasonDeniedOutOfScope (creator never
    runs, body never echoes request data). The matrix additionally
    pins: (a) org-level VIEWER grant denies via
    ReasonDeniedNoCapability — the load-bearing distinction from
    BE-0120's project.read containment where an org-level Viewer grant
    is sufficient; (b) org-level DEVELOPER grant allows via
    ReasonAllowedByGrant, with the wire returning 201, the creator
    reached on the principal's home org id, and the actor id being the
    grantee's id verbatim; (c) a project-scoped grant whose carrier
    principal is planted in a foreign org denies — stealing a key
    cannot smuggle a write across tenants.
- Files changed:
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/projects_create_policy_test.go (new)
  - ralph/prd.json, ralph/progress.txt
- Verification run:
  - gofmt -w .
  - go mod tidy
  - go vet ./...
  - go test ./...
  - go test -race ./...
- Results: all green. goimports is not installed on this machine; gofmt
  and go vet are both clean. The new file is the only addition; no
  existing file changed.
- Remaining risks or follow-ups:
  - Next failing project-family stories: BE-0124 (implement GET
    /v1/projects/{project_id}) and BE-0126 (its policy matrix). The
    matrix shape will diverge again — GET /v1/projects/{project_id}
    will use a project-id resolver (not the nil resolver and not the
    org-id resolver), so the scoped-grant containment property
    inverts: a project-scoped Admin grant for THAT project is the
    allow path, while an org-scoped Viewer grant is the role-allowed
    path. The cross-tenant deny will be a 404 (path id not found in
    the principal's home org) rather than a 403 cross-tenant, since
    the resolver would not surface a foreign-org id.
  - The route description string in routes.go for POST /v1/projects
    asserts "owner, admin, developer, ci" as the admitted role set,
    but the actual policy engine denies CI (CI's capset is
    CapSelf+CapRead+CapDeploy, no CapWrite). The matrix here pins the
    engine's truth (CI denied). A future BE-0XXX could correct the
    description to "owner, admin, developer" so the OpenAPI document
    stops misleading agents reading the discovery surface; the matrix
    will catch the wire/catalog half of any such drift.
- **Learnings for future iterations:**
  - The CapWrite-on-nil-resolver matrix shape is structurally NEW in
    this codebase. Prior CapWrite matrices (variables put/patch/delete,
    organization patch) all use organizationIDResolver, so they prove
    "cross-tenant {org_id} denies as 403" at the WIRE. The project.create
    matrix proves the same property STRUCTURALLY: the wire offers no
    foreign-org path at all, so the only place the cross-tenant
    boundary can fail is the engine. The matrix therefore drops the
    wire-cross-tenant test and replaces it with a sub-test loop that
    walks every role through the engine's foreign-org Resource probe.
    Future CapWrite-on-nil-resolver endpoints (none yet planned, but
    BE-0103-style CapAdmin-on-nil-resolver may appear) can copy this
    shape verbatim.
  - The CapWrite-vs-CapRead policy distinction at the grant boundary
    is load-bearing: an org-level Viewer grant SATISFIES the scope
    requirement for project.create (covers() the org root) but FAILS
    the capability requirement (Viewer's capset doesn't include
    CapWrite). The engine reports this as ReasonDeniedNoCapability,
    not ReasonDeniedOutOfScope — distinguishing it from the project-
    scoped grant denial. Both denies are asserted in the matrix so a
    future engine regression that conflated the two reasons would
    fail here. The sister allow assertion — that an org-level
    Developer grant IS sufficient — locks the CapWrite requirement
    against the grant path: a future catalog change that downgraded
    project.create to CapRead would silently allow every org-level
    Viewer grantee to create projects, and the deny assertion here
    would fail before it could regress a customer.
  - The strict JSON decoder is doing structural work in the
    wrong-organization test: a foreign org id smuggled into the body
    is rejected as 400 (unknown field), so a non-attacker test (which
    posts a clean body) STILL never receives a path to point the
    write at another tenant. The matrix doesn't need a separate
    "body-injection cross-tenant" case because the contract suite
    (TestCreateProjectIgnoresOrganizationIDInBody) already pins that
    decoder behaviour. Cross-tenant on a nil-resolver POST is
    therefore the engine's job, not the wire's; the matrix probes
    the engine directly.
  - A deny-path leak guard for a CREATE endpoint needles for the
    REQUEST-body fields (caller-supplied) AND the canned-creator-
    output fields (server-side). Both are recognisable on purpose
    (prj_matrix_alpha / matrix-alpha / Matrix Alpha). A denied
    response that accidentally rendered request fields would mean the
    handler echoed user input after policy denial (a tenant boundary
    smell); a denied response that accidentally rendered creator
    output would mean the creator ran and the response leaked even
    though the wire said 403. Two needles, two different regressions
    — keeping them in one guard means a future leak in either
    direction fails the same assertion.
  - Subtest-loop pattern for engine-only probes inside a primarily-
    wire test: TestCreateProjectPolicyWrongOrganizationPrincipal
    opens with one wire probe (own-tenant 201) and then nests t.Run
    sub-tests for the cross-tenant engine probes per role. Each
    sub-test is t.Parallel() (each closes over its own tc shadow);
    the parent test is also t.Parallel() at the file level. Mixing
    one wire setup with a per-role engine sub-test loop keeps the
    test name surface useful (cross_tenant_owner/cross_tenant_admin/
    ...) and lets a failing role show up by name in test output.
---

## 2026-05-15 - BE-0124 - Implement GET /v1/projects/{project_id}
- Added the third project-family endpoint: GET /v1/projects/{project_id}.
  Route is wired through the existing newRouteTable single source of truth,
  registered after POST /v1/projects so OpenAPI lists list/create/get
  contiguously. Action: project.read (CapRead). Resolver:
  projectIDResolver — derives the policy.Resource from the principal's
  home organization id (read from the request context the auth
  middleware attached before the resolver runs) plus the {project_id}
  path parameter. The resolver never trusts a caller-supplied org id;
  cross-tenant project ids reach the tenant-scoped repository query
  with the principal's home org id and are reported as a deterministic
  404 by the persistence layer, never revealing another tenant's data.
- Persistence layer: extended *store.ProjectReader with GetProject(ctx,
  organizationID, projectID) (Project, error), opening a short-lived
  read-only transaction and delegating to the existing
  ProjectRepository.Get — which already does tenant-scoped filtering
  by (organization_id, id) and maps pgx.ErrNoRows to apierr.NotFound.
  Added a new integration test TestProjectReaderGetProject proving:
  (a) the owner of a project gets the row, (b) a cross-tenant id is
  reported as NotFound (E_NOT_FOUND), and (c) an unknown id is
  reported as NotFound. Validated against an isolated migrated
  Postgres via docker-compose postgres + YALLA_TEST_DATABASE_URL.
- HTTP layer: extended the ProjectReader interface in httpapi with
  GetProject (the list-and-get reader is now a single port, mirroring
  the OrganizationReader pattern). Added getProjectHandler,
  getProjectPayload, and projectIDResolver in projects.go. Extended
  the fakeProjectReader in projects_test.go with project/getErr/
  gotGetOrgID/gotGetProjectID fields so the new handler tests can
  assert which (org, project) pair was forwarded to the store
  without re-implementing the fake.
- Unit tests (new file projects_get_test.go, 11 tests):
  - TestGetProjectReturnsRequestedProject — happy path, full envelope
    shape, every wire field projected from the store row.
  - TestGetProjectForwardsHomeOrganizationAndPathID — proves the
    reader is called with principal.OrganizationID + path
    project_id, never with caller-controlled input.
  - TestGetProjectReturnsNotFoundForUnknownProject — typed 404
    E_NOT_FOUND.
  - TestGetProjectCrossTenantIDBehavesAsNotFound — cross-tenant id
    surfaces as 404 (not 403, not 200), and the response body does
    not echo the foreign organization id.
  - TestGetProjectRequiresBearerToken — 401 E_AUTH; reader sentinel
    proves the handler never ran.
  - TestGetProjectRejectsInvalidCredential — 401 E_AUTH on
    auth.ErrInvalidCredentials.
  - TestGetProjectRejectsDisabledPrincipal — 403 E_FORBIDDEN at the
    policy engine; reader never ran.
  - TestGetProjectAllowsOrganizationWideViewer — Viewer (the
    lowest CapRead-bearing role) is sufficient.
  - TestGetProjectRejectsGrantOnlyKeyForSiblingProject — a grant for
    prj_alpha cannot read prj_beta (containment is one-way).
  - TestGetProjectAllowsGrantOnlyKeyForTargetProject — a grant for
    prj_alpha can read prj_alpha (covers itself).
  - TestGetProjectPropagatesDependencyFailure — store outage is 5xx,
    never collapsed to 404; raw driver text never leaks.
- Files changed:
  - internal/controlplane/store/project.go — added
    *ProjectReader.GetProject (opens a read tx and delegates to the
    existing repository Get).
  - internal/controlplane/store/project_test.go — added
    TestProjectReaderGetProject (integration; tenant-scoped owner /
    cross-tenant 404 / unknown-id 404 paths).
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/projects.go — added domain import;
    extended ProjectReader interface with GetProject; added
    getProjectPayload, projectIDResolver, getProjectHandler.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/projects_test.go — extended
    fakeProjectReader with the by-id fields used by the new tests.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/routes.go — registered GET
    /v1/projects/{project_id} between POST /v1/projects and GET
    /v1/organizations/{org_id}, with projectIDResolver and an
    OpenAPI Endpoint that documents the path param, the required
    action, and the cross-tenant 404 contract.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/projects_get_test.go (new) —
    11 handler tests covering success, validation/auth/authz
    failure, not-found, dependency failure, sibling-project deny,
    target-project allow.
- Verification run (from repo root):
  - gofmt -w . — clean.
  - go vet ./... — clean.
  - go mod tidy — no go.mod / go.sum changes.
  - go test ./... (no DB) — all packages pass.
  - go test -race ./... (no DB) — all packages pass.
  - YALLA_TEST_DATABASE_URL=postgres://yalla:yalla@127.0.0.1:5432/yalla
    go test -run 'TestGetProject|TestProjectReaderGetProject|TestListProjects|TestProjectRepositoryGet|TestCreateProject'
    ./internal/controlplane/httpapi/ ./internal/controlplane/store/ — pass.
  - go test -race for the same set under DB — pass.
- Results: all green. goimports is not installed; gofmt formatted the
  changes cleanly. The 9 pre-existing TestAPIKeyService* /
  TestOrganizationVariableServicePatch* failures under
  YALLA_TEST_DATABASE_URL are still present on this branch — they
  reproduce on the parent commit (documented in the BE-0118
  progress entry) and are not regressions introduced by BE-0124.
- Remaining risks or follow-ups:
  - BE-0125 (contract tests for GET /v1/projects/{project_id}) and
    BE-0126 (policy matrix coverage for GET /v1/projects/{project_id})
    are the next failing project-family stories. BE-0125 will
    consolidate envelope-shape, request-id propagation, and the
    OpenAPI operation-registration assertions into a contract suite;
    much of what it requires already ships in projects_get_test.go.
    BE-0126 will add the deeper policy matrix (owner/admin/developer/
    viewer/ci/support, revoked key, expired key, wrong-org principal)
    against the new projectIDResolver scope shape — the containment
    inversion noted in the BE-0123 follow-up: a project-scoped Admin
    grant for THAT project is the load-bearing happy path, where the
    list endpoint's matrix required an organization-level grant.
  - PATCH /v1/projects/{project_id} (BE-0127) can reuse projectIDResolver
    as-is; the store layer already has ProjectRepository.UpdateDisplayName
    with the If-Match optimistic-concurrency contract wired and
    integration-tested.
- **Learnings for future iterations:**
  - Resolver pattern for project-id and deeper-scoped endpoints:
    *do NOT* try to read the project's organization from the database
    inside the resolver — keep the resolver pure path+context. The
    correct trick is to pull the principal's home OrganizationID from
    the request context the auth middleware attached BEFORE the
    resolver runs (policy.PrincipalFromContext(r.Context()) — yes,
    the ResourceResolver signature is func(r *http.Request)
    policy.Resource but the context already carries the principal).
    Then the resolver returns Scope{OrganizationID: principal.HomeOrg,
    ProjectID: r.PathValue("project_id")}. Cross-tenant attempts are
    absorbed by the tenant-scoped repository query (404), not by the
    policy engine (which sees the principal's own org). This is the
    same shape PATCH/DELETE /v1/projects/{project_id} should adopt;
    keep the test "TestGetProjectCrossTenantIDBehavesAsNotFound" as
    the canonical regression check that this stays true.
  - The store reader-adapter pattern is now codified for project:
    *Reader composes *Repository, never issues its own SQL, opens a
    short-lived read tx per call via Store.Read. New methods on the
    reader should be a thin transactional wrapper around an existing
    Querier-accepting repository method so the tenant-scoping
    guarantees proved in the repository's integration tests are
    inherited for free. Mirrors OrganizationReader.GetOrganization
    and ProjectReader.ListProjects exactly.
  - When extending an httpapi port interface that fakes already
    satisfy in test files, prefer extending the fake in-place over
    duplicating it. The fakeProjectReader now carries both List and
    Get state (projects/err/gotOrgID for list, project/getErr/
    gotGetOrgID/gotGetProjectID for get); the zero value still works
    everywhere the public-surface and unrelated route suites used
    it, so no cross-test churn was needed.
  - auth.ErrInvalidCredentials (plural; not ErrInvalidCredential).
    The middleware writeAuthError matches by errors.Is on the
    pluralised sentinel; tests for endpoints that exercise the
    invalid-credential path should use the same name.
  - For dependency-failure tests, the message-redaction assertion
    (substring check on raw driver text like "connection refused")
    catches accidental leakage cheaply and is a good pattern to
    propagate to every new endpoint test suite. It complements the
    typed-status check rather than replacing it.
---

## 2026-05-15 - BE-0126 - Add policy matrix coverage for GET /v1/projects/{project_id}
- What was implemented: policy-matrix coverage for the deep-resource read
  endpoint GET /v1/projects/{project_id}. Where projects_get_test.go pins
  the wire contract (BE-0124), the new file
  internal/controlplane/httpapi/projects_get_policy_test.go pins the
  authorization contract: that action project.read cannot be bypassed by —
  or leak data because of — the principal's role, revoked credentials,
  home organization, or scoped grants. Four tests, all running through
  the real NewHandler + real policy.NewEngine() (production request
  path):
    * TestGetProjectPolicyMatrixRoles — every built-in role drives the
      route in its own org. All six roles hold CapRead, so the matrix is
      "all allow"; each subtest pins the engine reason
      (ReasonAllowedByRole), proves the reader is called with the
      principal's home org id AND the {project_id} path parameter, and
      asserts the response carries the canonical project's full
      projectResource.
    * TestGetProjectPolicyRevokedAndExpiredKeys — Disabled principal
      whose underlying role would have allowed it: 403 carries
      ReasonDeniedPrincipalDisabled, the reader never runs, and the
      denied body never echoes the principal id, the organization id,
      or the canonical project's identifiers.
    * TestGetProjectPolicyWrongOrganizationPrincipal — wire-level AND
      engine-level cross-tenant. Wire: a foreign project_id reaches the
      reader with the attacker's home org id (the resolver pins
      Scope.OrganizationID to principal.OrganizationID, never the
      caller) and the tenant-scoped store returns NotFound — surfacing
      as a deterministic 404, never 200 with foreign data and never 403
      that would confirm existence. Body must not echo the foreign org
      id. Engine: pins ReasonDeniedCrossTenant for every non-support
      role and ReasonAllowedBySupport for Support (CapRead is INSIDE
      the engine's cross-tenant exception), as defence-in-depth for a
      future endpoint that constructs a resource with a foreign-org
      scope.
    * TestGetProjectPolicyGrantContainment — scoped grants. Engine: a
      project-level Admin grant naming THIS project allows project.read
      (ReasonAllowedByGrant), but a SIBLING project denies via
      ReasonDeniedOutOfScope; an env-level grant denies project.read on
      the parent project (covers() is one-way); a service-level grant
      denies project.read on the parent project AND env.write on the
      parent environment AND service.update on a sibling service; an
      org-level Viewer grant allows project.read on any project
      (Viewer holds CapRead, ReasonAllowedByGrant). Wire ties: the
      project grantee on the target project receives the project; the
      same grantee on a sibling project, an env-scoped grantee on the
      parent project, and a service-scoped grantee on the parent
      project all 403 with the stable out-of-scope reason, the reader
      never runs, and the canonical project's id/slug/display name
      never appear in the body. The org-level Viewer grantee is
      end-to-end allowed at the wire.
- Files changed:
    * internal/controlplane/httpapi/projects_get_policy_test.go (new,
      pure test file, no production code).
    * ralph/prd.json — flipped BE-0126.passes from false to true.
    * ralph/progress.txt — this entry.
- Verification run: gofmt -w ., go mod tidy (no diff), go vet ./...,
  go test ./..., go test -race ./.... All passed. goimports not
  installed (documented as a no-op).
- Results: 0 failures, every existing test still passes. The new file
  drives the real NewHandler + real policy.NewEngine() so a regression
  in the action catalog, the projectIDResolver, builtinRoleCaps, the
  engine's cross-tenant or grant clauses, or the wire-renderer fails
  here.
- Remaining risks or follow-ups: none for this story. BE-0127 (PATCH
  /v1/projects/{project_id}), BE-0128 (DELETE /v1/projects/{project_id}),
  and similar deep-resource policy matrices are next; they can copy
  this file's shape verbatim because the resolver scope is identical —
  swap project.read for the relevant CapWrite/CapAdmin action and
  re-derive the role allow/deny split from defaultActionCatalog (a
  CapWrite policy matrix on a project-level resource would look like
  this file with Viewer/CI/Support denying via ReasonDeniedNoCapability
  and Support's cross-tenant exception withdrawn, mirroring the
  CapWrite-on-nil-resolver shape in projects_create_policy_test.go but
  on a deeper resource).
- Learnings for future iterations:
  - The "deep-resource resolver" policy matrix (project_id, future
    environment_id, service_id) is the third distinct shape after
    nil-resolver (POST /v1/projects, projects_create_policy_test.go)
    and organizationIDResolver (env.write/env.read on {org_id}). The
    key load-bearing distinction is that the resource scope is built
    from BOTH the principal's home org AND the path id — so the
    cross-tenant clause CANNOT fire on the wire (same-tenant by
    construction), and tenant-isolation for a foreign path id is
    enforced at the PERSISTENCE layer as a 404, not at the policy
    boundary as a 403. The test must pin both: the wire 404 with no
    foreign-tenant body echo, and the engine 403/allow defence-in-depth
    for a hypothetical foreign-scope resource. Both are necessary
    because the wire surface can never reach the engine's cross-tenant
    clause through this endpoint, but a future admin-tool variant
    might.
  - A project-level resource is "reachable" from an org-level grant
    (org scope covers everything in it) AND from a project-level grant
    naming THIS project. It is NOT reachable from env- or
    service-level grants (covers() is one-way). The grant matrix at
    this depth therefore has a richer allow-by-grant row than the
    nil-resolver matrix (where ONLY an org-level grant could ever
    cover the org root): a project-level Admin grant allows, an
    org-level Viewer grant allows (both via ReasonAllowedByGrant),
    while env-/service-/sibling-project grants all deny via
    ReasonDeniedOutOfScope. The wire suite must exercise the
    target-project allow row alongside the sibling-project deny —
    they're different engine paths even though the principal and the
    handler look identical.
  - The fakeProjectReader was already extended for GET by BE-0124
    (project / getErr / gotGetOrgID / gotGetProjectID). No fake-fixture
    extension was needed for this story — the existing leak-guard
    pattern (canonical canonical*Project + *DenyBodyLeak) carries
    cleanly. Future deep-resource matrices that reuse a multi-route
    fake should adopt the same convention: keep capture pointers
    distinct per route (got*OrgID, got*ProjectID, got*EnvID,
    got*ServiceID) so a single fake satisfies multiple route ports
    without leaking state between them.
  - listProjectsHandlerFor wires both the list AND get routes (it
    builds the full NewHandler stack with the same ProjectReader). The
    helper name has historic baggage from when only the list route
    existed; the get-route policy tests reuse it as-is rather than
    introducing a "projectsHandlerFor" sibling, matching the pattern
    BE-0124 established. A future cosmetic rename should sweep both
    suites at once.
  - Variable named projectResource shadows the unexported
    projectResource struct from projects.go inside the test function
    scope. This is legal Go and harmless here (the test never refers
    to the type by name in that function — it accesses fields through
    env.Data.Project), but it's a smell worth avoiding in future
    suites. Prefer a non-colliding local name like
    `projectScopeResource` or `targetResource` in new files.
  - apierr.NotFound(kind, id) is the canonical way to make a fake
    persistence port return a typed 404. It maps to E_NOT_FOUND
    through the apierr -> apienvelope plumbing; tests should decode
    against that code, and assert the foreign tenant's org id does
    not appear in env.Error.Message (the persistence layer must
    redact foreign identity even when the request body is empty).
---

## 2026-05-15 - BE-0127
- What was implemented: PATCH /v1/projects/{project_id}, the partial-update
  endpoint for projects through the Yalla control-plane API. The store-layer
  orchestrator commits desired-state and the immutable audit record in one
  transaction; the HTTP layer is thin and threads the principal's home
  organization + path project_id into the store, never trusting a
  caller-supplied org id.
  - internal/controlplane/store/project.go: added ProjectRepository.Update
    (slug + display_name, tenant scoped, *Tx-only mutation, optimistic
    concurrency via ifMatchVersion *int64) and classifyProjectConcurrencyMiss
    (disambiguate gone vs stale on the version-checked UPDATE returning zero
    rows — runs inside the same tx so the disambiguation is consistent with
    the failed UPDATE). Refactored existing UpdateDisplayName to share the
    classifier so a future caller cannot regress the disambiguation path.
  - internal/controlplane/store/projectservice.go: added UpdateProjectInput
    (OrganizationID, ProjectID, *Slug, *DisplayName, *IfMatchVersion,
    Actor*, RequestID, CorrelationID), ProjectService.Update orchestrator,
    buildProjectUpdate validator (split out + unit-testable, rejects empty
    patches as 400 InvalidInput), validateProjectDisplayName (mirrors
    organization display-name rules: blank, UTF-8, control chars, max length).
    Added project.update action constant and projectDisplayNameMaxLen.
  - internal/controlplane/store/project_test.go: 4 new integration tests —
    TestProjectRepositoryUpdateRejectsNilTx (nil-tx structural guard),
    TestProjectRepositoryUpdateApplies (slug+display_name commit + trigger
    version bump), TestProjectRepositoryUpdateNotFoundIsTenantScoped
    (cross-tenant id surfaces as NotFound, never another tenant's row),
    TestProjectRepositoryUpdateStaleVersion (ConflictStale carries
    current_version under Details), and TestProjectRepositoryUpdateDuplicateSlugConflict
    (UNIQUE (organization_id, slug) is authoritative on Update).
  - internal/controlplane/store/projectservice_test.go: 5 new integration
    tests covering the service orchestrator — TestProjectServiceUpdateSuccess
    (the row mutates AND the audit event commits in one tx, metadata records
    "updated_fields"=slug,display_name without leaking values),
    TestProjectServiceUpdateRejectsEmptyPatch (validation runs before tx is
    opened), TestProjectServiceUpdateNotFound, TestProjectServiceUpdateStaleIfMatch
    (audit count is unchanged on stale rejection — no orphaned audit row),
    TestProjectServiceUpdateInvalidSlug (validation rejects before tx).
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/projects.go: added ProjectUpdater port,
    updateProjectRequest DTO (*string Slug/DisplayName, NO organization_id),
    updateProjectPayload, updateProjectHandler — reuses parseIfMatchVersion +
    writeOrganizationETag (the helper is body-generic; future endpoints can
    use it as-is or rename the helper later). The handler stays thin: read
    principal, parse If-Match, strict DecodeJSON, call port, write ETag,
    render.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/routes.go: added the PATCH /v1/projects/{project_id}
    apiRoute with RequiredAction=policy.ActionProjectUpdate and
    resolver=projectIDResolver (same deep-resource resolver the GET-by-id
    endpoint uses). Extended newRouteTable signature with projectUpdater
    ProjectUpdater parameter; updated the doc comment.
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/server.go: extended NewHandler signature
    with projectUpdater ProjectUpdater (passed through to newRouteTable).
  - cmd/yalla-api/main.go: wired projectService as the projectUpdater (the
    same store.ProjectService that already backs the creator implements
    Update too — one orchestrator, two ports).
  - internal/controlplane/httpapi/projects_update_test.go (new): 8 contract
    tests in package httpapi driving the real NewHandler + real policy.NewEngine()
    (fake Authenticator + fake ProjectUpdater) — happy-path forwarding
    (org_id from principal, project_id from path, both Slug and DisplayName
    threaded as *string, ETag mirrors version), If-Match parsing (absent,
    strong, unquoted-lenient, whitespace-stripped), stale-version 409 with
    details.current_version, body-injected organization_id rejection,
    malformed JSON, NotFound, missing-credentials 401, invalid If-Match
    matrix (weak ETag, wildcard, multi-value, non-numeric, zero). Added a
    decodedProjectErrorEnvelope alongside the package-level errorEnvelope
    because the central decodeError doesn't expose the Hint/Details fields
    a concurrency-aware test needs (same pattern as decodeOrganizationError
    in organizations_concurrency_test.go).
  - 27 test files across httpapi/: extended each NewHandler call site with
    fakeProjectUpdater{} just after fakeProjectCreator{} (the new arg slot).
    7 files with raw newRouteTable(BuildInfo{}, nil...) call sites had one
    more nil appended. fakeProjectUpdater itself lives in the new
    projects_update_test.go alongside its contract tests.
- Verification run: gofmt -w ., goimports (not installed; documented),
  go mod tidy, go vet ./... (clean), go build ./... (clean), go test
  ./internal/controlplane/httpapi/... -count=1 (PASS), go test -race
  -count=1 -run "TestUpdateProject|TestProjectRepository|TestProjectService"
  ./internal/controlplane/httpapi/... ./internal/controlplane/store/... (PASS
  with race), go test ./... (BE-0127 tests all PASS; pre-existing failures in
  TestAPIKeyServiceCreate* and TestOrganizationVariableServicePatch* on HEAD
  are confirmed unrelated — reproduced by stashing the BE-0127 change).
- Remaining risks / follow-ups:
  - writeOrganizationETag's name is misleading now that two resource families
    use it; a future incidental refactor could rename to writeETag without
    changing behaviour. Skipped here per "do not refactor beyond the task".
  - The pre-existing TestAPIKeyServiceCreate* / TestOrganizationVariableServicePatch*
    failures should be addressed by a future story; they appear to be
    testutil.Factory slug/id format vs domain.ParseSlug/ParseID format drift.
- Learnings for future iterations:
  - When extending the NewHandler signature, batch the test-file updates as
    a single message with many parallel Edit tool uses (replace_all=true).
    The unique anchor `fakeProjectCreator{}, nil)` / `fakeProjectCreator{}, logger)`
    works for most call sites; named-parameter helpers (`creator, nil)`,
    `updater, nil)`, etc.) need their own anchor. Always grep for the
    `fakeProjectReader{}` anchor too, because the named-param wrappers
    follow it.
  - The 25-nil `newRouteTable(BuildInfo{}, nil, ...)` raw-args pattern in 7
    test files is a maintenance smell. A helper `newEmptyRouteTable(t)` would
    let the signature evolve without churning every file. Out of scope here,
    but worth a small future refactor.
  - For partial-update endpoints, the buildXUpdate helper (organization,
    project) is the same shape every time: detect nil fields, push field
    names onto `fields` for the audit metadata, validate non-nil values
    with apierr.FieldViolation, error on empty-patch with a stable "at
    least one of X or Y must be provided" violation. Future endpoints
    (environment.update, service.update) should copy this shape and the
    `change.fields = append(change.fields, "...")` audit-metadata pattern
    verbatim.
  - testutil.Factory.Project's slug is `f.slug(label, "project", n)` —
    NOT literally the label. A test that needs the existing slug to assert
    a duplicate-slug conflict must capture the inserted row's Slug field,
    not assume the label is the slug.
  - parseIfMatchVersion + writeOrganizationETag in organizations.go are
    already generic in body — they care only about a version int64, not
    the resource kind. New endpoints can call them directly. A future
    rename (writeETag) would clarify the contract.
